  - A quest is `produced-by` the ask its sender names, and the ask `produced` it. A quest `follows` its chain's step before
    and `awaits` its D79 question.
  - Inverses are kept where both ends were read. A quest unit holds none of its branch units' entries.
- **Read-only:** `WorkUnits.ReadAsync` gathers the home as MILESTONE1a does and asks the service three GETs: every quest, every
  ask and every session record. Without the service, or where it does not answer, the branch units come alone, and `unread`
  says so; that is never read as there being no quests.
- **What the milestone reads through:** `WorkMilestone.Of(unit)` reads the cleanup, the result and the recovery from any unit.
  `WorkInventory.Derive` is now the branch units' milestones, so `WORK_STATE`, `work state`, `trees state` and *Where work has
  got to* read through the projection. Their answers are unchanged: `WorkInventoryTests`, `WorkWordsTests`, `DriverModuleLinesTests`
  and `HelpRoomWorkTests` pass as they were.
- **No door yet.** Quest and ask units reach no route and no verb; NODE1 is their first reader.
- **Proof:** `WorkUnitsTests` (fast):
  - the milestone read from the unit, field for field;
  - retirement, and an unknown owner never retiring;
  - each quest status and each ask state;
  - the evidence pointers;
  - the links and their inverses, and a landing carrying several units linking none;
  - a quest's milestone read as a branch unit's is;
  - the read asking only by GET, writing nothing in the home, and saying what it could not read.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-NODE1, one node contract and its state table).** The design's §E2, with §E4's observed built-ins, for the
owner's *"each step can have its own state, so we don't have any difference between different types of steps"*. It is pure, in
`Trees/Nodes/` beside the work unit (`Trees/Inventory/`): nothing is stored, no wire is evaluated, and no graph is read.

- **The contract** (`NodeContract.cs`): `NodeDeclaration { Id, Kind, Version, Reach, Inputs, Outputs, Evidence, Retry, TimeoutSeconds,
  Rejectable }`, ports as `NodePort { Schema, Ref, Revision, Acceptance }` (a record and a name, never a path), and
  `NodeDeclarations.Problem`, the node's own half of §D4's checks. The unit a node acts on is its status's `Unit`, a `WorkUnit` id.
- **One table** (`NodeLifecycle.Table`): §E2's rows, expanded over the live and the bound states, 29 in all. `Apply` is the only judge
  of a move, for every kind. **What differs by kind is only `NodeAuthorities`**: the `NodeSource` that reaches it, the one that fails
  it (none for git's proofs and the person's kinds, so not yet stays pending), whether a person may reject or skip it, and whether it
  may be the end. Daoris never reports an outcome, so a deadline cannot fail a node. The agent's claim, `ledger`, reaches `claimed`
  alone.
- **Retries:** `MaxAttempts` counts attempts on one binding, the first included; none declared is one. Only a failure declared
  transient retries without the person. The person's new attempt and a fresh binding count afresh, while the attempt number only
  grows, since an acceptance binds it.
- **Acceptances** (`NodeAcceptances.cs`): the id is a digest of §E2's binding (`acc-` and 32 hex), so the same binding is the same
  acceptance and a replay records it once. The evidence and the outputs are not part of the binding. `Invalidate` walks prerequisite
  receipts and inputs' acceptances breadth first, across units, naming each once with why. It removes nothing.
- **The built-ins observed** (`BuiltInNodes.cs`): `claimed` on a quest unit, and `handed`, `pushed`, `pull-request` and `line` on a
  branch unit. Each is read from `WorkUnit.Records`, the entries UNIT1a's adapters already read (the session branch's entry, the
  landings carrying it, the quest), never joined twice. The observation is a move through the same `Apply`. Each fact keeps its one
  writer. An observed acceptance binds the commit its evidence names (the landing's tip, the pushed tip, the held tip); comparing it
  with the live tip is git's, at the reader.
- **Choices §E2 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. *Live* is blocked, ready, active, unknown and invalidated: every state with no settled outcome, so the person may stop each.
  2. *Bound* is active, reached, failed, rejected and unknown: an attempt or its outcome. A skip and a stop are the person's word
     for this work, not for a revision, so a new tip keeps them. *Changed by FABRIC-NODE1c:* a skip holds for its graph and run
     alone (`NodeScope`), another graph or run lapses it (skipped to invalidated, by Daoris), and the person may withdraw it
     (skipped to blocked). A stop still survives a new tip.
  3. **Skips:** never on `claimed`, `handed` or `line`, since *held* and the cleanup rest on git's proofs and a skip would read as
     evidence. Never on `quest.close`, whose override is the person's own done (§E5), nor on an `all` join, whose members are
     skipped instead. Every other kind may be skipped. *Changed by FABRIC-NODE1c:* a plugin's own node may be skipped unless its
     declaration forbids it (`Skippable: false`); a built-in's skip stays its kind's, so a built-in declaration that sets it is
     refused. D157's group skip is untouched for WORKFLOW2's decoding.
  4. **Reconciliation:** the gate is the start (ready to active). It is refused while any earlier attempt's unknown outcome is open,
     whatever came between: a stop, the person's ask, an invalidation. `Reconciled` lifts it once that attempt's outcome is
     recorded as a stale attempt's (§D3). *Changed by FABRIC-NODE1c:* a dispatched attempt (a performed or waited reach) holds the
     gate from its start until its authority's outcome, whether or not it went unknown, so a stop, skip, rejection or invalidation
     while it runs cannot start a duplicate.
  5. **Derived, not stored.** An observed built-in's state is derived at each read from its records (§C7: one store each, *ended*
     never stored). A performed, pressed or waited node's attempts go on the unit's canonical record (§E7, *unit records keep §D6's
     attempts*), with the driver as their one writer under the home's lease. The first row that starts an attempt writes them
     (PERFORM1a, HUMAN1, WAITUNIT1).
  6. `look` is observed (§E4: its gate), reached by the person's *reviewed*, though §E2's authority row lists it with the pressed
     kinds. An `all` join is observed over its members.
  7. A held done is still `claimed`: the hold waits on the person, but the claim is in.
  8. **Records alone leave two outcomes unknown.** `pushed` needs git's remote-tracking ref. A `pull-request` completed from a newer
     commit needs git's ancestry. A reader hands both in as `NodeGitReads`. The landing entry keeps no failed push, so `pushed`
     never fails from the records.
  9. A retry declared on a kind nothing can fail is refused, and so is a built-in's declaration granting itself a person's rejection.
     Only a plugin's own node declares `Rejectable`.
- **The tidy is no node** (§E9, *deletion driven by a graph*). What it removed stays the inventory's (§D3).
- **Not built here:** wires and eligibility (WIRE1a, WIRE1b), `work.node`'s emission, persisted deadlines, D157's `opinion`, `work`
  and `go-ahead` read from their gates' records, and a reader handing in the git reads.
- **Proof** (fast): `NodeLifecycleTests` (the table row for row; canary 43, every kind through every row and every move outside the
  table refused; authority, retries, reconciliation, the declaration's checks), `NodeAcceptancesTests` (the binding's id, the lapse,
  transitive invalidation across units, a node invalidated through the table) and `BuiltInNodesTests` (each observed built-in from
  records alone; pending never failed; an unknown owner unknown).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-EVENT3b, the service's change stream).** §A4.2 is built as revised. `ChangeStream`
(`Service.Core/Platform/ChangeStream.cs`) is one sampler on an observer connection of its own, unpooled and apart from the
store's. It reads `PRAGMA data_version` only while a wait is held, every 250 ms and at each wait's arrival, so it hears the
host's own commits and a connector's from its own process. When the counter moves it reads each family's revision in one
autocommit read:

- **`sessions`** is `session_revision.revision`, the highest revision issued. It moves on every write that issues a
  record's revision: an open, a state, a mirror, a took, and a note kept with words. It does not move on words kept or
  taken without a note, a push's mark or the cursors.
- **`quests`** is the newest `quest_log.position`. It moves on every operation kept, whether made here or integrated
  from a remote. It does not move on a pass's record (`quest_passes`, with its `tried`), a cursor, a push's numbers, a
  rebase's rewrite in place, or a delete of older operations.

`channel` stays on the look's clock until its check, and so does any change neither revision carries. A delete and an
insert that reuse the newest position between two samples is missed, and the deadline catches it.

`GET /api/changes?since=&wait=` is `Open` in `PersonDoors` and is mapped by a local host only. At most 8 waits are held.
A ninth is answered at once with what the last sample counted, and it never samples itself, so it cannot queue behind
a sample that a writer's lock holds. A wait lasts 30 s at most, counted from its arrival. The host's stop answers a held
wait, and a wait is never logged as slow.

The epoch is new at the start, after a lost read reconnects (`changes.lost`, then `changes.epoch` with `reconnected`),
and when the database at the path is another. It is also new when the observer first opens after a cursor already went
out (`opened`), so a start whose open failed never takes what moved meanwhile as its baseline.

On Windows, the observer's open handle keeps a replacement out, because SQLite shares no delete. On other systems the
path is read apart every 30 s. The database there is another if its stamp differs or is missing (`store_identity`,
written once into the file by the observer). It is also another if its revisions differ from the observer's while the
observer's held still around the read, since a copy carries the stamp. .NET answers no inode, so that comparison stands
in for the file's identity.

Two cases are left to the reconciliation deadline, so they wait at most `pollSeconds` (15 s by default):

- A copy of the current state put in place with nothing written since. The first write that reaches only one of the two
  files tells them apart at the next read of the path, within 30 s.
- A restore made in place that leaves every family's revision as it was.

The service has no restore or import path for its database. The only move of `knowledge.db` is the install's home being
established at the desktop's start (`InstallHome.Establish`), before any host opens the file.

The driver's `LedgerChanges` holds one wait for the loop's life (`wait=20`, under the client's 30 s timeout). One nudge
per answer wakes the `ledger` topic, with the families joined as its subject, and runs the plan alone.

The bootstrap wakes the plan once too. It is asked beside the loop's first look, not before it, so its cursor may
already hold a quest published after that look read the quests. Without the wake, the quest would wait for the deadline.
The cost is one plan at the start; a pass that moves nothing still wakes nothing after it.

Back-off is 1 s doubling to 30 s for a refused or dropped ask and for an empty answer that came early, a host without
the door is asked again after a minute, and asks are never less than 250 ms apart.

`ChangeStreamTests` holds canary 5: two sync passes, one of them pushing what the machine made, are seen and move no
family. `ChangeStreamProcessTests` holds a connector's write from another process, and `DriverWatchTopicsTests` holds
an agent's quest that starts with no nudge under `pollSeconds: 60`.

**Note, 2026-10-19 (PUSH1d1, the asks and the channel join the stream; D182 point 5).** Two families are added. Each is a count
kept by triggers in the file (`AskStore`, `ChannelStore`), so a connector built before them still moves it, and an older process
opening the file creates nothing that is already there:

- **`asks`** is `ask_revision.revision`. It moves on every insert, update and delete of an ask: an ask kept or saved again,
  published, closed, served by an intake, a word, a choice or a proposal appended, its go-aheads decided, and deleted. A write
  that finds no row (an id no ask has, go-aheads decided to stay as they are) moves nothing.
- **`channel`** is `channel_revision.revision`. It moves on every message kept (a post, or a teammate's mirrored by a sync pass),
  changed or deleted. The newest `seq` would miss an ask's channel forgotten with the ask, which deletes older messages. It does
  not move on a read mark (one reader's place, moved by a read and by the driver as a part starts), a sync's cursor, a
  teammate's message mirrored again, or a post its session's cap refused.

Canary 5 holds with the channel's sync pass beside the other two and an ask in place. `ChangeStreamTests` holds each family's
every write path and the writes that change nothing; a bare statement, from a connection that never opened the store, stands in
for an older connector. `ChangeStreamProcessTests` holds an ask written from another process waking a held wait.

A rebase's rewrite in place keeps no revision of its own. Every rewrite runs inside `IntegrateAsync`, in the commit that kept
the remote's winning operation, which moves the newest position (`ChangeStreamTests` holds a lost take rewritten as a conflict
moving `quests`). The one rewrite with nothing kept is a stray an older build left pending on a lost take (WAITCLAIM4), rewritten
once; the deadline catches it.

Words kept or taken without a note stay off `sessions`: they never travel, and its revision is the push cursor's. A word the
person says comes through the window's door, which nudges the loop. A helper's or a second opinion's word, kept by a connector
(`HelperDesk`, `OpinionDesk`), waits for the look; a family of its own would have to move on a word kept and not on the take a
look makes, and is left as a proposed row.

The driver's wake names the families its answer named (`sessions,quests,asks,channel` at a bootstrap). `HelpFacts.Woken` wakes
the history's fact on `asks` too, since the history counts the asks kept; every ledger move already woke the machine's fact.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-EVENT6, the knowledge server's freshness).** The service's `CheckoutFreshness` is its own small `FileSystemWatcher` set, not the driver's `ChangeWatch`: that lives in the driver the desktop hosts, and the service sits beneath it, so it cannot reference it, and a flag needs no topic queue. It watches the served checkout's top-level records, `.claude/`, `docs/`, the named documents and index, HEAD, `packed-refs` and `refs/heads/**` (a worktree's through its `gitdir` and `commondir`). An event only marks the reading stale; the next search reads, taking the mark as the read begins. An overflow marks stale; any other watcher error marks stale and drops the checkout back to the one-minute window. A watched reading is also read again at a 5-minute deadline (the low end of the 5-10 min: a silenced watch costs at most 5 minutes of stale answers, and a 1.2-1.7 s read is paid at most once a window). Unwatched ground: nested rooms' own `AGENTS.md`, and a folder made after start, both left to the deadline.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-END4a, the default cleanup as link 2).** The design's §C3, §C6 link 2 and §D3, for the default cycle
(`until: held`); the configured wait and the result per cycle are END4c's.

- **The candidates are the kept facts.** `AutoTidies.TidyEmptyAsync` keeps the repository's ends first, as before, then takes only
  the session branches and landing branches whose `HeldFact` holds at the tip they stand at now and names the line the tidy reads
  (read from the refs already taken: no git start). The press's list judges those alone; every other branch is no longer judged
  at each pace. Each still goes only through the press's path, judged again inside the hold and once more right before it goes
  (`TreeSetAside.LastLookAsync`), with every guard. A branch the look leaves, held or not, still leans on a landing's branch (D102).
  The pace's pass and link 2's tries tidy one repository at a time in a process.
- **The effect.** `Trees/Branches/TidyEffect.cs` is link 2's `IChainEffect`, bound on the ref watch's chain beside link 1, so a
  `work.held` tries that repository in the same wake. Pending is read through MILESTONE1a's inventory over the records alone, with
  no git: a unit whose end is held with a session or landing branch standing. The chain's subject is the repository link 1 names;
  what is acted on is that repository's held units. An empty branch whose tree stands is a conversation's place (D137), the
  press's, so it is not pending and no wake retries it for nothing.
- **The wakes.** The loop hands the ref watch `ledger` (a session ending, a ledger or requests nudge), `press` (a plain nudge) and
  `home` (the home's files); each is a topic of that chain, so a burst is one wake. The `refs` deadline is the effect's deadline,
  and `RefWatch.RecoverAsync` runs once beside the first looks (canary 16). A try is refused with no git where a session holds every
  pending unit's tree (canary 17); one that removes nothing is a refusal, and the unit stays pending.
- **Dispositions, in the existing records.** A removal leaves the entry a trace with `GoneAt` and its kept fact, which the inventory
  reads as *removed* with its proof. A guard that kept a held session branch is written on its entry in `session-branches.json` as
  `blocked { why, at }` (the guard's code: `TidyKept`'s, or the press's `in-use` and `dirty`). It is written only where it changes, and
  it is dropped when the branch is recorded again or goes. The inventory reads it as *blocked*, saying why. It is no second copy of
  a fact: the machine log's `branch.kept` is said once and is no record. A landing branch's guard is not written yet.
- **An unknown owner blocks** (canary 27): a unit no record names is never ended, so never pending, and nothing is tried for it.
  **A branch's removal never retires its unit** (canary 35): a trace whose unit a standing landing carries stays open until the line
  holds that landing (END2's rule, unchanged).
- **Unchanged:** the sweep's press (`SweepAsync`), LAND3's tidy (`TidyHeldAsync`), and the pace's pass, which stays the
  reconciliation. The look's tidy fixture now holds its home's lease, as the instance's loop does, since only the lease's holder
  keeps ends.
- **The git starts counted** (`TidyEffectGitTests`, this machine): a pace's tidy of a repository with one branch the line holds
  none of started 16 git processes beyond the keeper's own before, and 43 with four. Now it starts 5 and 8: the three left are the
  follow's one read of each tree (TREEFOLLOW1).
- **Proof:** `TidyEffectTests` (fast): canaries 15, 16, 17, 27 and 35 over the records. `TidyEffectGitTests` (Git): canaries 15
  and 8 over real git, and the count. `BranchTidyingProcessTests`, `BranchTidyingFolderTests`, `SweepTests`, `LandingTidyTests`
  and `HeldFactsGitTests` green by class.
- **After review**, before the merge:
  - *The delete looks again at what its proof rested on* (`ContentHolds.DeleteJudgedAsync`, the press's path too). `update-ref -d`
    compares the branch alone. So once it went, each form of the line is read again: one no longer at or past the commit the
    judgement read (rewound or replaced) puts the branch back at the commit judged, kept. So does a tree that checked the branch
    out after the trees were read. git's `update-ref --stdin` transaction would check both under git's locks, but `GitAsync`
    writes no input. The commits never leave the object store between the delete and the put-back, so nothing is lost.
  - *A recorded branch the keeper cannot prove is not abandoned.* The keeper makes no `SquashedHolderAsync` or `Inside` proof, so an
    older squash with no unit, and a landing inside another, would never be removed automatically again. So the tidy keeps the
    old full judgement for recorded branches and landings with no kept fact, in the lease's process alone. It remembers each by
    its tip and the line's commit, in memory, and judges it again only when either moves. The count above is the second pass's.
    The first pass of a process, and the first after the line moves, judges each such branch once, as before END4a.
    Teaching the keeper those two proofs would remove that cost, and is left as a row.
  - `blocked` is written by the lease's holder alone, and its reason is compared again inside the record's lock.
  - A removed branch whose tree moved onto a branch of its own (`Now`) becomes its trace at once (`SessionBranches.Gone`), keeping
    `Now`. The branches page reads such a trace for where the tree went. Before, it stayed standing and pending for ever.
  - A refused repository is settled by what it rests on, with no git: its units, kept tips, reasons, the trees sessions hold, and
    those trees' folder and index times. A wake tries it again only once one of these changes or the `refs` deadline passes, so a
    burst over the three unblocking topics is one try.
- **After the second review** (its merge reverted, the parent's rulings applied; this replaces the proposed row FABRIC-DELETETX1):
  - *The delete is one ref transaction* (`ContentHolds.DeleteJudgedAsync`). `git update-ref --stdin` holds a `verify` for each ref the
    proof rested on, at the commit read, and the `delete` at the commit judged, so git checks them all under its locks; `GitAsync`
    now writes git's input. The look-again for the line above is gone. Every caller passes its snapshot: the press and the look
    (the line's forms the judgement read, and a branch of the person's holding the tip where the line does not), LAND3's door and a
    tree's removal (the line's forms read before the proof, and that holder), the held and carried tidies (the line's forms read
    before the proof or the carriers, and any ref holding the tip), a cut-off opening (the ref that held its tip), the landings'
    clean-up (the line's forms). A line moved *forward* in that moment refuses too, and the branch is judged again at the next pass:
    that wait, and one more git start per removal (the holder's read), are the cost taken for the guarantee.
  - *What a reader can still see.* Where the line moved, nothing: a refused transaction deletes nothing, so no look, page or session
    sees the branch absent. The one window left is a checkout made after the trees were read and before the transaction, which no ref
    says (`update-ref` asks no tree). It lasts milliseconds, and Daoris itself never checks an existing branch out (it opens a tree
    on a new branch, or detached), so only a person's or a session's agent's own checkout of that `daoris/` branch reaches it. That
    tree sees an unborn branch,
    and a commit made in it is a root commit holding the tree's whole content: nothing is lost, its parentage differs. The trees are
    read again once it went: a tree on the branch, *or a list git could not give*, puts it back create-only at the commit judged. The
    put-back's exit is read: a refusal (a lock, the person's commit made meanwhile) is said as such, naming what stands, and the
    commits stay where the proof found them. A recovery ref goes only in a transaction that verifies the branch at a commit holding
    it (`DropKeptAsync`), so it outlives any put-back that did not happen.
  - *The caches settle, never suppress for good.* The fallback's memory (`AutoTidies.Judged`) keeps when it judged, and judges again
    once the `refs` deadline passed, with neither commit moved: a busy or dirty tree, or a landing checked out inside another, clears
    without moving one. The effect's settle counts from its last real try's start; a deadline wake is always a real try (the
    chain's `due`); and a settled answer is `ChainOutcome.Settled`, no try, which leaves the chain's deadline where the last real try
    put it.
  - *Traces keep live trees.* A removed branch's trace that names where its tree went (`Now`) is the one followed
    (`SessionBranches.Following` and `Follow`), so the look learns the tree's next switch and `BranchNowAsync` still answers once the
    tree is gone; and it is never pruned while that tree's folder stands.
  - *`blocked` carries the reason*: `TidyBlock.Reason`, the tidy's own sentence, kept as `blocked.reason` (additive: a block from
    before reads as it did), and the inventory says it.
  - Not covered: a proof by a squashing landing's record (`SquashedHolderAsync`) rests on the landing's branch, which the snapshot
    names only where a ref holds the tip by ancestry; the line's forms are verified as ever.
  - *Proof:* `JudgedDeleteGitTests` (Git): LAND3's door and a tree's removal keep a branch whose line moved as it went; an unread
    list after the delete puts it back; a put-back git refuses is said so. `TidyEffectGitTests` (Git): the line rewound keeps the
    branch with no put-back; a removed branch's live tree is followed to its next branch; a kept unproven branch is judged again at
    the deadline. `TidyEffectTests` (fast): a settled wake never moves the deadline; a live tree's trace is never pruned; `blocked`
    carries the reason.
- **After the third review** (a second maker's; the parent's rule and ruling applied):
  - *The proof returns what it rested on, and the delete verifies exactly that* (the parent's rule). Round 3 read the holder again after
    the judgement (`ProofRefsAsync`) and fell back to the line's forms where none was found, so a person's branch deleted in between let a
    line that never held the tip authorize the delete; it verified only ancestry holders, so a content holder, an `Inside` landing's
    holder and a squashing landing's branch went unverified; and a landed branch's proof asked the line by its live name while its
    verify read the line again after the recovery ref was made. Now each proof names each ref and the commit it read, and the delete's
    transaction verifies those and nothing else; no helper looks for them afterwards (`ProofRefsAsync` and `LineRefsAsync` are gone). By
    ancestry, the holder out of one `for-each-ref --contains` read (`ContentHolds.HoldersAsync`, `HolderOf`): a form of the line first,
    else a branch of the person's; none found is a refusal. By content, the ref compared (`ContentProof.At`). A squashing landing's
    record, its branch at the commit it was read standing at (`TreeSquashes.SquashedHoldAsync`). A carried branch, the form its pull
    request's merge was found on, and the cleared tip's branch where that tip carried it (`PullRequestCarrier.Rests`). An empty branch,
    the form compared. The look's *on the line*, that form or the landing's branch at its judged tip. LAND3's held tidy, the landed ref
    read once at its commit (the branch a squash made, where the landed revision is a squashed tip). A landed branch, the form it was
    proved on (`LandedProofs.Proof.Rests`): each form is read with its commit (`LineForm.At`) and every comparison is made at that commit,
    never by the live name; a pull request's, its merge's form; one inside another, that branch at its judged tip and what that one
    rests on. A delete handed nothing refuses. The cost, in git starts: the holder's read is the judgement's own (the sweep's replaces
    its `branch --all --contains`, and the look's line check reads the same output), and the read after the judgement is gone, so a
    removal starts one git fewer than in round 3; an empty branch's line commit is one read more where the judgement holds no snapshot.
    A line moved forward no longer refuses a branch whose proof rested on a branch of the person's.
  - *Unread is never gone.* After a refused transaction, only git's own "no such ref" (exit 1 of `rev-parse --verify --quiet`) says the
    branch went; any other failure is said kept, unread, and the recovery ref stays (`ContentHolds.RefusedAsync`).
  - *One trace follows a tree.* The pruning exempts only the trace `Following` reads, the newest of its name where none of it stands; a
    name recorded and removed again and again leaves older traces naming the same folder, which retire like any other.
  - *Accepted: the late-checkout window* (the parent's ruling, not built). A symbolic `HEAD` can become unborn between the delete and the
    put-back, and a commit made there in that moment gets root parentage. The window exists in main's press today, whose `update-ref -d`
    was never serialized against a checkout. Daoris's own sessions take `TreeLock`, and the branch's work is proved on what its proof
    rested on (the line, or the holder it named), verified in the same transaction as the delete. So the residue is a person's own
    checkout of a fully landed `daoris/` branch in that moment, recoverable from the line. The look-again and the checked put-back stay.
  - *Proof*, each red on round 3: `JudgedDeleteGitTests` (Git): a vanished ancestry holder refuses the delete; a content holder moved as it
    went keeps the branch; a squashing landing's branch moved keeps it; an `Inside` landing's holder moved keeps it; a rewind between the
    proof and the recovery ref keeps the landing; a failed read after a refused delete is unread, not removed. `TidyEffectTests` (fast):
    repeated re-record and removal of one name keeps one trace.
- **After the fourth review** (a second maker's, before staging; what round 4 closed stays closed):
  - *A landing's proof travels with what it holds* (`LineLanding.Rests`). The automatic tidy took a session branch whose work the line
    holds through a landing's branch on that branch alone, at its judged tip, so the line rewound after the landing was proved let it go
    though its work was no longer on the line. The landing now carries what its own proof rested on (the form of the line at the commit
    read, or the landing it is inside and what that one rests on), and the session branch's delete verifies those too. A landing whose
    proof names nothing holds nothing for the look.
  - *LAND3's squash binds the branch it made* (`TreeTidies.TidyHeldAsync`, `madeAt`). The other branches are compared with the session
    tip the squash squashed, which no ref holds, so the branch made is read at a commit that is, or contains, the commit the landing made
    (`Branched.Commits`' last), else nothing is judged. The cost: one git start more (its ancestry) where that branch moved since the
    landing made it, and a branch kept for the next clean-up where it moved elsewhere.
  - *What could not be judged is said* (`TidiedBranch.Unjudged`, internal, so no answer's shape changes). Where the branch holding the
    landed work was not read, or not at what the landing made, each other session branch that stands is kept, and the landing says the
    clean-up could not judge it, never that the landed work holds its commits. Naming only those that stand costs a read of each, in that
    case alone. A branch the carried tidy judged is said as that tidy judged it.
  - *The carried tidy asks about the tip it read* (`TreeTidies.TidyCarriedAsync`): `CarrierOfAsync` is handed that commit, never
    `refs/heads/<branch>`, so a branch moved from A to B, which a pull request carried, and back to A as it was asked keeps A.
  - *Absent is git's own "missing"* (`ContentHolds.RefusedAsync`). `rev-parse --verify --quiet` exits 1 on any failure to resolve, a
    broken ref's too, so after a refused transaction its exit 1 is asked of `show-ref --exists`, whose exit 2 alone reads as gone. Any
    other answer reads as unread: kept, with its recovery ref. A git older than 2.43 has no `--exists`, so there a branch that went is
    said kept, never the reverse; the bundled MinGit is 2.56. One git start more, only where the branch read absent after a refusal.
  - *Proof*, each red on round 4: `TidyEffectGitTests` (Git): a line rewound after a landing was proved keeps the session branch it holds.
    `JudgedDeleteGitTests` (Git): a squash holder made again before the tidy's read keeps the earlier branch, said as not judged; an
    unread holder says the clean-up could not judge; a broken ref after a refused delete is unread, not gone. `PullRequestStateTests`
    (Git): a carried tidy whose branch moved away and back keeps it.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-NODE1c, the node contract's fixes before the wires).** A second maker reviewed FABRIC-NODE1: the one
lifecycle stands, and the attempt and binding gaps are closed before WIRE1a builds on it. Choices 2, 3 and 4 of that note are
changed in place. Still pure: nothing is stored, and `Apply` stays the one judge of a move, with what differs by kind only in
`NodeAuthorities` (two facts added: `Evidenced`, and `Dispatches`, from the reach).

- **Every outcome names its attempt.** A reach, a failure, a rejection and an unknown carry `NodeMove.Attempt`; none is
  `no-attempt`, another is `stale-attempt`. A late failure of attempt 1 no longer fails attempt 2.
- **A reach is its attempt's whole binding.** The start carries the binding Daoris evaluated (`NodeMove.Binding`: this unit, this
  node at its declared version, the next attempt, its revision, graph, inputs and receipts), kept as `NodeStatus.Binding`. A reach
  must have its id, so an old graph, revision or version, or other inputs or receipts, is `not-bound`. A kind that is `Evidenced`
  (every record's or answer's kind; not the person's kinds, nor a join) needs an evidence reference, of the declared schema where
  one is declared (`no-evidence`). A declaration that is not the status's node is `not-this-node`. That a `plugin` answer came
  from the declaring plugin stays PERFORM1c's authenticated sender check.
- **A dispatched attempt holds the gate.** A performed or waited node's start sets `Unreconciled` to its attempt, and only its
  authority's reach or failure, or `Reconciled` once it is no longer current, clears it. A stop, skip, rejection or invalidation
  while it runs leaves it set, so no duplicate starts. An observed or pressed node dispatches nothing.
- **The observation takes an evaluated binding** (`BuiltInNodes.Observe(unit, node, binding)`): its inputs and receipts are in the
  acceptance, so a QA wired before `handed` reaches `handed` when the QA is invalidated. The acceptance's revision is still the
  commit its evidence names, and `Apply` now compares it with the binding's, where the reader's live tip was compared before.
- **Issued ids are immutable:** an acceptance copies its inputs, receipts and outputs when they are set.
- **One result per binding:** `NodeAcceptances.Keep` keeps a replay once and refuses a different result for a binding that has one
  (`other-result`), for the record writer PERFORM1a adds.
- **History before reset:** a move that clears a binding, an acceptance, an outcome's source, code or words, or a skip's scope
  returns the status as it stood (`NodeMoved.Cleared`), for that writer to keep. A start and a reset to ready or blocked clear
  the whole attempt record; an invalidation keeps it as history until then.
- **Proof** (fast): `NodeLifecycleTests` (the table, 31 rows with the skip's withdrawal and lapse; a dispatched attempt through a
  stop, an invalidation, a withdrawn skip and a rejection; late outcomes; the whole binding; what a move cleared; the skip's scope;
  a plugin's forbidden skip), `NodeAcceptancesTests` (an issued id under mutation; one result per binding) and `BuiltInNodesTests`
  (an observation on an evaluated binding).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-WIRE1a, the wires' validation at save; with FABRIC-NODE1d).** The design's §E3, for the owner's *"we have
different ways to wire them"*. Pure, in `Trees/Nodes/NodeWires.cs`: `NodeWires.Problems(nodes, wires)` judges a wire set over node
declarations and names everything a save refuses, each a `WireProblem { Code, Wire, Node, Words }`. Nothing is evaluated (WIRE1b)
and nothing stored (GRAPH1); a declaration's own checks stay `NodeDeclarations.Problem`, for GRAPH1's save to run beside it.

- **The shape:** `Wire { Id, Kind, From, To, Waits?, Group? }`. An end is `WireEnd { Node, Port? }`, a port on a `data` wire alone
  (`qa.evidence`, `deploy.approval`). A `wait` wire has no `From`; its `Waits` is `WaitTarget { Unit, Revision, Node | End,
  Commitment? }`. A group is `WireGroup { Id, Mode }`. The kind is the wire's only condition: no expression, script or command.
- **What each kind connects:** `after` and `trigger` join two of this graph's nodes; `data` a source's declared output, or its
  `evidence`, to a target's declared input of the same schema; `wait` a target to a `wait.unit` only; `failed` leaves a node
  something can fail, and `rejected` one a person may reject.
- **The refusals,** 20 codes (`WireRefusal`), each with words naming the wire and node in backticks: `no-id`, `wire-twice`,
  `node-twice`, `unknown-kind`, `shape`; §E3's missing reference (`missing-node`, `missing-port`), incompatible schema
  (`incompatible-schema`) and wire to another repository's node (`foreign-node`); the wait's (`not-wait-unit`, `wait-target`,
  `no-wait`, `wait-twice`); the mandatory inputs (`input-unfed`, `input-twice`); the groups (`grouped-input`, `group-mode`,
  `group-split`); paths never taken (`never-fails`, `never-rejected`); and the local cycle, a node's own `trigger` loop included
  (`cycle`). Every problem is named at once, not the first alone, so a save says all it refuses.
- **Choices §E3 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **A group is declared on the wire**, `{ id, mode }`, and is one node's. Only the eligibility kinds (`after`, `trigger`,
     `failed`, `rejected`) are grouped; a `data` or `wait` wire is an input, mandatory whatever the groups say. An ungrouped wire is
     a group of its own, so it is required. `NodeWires.Groups` hands WIRE1b each node's groups, every group's wires in wire-id order
     (an `anyOf`'s witness order).
  2. **Compatible is the same schema**, compared ordinally. No version range is read into a schema's name, and alternative producers
     stay an explicit choice input (§E3).
  3. **The evidence port is `evidence`.** Its schema is the declared `Evidence`, or an observed built-in's record schema
     (`BuiltInNodes.EvidenceSchema`, `daoris/<kind>@1`); a node with neither has no evidence port. An output named `evidence` is
     refused by the declaration's check (`outputs`).
  4. **A `wait.unit` takes exactly one `wait` wire**, naming a unit, its agreed revision, and one of its nodes or its end; several
     waits are several `wait.unit` nodes, joined by groups. The commitment is optional when saved, since whether a unit is another
     repository's is the adapter's to judge (WAITUNIT1).
  5. **Other work is named by a wait target alone.** An end naming another unit's node is `foreign-node` on every kind, a local
     unit's included, since waiting on a local unit is `wait.unit`'s too (§E6).
  6. **A path that can never be taken is refused**, as NODE1's choice 9 refuses a retry on a kind nothing can fail: `failed` from a
     node with no failure authority (git's proofs, the person's kinds) and `rejected` from a node no person may reject.
  7. **Every kind but `wait` closes a cycle**, `failed` and `rejected` included, since a send-back is the adapter's bounded return
     (§E7). A wire with another problem still counts while both its ends are this graph's nodes, so a cycle is said at the first save.
  8. **A `trigger` fires on its source's accepted reach.** It names no other transition: a failure or a rejection is its own kind's
     path. A skipped source satisfies `after` alone (§E2).
- **Room for WIRE1b:** wire ids are unique (`wire-twice`), so a trigger's once-per-receipt key is the wire, the receipt and the
  binding, and a witness is a wire id. The transition receipts for a skip, a failure and a rejection are typed by the wire's kind and
  scoped by WIRE1b to their graph, run and binding.
- **Left to GRAPH1:** the graph's bounds (24 authored nodes, 96 wires, synthetic nodes counted apart), its entries and end, every
  node reachable, D157's protected order, and canary 40's closure deadlock.
- **FABRIC-NODE1d:** a `NodeDeclaration` copies its `Inputs` and `Outputs` into ordinal-sorted immutable dictionaries, and a
  `NodeRetry` its `DelaysSeconds` into an immutable array, when each is set, so a digest over declarations and a declaration's
  `Problem` never move with a caller's collection. A reach's evidence or output naming another acceptance is refused
  (`other-acceptance`), and a port naming none is kept naming the reach's own, since a consumer's invalidation follows the
  acceptance a port names.
- **Proof** (fast): `NodeWiresTests` (the design's example with every kind; each of the 20 codes alone, by a case, with its wire,
  node and words, and every code held by a case; what each kind connects; a node's groups in wire-id order; the stable order of
  several problems) and `NodeLifecycleTests` (NODE1d: a declaration under its caller's mutation; a reach's ports naming another
  acceptance).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-WIRE1b, the wires evaluated; with FABRIC-NODE1e).** The design's §E3, with §E2's eligibility and
invalidation and §E7's `all` join. Pure, in `Trees/Nodes/WireEvaluation.cs`: `WireEvaluation.Evaluate(nodes, wires, statuses, run)`
returns the statuses after its moves, each made through `NodeLifecycle.Apply` and named with its why (`WireMove`); each node's
eligibility (`WireEligibility`: its `anyOf` witnesses, the receipts it would consume, the wires it waits on, the binding a start
takes); and the starts it proposes (`WireStart`, each with the trigger firings it consumes). Nothing is stored: the statuses and the
fired triggers are the record writer's (PERFORM1a), and the graph is GRAPH1's to hand in.

- **Four passes, one move per node:** the active observed built-ins read from their records, the invalidations to a fixpoint (so a
  cycle handed in still ends), the started joins, then each blocked, ready or invalidated node's eligibility. *Changed by
  FABRIC-WIRE1e:* the invalidations come first, and only the observed built-ins still bound are observed.
- **Typed transition receipts** (`WireReceipt`), each scoped: a reach's is its acceptance's id; a skip's (`skp-`) digests its graph,
  run, unit and node, and holds only for its own; a failure's (`fld-`) and a rejection's (`rej-`) digest the graph, the run and the
  binding they ended, and only a binding of this graph gives one (*changed by FABRIC-WIRE1e:* only an attempt started in this graph
  and run gives one). Each wire kind reads its own: `after` a reach or a skip, `data` and
  `trigger` a reach, `failed` a failure, `rejected` a rejection. A downstream binding names them in its `PrerequisiteReceipts`, so
  losing one invalidates what rested on it.
- **Choices §E3 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **Starts are proposed, never applied.** The evaluation makes Daoris's own moves (blocked and ready, invalidations, a join's and an
     observed built-in's outcome). A start is where a performed node dispatches, a pressed one offers its task and a waited one waits,
     so it stays its caller's (PERFORM1, HUMAN1, WAITUNIT1), through the same `Apply`, on the binding the evaluation offered.
  2. **One move per node per evaluation.** A node invalidated here is judged again at the next, so its lapse is seen and kept as
     history (`NodeMoved.Cleared`), and a lost witness is never replaced silently (canary 41). The caller evaluates again after
     applying what it starts, until `Settled`. *Changed by FABRIC-WIRE1e:* `Settled` means the evaluator moved nothing, not that no
     start is pending (`Starts`, `Queued`) nor that any execution finished; a refused evaluation (choice 9) moved nothing either, so
     its caller reads `Refusal` first.
  3. **A failure is a receipt once Daoris will not retry it:** a code not transient, or the declared attempts spent (§E3, *once the
     source's declared retries are exhausted*). Daoris's retry itself (failed to ready, after `DelayBefore`) stays the scheduler's.
  4. **A trigger fires once per receipt, at the start.** It makes a blocked or invalidated target eligible only on a receipt it has
     not fired on: one whose key (`trg-`: graph, run, wire, receipt) is neither in `WireRun.Fired` nor in the target's binding. A
     ready target keeps it, so a declared retry or the person's new attempt on the same receipt is no second firing, and a started
     attempt rests on it. The start carries the firing for the record writer to keep; a second evaluation before the start offers the
     same key, never a second. So a trigger is edge-triggered where `after` is level-triggered: a target invalidated by another
     upstream is not started again on a receipt it already fired on, and waits for a new one.
  5. **A witness is kept while it holds.** A bound node's `anyOf` witness is the member its binding rests on, even when a lower wire id
     qualifies later (it cancels nothing); otherwise the first by wire id.
  6. **An `all` join's members are its `after` wires' sources.** It is eligible when every member is reached or skipped (consuming
     each receipt), or as soon as one has failed with its retries spent (consuming that failure's, the first by wire id); once
     started, `members` reaches or fails it (`member-failed`) on its binding's receipts. A rejected member holds it, as a running one
     does: a rejection is the member's own path. A join's other eligibility wires are judged as any node's. *Changed by
     FABRIC-WIRE1e:* a started join fails on its members' failures alone; one it consumed through its own `failed` wire is a condition
     of its start.
  7. **A fork's limit counts dispatched attempts alone** (a performed or waited reach that is active, unknown or unreconciled), 4 by
     default (`WireRun.Limit`; §A3, PROC1a's measured four process starts at once). Observed and pressed nodes send nothing out and
     start beside them. Ready nodes start in node-id order; the rest are `Queued`. *Changed by FABRIC-WIRE1e:* counting unresolved
     dispatched attempts stands, but waiting capacity is apart from execution capacity, since four parked `wait.unit` attempts could
     queue an unrelated performed node for ever. Performed attempts run within `WireRun.Limit`, 4 (PROC1a's measured four process
     starts at once); waited ones within `WireRun.WaitLimit`, 24 (`DefaultWaitLimit`: a graph's bound of nodes, §E7, so every wait of
     one graph may wait at once), since a parked wait starts no process and its adapter reads a local projection (§E6).
  8. **A `wait` wire is never an eligibility condition** (§E6): it is its `wait.unit`'s input, read by the adapter once the node is
     active, and the evaluation hands it on (`WireEligibility.Waits`).
  9. **Revisions are the caller's.** `WireRun.Revisions` names each unit's current revision (a quest's null), and a unit it does not
     name is read as having none, so the caller names every unit its nodes act on. A bound node whose binding's revision, graph or
     version is not current is invalidated (`lapsed`), as is one resting on a receipt or an input no longer current (`upstream`),
     transitively; a skip of another graph or run lapses (`skip-scope`). *Changed by FABRIC-WIRE1e:* the revisions are a complete
     snapshot. A unit a node acts on that `Revisions` leaves out, or a branch it names with none (where `WireRun.Units` says it is a
     branch), refuses the evaluation as a whole (`WireEvaluated.Refusal`, `no-revision`, with the units in `Missing`): nothing moves,
     nothing is judged eligible, nothing starts. The whole, not the unit's nodes alone, since a node on another unit may rest on a
     receipt of that unit, and judging it would bind or lapse on a guess. A quest's revision stays null by design, and is named so.
- **The git reads (NODE1's choice 8):** an active or unknown observed built-in whose unit is in `WireRun.Units` is observed through
  `BuiltInNodes.Observe` with `WireRun.Git`. The caller supplies, from the look's git reads, `RemoteTip` (the commit git's
  remote-tracking ref holds for the landing's branch, for `pushed`) and `Holds` (whether the first commit holds the second, for a
  `pull-request` completed from a newer commit). Absent, both outcomes stay unknown.
- **FABRIC-NODE1e:** a reach exposes exactly its node's declared outputs, each at its declared schema. An undeclared output
  (`undeclared-output`), a declared one at another schema (`output-schema`) and a declared one missing (`no-output`) are refused,
  so WIRE1a's schema check on a `data` wire holds at run time.
- **Not built here:** the graph store and homes (GRAPH1), dispatch (PERFORM1), the editor, the declared retry's move, and a reader
  that gathers the statuses, revisions, units and git reads for a run.
- **Proof** (fast): `WireEvaluationTests` (each kind's receipt; an `anyOf` witness and canary 41; an `allOf` waiting on its last; a
  trigger once across evaluations, a fresh binding and a new receipt; a failure read by its `failed` wire alone, and scoped; a fork
  at its limit; a join reached and failed; transitive invalidation and a moved revision; a cycle that ends; an observed built-in with
  and without the caller's git reads) and `NodeLifecycleTests` (NODE1e's three refusals, and the example's reach).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-WIRE1e, the wire evaluation's fixes before dispatch).** A second maker reviewed FABRIC-WIRE1b: GRAPH1 may
build on it, but PERFORM1 and CLOSE1 must not dispatch from it unchanged. Three defects are fixed, and choices 2, 7 and 9 of that note
are changed in place. Still pure and stored nowhere, with every move through `NodeLifecycle.Apply`.

- **A failure's or a rejection's receipt is its attempt's run's alone.** The receipt hashed the caller's current run into an outcome
  of another run's, so a failure kept from run 1 took a `failed` path in run 2 of the same graph and revision. A start now names its
  graph and run (`NodeMove.Scope`, as a skip does: `no-scope` without one, `not-bound` when its graph is not the binding's). The
  status keeps it as `NodeStatus.Scope` through the attempt's outcome, and a failure or a rejection is a receipt only where that scope
  is the evaluated run's. The acceptance's id still omits the run (§E2's binding), so a reach stays a receipt in any run of its graph
  and revision. A failure or a rejection kept from another run is left as it stands: it is no receipt here, and the declared retry
  or the person's new attempt starts the node in this run.
- **Validity before observation.** The invalidations come first, and only the observed built-ins still bound are observed. An active
  `pushed` bound to tip 1 and observed after the unit moved to tip 2 is invalidated, never reached, so no receipt the same evaluation
  would invalidate is exposed and nothing starts on one; an obsolete graph or a lost prerequisite is caught the same way. An
  observation moves only an active or unknown node, which holds no receipt, so it takes none away from what the invalidations judged
  current.
- **A join's outcome is its members' alone.** A started `all` join fails (`member-failed`) only on a failure in its binding of one of
  its `after` wires' sources. A failure it consumed through its own `failed` wire is a condition of its start, not a member's failure.
- **For PERFORM1** (a note, not code): acceptance ids omit the run, so callers must keep attempt numbers increasing across runs, a
  node's status carried from one run to the next rather than started afresh. PERFORM1 must persist the moves and their history
  (`NodeMoved.Cleared`), handle refusals (`WireEvaluated.Refused` and `Refusal`), apply starts and their firings atomically, schedule
  retries and reconciliation, and revisit queued work.
- **Proof** (fast, each test seen failing before its fix): `WireEvaluationTests` (a failure and a rejection of run 1, each no receipt
  in run 2 and a receipt in run 1; an observation on a moved tip invalidated, with no receipt and no start; a join not failed by a
  failure that is no member's; parked waits not queuing a performed node, and waits held to their own limit; a missing revision and a
  branch's null refusing the evaluation, and a quest's null named) and `NodeLifecycleTests` (a start naming its run, kept through its
  failure and its rejection).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-GRAPH1, the graph and its homes; with FABRIC-WIRE1d).** The design's §E7, for the owner's *"we can define
a clear end of work; each repo/workspace might have a different cycle of work"*. `Trees/Nodes/NodeGraphs.cs`: `NodeGraph { SchemaVersion,
Nodes, Wires, Entries, End, Cleanup }`; `NodeGraphs.Problems` composes `NodeDeclarations.Problem` and `NodeWires.Problems` with the graph's
own 19 codes (`GraphRefusal`), every problem named at once; `NodeGraphs.Digest` (`gph-` and 32 hex); `NodeGraphs.Read` from JSON, never in
part; and `NodeGraphHomes`, Current derived from the rules. Nothing is evaluated (WIRE1b's `WireEvaluation` takes the graph) and no door
or page sets one yet.

- **FABRIC-WIRE1d:** a group on an `after` wire into an `all` join is refused (`grouped-member`, WIRE1a's 21st code), since WIRE1b reads
  every such wire's source as a member whatever its group. A join's other eligibility wires keep their groups.
- **The homes:**
  - a branch's graph is its effective landing rule's (`LandingRules.Choose`): the landing's own nodes, `handed`, then `pushed` and
    `pull-request` where a branch rule names a plugin, then `line`, each after the one before, entered at `handed`; and the rule's
    `cycle` after them, a fragment `{ schemaVersion?, nodes, wires, entries, end, cleanup }` whose wires may leave the landing's nodes,
    whose entries start beside `handed`, and whose `end` (default `line`) and `cleanup` (default `held`) are the graph's;
  - a quest's or an ask's is a whole graph under `workCycles` (repository, then kind) or `workspaceWorkCycles`; a branch's never is;
  - inheritance is D164's: a repository's own rule replaces its workspace's, cycle and all, and a work cycle replaces its workspace's
    for its kind alone;
  - a named graph as a named workflow's version is not built here (left as FABRIC-GRAPH1b, below).
- **An install's existing `driver.json`** (no `cycle`, no `workCycles`) reads exactly as today: each branch's graph is its landing's
  alone, ended at `line`, cleaned up at `held`; quests and asks have none; and the file is written back byte for byte, neither key added
  (`NodeGraphHomesTests`' first case, `cycles.test.ts`).
- **Choices §E7 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **Synthetic nodes, and every wire to or from one, count apart**: at most three synthetic nodes and four such wires to each authored
     node. A decoded step's worst is three such wires (a member's into its join and its failure's into a sink; the join's out and its
     own failure's, shared by at least two members), so a maximal D157 version always decodes. A graph read from a file declares no
     synthetic node: `synthetic` there is unreadable, so authoring cannot escape the 24.
  2. **Current's landing nodes are the graph's own**, counted toward the 24 and the 96: Daoris derives them, but they are not decoding's.
  3. **The cycle is kept as JSON text on the rule** (`LandingRule.Cycle`, `WorkCycles`), compact and as written, and judged where it is
     read. A cycle that does not read or hold is kept and said (`GraphHome.Problems`), its rule standing: a mistyped end never changes
     how the work lands, and a rule whose form changes under a cycle keeps it, said unreachable (§C2). Only the doors that set a cycle
     (`WithLandingCycle`, `WithWorkCycle`) refuse one, naming its first problem; a door with no field for it (Setup's landing row, Ask
     Daoris, `daoris driver landing`) keeps the one there is, and clearing the rule takes it.
  4. **An entry takes no wire from another node** (`entry-wired`); a `wait` wire into it is its input from outside, kept.
  5. **The protected order** (D157 §2.2) is checked on the one gate that is a node yet: every `look` reaches every `handed` through wires
     a reach satisfies (`after`, `trigger`, `data`). Holds and the second opinion join it once they are nodes (WORKFLOW2).
  6. **`quest.close`'s only way out is its `failed` wire**; it is the end where it stands (`close-end`); and canary 40's deadlock is a
     `claimed` upstream of it (`closure-deadlock`), since a closure graph never writes the quest's done before its end. *A landing in a
     closure graph runs on `ready`* is CLOSE1a's.
  7. **A fragment declares no landing's kind** (`landing-kind`) and wires into no landing node (`into-landing`): what comes before the
     landing is the rules' gates, and the landing's proofs are the rule's.
  8. **The cleanup's wait is refused for an end at or before the line** (`claimed`, `handed`, `pushed`, `pull-request`, `line`), and a
     bound outside 1-90 days or at `held` (§C2). Schema 2 is the graph's; D157's versions stay schema 1.
  9. **The digest** covers every declaration (a node's options and its `synthetic` mark included, so NODE1's declaration gains
     `Options`, its JSON values' text, and `Synthetic`), nodes and wires in id order, entries by name: written order never moves it.
  10. **`workCycles` is per kind** (`quest`, `ask`): a repository naming one kind takes its workspace's cycle for another, since kinds are
      apart; the landing rule stays whole, its cycle with it.
- **Not built here:** the evaluation runtime (PERFORM1), D157's versions decoded (WORKFLOW2), the editor (GRAPHEDIT1), the supported
  adapter's check against the plugin catalogue (the door's, with the catalogue), and the readers that would act on a derived graph.
- **Proof** (fast): `NodeGraphsTests` (the design's example, read from its JSON to the same digest; each of the 19 codes alone with its
  words; the bounds at their limits, synthetic nodes apart; the composition with WIRE1a's codes; the digest stable under reordering and
  moved by each of 28 changes), `NodeGraphHomesTests` (today's cycle from an existing config; D164's inheritance; a fragment's end and
  entries; the work cycles kind by kind; the doors; a cycle that does not hold, kept and said; the shared table, 13 rows),
  `NodeWiresTests` (WIRE1d's refusal and a join with plain members), and the CLI's `cycles.test.ts` and `driverconfig.test.ts` (the same
  table; the write; the `landing` verb keeping the cycle).
- **After review** (2026-10-17, a second maker's; FIX-LOG 2026-10-17). Five findings, fixed in both twins over shared tables:
  1. **An edit of `driver.json` changes only what it edits** (live in the installed build: Setup and Ask Daoris deleted every setting
     `ToJson` had no field for, and the CLI moved them first and dropped a landing rule's). Both write over the text they read
     (`DriverConfig.Source`, `DriverChoices.source`; `Platform/JsonKeeping.cs`, `platform/jsonkeep.ts`), from three texts: the file, the
     file as its reader understood it, and the edited choices, the last two by the same writer. A key neither holds is kept in place; a
     value the edit left as read is written as the file has it; an object an edit changed is merged key by key, so a landing rule's or a
     workspace rule's own keys stay; one it took goes, a top-level map it emptied keeping the entries its reader never read; a key it adds
     goes after the edit's key before it; a default the file never wrote stays unwritten; no edit is the file's own bytes. A config built
     in memory is written whole. The CLI writes in the driver's order now, what it does not own after it. Proof: `driver-config-keeps.json`
     (an existing file in the writer's layout with unknown keys at the top, in a landing rule and in a workspace rule; nine edits; the bytes
     both write), `DriverConfigKeepsTests` and `configkeeps.test.ts`; its row in `tools/merge-branch.mjs` and `merge-branch.test.ts`. A
     test that used the writer to show what was read (`WorkflowSelectionTests`, the CLI's workflow table) writes the choices without their
     text. Limit: a name JavaScript reads as an array index is laid out first in its object when the CLI rewrites the file.
  2. **Choice 3 holds for every cycle:** a landing rule's `cycle` and each kind under `workCycles` are kept as written whatever they are,
     only null being none; one that is not an object governs and is said unreadable (`GraphHome.Problems`); a kind with no work cycle is
     kept and said (`NodeGraphHomes.Strays`, `cycleStrays`: `kind`). An empty `workCycles` is kept by (1).
  3. **The first written in any case governs**, in both twins, for a landing rule and a work cycles' owner (the file's languages, releases,
     pauses and standing answers read so already); an entry that does not read shadows none; a later spelling is kept and said
     (`shadowed`). The driver kept the last and the terminal the first. `graph-homes.json` gains six rows, two change, and a `strays` section.
  4. **Choice 5 holds on every way to the landing:** besides every look reaching every landing, no landing may start while no look has
     passed (`StartsWithNoLook`: an `allOf` group needs all its members, an `anyOf` one, a reach's wire a node that is no look, a failure's
     or a rejection's any node, a look included). One problem a landing, naming the look. `NodeGraphsTests`' bypass case.
  5. **Numbers the CLI did not change keep their text** (`9007199254740993`, `1.50`): it parses the file with `JSON.rawJSON` (Node 21
     and later), so a cycle's digest never moves on an unrelated edit; covered by (1)'s table and `configkeeps.test.ts`.
  Choice 6 also stands as said, with this caveat: it catches a direct `claimed` upstream of `quest.close` and lets failed exits be
  failure sinks, but does not prove all of canary 40, whose submission-based landing is CLOSE1a's.
- **After the re-check** (2026-10-17, a second maker's; FIX-LOG 2026-10-17). Four findings, fixed in both twins over the shared tables:
  1. **An edit is made onto the file as it stands.** Written over the text read, a change another writer made between this writer's read
     and its write was lost, and the driver overwrote a file that had stopped reading. Both read the file again at write
     (`DriverConfig.Save`, `writeDriverChoices`) and make the edit, the difference between the text read as understood and the edited
     choices, onto it under the same keep rules: what another writer changed and the edit did not stays as it is. A file that does not
     read then is refused, said, and left as it is; one that is gone is written from nothing, the edit's changed keys alone. The driver
     does so where the config was read from that file (`SourceFile`); one parsed from a text, as tests do, is written over that text
     (`ToJson`), as before. Limits: the read and the move are two steps with no lock, so a change landing between them is still
     lost; and where both writers change one value or one array, the later writes its own whole.
  2. **The CLI stages each write under its own name** (`fsx.ts`, `<file>.<uuid>.daoris-tmp`), removed when the write fails, as
     `AtomicFile` does: two writers of one file shared `<file>.daoris-tmp`.
  3. **An edit keeps the file's line ending** (CRLF where its first line ends so). **A key written twice in one object** reads as its last
     value in its first place in both twins, as `JSON.parse` reads it (the driver reads the text through `JsonKeeping.Once`; work
     cycles' kinds read the first there before), and **every edit of such a file is refused**, naming the key (`JsonKeeping.Twice`,
     `twice`): the driver edited both and the CLI kept the last. First-written was not taken, since the CLI has no parser without
     dependencies that sees the first; nor a refused read, since the loop would stop driving on a hand-made duplicate, a cycle's included,
     against choice 3. `driver-config-keeps.json` gains seven rows (`between`, `crlf`, `refused`) and `twice`.
  4. **A join's early failure passes no look.** `StartsWithNoLook` follows `WireEvaluation`'s `all` join: its `after` wires are its
     members and no group's; all of them met with no look passed, it starts as any node does; else a member that may fail (a look among
     them) starts it only to fail, so only its `failed` and `rejected` paths are met. A landing on a join's pass or its failure, the join
     holding a look and a plugin, is refused; on its pass alone it holds (`NodeGraphsTests`).

  **What an edit keeps, exactly** (so nobody claims more): the whole file is written again in the writer's layout, two spaces deep with
  one final newline, so whitespace changes wherever the file was laid out otherwise; keys and string values are written in the writer's
  own escapes; a changed array is replaced whole; deleting a nested object removes the descendants its reader never read (the emptied
  map keeps them at the top level only); a BOM is stripped; comments are not JSON, so a file holding one does not read and is never
  edited. The CLI keeps numbers by their text through `JSON.rawJSON` (on by default from Node 22); without it, a number JavaScript cannot
  hold and `1.50` change.
- **After the second re-check** (2026-10-17, a second maker's; FIX-LOG 2026-10-17). Three findings, fixed in both twins over the shared
  table:
  1. **An edit is the set of key paths it changed** (a value set, a key added, a key removed), measured from the text read as understood
     to the edited choices, and making it on the file as it stands touches those paths alone (`JsonKeeping.Plan`, `jsonkeep.ts`' `plan`).
     Merged key by key, an object another writer had taken out came back whole: with `landings` gone, setting one rule wrote every rule
     read. An object missing now is made again with only the edit's own keys under it, so a rule another writer cleared and this edit
     changed holds the changed key alone (no `form`, so it reads as no rule); one that is now a string, a number or an array refuses the
     edit, naming the path (`lines.orders`), and nothing is written (`JsonEditRefusedException`, `EditRefused`; Setup and Ask Daoris say
     it as any refusal); a key the edit took that is already gone is nothing. An emptied map at the top still takes only the entries its
     reader read. A map the reader never saw is no whole value either: its keys are the edit's paths, merged into an object another
     writer wrote there meanwhile, made with the edit's keys alone where there is none, and refused where that writer wrote a string or
     an array (a further re-check of this round: written whole, a first line set after a missing read deleted the other writer's
     `lines`).
  2. **Choices read where no file was carry `{}` and the path** (`DriverConfig.Load`, `readDriverChoices`), so their save reads the file
     again and makes the edit like any other: with no text, both wrote them whole over a file another writer made before the save, and
     the driver over one that did not read or held a key twice. Saved where there still is none, they write the edit's keys alone, the
     defaults unwritten. `Save`'s other branch (a config built in memory, parsed from a text, or read from another file) still writes
     without reading the target, so it can overwrite one that does not read; no production door reaches it (every door loads
     `driver.json` and saves it to the same path: `DriverContext`, `WorkPausing`, `WorkAbandoning`, `HistoryHome`), only tests, so it is
     left as it is.
  3. **A member fails a join only where its kind's authority lets something fail it** (`NodeGraphs.MayFail`: a kind whose `FailureBy` is
     named, or a join with such a member), as `WireEvaluation` fails a join only on a member's failure: a join of a look and a person's
     press has no failed path, so its failure's wire beside its pass into the landing holds.

  Also: the CLI writes its maps in the driver's order (names as `StringComparer.Ordinal` sorts them), so an entry an edit adds goes where
  the driver's goes; written in the order they were set, a rule set again went after its neighbours there and before them here. Proof:
  `driver-config-keeps.json` gains nine rows and the row field `missing`; a save where no file is (`DriverConfigKeepsTests`,
  `configkeeps.test.ts`, now a test a row); `ConfigReadTests`' missing file; `NodeGraphsTests`' join with no failed path, and a join of
  joins either way.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-END7, auto-landing on its wakes).** A wake's plan now lands only the due entries its wakes can
release, and the whole look stays every entry's deadline (`Trees/Landing/LandingWakes.cs`).

- **What an entry waits on** comes from its last try alone (`LandingWaits.Of`). An entry with no try yet is looked at by every
  wake. A refusal kept with its tree's tip (`uncommitted`, `exists`, `completed`, `refused`) waits on the tree. `held` and
  `unreviewed` wait on the `quests` family. Then there is a second opinion, and a workflow's hold (`refused` kept with no tip).
  Anything else waits for the whole look alone.
- **The release paths and their wakes** (`LandingConcerns.Concerns`):
  - A quest's hold released (D133, D144) and the review's verdict or skip move the ledger's `quests` family (EVENT3b).
  - A second opinion settles with its reviewer's session: any ledger family, or the loop's own session end. The person's answer
    is kept in `opinions/gates/`, which is the new home topic `gates` (link 7; `ChainTable.Fixed`'s read row hears its
    `timer(gates)`).
  - A workflow's hold is released in three ways: by a declaration (`config`, or a version under `workflows/`), by the run's
    chain (`quests`), or by the say-so on its binding (`workflows/runs/`, `gates`).
  - A tree's tip moving is `refs`.
  - `config` concerns every entry, since it holds the switch and every gate's rule.

  The person's press still nudges, and a plain nudge runs the whole look.
- **Found without git.** `TreeTips.Read` reads a tree's tip from git's own files: the git directory's `HEAD`, then the branch as
  a loose ref or a `packed-refs` line in the common directory. The tip is compared with the one the try kept. Where the files
  cannot say the tip (an unborn branch, reftable, a file being replaced), git must read it, so the entry is looked at. A status
  that moves without the tip is not a ref, and waits for the whole look.
- **Refs.** `RefWatch.Heard` tells the landing each repository that a hint, an own move or a recovery judges, before its ends
  are kept. A deadline tells it nothing. `LandingWakes.Moved` nudges `refs` only where a due entry of that repository is
  concerned, so a ref move that releases nothing runs no plan.
- **The loop** (`DriverWatch`) keeps each topic's wake until a look takes it (`LandingWakes.For`). A plan hands its wakes to
  `Driver.Woke`. A plan no wake asked for is a session's end, the ledger's `sessions`. The whole look takes the wakes and lands
  every entry.
- **A landing's own moves wake nothing.** Its branch moves the repository's refs but no due tree's tip, so the `refs` wake it
  causes concerns no entry. Its due list, landing record and conversation note are files no watch hears. The writers in this
  process that write the gate's files mark what they write (`OpinionGates`, `WorkflowRunFiles`, `WorkflowFiles`, through
  EVENT4's `HomeWrites`), so the look's own gate records wake nothing.
- **Counted** (`AutoLandingWakesTests`, Git, one scratch repository, with a held done and a tree holding uncommitted work):
  - A whole look that finds nothing moved reads the waiting tree again (2 git starts), as every plan did before.
  - A ref move that no due tree made starts no git, and neither does a `sessions` wake.
  - The hold's release lands its entry on the `quests` wake without reading the other tree. That costs 52 starts: 2 to read the
    tree, and 50 for the landing itself.
  - The commit wakes `refs`, which lands the second entry (52 starts).

  `LandingWakesTests` (fast) holds the table, the tip read, the loop's wakes and the refs' pre-check. It also holds a wake that
  reads no world and starts no git, the ref watch's `Heard`, and the gate's files heard while this process's own writes are
  marked.
- **Left:**
  - A held or unreviewed entry whose tree a live session held when its release came waits for the whole look.
  - The `gates` deadline (30 s) lists every record under `workflows/`, one per run.
  - The landing's own 50 git starts ask several facts more than once (`rev-parse --show-toplevel` five times, `symbolic-ref`
    four times), as a start did before STARTGIT1.

**After review (2026-10-17).** A second maker's review found three faults. Two were inherited from main, and the wakes made them
reachable more often; the third was END7's own. Each is fixed here.

- **What lands is what was judged.** A pass lands its choices one after another, and an earlier one's plugin may take minutes,
  yet each landing used the facts and the gate the look had read. Now each choice is judged again just before its own landing
  (`AutoLander.LandAsync`, through the look's own `JudgeAsync`). It reads the due entry as it stands, the home's configuration and a
  fresh world (`IAutoLandingWorld.Again`), and checks again:
  - the switch, a landing recorded since, the tree gone, a live or newer session;
  - Done, D133/D144's holds, the workflow's hold;
  - the opinion, the review, and uncommitted work.

  What moved is said in the look's own words. The gate handed to the landing is the one read there, pinned to the tip read there
  (`LandingGate.Tip`). `TreeLands.BranchAsync` refuses where the session's branch no longer stands at that tip: the try is kept as
  `refused` with the tip, so the tree's next tip wakes it. Otherwise it makes the branch at that commit, or advances to it. The cost
  is the tree's two reads again for each landing (52 → 54 git starts in `AutoLandingWakesTests`), plus the gate's own reads where
  a review or an opinion reads git.
- **Only the lease's holder lands.** `Driver.LandDueAsync` selects and launches only where `HomeLease.HeldHere`, as the end keeper
  does. A `drive --share` loop beside the holder reads nothing of the due list and drops its wakes. Its looks say nothing of the
  list; its start line says that done work lands from the holder alone. The person's press is unchanged. The desktop never runs
  beside another loop: it waits for the lease.
- **No wake is lost.** A wake is spent only where a selection judged with it (`Driver.WokeSpent`). Three cases put the wakes back,
  ahead of those heard since (`LandingWakes.Unspent`): a look that failed first, a selection that failed, and one left because a
  landing still ran (`LandingBusy`). A plan's session end goes back as a wake. A whole look's goes back as every entry, which the
  next plan judges. When a landing ends (`RunningSessions.Beside`'s `ended`, told once it no longer runs), what was held back for it
  wakes a plan, as a session's end does (`DriverWatch.Reoffered`). A landing that ended before the wakes went back is covered by the
  look that put them back, which offers them itself.
- **Held by.** Each test was seen red against END7 first.
  - Fast: `AutoLandingLookTests` holds the lease and a look while a landing runs; `LandingWakesTests` holds the put-back and a
    failed look's wakes over the loop; `RunningSessionsTests` holds the end being told.
  - Git: `AutoLandingTests` holds a hold that came after the choice, and the pin; `ReviewLandingTests` holds a commit after the
    choice waiting as unreviewed.
- **Left:**
  - A person's press racing an automatic landing of the same entry. The re-judgement reads a landing the press recorded before it
    (`already`), but not a press still running. Two landings of one entry at once are kept apart only by git's own ref writes (a
    branch made once, an advance by compare-and-swap), and each may call the plugin.
  - A tree on a branch of its own (TREEFOLLOW1) is checked against the pin, but no ref is made for it, so its plugin pushes the
    branch by name as it stands at the push.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (RECORDUNREAD1 and DETACHTX1, after FABRIC-END4a).** A second maker's fifth review of END4a found no unsafe
automatic branch delete left, and named older record and removal paths that END4a's fourth-review rule ("absent is git's own
*missing*") did not yet reach. The git facts are the knowledge note `git-ref-absence`.

- **An unread read never forgets a record** (RECORDUNREAD1). `ContentHolds.MissingAsync` asks `show-ref --exists` where a read found
  nothing, and answers missing only on its exit 2; `RefusedAsync` now asks through it. LAND3's door
  (`TreeTidies.RemoveBranchAsync`) forgets a branch that did not resolve only then, and otherwise answers that it stays, kept as
  unread. The landings' clean-up (`LandedCleanups.JudgeLandedAsync`) retires a landing as gone only then, and as no longer the
  landing's only on `merge-base --is-ancestor`'s exit 1; any other failure is a row of kind `unknown` saying it is kept as unread, at
  no tip, so nothing removes or retires it (no new code: `unknown` is "git could not say — kept"). The record of where session branches
  grew from (`BranchReplays.ForgetGoneAsync`, each look's and each press's) forgets an entry only where its branch, and the branch its
  tree moved onto (`Now`), are missing on git's word: `for-each-ref` leaves a broken ref out of its list and still exits 0, so the list
  alone is no census. The cost: one git start for each ref read absent, once, since a missing one is then forgotten. On a git
  the person chose older than 2.43, which has no `--exists`, nothing is forgotten this way: each record stays, said unread.
- **A detached tree's removal verifies its holder** (DETACHTX1). A detached tree's commit was held by the tree's own HEAD and by what
  its proof rested on, and no branch delete carried a `verify`, so a holder rewound after the proof left that commit on no ref. Now
  `TreeRemovals.RemoveAsync` keeps it on a recovery ref (`KeepDiscardedAsync`, under the layout's branch name) before the tree goes,
  and drops the ref only in one transaction that verifies what the proof rested on (`ContentHolds.DropKeptVerifiedAsync`, the judged
  delete's `verify` lines, shared as `Verified`). Refused, the ref stays and the answer names it. A content-held detached tree keeps
  its recovery ref as before. The cost: two git starts per unforced detached removal.
- **Held by** (each seen red on END4a's branch first): `UnreadRecordGitTests` (Git): LAND3's door, the clean-up's unread tip and
  unread ancestry, the followed branch and the session branch that do not read each keep their record; a branch git says is missing
  is still forgotten. `JudgedDeleteGitTests` (Git): a detached tree's holder rewound as the tree goes keeps its commit on a recovery
  ref; one whose holder stood leaves none.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (CLEANUP1, the leftovers that never cleared).** The owner's install listed 40 units in `work state` that no look
would ever clear: 18 landings whose branch the person deleted after its pull request, 7 recovery refs and 7 folders moved aside, each
of the last two a unit of its own with its end unknown, so *What needs you* listed it. Safety first: nothing that holds the person's
