unlanded work goes without the person.

- **A landing whose branch git says is missing is the person's end of it.** A landing entry now keeps why it went where Daoris did
  not remove it (`LandedBranch.GoneAs`, additive): `missing`, git's own word (`show-ref --exists` exit 2, through
  `ContentHolds.MissingAsync`) with no branch of its name in another case standing; or `stands`, a branch of its name standing that
  is not the landing's (another commit, or the name in another case: packed refs compare case exactly, so git calls such a name
  missing on a disk blind to case). The clean-up writes it as it retires the entry; the look's tidy, in the lease's process, writes
  it once for a standing entry whose branch the refs no longer list and for a trace from before the field
  (`LandedCleanups.MarkAbsentAsync`). A read that failed writes nothing and is asked again at the next look.
  - The inventory reads a unit whose every landing went as `missing`, with nothing of it standing, as **dropped** (UNIT1a's word,
    §D3's "or the person drops it"): its cleanup completed, the unit retired. **This amends §B2's "dropped is never inferred"** for
    this one case, by the owner's direction: the record is git's word that the branch is gone and Daoris's that it did not remove it,
    so it is read, not guessed. The end says its commits may survive only in the person's remote or on another branch, and never
    that the line holds them.
  - **A doubt keeps it, and says which**: a branch of its name standing; git not yet asked; or its session branch still standing,
    whose work nothing proved anywhere. Those read *not ended*, pending and not blocked, so they are not in *What needs you*.
  - The trace stays only as END2's rules keep landings' traces (the newest 50 per repository, LEFT3).
- **Recovery refs get a retention, amending D102's SQUASHTIDY1c note** (its note there, CLEANUP1): a ref under
  `refs/daoris/discarded/` whose commit a branch or a tag here holds by ancestry keeps nothing no other ref keeps, so the look's tidy
  removes it once it is 30 days old, in one ref transaction that verifies that holder at the commit read
  (`RecoveryRetention.TidyAsync`, through `ContentHolds.DropKeptVerifiedAsync`). Remote-tracking refs and the recovery refs themselves
  are no holders. One whose commit nothing else holds is kept, at any age, and its entry says how to delete it
  (`git update-ref -d <ref>`, D102's own sentence: no new verb). Thirty days because git keeps an unreachable reflog entry as long
  by default, and a ref holds history the folders' fourteen days do not; counted from its commit's date, since git keeps none for a
  ref, so a ref made for an older commit may go sooner, losing nothing its holder does not keep. Held to its retention at most once a
  day for each repository in a process; the look's report says each removal and the holder.
- **Daoris's own recoveries no record names are no unit** (`WorkInventory.Recoveries`): a folder moved aside with its purge date, and
  a recovery ref. Each is kept by its own rule, owned by it, and waits on nobody, so listing it as a unit with an unknown end read as a
  problem when it is a recovery waiting its date. `work state` lists them after the units, and `WORK_STATE` answers them as
  `recovery` (additive). A folder whose name says no date is never purged by itself, so it stays a unit whose owner is unknown: that
  one does need the person.
- **The page** says `dropped` as *Over without its result* (已结束，未留成果), on the work's foot and on a branch's line.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `CleanupLeftoversTests` (fast: the dropped end and its retirement, the three doubts, each recovery
  rule's words, the folder before its date as a recovery, the folder with no date still a unit, none of the three kinds waiting on the
  person, the terminal's words, the record's field), `CleanupGitTests` (Git: a deleted landing branch marked and read dropped, an older
  trace asked once, an unread branch kept, a name in another case kept, a held ref past retention removed, one nothing holds kept and
  listed, a held one within retention kept, and the look running it and saying so), `WorkMilestone.test.tsx` (the page's words in
  both languages).
- **Not built here**: Ask Daoris's *Where work has got to* still reads units alone, so it no longer names a recovery no unit names.
- **After review** (a second maker's, four findings and two notes; not for an automatic look on the owner's machine before them):
  1. **Only the person's refs hold a recovery ref's commit** (D102's CLEANUP1 note says which, exactly). Round 1 took a session branch
     as a holder; the same look could then remove that branch on a squash's record (`TreeSweeps`, with no recovery since nothing held it
     by content), and the commit was held by nothing.
  2. **A rename is not an end.** Before a landing reads `missing`, the clean-up and the look ask what holds its recorded tip
     (`LandedCleanups.WentAsync`: one `for-each-ref --contains`, once per landing read missing). A local branch that holds it by ancestry
     writes `moved` with that branch (`LandedBranch.MovedTo`, additive; a branch of the person's named first, a session branch only
     where no other holds it), read *not ended*: its work goes on there. A moved trace is followed: the look asks it again once the refs
     no longer list the branch it moved to, which costs no git of its own while that branch stands. Only work no local branch holds is
     dropped.
  3. **No breadcrumb retires alone.** Where neither a tag nor a recovery ref holds the recorded tip (a remote-tracking ref does not
     count: a fetch prunes it), the commit is first kept on a recovery ref of the landing's name (`ContentHolds.KeepDiscardedAsync`,
     recorded as `LandedBranch.KeptOn`, additive). Nothing of the person's holds it, so it stays at any age, is listed with its delete
     command, and the dropped end names it. A ref not made writes nothing, asked again; a commit the repository no longer has
     (`cat-file -e` exit 1, git's collection having taken it) is missing with nothing to keep. The cost, taken for the guarantee: a ref
     for each such landing, one the remote still tracks included.
  4. **An observation names its landing.** The look's `GoneAs(entry, …)` and the clean-up's `Gone(entry, …)` apply to the entry read,
     by its identity (repository, branch, session, recorded time) and the tip read, never by name: a landing of the same name recorded
     between the read and the write keeps its standing.
  5. **A purge that cannot finish needs the person.** A folder moved aside past its date that the purge keeps (a write after the move,
     which the purge compares with at every pass; a link; a folder that cannot be read) is read by the inventory's gather as the purge
     reads it (`TreeSetAside.Kept`, now shared) and is a unit of its own, its entry *blocked* with the purge's reason, so *What needs
     you* lists it. The cost: the gather walks such a folder to its first late write, and one the purge will delete is walked once more
     before the next pass takes it.
  - **Held by**, each red on round 1's branch first: `CleanupLeftoversTests` (a moved landing kept, saying where; a dropped end naming
    its kept ref; a late-written folder in *What needs you*, read from disk by the gather; an old observation beside a new landing; the
    record's new fields) and `CleanupGitTests` (Git: a session branch or a landing branch never holds; a renamed branch reads moved; a
    dropped landing whose tip nothing holds gets a recovery ref; a moved landing asked again once its branch goes).
  - **The re-check** (the coordinator's, after those; finding 3 held):
    1. **A symbolic ref is never a holder**, read by git's own `%(symref)` (`ContentHolds.Unaliased`), never by its name: in the
       retention, in its read, and in every proof by ancestry (`ContentHolds.HoldersAsync`, which the sweep's and the removals' proofs
       share). A `personal` that names `daoris/<session>` passed the name check, and the sweep could then remove the branch it named.
    2. **A moved landing keeps the commit its branch held the work at** (`LandedBranch.MovedToAt`, additive). The look asks again once
       that branch stands anywhere else, so a name made again at an unrelated commit is never taken for the work; while it stands there
       the look reads it from the refs it already holds. The cost: a branch that goes on moving (a session branch still worked on) is
       asked again, one `for-each-ref --contains` and one write, at each look where it moved. Only a remote's `HEAD` is left out of the
       holders by name, and a symbolic ref by git's word, so a local branch called `feature/HEAD` is a branch like any other.
    3. **The recovery for a dropped landing is made in one ref transaction with git's word that its branch is still absent**
       (`update-ref --stdin`: `verify refs/heads/<branch>` with no id, then `create`; `ContentHolds.KeepDiscardedAsync`'s `absent`). A
       branch made again between the read and the keep refuses it: no ref, nothing written, and the next look reads the branch standing.
       A rename in that same instant still reads dropped with its commit kept, so nothing is lost; that window is not closed.
    4. **A delete the purge could not finish** after its read found nothing late (a file held open against it) is kept where the gather
       reads it, `trees/.tidied/purge-failed.json`, written by each pass with the folders it met so and gone where none; the gather reads
       it beside its own read, so the folder is a blocked unit with the purge's reason until a later pass deletes it.
    - **Held by**, each red on the round's branch first: `CleanupGitTests` (Git: a symbolic branch never holds; a moved-to name made
      again elsewhere is asked again; a rename to `feature/HEAD` reads moved; the keep refused once the branch stands again) and
      `CleanupLeftoversTests` (a folder whose delete failed is in *What needs you* until a pass takes it, on Windows, where a file held
      open blocks its delete).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (LANDGIT1, a landing asks git once per fact).** One landing started 61 git processes (the look's automatic one), 54 (the press on a squash rule) or 45 (on a merge rule), some facts four to six times (`rev-parse --show-toplevel` 6, `symbolic-ref --short -q HEAD` 5, `rev-parse --verify refs/heads/main^{commit}` 4, `merge-base` 2 to 3): each step of the landing, its proofs and its tidy asked what it needed. They now start 52, 51 and 42, a smaller saving than a first version's (34, 42, 34), which review refused: it kept every ref and head read, and each of three traced cases acted on a stale answer. The shape is `GitFacts`, the landing's planning table, beside STARTGIT1's passing down: the one runner, `WorkingTree.GitAsync`, answers a read it has already been asked in the flow. **Cached**: what an id answers (`rev-parse --verify`, `merge-base`, `log` of full commit or tree ids and ranges between them, with no option that reads refs), `rev-parse --show-toplevel` and `--git-common-dir`, and `symbolic-ref --short refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`. **Never cached**: anything naming `HEAD` or a ref (the session's head, a branch's tip, `for-each-ref`, `log --branches --remotes`), which another process moves, and `status` and `diff`. So a judgement, a replay's source, the tidy's freshness re-reads, `UnlandedAsync` and a fresh `RefSnapshot` each read git as it stands (the three cases: the cleanup deleting the session branch on a cached "nothing unlanded" after the landing branch was moved; a replay built from a cached head; the tidy's "re-read" returning the scan's tip before `worktree remove`). The table is opened by `AutoLander.LandOneAsync` and `TreeLands.LandingAsync` and closed by `GitFacts.Close()` before the tree is followed, the end is kept and the tidy runs; a closed table answers nothing in any flow that still holds it (a child task is never answered from a table after it ends).

🔴 It is never kept across a write, as `RefSnapshot` (PROC1d-ii) is not: every git that `RefSnapshot.MayWrite` names drops the table before it starts and after it ends, so what a merge, a replay, the branch made or the worktree removed changed is read again, and a command a landing does not know drops it too. The plugin's push is no git of ours, so `TreeLands` drops the table around it. A call with its own environment or bound (a fetch) and a test's stand-in answer are never kept; a git that did not start and a cancelled read are not kept either. A question of commits by id asked in the session's tree or in the repository's checkout is one question: `CommonDirAndBranchAsync` pairs the two once it has read the tree's common dir, moving what the tree was asked before, and a `worktree` write unpairs them. What the landing still asks as often as its steps do: the head, the line's and the branches' tips, `status`, and everything the tidy reads. `LandGitCountTests` holds the ceilings per door, that no id question repeats without a write of the landing's between, and a regression per review case with an outside ref move between the asking and its use (`What_another_process_moves_is_asked_of_git_each_time`, `A_closed_table_is_inert_in_every_flow_that_holds_it`). Left: a safe way to save the head and tip reads (passing them down with a re-read where an act follows), as a row of its own.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-UNIT1d, a plugin's steps in its manifest: the one node declaration).** The design's §D9, with §D6's
performer's contract and §D3's resources as far as a manifest declares them, for the owner's *"a system that's easier to work with
plugins and new steps, maximizing the automation"*: a plugin declares a step once, and it is a node exactly as a built-in is. Only
read: nothing is sent, dispatched, supervised or cleaned (PERFORM1a onward).

- **The shape:** `steps: [{ id, version?, reach, inputs?, outputs?, evidence?: { schema }, retry?: { maxAttempts, delaysSeconds? },
  timeoutSeconds?, skippable?, rejectable?, perform?: { point?, idempotent?, reconcile?, transient? } }]` and
  `resources: [{ schema, role?, cleanup }]`. Absent keys read as before, so an older manifest is unchanged.
- **One model.** A step is read into `NodeDeclaration` (`Plugins/Domain/PluginSteps.cs`, `PluginManifest.Steps` and `Resources`),
  which gains `Perform` (`NodePerform { Idempotent, Reconcile, Transient }`), and is judged by `NodeDeclarations.Problem`. Three
  checks join it: a `perform` on any node but a plugin's performed one (`perform`); a transient code not lowercase letters, digits
  and dashes, named twice, or with no retry to take it (`transient`); a plugin's retry of more than one attempt that nothing can take
  (`retry`). A declaration, a `NodeRetry` and a `NodePerform` are now equal by what they say, so the step read is the one built by
  hand.
- **The refusals:** 21 codes, each with its words: a field's name (a step's shape and `Problem` share one code per field), or
  `step-twice` and `resource-twice`; `PluginStepCodes` holds those `Problem` does not return. The first broken rule is said, and it
  refuses the plugin, as a skill's does (§C4). A refused plugin keeps no steps or resources.
- **The twin:** the CLI's `plugins/steps.ts` (`stepsDeclared`, `declarationProblem`, `isSchema`), read by `readManifest` after the
  skills. `plugin-steps.test.ts` parses `PluginStepsTests`' theory (63 rows) and holds its own table to it, cell for cell.
- **Choices §D6, §D9 and §E2 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **The kind and the id are `<plugin>/<id>`.** The manifest writes the short id (§C4: lowercase letters, digits and dashes, at
     most 40). A plugin declares its own kind only, so no kind is written. The declaration's id is the kind, as §D4's example names
     `acme.deploy/deploy`, so two plugins' `deploy` never meet in one graph; a second placement's id is GRAPH1's.
  2. **No `after`, and inputs are ports.** §D4's `after` and its `inputs: { step, output }` references are part E's wires (§E3).
     A manifest declares a node's ports, `inputs` and `outputs` as a name and its schema, as `NodeDeclaration` holds them. A field
     the reader does not know (`after`, §C4's `label`, §D4's `creates`) is passed over, as a tool's is.
  3. **`reach` is required**, `observed` or `performed`. `version` is 1 when absent, and `evidence` is `{ schema }`, as §D4 writes it.
  4. **A schema is `<owner>/<name>@<version>`**, its owner a plugin id by `PluginCatalog.IsId` and `isPluginId` (REFAC1's one
     owner of that shape), its name words parted by single dots or dashes, its version from 1. It holds no `:`, since a reference
     is `<schema>:<id>` (§D6). Ports are named as steps are.
  5. **`perform` is a performed plugin step's alone.** Absent on one, nothing is declared: the step is dispatched and never
     redelivered by itself. `point` may be absent or `work/perform`. `reconcile` is the point's name, `work/reconcile`, read as a
     flag, never `true`.
  6. **A retry that can never be taken is refused**, as NODE1's choice 9 refuses one on a kind nothing can fail. A plugin's retry of
     more than one attempt needs `transient` codes, and `idempotent` or `reconcile` (§D6: only a listed code retries, and a step with
     neither is never redelivered by itself); transient codes need such a retry. So an observed plugin step, which declares no
     `perform`, takes no automatic retry. Daoris's own performed `quest.close` keeps its kind's retry.
  7. **Who cleans a resource is said, never defaulted.** `cleanup` is required, and only `work/cleanup` (§D3: an unknown owner blocks
     a cleanup). `role` is `temporary` or `result`, `temporary` when absent (§D3's table: *temporary or result, as declared*). No step
     names the resources it creates: a receipt's schema is checked against the plugin's resources (PERFORM1d).
  8. **`hooks.points` is not yet held to the steps and resources** (a performed step to `work/perform`, a resource to
     `work/cleanup`): those points join `HookPoints` with their transport, so PERFORM1a and PERFORM1d hold them.
  9. **The twins read JSON alike:** `2.0` is `2`, and a fraction, text or a number past 32 bits is refused; of a name written twice
     in one object, the last is kept.
- **Proof** (fast, each test seen failing first): `PluginStepsTests` (the theory; a manifest's step equal to the declaration built
  by hand, its authority a plugin's own node's; an older manifest unchanged; a step's problem refusing the plugin; a plugin refused
  by the catalogue keeping no steps), `NodeLifecycleTests` (the three checks, built-ins untouched; equality by what a declaration
  says) and `plugin-steps.test.ts` (the driver's theory parsed and held, each row read by the catalogue and by `stepsDeclared`, the
  declaration built by hand, an older manifest's whole shape, the refusal).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-PERFORM1a, the work wire: its transport and supervision).** The design's §C4 transport and §D6's
`work.perform@1`, for the owner's *"maximizing the automation"*: the wire a performer and an observer speak, and the lease's loop
owning their processes. Nothing is dispatched, believed or recorded yet: no caller tells the awaited set or takes the events (GRAPH1,
PERFORM1b), so no work process starts on an installed Daoris.

- **The wire** (`Plugins/Domain/WorkWire.cs`, the one writer and reader of each message, Daoris's side and a plugin's):
  `hook/work/watch` `{ generation, resync, units: [{ repository, workspace, root, branch, tip, line, pullRequest, awaiting }] }`,
  answered and never read; `work/step`, the plugin's notification with no id, `{ step, state, code?, evidence?: { ref, url,
  environment, message }, outputs?, at, id, repository?, branch?, commit?, invocation? }`; `hook/work/perform` `{ unit, step,
  stepVersion, invocation, idempotencyKey, subjectRevision, inputs, deadlineAt }`, answered `{ invocation }`; `hook/work/reconcile`
  `{ unit, step, invocation, idempotencyKey, subjectRevision }`, answered `{ state, outcome? }`. The handshake keeps `work/watch`,
  `work/step` and `work/perform` (`HookPoints.Work`) and the capability `work.perform@1` (`IHookChannel.Capabilities`);
  `protocolVersion` stays 1.
- **Bounds, before anything is believed:** a frame over 64 KB from a work plugin's process (choice 5) is dropped unread (`BoundedLines` reads stdout
  in blocks and passes an over-long line over without keeping it); 60 events a minute are taken from one plugin (`EventRate`); 256
  wait, the oldest dropped first; four requests are in flight per process. Each refusal is a `plugin.step` line of plugin, code and
  count, at most once a minute per plugin and code (`RefusalTally`; counts held past their minute are written at a later look), never
  the frame's words. The codes: `too-long`, `too-fast`, `queue-full`, `shape`, `not-sender`; §C4's belief codes are PERFORM1c's.
- **Supervision** (`HookSet`, holding `WorkPlugins`): a plugin whose `hooks.points` names a work point is started by the loop that
  holds the home's lease (`HomeLease.HeldHere`, asked at each look) when the awaited set (`HookSet.Watch`) names one of its steps, and
  stopped (`shutdown`, ended 2 s later) once none does (`plugin.stopped` why `idle`), once the lease is not this process's
  (`not-held`), when it is switched off or updated, and when the loop ends. A verb never holds a hook set, and a `--share` loop never
  starts, hears or tells one. An exit Daoris did not ask for waits 1, 5, then 15 minutes (`WorkRestarts`), and three within an hour
  mark the plugin unsound; Daoris's own stop never counts. **What the person sees:** the console's line (*started again in 1
  minute*, or *not started again until you try again*) and the plugin's health failing at `process`, `exited`, then `unsound`.
- **Generations and resync:** each start is the plugin's next generation, every event read is tagged with the generation of the
  process it was read from, and one not of the current generation when it arrives or is taken is refused (`not-sender`), so an answer
  from a process since restarted is never applied. `HookSet.TakeSteps` gives `WorkStepHeard(plugin, generation, frame)`, read by
  shape. The first frame to each generation says `resync: true`, and so does the next after any drop. `PerformAsync` and
  `ReconcileStepAsync` go only to the current, listened process that named `work/perform` and the capability, and return its
  generation; a request cannot cross generations, since a process's pending calls fail as it exits.
- **What is persisted: nothing.** Generations, waits and queues are the loop's own, in memory, as its record of a plugin's health is
  (D119 §2): a new loop starts at generation 1 with nothing queued, so nothing a dead loop heard can be taken. **PERFORM1b owns the
  attempts:** written on the unit's canonical record by its one writer before dispatch (§D6), with NODE1c's `Cleared` history and
  WIRE1e's attempt numbers increasing across runs, keeping the generation `PerformAsync` returns.
- **The manifest** (UNIT1d's choice 8): a performed step whose `hooks.points` does not name `work/perform` is refused when read
  (`perform-point`), after the step's own shape, on both twins. A resource's `work/cleanup` stays PERFORM1d's.
- **Choices §C4 and §D6 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **Daoris's calls keep the wire's `hook/` prefix** (`hook/work/perform`, as every call on this wire is, D64 §4), where §D6's
     example writes `work/perform`: the kit's one dispatcher routes them. The event stays `work/step`, the one method a plugin sends.
  2. **The work points stay out of `HookPoints.All`** until PERFORM1e gives them their kit entries, Ask Daoris's sentence and the
     page's gloss: `All` is what the kit scaffolds and lets a person try. The handshake knows both lists.
  3. **`work/watch` is a request answered and never read**, as `session/ended` is, sent at once and never waited for by a look or
     by `Watch`'s caller; one that fails is a `plugin.failed` at `work/watch`, and the set is told again with `resync`.
  4. **The generation is the process's**, from 1 in each loop and never reused in it, and the frame carries it. A stale process's
     event is `not-sender` (§C4's first rule: its sender is no longer the plugin's process), so no new code was needed.
  5. **The 64 KB bound is a work plugin's stdout**, every frame of a process whose `hooks.points` names a work point. *Changed by
     round 2:* it was every plugin process's, so a valid landing answer carrying a 70 KB diagnostic was dropped and waited out its
     patience, while the kit's trial, reading whole, passed it. Any other plugin is read under `HookPeer.AnswerBytes` (4 MB): far past
     any answer its points read (a decision, a landing's sentence and link, a pull request's state), and still a bound on one line.
     `HookProcess.FrameLimit` names the bound once, for the driver and the trial alike. An answer over its bound fails its call at once
     as `unreadable` (a scan of a few fields keeps the dropped line's top-level `id`), never as a late one; and a line ends at LF, CRLF
     or a bare CR, as `ReadLineAsync` ended one before the bound. A frame dropped on a process nobody listens to is said under the
     plugin's name.
  6. **Four requests in flight per process**; a fifth waits the peer's patience for a place, and is `late` without one.
  7. **The wait grows with each unasked exit since the process last stayed up an hour**, so exits an hour apart are never unsound
     and wait 15 minutes from the third; a start that fails counts as an exit. Switching the plugin off, updating or removing it
     starts its record afresh. *Changed by round 2:* an update was noticed only when the id left the catalogue, so an unsound plugin,
     whose process no longer runs, stayed unsound after its update; each plugin's version and install (`HookSet.Build`, what `Why` calls
     an update) are kept, and another starts the record afresh whether a process runs or not. An exit seen partway through a look is an
     unasked exit too: it was removed uncounted and started again in the same look.
  8. **Unsound** is a `plugin.failed` at `process` of kind `unsound`; **Try again** is `HookSet.TryAgain`, with no door yet.
  9. **A drop's resync waits for the first look a minute after the last resync**, so a plugin that floods is not asked to flood again
     at once; a restart's is its first frame. *Changed by round 2:* the minute after any resync takes, beside its 60, one event per
     awaited step the frame named, and the queue holds as many more (`EventRate.Allow`): held to the 60, a replay of 61 in a stable
     order lost its last at every resync, for ever. Daoris's own awaited set sizes that room, never the plugin.
  10. **A completion binds to its `invocation`**: only an event without one needs a repository, a branch and a full commit (40 or 64
      hex). `outputs` only on `reached`, at most 8, each a declared schema's reference of 200 characters at most; `evidence.url` a web
      page; a field the reader does not know (a `plugin` above all) passed over. `pull-request`'s evidence fields are PERFORM1c's.
  11. **A plugin speaking a loop point and a work point keeps one process**, started by any loop for its loop point; only the lease's
      loop hears it and tells it the set, and its restarts wait as a work plugin's.
- **Proof** (fast, each test seen failing first): `WorkWireTests` (45: every message round-trips, a step event read by shape field by
  field, the bounds on its words), `WorkBoundsTests` (10), `WorkPeerTests` (9: `work/step` reaches only a listener on a process that
  declared it, the frame bound, the capability, the receipt, four in flight), `WorkSupervisionTests` (14: no process without the
  lease, started when awaited and stopped when not, the waits, unsound and Try again, a stale generation refused, the rate, the
  queue, the refusal lines and counts, the performer's checks), and the `perform-point` rows of `PluginStepsTests` and
  `plugin-steps.test.ts`. Process: `WorkProcessTests` (a node stand-in crashed unasked, restarted as generation 2, and its generation
  1 events refused; a 70 000-character frame on a real pipe dropped and the next event heard).
- **After review** (round 2, the same day: seven findings, each tested first). The bound and the bare CR (choice 5); an update starting
  the record afresh, and an exit partway through a look counted (choice 7); a resync's replay taken whole (choice 9). **The generation
  fence:** an answer is handed back only while the process that gave it is still the plugin's current generation (`HookSet.HandedBack`),
  so one completed just before a stop, or read from stdout buffered past an exit, after the next start had the plugin's name, is refused
  as `exited`, never returned as the new process's. **A hybrid** kept for its loop point while the lease was elsewhere is heard again on
  its own generation and told the set with a resync once the lease returns (`WorkPlugins.Reattach`); it had stayed deaf until it
  restarted. **One grace bounds a disposal**, the write of `shutdown` included (`HookPeer.ShutdownAsync`): a plugin that stopped reading
  its stdin held that write, and its process outlived the lease and the loop. **What PERFORM1b must still check:** the fence holds at
  the moment of hand-back only, so the attempt is written with its generation before dispatch and its receipt after; a call refused as
  stale, late or exited leaves its receipt unknown, which `work/reconcile` on the current generation answers, never a blind second
  dispatch; and a completion binds to its invocation, not its generation, so a later generation's replay of it is believed. **Proof:**
  `WorkBoundsTests` (the CR, the scan of a dropped frame), `WorkPeerTests` (a bare-CR answer, an answer too long failing at once wherever
  its id stands, the shutdown's write bounded), `WorkSupervisionTests` (the fence for perform and reconcile, 61 and 300 awaited steps
  across three resyncs, the update, the hybrid, the bound's rule, the exit partway through a look); Process: `WorkProcessTests` (a 70 KB
  landing answer from a real process delivered; a plugin that stops reading killed within the grace) and `PluginKitTests` (the trial
  reading a long answer as the driver does).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-WORKFLOW2, D157's versions decoded as graphs).** The design's §E7 migration contract, with §E4's kinds and
canaries 49 and 50. Pure, in `Trees/Nodes/WorkflowGraphs.cs`: `WorkflowGraphs.Decode(NamedVersion)` reads a stored version as a
`NodeGraph`, or as unreadable with its reason, never in part; nothing is written, so a workflow's file and a run's binding stay byte for
byte. The CLI's `workflows/workflowgraphs.ts` is its twin, and both read the CLI's `test/fixtures/workflow-graphs.json`, row for row.

- **The mapping:** step order is `after` wires; a group is a synthetic `all` join with the group's id, its members wired into it and it
  into the next step; `failed: stop` is a `failed` wire to a synthetic sink, `wait` one to a synthetic person's task; a person's skip is
  the node's `skipped`. The first step is the entry, the last the end (a group's join where a group is last), the cleanup at *held*.
- **The identity** is `WorkflowNamed.Digest`, unchanged. A decoded graph's digest (`WorkflowGraphs.GraphDigest`, `gph-`) is derived
  from the decoder's version (`DecoderVersion`, 1), that identity and the graph's own `NodeGraphs.Digest`: a run on a decoded version
  binds it, never the plain digest, so a changed mapping binds afresh. `ForRun` gives a bound run its version with the binding's digest
  as its identity; a file that does not read, a version it no longer holds, or a version whose digest is not the binding's reads as
  unreadable, never decoded on the file's version in its place.
- **Choices §E7 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **What decodes is what reads.** A version naming a kind this build does not run (`check`, `stage` and `all` today) is unreadable
     with D157's own sentence, as §E7's *unsupported* says. The mapping covers every kind and is proved on the steps' shape
     (`ReadShape`; the CLI's new `readShape`, split out of `readSteps`), so the build that runs them decodes them unchanged.
  2. **Schema 2.** The decoded graph is a graph, in the graph's schema; the stored version stays schema 1 and is never saved as one.
  3. **The kinds** (§E4): `work` is `claimed` (a held done is still `claimed`; the holds stay the rules' gate at the landing, no node
     yet); `opinion` is a new built-in, `opinion`, observed, reached by its gate's record (`NodeSource.Opinion`), never failed and
     skippable; `landing` is `handed` alone, with no `pushed` or `line` added, since no version names them and the end judgement keeps
     reading a bound version's landing (END2a); a check is `<plugin>/work.checks`, observed (the plugin point's name, its slash a dot,
     which no stage's id can hold); a stage is `<plugin>/<stage>`, performed, as §E7's `acme.deploy/deploy`.
  4. **Every field a step takes is its node's option**, as written, absence as its default: what the adapter reads (`start: you` is the
     stage adapter's, PERFORM1b and HUMAN1), never a condition.
  5. **The sink is a new built-in kind, `stop`**: observed, reached on the receipt of the path into it (`NodeSource.Path`), never
     skipped and never the end. The person's task is a synthetic `human`. Each carries `{ outcome, step }`, with the id
     `<step>:failed` or `<step>:rejected`, which no D157 id can take. Wires are `<from>><to>`.
  6. **`send-back` is the adapter's one return, then the person's task:** a `failed` wire to a synthetic `human`, since D157 §3.7 reads
     the step after its return *as failed*, the person's doors.
  7. **A member's own `failed` takes no path:** a member failed is the group failed (D157 §3.3), so the group's policy governs; the
     member's field stays its option.
  8. **A go-ahead's `refused` decodes as `failed` does**, on its `rejected` wire: `stop` the sink, `wait` the person's task.
  9. **D157's skip** (NODE1c's choice 3 kept for decoding): a group's is its members' (`WorkflowGraphs.Skipped`), since a join is never
     skipped; the work and the landing are never skipped, as D157's run never took the person's skip on them.
  10. **The protected order holds the opinion** (GRAPH1's choice 5): every opinion reaches every look and every landing, as a look
      reaches every landing.
  11. **No `data` wire:** D157's steps declare no inputs, a step's commit being its run's binding (D157 §5.3).
- **Not built here:** the evaluation of a sink reached on its path and of the opinion observed from its gate's record; a D157 run's step
  states read as node statuses (canary 38's equivalence over recorded runs); a graph stored as a version (GRAPH1b); the editor
  (GRAPHEDIT1); the cutover that binds a run to a decoded graph.
- **Proof** (fast; the decoder's and the opinion's order seen failing first, on the members alone): `WorkflowGraphsTests` (the shared table, 6 rows: the smallest version, a default written
  and left out, every kind this build runs with a go-ahead refused each way, a kind it does not run, a version that does not read, and
  a group with every policy from the shape; a maximal 24-step version three ways within its synthetic bound; each kind's node; a skip
  on each kind; a graph that does not hold, unreadable whole; decoding twice; a bound run's identity, with both files byte for byte),
  `WireEvaluationTests` (canary 49: a group skipped by its members reaching its join and the next step, a member's failure failing the
  group under the group's policy, a `stop` taking its sink), `NodeGraphsTests` (the opinion after the look and after the landing, a
  sink as the end), `NodeLifecycleTests` (the two kinds through every row), and the CLI's `workflowgraphs.test.ts` (the same table,
  digests included; the driver's constants; the shape; decoding twice).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-PERFORM1b, durable execution; with FABRIC-WIRE1f).** The design's §D6 and §E2's attempts, on PERFORM1a's
wire: a plugin's performed node dispatched so that no attempt is lost or made twice across a restart. Nothing calls it from the look
yet (the runtime's row below), so no installed Daoris dispatches.

- **The unit's record** (`Trees/Nodes/NodeRecord.cs`, `NodeRecordFiles`): `<home>/workflows/units/<digest>.json`, one per unit, its name
  the first 32 hex of the SHA-256 of the unit's id (which the file holds), beside D157's `workflows/runs/`. It keeps every node's status
  on the unit (bindings, scopes and acceptances whole), the attempts (`NodeAttempt`: node, attempt, plugin, the `work/perform` call
  with its invocation, key, revision, inputs and deadline, and its state `recorded`, `sent`, `unknown`, `settled` or `held`, with the
  generation that last answered, its deliveries, outcome, code, `retryAt` and `askAt`), the trigger firings, and the history moves
  cleared (`NodeMoved.Cleared`, with when and why). Written whole, atomically, BOM-less and LF, *changed by review:* only by
  `NodeRecordFiles.Edit`, one read-modify-write in one hold of `RecordLock` with the lease asked inside it. A file that does not read
  is never taken for an empty record, and neither is one whose structure does not hold or that does not agree with itself (*review*):
  nothing is started or applied on that unit.
- **The performer** (`Trees/Nodes/WorkPerformer.cs`), over `IWorkPerformPort`, which `HookSet` implements with PERFORM1a's fenced
  calls. `StartAsync` applies the evaluation's start through the one table and writes the status, the attempt (`recorded`) and the
  start's firings in one write, then sends; acknowledged, the attempt is `sent` with its generation; a failed or unanswered call
  makes the node and the attempt unknown; a cancelled token (the loop ending) writes nothing more. `Hear` applies a completion to the
  attempt its invocation names; `ReconcileAsync` asks after every attempt due; `Due` makes ready the nodes whose retry delay passed;
  `Keep` writes an evaluation's moves and their history.
- **Choices §D6, §E2 and the PERFORM1a review left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **The record is the driver's own file per unit**, not the unit's existing record: a quest's and an ask's are the service's to
     write (UNIT1a), and a branch's (`session-branches.json`, `landings.json`) hold trees and landings. One writer, the driver in the
     loop that holds the lease (PERFORM1a's `HookSet` refuses a call elsewhere); the lock guards against a verb or a second loop.
     *Changed by review:* the lock held only the final replace, so two starts read the same ready node and both dispatched; it now holds
     the read, the judgement and the write, and the lease is asked inside it (`not-held`), so a loop that lost it writes nothing.
  2. **The key and the invocation are random per attempt** (`k-` and 32 hex, `i-` and 24 hex), never derived from the unit, node and
     attempt: a record lost or reset would number its attempts from 1 again, and the plugin's durable deduplication would take a new
     attempt for an old one. Both are written before the first send and never change; a redelivery sends the same call.
  3. **What is asked after** (§D6's *after a restart, after its deadline, after a lost connection*): an attempt `recorded` (a loop
     ended between the write and the send), `unknown`, or `sent` by a loop since ended, to a process since replaced, or past its
     deadline. Since a new loop's generations start again at 1, the performer keeps the invocations its own loop delivered and asks
     after every other. The node is moved active → unknown first and written, then asked; reconciliation needs the plugin's process.
  4. **The answers, by §D6's table:** `done` records its outcome as `work/step` would; `not-started` delivers again with the same key
     where the attempt is still current, and otherwise settles it and lifts the gate (`NodeLifecycle.Reconciled`); `running` waits to the
     deadline, or a fresh timeout past it (`askAt`); `failed` follows `transient`; `unknown` holds it for the person. A step declaring
     `idempotent` alone is delivered again with its key (the plugin deduplicates); one declaring neither is held: never redelivered by
     itself. *Changed by review:* an `idempotent`-only attempt was given a made-up `not-started`, so one whose node had been stopped was
     settled and its gate lifted while its effect might still run, and its completion was later dropped as a replay. Idempotency allows
     the same key again; it does not say the effect never began. Such an attempt is redelivered only while it is current, its answer
     awaited by `work/step`; one its node has moved off is held (`not-current`), its gate kept, and its late completion still settles it.
  5. **The generation that answered:** an answer is taken only from the attempt's plugin (§C4's first rule: the sender is the process)
     and only at the generation now current (`not-sender` otherwise, PERFORM1a's code). A completion binds to its invocation, not its
     generation, so a later process's replay of an outcome lost with the earlier one is taken; an outcome the attempt already has is a
     `replay`, applied once. An earlier attempt's outcome is a stale one's (§D3): it settles that attempt and lifts the gate, moving
     nothing.
  6. **Retries:** only a failure whose code the step's `perform.transient` lists, with tries left under its `retry`, gets a `retryAt`
     (`NodeLifecycle.DelayBefore` from the failure), persisted on the attempt, so a restart keeps the delay; `Due` makes the node ready
     after it, and its next start is a new attempt with a new key. A failure without a code is `unspecified`, never transient.
     *Changed by review:* the delay is the next try's within the binding (`Tries + 1`, what `retry` bounds), not the attempt's lifetime
     number, which keeps growing across bindings and runs and indexed a later delay after a person's new attempt or a new run.
  7. **An outcome that does not bind** (the one table refuses it: an undeclared output, another schema, the declared evidence schema
     missing) holds the attempt with the refusal's code; the node stays where it is, its gate held, for the person. Evidence is the
     plugin's reference at the declared schema, else the node's kind; an output's schema is the one its reference names. Whether any of
     it is believed is PERFORM1c's.
  8. **`Keep` writes over nothing it did not read:** a status is written only where the record still holds what was evaluated, so an
     outcome applied between the read and the write is never lost (`moved` otherwise). *Changed by review:* compared inside the lock, and
     the same holds for every answer that comes after an await: a send's receipt and a reconciliation's answer are applied to the record
     as it is then, to their own invocation alone, and only while that attempt still waits for them. A receipt counts its delivery and
     moves to `sent` only an attempt still `recorded` or `unknown`; an answer applies only to an attempt still `unknown`. Before, a
     receipt wrote back the snapshot taken before its send, dropping another node's start made meanwhile, and a `running` answer put a
     node heard reached back to active.
  9. **Bounds:** every unsettled attempt is kept, with the newest 50 settled ones (the invocation binding a replay needs) and the newest
     200 history entries. *Changed by review:* every attempt a status still needs is kept, whether settled or not: each node's current
     one (a pending retry is its failed node's current attempt, and `Due` reads its delay there) and one a node holds unreconciled; the
     newest 50 settled others beside them. A pending retry was trimmed as a settled attempt, and its timer lost.
- **FABRIC-WIRE1f, a failure or a rejection kept from another run:** a new run lapses it (`WireWhy.RunScope`, `run-scope`), as
  `skip-scope` lapses a skip: invalidated with its history kept, judged again, and started afresh in this run, its tries counted afresh
  and its attempt numbers still growing (WIRE1e's note). In its own run a failure Daoris will not retry is kept for the person. Why: an
  outcome other than a reach is its run's alone (WIRE1e), and a run is a pass the person or the rules began afresh, so keeping run 1's
  failure in run 2 would leave run 2 stalled on a node that neither proceeds nor says anything new; a reach is kept across runs, since
  its acceptance omits the run. A stop has no run and stands. A dispatched attempt still unreconciled keeps its gate through the lapse.
  The two WIRE1e tests that went back from run 2 to run 1 now judge run 1 first: a run never returns to an earlier one.
- **Not built here** (rows): the runtime in the lease's loop that reads the governing graphs, the units and their revisions, tells
  `HookSet.Watch` the awaited set, takes `TakeSteps`, and runs `Hear`, `ReconcileAsync`, `Due`, the evaluation, `Keep` and the starts
  at each look and wake; what the person sees of a held or unknown attempt, and their new attempt and stop on it; evidence
  authorization (PERFORM1c); resources and their cleanup (PERFORM1d).
- **Proof** (fast; the performer's and WIRE1f's lapse tests seen failing first, the record's written with it): `WorkPerformerTests` (8: the attempt on the record before its send; a loop ended
  between the record and the send reconciled, never sent twice; a duplicate answer applied once; a stale generation's answer and
  another plugin's refused, the current one's replay taken; a transient failure retried with a new key after its persisted delay, over
  a restart; a non-transient one never; an unknown answer held with no new attempt; a done answer recorded), `WireEvaluationTests`
  (WIRE1f: a new run lapsing a failure and starting it afresh, a rejection likewise with a reach kept; in its own run kept for the
  person) and `NodeRecordFilesTests` (3: the record whole across a write, a file that does not read never empty, the bounds). Process:
  `WorkPerformProcessTests` (a stand-in performer's attempt, sent by a loop that ended, reconciled by the next with its key: one
  delivery, one effect).
- **After review** (FABRIC-PERFORM1h, the same day: six findings, each tested first and seen failing on PERFORM1b). Found sound and
  kept: the unique beside-file and its atomic rename, `HomeWrites.Mine`, a 128-bit name's collision failing closed on the stored unit,
  an evicted replay's invocation refused, and the generations restarting at 1 given one performer for the loop (its empty `_sentHere`
  asks after everything). The fixes, in the choices above: **one locked transaction** (choice 1: `NodeRecordFiles.Edit`, the lease
  asked inside; a second start on a ready node reads the first and is refused); **answers after an await** (choice 8: reloaded,
  applied to their invocation alone, an outcome never taken back); **idempotency alone** (choice 4: redelivered while current, held
  otherwise); **trimming** (choice 9: every attempt a status needs); **a record that does not hold** is unreadable, never empty: each
  container present and of its type, each list's items texts, each state one a rule knows, every status and call on the record's unit,
  each invocation once, each attempt's node holding a status with no attempt numbered past it, and a node with attempts holding its
  current one (a node with none is a built-in's, whose attempts are not kept here; a node whose id moved from a performed kind to a
  built-in one while its attempts were kept would read as unreadable, which stops the unit for a person rather than risk a second
  send); **retry delays** (choice 6: by the try).
  **Proof:** `WorkPerformerTests` (15: two starts on one ready node under a held lock, one dispatched; without the lease no move kept and
  nothing written or sent; a receipt awaited while another node of the unit starts keeping that node's attempt; a receipt after its
  outcome was heard, and a `running` answer awaited while it was, never taking it back; an `idempotent`-only attempt of a stopped node
  held with its gate, its late completion settling it; a retry after a person's new attempt waiting the first delay) and
  `NodeRecordFilesTests` (20: the trimming that keeps a pending retry and an old node's current attempt; sixteen faults of structure
  or agreement each unreadable).
- **For RUN1** (the review's, before dispatch is switched on): keep a run's identity stable across wakes, since WIRE1f lapses a failure
  for a new run and retries even a permanent one there, so a run made afresh at each wake would bypass `retry`'s bound for ever; one
  `WorkPerformer` per loop, its `_sentHere` living as long as the loop; the awaited set's supervision (`HookSet.Watch`), completions
  drained each look (`TakeSteps` into `Hear`), timers scheduled from what the record persists (`retryAt`, `askAt`, deadlines), an
  answer about an attempt no longer current resolved to it (a stale one's), evidence authorization (PERFORM1c), and held and unknown
  attempts recovered by the person; and concurrency regression tests at the runtime's level (a wake and a look at once, a verb beside
  the loop) before dispatch is enabled.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-RUN1, the evaluation runtime in the lease's loop; with WIRE1c's open row).** The design's §E2, §E3, §E7 and
§D6 run where §A3 puts a judge: `Trees/Nodes/NodeRuntime.cs`, one per loop, over PERFORM1b's performer and PERFORM1a's wire. Each pass
drains the completions (`TakeSteps` into `Hear`), asks after every attempt due (`ReconcileAsync`) before any start, makes ready the
retries whose persisted delay passed (`Due`), evaluates each concerned unit's graph to `Settled` (at most a round per node and one
more), keeps its moves (`Keep`), starts what it proposes, and tells the awaited set (`IWorkRuntimePort.Watch`, which `HookSet` now
implements beside `IWorkPerformPort`).

- **Off unless a performed node governs** (the owner's ruling): `NodeRuntime.Armed` reads the choices alone, every landing rule's
  `cycle` and every `workCycles` and `workspaceWorkCycles` entry, for a plugin's performed node (`<plugin>/<step>`, reach `performed`;
  Daoris's own `quest.close` is not a plugin's). With none, a pass reads no unit, no plugin step and no record, takes and tells
  nothing, and writes and starts nothing: the owner's install today, and one whose cycles name only built-ins and people. A pass
  after the last such graph is removed tells the plugins once that nothing is awaited, so their processes stop, and reads nothing.
- **Choices left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **Where it sits: link 8 of the loop's chain** (`ChainRanks.Nodes`, a reader in `ChainTable.Fixed`, emitting nothing), bound by
     `DriverWatch` only where the loop holds a hook set. It hears what the plan hears (`ledger`, every wake the loop takes), the look's
     deadline (`timer(look)`) and `plugin.step`, and runs after the plan, so a wake's plan and its pass are one look. A wake judges only
     what it concerns: the units its answers, reconciliations and retries named, and those whose record holds a dispatchable node ready
     (a start owed); the look's deadline reads the units afresh and judges every governed one. Two wakes the loop makes itself, each a
     plan's `ledger` event on the new topic `nodes` (`ChangeTopics.Nodes`): `due`, at the earliest timer the governed records persist
     (an attempt's `retryAt`, a waiting attempt's `askAt`, a sent attempt's deadline), a moment already past left to the look's deadline
     so nothing spins on what the pass could not act on; and `awaited`, once, when the awaited set told changed, so the next look's
     supervision starts the process the start then goes to. *Limit:* a `nodes` wake runs the local plan too, since the loop's one cause
     is a `ledger` event; a cause of its own is a row (RUN1d).
  2. **A run's identity, and when one begins** (`NodeRuns.Of`): `run-` and 24 hex of the unit and the governing graph's digest. A run
     begins when a graph naming a plugin's performed node first governs the unit, and again only when that graph changes (another
     digest, so every binding of the old one lapses anyway); never at a wake, a look, a restart or a new revision, which lapses the
     bindings it moved (WIRE1e) within the same run. So WIRE1f's lapse of another run's failure is reached only by a person changing the
     graph, never by the runtime, and `retry`'s bound holds across wakes and restarts. Derived, not stored: the record's shape is
     unchanged, and a graph set back to an earlier one returns to its run, whose failures and skips the run between had already lapsed.
     A person's explicit new run has no door yet.
  3. **One performer per loop, one pass at a time:** the runtime holds the loop's one `WorkPerformer`, so its delivered invocations live
     as long as the loop, and a wake that comes while a pass is out waits for it, so a send's `recorded` attempt is never asked after by
     its own loop's next pass. A second loop or a verb holds no lease: its pass reads nothing (`holdsLease`, asked first) and every edit
     asks the lease under the record's lock (PERFORM1h).
  4. **What starts:** a plugin's performed node through `StartAsync`, only once its plugin's process is up (`GenerationOf`); before
     that it is awaited and left ready, never sent blind. An observed built-in through `WorkPerformer.Begin` (new: the start and its
     firings in one locked edit where the record still holds the evaluated status), observed at the next round. A pressed node's task
     and a waited node's wait are left ready for HUMAN1 and WAITUNIT1.
  5. **A graph's plugin node is performed under its plugin's step:** the catalogue's step of the same kind, version and reach gives
     its `perform`, and its retry, timeout and evidence where the graph's node declares none (`NodeRuntime.Govern`). A node whose plugin
     declares no such step, or whose combined declaration has a problem, is evaluated and never sent.
  6. **Revisions and git reads:** each unit's revision is its record's (`WorkUnit.Subject.Revision`, a quest's null), so a branch whose
     records name no tip refuses its evaluation (WIRE1e) and nothing on it starts. The units are read with no roots
     (`WorkUnits.ReadAsync`), so **the runtime starts no git**; `NodeGitReads` stay absent, and `pushed` and an ahead `pull-request` stay
     unknown, never guessed, until the look's git reads reach them (RUN1b). A wake judges with the units the last look read.
  7. **`Fired` is persisted with the start** (WIRE1c's open row, folded in): `StartAsync` and `Begin` write the start's firings in the
     same edit as the start; the runtime reads them back as `WireRun.Fired` each round, and the run's reader is the runtime itself.
  8. **Bounds per pass:** at most 32 units (`UnitsPerPass`, in id order; the rest at the next), a round per node and one more for each,
     and 4 sends across all of them (`SendsPerPass`, PROC1a's four process starts at once), within each run's `WireRun.Limit`.
  9. **Answers about attempts no longer current** are the performer's (PERFORM1b's choice 5): every heard frame goes to `Hear`, which
     binds it to its invocation and settles a stale attempt without moving its node. **Held and unknown attempts are left for the
     person:** the runtime never moves a held attempt, and the performer asks after only recorded, unknown and sent ones (PERFORM1g
     shows them).
- **Until PERFORM1c, what is accepted:** a plugin's `reached` is taken as its node's reach on the plugin's word (its evidence a
  reference Daoris does not check), and moves only that unit's node record and the wires after it. Nothing else reads a node record:
  the end judgement (END2a), the tidy, the landing and the milestone read their own facts, so a plugin node changes none of them, and a
  graph's `end` on a plugin node ends nothing yet. No evidence of a plugin's is believed as proof until PERFORM1c.
- **Not built here** (rows): a bound run's decoded graph governing its unit (WORKFLOW2's cutover; a decoded version names no performed
  node today, since `stage` does not decode, and evaluating one beside D157's engine would be a second engine, §E7); the look's git reads
  (RUN1b); the person's view and doors on held and unknown attempts (PERFORM1g); evidence authorization (PERFORM1c); a plugin's step
  waking the loop at once (today a completion is heard at the next wake or look).
- **Proof** (fast, each seen failing on the members first): `NodeRuntimeTests` (9: an install with no graph naming a performed node,
  and one whose cycle names only a person's task, reading no unit or step, telling and taking nothing, writing no record, across three
  passes; a quest's work cycle governing under its plugin's contract, the run's identity, and no step meaning never sent; a performed
  node started once across a look, a wake and a look, its unit awaited with its checkout; an outcome heard and kept, no longer awaited;
  a declared retry not before its persisted delay and after it over a restart, with a new key; a restart asking after the attempt the
  loop before it sent, with its key, and sending nothing new; a look and a wake at once with a verb beside them, one send; a node whose
  process is not up awaited and sent once it is) and `ChainTests` (link 8). Process: `NodeRuntimeLoopProcessTests` (the watch loop with
  the lease, a hook set and a stand-in performer: a governed quest's step sent once and its completion kept; the same loop with no cycle
  starting no plugin process and writing no unit record).
- **After review** (FABRIC-RUN1e, the same day: a second maker's six findings before anyone arms a graph, each tested first and seen
  failing on RUN1). The fixes, in the choices above:
  1. **Governance at a config wake** (choice 1): the governed units were read again only at the look's deadline or with none cached,
     so a wake after repository A's graph was removed, B keeping the runtime armed, still heard, reconciled, retried and started A under
     its cached graph. The choices a governance was judged under are kept as read (`DriverConfig.Source`), and a wake under others reads
     the units afresh before any attempt is heard, asked after, retried or started. Read afresh, every governed unit is judged, so an
     install armed at a wake starts then, not at the look's deadline.
  2. **Disarming** (the ruling above): a disarmed pass cleared only after a set naming something had been told, so a pending retry's
     timer (`NextDue`, which `DriverWatch` keeps) and the cached units outlived it; and the plan's supervision (`HookSet.ReconcileAsync`,
     link 5) ran before link 8 disarmed, so an old awaited set could start a plugin after the last graph was removed.
     `NodeRuntime.WithdrawAsync` clears everything held whatever was told, telling the plugins nothing is awaited where something was,
     and `DriverWatch` calls it at link 5 before the look.
  3. **A failure across graph edits** (choice 2; WIRE1f's lapse replaced): a run begins at each graph edit, so A → B → A gave three
     runs, each lapsing the last one's failure (`run-scope`), and a graph change lapses every binding (WIRE1e) with its tries counted
     afresh: a Setup save changing a derived graph restarted a permanent failure past `retry`, for ever. **The rule: a failure or a
     rejection of another graph or run is kept for the person.** `WireEvaluation` lapses it on a new revision alone (new work, as in its
     own graph), never on a graph, a version, a run or the upstream that graph lapsed. It is no receipt in the new graph, so nothing fires
     on it, and the person's new attempt (`failed → ready` by the person, PERFORM1g's door) starts it afresh under the graph governing
     now, its tries counted afresh. A transient failure with tries left keeps its count across an edit: `Due` makes it ready and the bound
     counts on. `run-scope` is no longer given; the constant stays, so a history entry written with it still reads.
  4. **Fair continuation** (choice 8): the units past 32 waited behind the same prefix at every pass. Past `UnitsPerPass`, a pass takes
     the 32 after the last unit the pass before reached, round again from the first.
  5. **One count for the pass** (choice 8): reconciliation ran before the send budget, asking after every attempt due and able to
     deliver each again. `WorkSends` is the pass's four: an attempt reconciliation calls about (its question and the redelivery its
     answer brings, together) takes one, a start takes one, and what is left waits for the next pass, still reconciled before any start
     there. An attempt whose plugin's process is not up is not asked, and takes none.
  6. **The lease fences the awaited set** (choice 3): `Tell` published after the pass's awaits without asking the lease again. It asks
     right before `Watch`; a loop that lost it tells nothing and keeps no timer.
  **Proof** (fast, each seen failing on RUN1 with its members added first): `NodeRuntimeTests` (17, eight new: a config wake after a
  unit's graph is removed retrying and sending nothing on it; disarming clearing a pending retry's timer and the units held with nothing
  awaited; the awaited set withdrawn before supervision, choices still armed withdrawing nothing; a failure kept across five graph edits
  back and forth, then started once by the person's new attempt under the graph now; the units past 32 judged at the next pass; a
  restart's four reconciliations and two starts sharing the four sends; a lease lost while a send is out publishing no set; an install
  armed at a wake starting then) and `WireEvaluationTests` (another run's or graph's failure kept for the person until they restart it,
  a rejection likewise with its reach kept, and a graph edit keeping a rejection, each red on RUN1; a new revision still lapsing a
  failure of another graph). The install with no cycle still reads, tells and writes nothing (RUN1's first test, unchanged).
  **Left** (rows): a graph edit still lapses every reach of the old graph (WIRE1e), so a Setup save re-performs reached work once per
  edit; reconciliation takes the attempts due in record order, so attempts whose questions keep failing with an I/O error can spend the
  four at every pass.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-CLOSE1a, the submission, its holds and the close door).** The design's §E5, the service's half. The
driver's half (the lock, the bind's moment, the override and *partially closed*) is CLOSE1b.

- **Two kinds, no new status** (D68's note of the day). `bound` names the closure graph's digest; `submitted` is the agent's
  `ready`, carrying a done's note and answers. Both are a take's own (`QuestLog.TakersOwn`): refused on a lost take inside the
  write and at a remote, and forgotten on a rebase. A lost submission takes the step it published with it, and the take's
  conflict names it.
- **The state machine.** `QuestTransitions` is unchanged, so no existing transition changes meaning. Submitted is a taken quest
  with a current `Quest.Submission`:
  - in: a `ready` on a taken quest a graph binds, from the machine holding the take;
  - replaced: a later `ready` is current, judged again from the start (its yes and evidence verdict are cleared);
  - out: Done by the close door or the person's done, Declined as today, or lost with the take.
- **Holds on the submission.** `Quest.Judged` (done, or taken with a submission) is what `EvidenceWanted`, `AwaitsEvidence`,
  `Hold` and the review's wait now read. The yes, the evidence door and the review verdict apply to a submission unchanged. An
  unheld submission publishes the chain's next step in its own transaction, and so does the release of a held one.
- **Sealed.** A submission whose next step stands takes no later `ready`, which is refused naming the step.
- **The close door** (`POST /api/quests/{id}/close`): one SQLite transaction over every quest named. Each quest is named with
  its submission (machine, sequence). Each is checked in this order: not closed, bound, held by this machine (claim held or
  unconfirmed), the named submission current, nothing holding it, and its next step published. Only then is Done appended to
  each, in the order named, with the submission's note and answers and `close: { key, token }`. Any failed check writes
  nothing and names the check and the quest. The same key again answers the first outcome and writes nothing. Another key on
  closed quests is refused.
- **Doors.** `/bind` and `/close` are `Driver` doors, mapped by a local host alone: the graph and the lock are the machine's,
  and the Done travels as an operation. `ready` goes through respond, an `Agent` door, and the connector's `quest_respond`. Its
  refusals and the agent's refused done answer 400, not 409, because 409 at respond means *stand down*.
- **What old readers see.** A submitted quest reads Taken. `closure` and `submission` are absent for every quest from before,
  since nulls are left out. An older build reads a done's `close` as a plain done.
- **Choices §E5 left open.**
  1. *How the service learns the binding*: an operation the driver posts (`/bind`), once, on a taken quest before its first
     submission. A second bind of the same graph is no move, and another graph is refused. Rejected: a field on the take (the
     session takes, and does not know the graph), and a repository setting in the service (the graph lives in the driver's
     homes, GRAPH1). Between the take and the bind, an agent's `done` still closes the quest as today. CLOSE1b binds at the
     take, and the session's prompt says `ready`.
  2. *The commit*: a submission carries no commit, as a done carries none. The evidence verdict names the commit it read, and
     the driver checks the revision under its lock (step 1).
  3. *Where the seal is judged*: in the store of the machine holding the take, since only it submits. The replay keeps no seal.
     The successor's link to its submission is its `parent`, which is enough while no reopen exists.
  4. *The fence's names*: the submission's machine and sequence per quest, with the key and token. The key is kept on the Done
     as its provenance, and the token beside it.
- **Held by.** Each was seen red first. `QuestClosureTests` covers submit, the hold, the release and seal, a replaced
  submission, the close, a stale close, a held quest refusing the whole chain, the same key twice, another machine's take, a lost
  take and an old quest. `QuestOperationKindsTests` gives both kinds and the done's `close` their census samples, with two new
  states. `LocalHostTests`, `SharedHostTests` and `PersonDoorTableTests` cover the doors.
- **Left:** the person's reopen of a sealed submission (FABRIC-CLOSE1c); the page's *Submitted* and the driver's client reading
  the two fields (FABRIC-CLOSE1d); CLOSE1b as cut.
- **After review (FABRIC-CLOSE1e, 2026-10-17).** A second maker found CLOSE1a safe to stage while no quest is bound, since an
  unbound quest's path is unchanged, and four fences missing before any is. Each is now held by a test seen red on CLOSE1a first.
  1. *A verdict is read for one submission.* `Covers` matched requirements and names, so a verdict read for a submission, arriving
     after a later `ready` replaced it, released the replacement and could publish and seal its step. A verdict now names the
     submission it was read for (`submission: { machine, sequence }`, in the log, on the wire, in the column and at the evidence
     door, written only when set). The replay applies it only to that submission (`QuestEvidenceVerdict.AskedFor`), and a done's
     verdict names none. The door refuses another with 409, naming the current submission. The driver's verdict must name it from
     CLOSE1b on.
  2. *A lost submission's steps go with it, taken or not.* The rebase skipped a step with more than its publication, so a step
     this machine had taken stayed, synced on its own, and its session ran on. `QuestStore.ForgetStepsAsync` forgets every step
     of the submission, and their steps, while every operation of one is this machine's and unpushed. A take of one becomes a
     conflict naming the parent's loss (`StepWentWith`), so the claim reads lost and the driver stops that session (D69). With
     its publication gone, the conflict is on a quest no list shows and no push carries, as after a delete (D95). If the
     winner's chain later publishes the same step (the id comes from the parent and the step), the conflict stands on it and goes
     up, like any lost take. A step a remote already holds stays (see *Left*).
  3. *A key is one call.* A replay found the key on any named quest's Done and answered success, so the key with another quest
     list succeeded without closing the rest. The store now keeps each close under its key (`quest_closes`: key, token, the
     submissions named in quest order, outcome, the quest refused on). Only the same key, token and submissions is that close
     again. Anything else under the key is `KeyReused` (409), and nothing is written.
  4. *A refusal is an outcome.* A refused close is kept under its key beside the closes, in the same transaction, and never as
     an operation, so it never syncs. The same close sent again answers that refusal, worded as it was then, even after its hold
     is released. A new attempt takes a new key. A malformed call keeps no key.
  - **Compatibility.** An older remote refuses a push holding `bound` or `submitted`, and an older machine refuses a fetch page
    holding one. An older build also reads a verdict's `submission` as absent. **CLOSE1b binds only once every machine of the
    circle and its remote run this build.**
  - **Left:** a step a remote numbered before the parent's take was found lost (a pass that pushed the step while the parent
    was behind) is not forgotten, since no machine rewrites a numbered operation. Its take stands until a person declines it.
    A close kept as a success whose Done a later rebase lost answers *made already* with the quests as they now stand. CLOSE1b's
    driver reads them and shows the partial close.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-END6, a fetch and a plugin's ask when due).** The design's §A4.3 and link 6 of §C6, by the owner's yes
(part C's acceptance, point 3). `Trees/PullRequestMoments.cs` binds link 6 on a chain of its own, with two topics, `refs` and
`focus`, and no root. Its deadlines wake no link, so the moments add no clock: D148's occasions stay the reconciliation, and the
look's pace stays every other topic's deadline. The asks go through `PullRequestAsks.AskAtMomentAsync`, a fifth occasion beside
D148 point 2's four.

- **The moments.**
  - *Origin's line moved.* The ref watch tells the moments each repository it judges (`PullRequestMoments.Moved`, one line in
    `RefWatch.JudgeAsync`; never at a deadline). Origin's line is read from git's files with no process (`TreeTips.Named`) and
    compared with the commit last seen in this process. The first reading is a baseline, so a start is no moment. The person's
    fetch brought the remote's facts, so nothing is fetched, and the fetch is counted.
  - *Daoris pushed.* `TreeLands.LandAsync` tells it after the landing and the rule's tidy, so LAND3's own ask is on the record
    first and counts. The hand-off (`TreeHandOffs.HandAsync`) tells it after its push is recorded.
  - *The window's focus.* The main window's `Activated` reaches `DriverLoop.Focused`, then `DriverWatch.Focused`, then the
    moments. It is a hint and returns at once. Every repository with an open pull request is a moment. A later main window
    (MAINWINDOWS2) says no focus yet.
- **What a moment concerns.** A standing landing a plugin pushed, whose kept answer is neither completed nor abandoned, and whose
  end is not held (`PullRequestMoments.Concerns`). A wake that concerns none reads no line and no registry, fetches nothing and
  asks nothing. The asks need no "could remove something now", since the answer now feeds the end (§B) as well as a removal. The
  lease's holder alone asks, as it alone keeps ends and lands.
- **The bounds.**
  - *Per pull request:* at most one ask a minute. D148's minute counts over the record's last ask (`PullRequestAsking.Due`) and
    over this process's own asks, so a plugin that was not ready, which keeps nothing, is not started again inside the minute.
  - *Per repository:* at most one fetch of the line a minute. `RemoteFetches` keeps when each line was last fetched or heard
    fetched. `TreeRefs.FetchAsync` stamps every fetch, made or failed: the landing press's, bringing up to date's and the
    moments' own. Origin's line heard moving stamps the person's.
  - *No double fetch:* a moment fetches only where nothing did within the minute, so a push after its press's fetch fetches
    nothing. The moments' own fetch is read as the new baseline, so its echo through the ref watch is no person's fetch.
  - *One at a time:* the moments run serially, so a second moment reads the first one's ask on the record.
- **Never at a removing press.** A `press` is no moment: link 6's row hears it, and the handler asks nothing on it. A removing
  press's own ref moves (a branch deleted) leave origin's line where it was.
- **An end with no press.** A moment fetches before it asks, so a completed answer's merge commit is here for git to confirm.
  Where an answer comes back completed, the repository is judged again at once through `RefMoves.Mine`, as Daoris's own move
  is. Link 1 then holds the end by `pull-request` (§B3).
- **What the person sees.** Nothing new on the window. The machine log has link 6's `chain.fired` (topic `refs` or `focus`, the
  count asked), the plugin's own lines, and a `failed` line where a moment failed. The watch under the chain writes nothing, so a
  focus or a ref move that asks nothing is no line.
- **Proof.**
  - `PullRequestMomentsTests` (fast, a counting world): each moment asks once, origin's line moving fetching nothing; a second
    moment inside the minute asks and fetches nothing; no fetch where the push's press fetched; the focus reads the
    registry once; a wake with no open pull request reads nothing; a press asks nothing; a completed answer judges again.
  - `PullRequestOccasionTests` holds the fifth occasion and that its door is the moments alone.
  - `PullRequestMomentsGitTests` (Git, run once alone in this branch, passing) is the whole chain over real git. A plugin
    landing's push is asked once. A squash on the platform, with the line moving on, is not held by git alone. The person's
    fetch, heard, asks. The completed answer's re-judgement holds the end by `pull-request` with no press.
- **Left:**
  - A line that moves inside a pull request's minute is not asked about until its next moment.
  - The first moment after a start is a baseline, so a fetch made while Daoris was closed waits for the focus or a later fetch.
  - A later main window's focus is not heard.
  - The moments' ledger and baselines are this process's alone: a terminal's `trees sync` fetch is heard as origin moving, not
    counted as a fetch.
- **After review (FABRIC-END6c, 2026-10-17).** A second review found the note sound for a branch-only setup, and five faults that
  matter once pushed landings are pending, since removing a plugin does not stop their asks. Each is fixed, and each has a test that
  failed first.
  1. *Fetches and holds.* A landing's fetch in flight was invisible, so a moment could start a second one beside it. Every fetch of a
     line now goes through `RemoteFetches.OnceAsync`, and a second fetch joins the one in flight. A repository is held, in this
     process, by the replay's lock (`TreeLock.TryReplaying`, so a removing press and bringing up to date) and by
     `TreeLands.LandAsync` (`RepositoryHolds`). A moment **skips** a held repository, fetching and asking nothing, and is owed: it
     asks once the hold is let go. It does not wait, because the moments run one at a time, and a landing's plugin push could hold
     every other repository's moment behind it. A press that starts while a moment runs never waits for it.
  2. *Order and the shared minute.* A slow `open` could return after a concurrent `completed` and overwrite it.
     `LandedBranches.Answered` now keeps an answer only where it was asked no earlier than the kept one, judged under the record's
     lock, and never replaces a kept `completed`, which is final by D148 point 6. A kept `abandoned` is not final there, since a
     platform may reopen the pull request, so only the order protects it. A failure older than the kept answer, or than the kept
     failure, is not kept. D148's minute is one per pull request in this process (`PullRequestMinute`). The looks and the tidy take
     it in `DueAsksAsync`, and a moment takes it just before it asks. *Ask again* asks whatever the minute says, and its ask still
     counts. A look gives back a minute it sent nothing for (a plugin not ready, or its bound first), so D148's "the next look asks
     again" holds. A moment's minute stays, as END6 meant.
  3. *Each request's own time.* `StateAnswer.AskedAt` is the time that entry's own request went out (`LandingPlugins.AskOneAsync`).
     The record and the minute are stamped with it, not with the occasion's start.
  4. *The person's fetch from git's files.* `RemoteFetches.InGit` reads two files from the main checkout's git dir, with no process.
     The first is `FETCH_HEAD`'s time, where it names the line: every fetch writes it, including one that changed nothing, while
     Daoris's own fetch writes none and an empty one is a fetch that failed. The second is the time of the newest entry in
     `origin/<line>`'s log. The moments count the later of these and their own ledger, so a fetch made before a restart counts too.
     A fetch made in a linked tree writes that tree's own `FETCH_HEAD`, and is still read only where it moved the line.
  5. *An early focus.* `PendingFocus` keeps one pending flag, not a queue. The loop (`DriverLoop.Focused`) and the watch
     (`DriverWatch.Focused`) each keep a focus that comes before their listener exists, and tell it once the listener does.
  - **Proof.** The fast tests are `PullRequestBoundsTests` (the join, a joined fetch whose caller cancelled it, the replay's hold, the
    shared minute, the record's order, each request's time, git's files, the pending focus); `PullRequestMomentsTests` (a held
    repository skipped, then asked once; another occasion's minute); and `DriverLoopFocusTests` in the modules. Over real git:
    `PullRequestStateTests` (two looks at once ask once; each pull request kept with its own request's time) and
    `PullRequestMomentsGitTests` (a fetch that changed nothing, read from `FETCH_HEAD`).
  - **Left.** A landing's hold is not shown by its own test, since a landing over real git is the moments' Git class's to drive. The
    hold and its release are held by the replay's lock and by the skipped moment.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (CLEANUP1b, a landing whose deleted branch's tip the line holds, after CLEANUP1).** The design's §B5. A pull request
merged with a merge commit and pulled, its landing branch then deleted by the person before a look judged it, read *moved to `main`*
under CLEANUP1 (`LandedGone.Moved`: the line holds its recorded tip by ancestry, and the line is a local branch), and stayed not ended
for ever.

- **The line's ancestry of the recorded tip is the end, proved by the end judgement, never by the holders' list.** A landing git says
