  is missing is the judgement's first: `EndKeeping.KeepGoneAsync` judges it through `EndJudgement` at the tip its landing recorded
  (`EndSubject.Gone`, additive), where only the line's ancestry ends it (`EndBy.Merged`, on the form of the line that holds it). Content
  and a pull request's word are read of a branch that stands, so they end nothing here; a branch of its name standing again holding that
  commit is unknown here, judged as one that stands. Where it holds, the fact is written as the entry retires
  (`LandedBranches.HoldGone`: `Held`, `GoneAs` `missing`, no `MovedTo`), on that landing alone, by the lease's holder only, under the
  keeper's own one-at-a-time per repository. The inventory then reads it *ended* from the fact, the landing *removed* with its proof,
  and the unit's clean-up *completed*. No recovery ref is made: the line holds its commits.
- **Who asks it.** The look's `MarkAbsentAsync` (now given the line it reads, read before it) and the clean-up's press, each before
  CLEANUP1's `WentAsync`. Only where the keeper wrote nothing does `WentAsync` read where the work went, as before, so a rename to a
  branch of the person's still reads *moved*, and a tip only a session branch holds still reads moved there (a session or landing branch
  never counts as the line). A trace with no fact that an earlier look read as moved to the line is asked again whatever the line did
  since; one with a fact is not.
- **Amending FABRIC-END2's "a trace is never judged again"** for one case: a trace with no fact, retired outside Daoris (from before
  CLEANUP1's field, or read moved), is judged once at the tip its landing recorded. A trace with its fact is never judged again.
- **The cost:** one `merge-base --is-ancestor` for each form of the line, once for each landing read missing, before CLEANUP1's
  `for-each-ref --contains`; nothing for a landing whose branch stands.
- **Held by**, each red on CLEANUP1's branch first: `EndJudgementTests` (a gone landing ends by ancestry alone), `HeldFactsTests` (the
  write on that landing alone; canary 9 counts `HoldGone` as the records' `Hold`), and `CleanupGitTests` (Git: the line merged it and
  the person deleted it, read ended with its clean-up completed by the look and by the press; a trace from before and one read moved
  to the line, each ended). The rename (`Held` stays null) and a tip only another session branch holds are guards, green before and
  after.
- **Not built here:** a squashed landing whose deleted branch's recorded tip reads on the line by content still reads dropped, with its
  commits kept on a recovery ref (CLEANUP1); its end by content is a row of its own.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (KNOWWALK1, no watch or walk enters what git ignores).** Amends the FABRIC-EVENT6 note. The watch of
`.claude/`, `docs/` and the named folders is now opened around what git ignores (`CheckoutFreshness.OpenTree`, with git's
answer read once per process by `IgnoredPaths`). A folder with an ignored folder in it is watched alone and hears none of its
ignored entries; each of its other folders is watched the same way. So `.claude/worktrees/`, every agent's checkout, wakes
nothing. A folder made later inside a folder watched alone is heard being made and is watched from the next start, with the
deadline covering it until then. Where git cannot answer, the watch is the one recursive watch it was. The rehearsal's hold was
not this watch but the connector's host, whose default configuration watched the whole checkout and walked each folder renamed
in it. `KnowledgeServer.HostBuilder` now watches nothing. Root cause, fix, cost and proof: the fix log's KNOWWALK1 entry.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (KEEPALL1, every home file a door edits keeps what its writer does not own; FIX-LOG 2026-10-17).** GRAPH1's
review made `driver.json`'s writers keep what they do not own. The other files in the home that a door edits were audited for the
same fault, a writer that rebuilds its file from the fields it knows.

- **The keeping write, shared** (`JsonKeeping.Save`, `jsonkeep.ts`' `writeKeeping`): a writer hands the file as its reader understood
  it and the edited choices, both written by itself, and whether a file was there at the read. The edit, the key paths that differ, is
  made onto the file read again then under `Kept`'s rules. A file that does not read, holds a key twice or made a changed object a
  scalar is refused and left as it is; CRLF is kept; no edit writes nothing; a file there at neither the read nor the write is written
  whole in the writer's layout, since nothing can be lost (`driver.json` writes the edit's keys alone there, and keeps doing so).
- **Changed, in both twins:** `plugins.json` (`PluginState`, `writePluginState`: rebuilt as `{ "disabled": [...] }` alone);
  `permissions.json` (`PermissionRules.Save`, `writePermissions`: the unknown top-level keys moved first, a scope's own unknown keys
  dropped); `keys.json` (`HarnessKeys`, `writeKeys`: rebuilt from the keys read, and the driver read a file that did not read as no
  keys, then wrote over it, every other account's key lost; it now writes the key before the account's directory, as the CLI does).
  Rules or keys built in memory are written whole, as before.
- **Already kept, not changed:** `tools.json`, `resources.json`'s locations among it (`Tools.SetWay`, `AddLocation`,
  `RemoveLocation`: a node read at the write and edited in place; the CLI spreads each level), `accounts.json`, `cooling.json` and
  `reads.json` (a node read at the write, edited in place, the CLI likewise; `reads.json` replaces an entry whole by its own contract),
  and `browser/settings.json` (the same). Not kept there: CRLF, a key written twice, and in the CLI a number JavaScript cannot hold,
  since it parses through `JSON.parse` (KEEPALL1c).
- **Compared, not changed:** `harnesses.json`. `HarnessSettings.Kept`, as the CLI's `rest`, keeps the unknown top-level keys, but
  moves them first, and rebuilds every section from what it read: a default that is not text, a workspace that is not an object, the
  retired `prefer` and `parallel` and a workspace written twice in any case go or collapse, and the old `early` and `near` are written
  as `switch` and `curve`. Those normalisations are the twins' contract (`RotationTwinTests`' file rows, `rotation-use.json`'s `write`
  rows, D165 §6), which the keeping write would reverse, since a save with no edit would write nothing and an entry no edit touched
  would keep its old names. That is a decision of its own (KEEPALL1b). Nor does either writer read the file again at write.
- **Written by Daoris alone, no person's key possible:** `home.json` (minted once as a new file, never changed in place),
  `registry-followed.json` (what following a registration came to, rebuilt by the driver under its lock from its own entries; no door
  and no terminal verb writes it, and the next follow writes it again), and `windows.json`, `usage.json`, `update.json`,
  `abandoned.json` and the landing records, which record what the driver observed. Still a door's and still rebuilding:
  `plugin-approvals.json` (`HelpGrants`, KEEPALL1d) and `remotes.json` (three twins, not read here, KEEPALL1e).
- **Proof** (fast): `home-files-keep.json`, one table both twins read (`HomeFilesKeepTests`, `homefileskeep.test.ts`; its row in
  `tools/merge-branch.mjs`): each file in its writer's layout with unknown keys at each level, 16 rows (no edit, each door's edit,
  CRLF, another writer's change between the read and the write, a scope made a string, a key twice, a key over a torn file).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (CLEANUP1c, a squashed landing whose deleted branch's recorded commit reads on the line by content, after
CLEANUP1b).** The design's §B5 and D102. A pull request the platform squash-merged and the person pulled, its landing branch then
deleted by the person, read *dropped* under CLEANUP1b: the squash is a commit of its own, so the line's ancestry holds no commit of the
landing. Its commits were kept on a recovery ref held by nothing, so kept for ever and listed for the person. Most of the 18 landings
the owner's install lists as deleted after their pull requests were squash-merged.

- **The content proof at the recorded commit ends a gone landing too.** Amends CLEANUP1b's "content ends nothing here". Where the
  line's ancestry does not hold the commit the landing recorded, `EndJudgement.GatherAsync` reads that commit's content proof
  (`LandedProofs.ProveAsync`, the proof a standing landing's squash end already reads). `JudgeGone` then ends it `on-line`
  (`EndBy.OnLine`) only where every file it changed reads as it left them on a form of the line (`main`, `origin/main`).
  - Still never an end: content only a branch of the person's holds (D88), and a pull request's word, read of a branch that stands.
  - Nor a proof git could not read (`Unknown`, PROOF-UNREAD1), so a recorded commit git's collection took reads as CLEANUP1 says:
    missing, with nothing to keep.
- **The writer is CLEANUP1b's.** `EndKeeping.KeepGoneAsync` keeps the fact as the entry retires (`LandedBranches.HoldGone`), by the
  lease's holder only, under the keeper's one-at-a-time per repository. The inventory reads it ended, its clean-up completed.
- **No recovery ref is made**: the line holds the work by content. Daoris's own removal of an `on-line` branch keeps its commits on a
  recovery ref first (SQUASHTIDY1e), because there Daoris deletes the only ref to the branch's own history. Here the person deleted
  it, and a recovery ref nothing holds would be kept for ever, more for the person to clean.
- **A file that still differs** (the line changed it again after the squash) leaves the proof unheld, and CLEANUP1's reading stands:
  dropped, its commit kept on a recovery ref first.
- **The cost:** once for each landing read missing whose recorded commit the line's ancestry does not hold, the content proof's reads
  (`rev-list --count`, then a merge base and two `diff --name-only` for each form of the line), before CLEANUP1's
  `for-each-ref --contains`.
- **Held by**, red on CLEANUP1b's branch first: `EndJudgementTests` (a gone landing ends by content on a form of the line, never on a
  branch of the person's) and `CleanupGitTests` (Git: squash-merged on the platform, pulled and deleted, read ended by content with its
  clean-up completed and no recovery ref). Guards, green before and after: a file that still differs reads dropped and kept, and a
  commit git collected reads missing with nothing to keep.
- **Not built here:** a trace already written dropped (`missing`, no fact) is never judged again. Where CLEANUP1 runs on an install
  before this does, its squashed landings keep CLEANUP1's reading and their recovery refs.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-END2d, the keeper learns the squash-holder and inside proofs; END4a's fallback removed).** The design's §B3,
§B5 and §C3. FABRIC-END4a's "After review" kept the press's full judgement in the look's tidy for each recorded branch with no kept fact,
once per tip and line commit (`AutoTidies.Judged`), because the keeper made no `SquashedHolderAsync` or `Inside` proof.

- **What the fallback covered**, compared proof by proof with the press's (`TreeSweeps.JudgeAsync`, `LandedCleanups.JudgeLandedAsync`). The
  keeper already proved the line's ancestry, an empty branch, content on a form of the line, a carried branch and a pull request's
  confirmed merge as the press does. Three cases were the press's alone:
  1. *A landing inside another* (`Inside`): its files differ on the line, and a landing whose own proof ended it holds its commits.
  2. *A session branch whose work only such a landing holds* (`LandingHoldingAsync`): with no unit (an install from before END2), or with
     its unit's landing itself only inside another. Its own commits are not on the line and its files differ there.
  3. *An older squash record with no unit* (`SquashedHoldAsync`, then the line's ancestry of the landing's branch or the content). This
     one was already the keeper's: the squash commit Daoris makes has the session tip's tree, so where the line holds that branch by
     ancestry, the content proof finds that tree in the line's history and ends the session `on-line`. Where the platform squashed the
     landing, the press asks the same content proof the keeper does. So the review's second case had no gap.
- **The keeper now proves the first two** (`EndKeeping.KeepAloneAsync`). It judges every landing before it writes any. A landing
  whose own proofs did not end it, whose files were compared (`EndFacts.Judgeable`, the clean-up's `Judgeable`), is ended `inside`
  (`EndBy.Inside`). That needs a landing ended by its own proof (`EndJudgement.OwnEnd`: merged, on the line, its pull request) that
  holds its tip by ancestry (`WithinAsync`). Its fact rests on that branch at the commit read, as the press's `Inside` rests.
  A session branch that its own proofs and its unit's landing do not end is ended `landed` through the newest such landing
  whose record holds its tip (`HolderAsync`): the squash's recorded session tip, else the tip it made, as `InsideAsync` reads it. The
  newest-first order is `SquashedHoldAsync`'s. A session branch's landing end is read from the pass, never judged again for it. The
  inventory and the work's milestone say the new code (`WorkInventory.ProofSaid`, `work.milestone.by.inside` in English and Chinese).
- **Two lapses added on standing branches** (amending FABRIC-END2's list): an `inside` fact holds while the landing it is inside still
  ends by its own proof in the same pass, and a `landed` fact while its landing's end holds in that pass. A fact resting on another
  fact lapses with it, and its branch is judged again.
- **The fallback is removed** (`AutoTidies`): the candidates are the kept facts alone. A branch the press keeps (busy, dirty, a landing
  checked out) whose fact holds is a candidate at every pace, so what kept it clearing needs no deadline. Safety is END4a's, unchanged:
  every branch still goes only through the press's path, judged again inside the hold and right before it goes. Each proof returns
  what it rested on, and the delete's transaction verifies exactly those. Only git's "missing" reads as gone, and only the person's
  non-symbolic refs hold a recovery ref.
- **The git starts** (`TidyEffectGitTests`, this machine), beyond the keeper's own, first pass then second: 16 then 5 with one branch the
  line holds none of, and 43 then 8 with four, before. Now 5 then 5, and 8 then 8. The keeper adds one `rev-list` for each session
  branch nothing ended, only where a landing of the repository is ended by its own proof. It saves the landing's judgement it made
  again for each session branch that landing carries.
- **Held by**, red on END4a's code first: `EndJudgementTests` (fast: a landing inside one ended by its own proof ends inside it; its
  refusals are guards: not inside one ended only by being inside, nor one not ended, nor uncompared, nor gone). `HeldFactsGitTests` (Git: a landing inside another
  is kept `inside`, with and without units, and the session branch it carries `landed`). `TidyEffectGitTests` (Git: the count's first pass
  equals its second; a session branch with no unit that only a landing inside another holds, kept for a dirty tree, goes at the next pace
  once the tree is clean, which the fallback's memory kept until the deadline). Guards, green before and after: an older squash record's
  session branch is kept ended and goes at the look's tidy, and a landing inside another goes with it there.
- **Left:** a squashing chain's earlier part whose landing ended by content (the platform squashed it) and whose own files differ is
  ended `landed` by the keeper, a unit's or not. The press's squash path keeps it (it asks the landing's ancestry, or the session's own
  content), so it stays a candidate the press judges at every pace. That was so for a unit's branch before this row.

**Note, 2026-10-17 (RECOVERYVIEW1 and RECOVERYASK1, the recoveries no unit names, shown, after CLEANUP1).** The design's §D0 and §D3.
CLEANUP1 moved a folder set aside and a recovery ref no record names out of the units into `Recoveries`, which only `work state`
printed. The owner asked Daoris to show plainly what is left, so the page and Ask Daoris say them too.

- **Each recovery says whose it is** (`InventoryEntry.Repository`, additive; `WORK_STATE`'s entries carry it as `repository`, so a shell
  without it answers none). A recovery has no workspace of its own, and the page scopes by its repositories' names.
- **The page** (`WorkspaceRecoveries`, under *Cleaned up on its own* on the workspace's Branches, the nearest place: it is the section
  that says what Daoris did with branches and trees): a folder set aside by its name, never its path, *kept until* its date; a recovery
  ref a branch or a tag of yours holds, from when Daoris removes it; and a ref nothing holds, with `git update-ref -d <ref>` in a copyable
  line, since only the person deletes it. A clause the page has no words for (a ref whose holders were not read; a folder whose purge is
  kept) is the driver's, as recorded. Nothing is drawn where there are none, and `readWorkState` reads a shell older than the field
  as none. The delete command is rebuilt from the ref (D102's own sentence, no new verb); an unheld ref is told from the driver's clause
  (no `until`, and the clause names the command), since the entry has no code of its own.
- **Ask Daoris's *Where work has got to*** lists them after the units (`HelpRoomWork`), at most five, those only the person can delete
  first, the rest counted, each in `work state`'s own words (what keeps it and, for an unheld ref, its `git update-ref -d` line). Said
  even where no unit is recorded; not said where the milestones were not read. This closes CLEANUP1's *Not built here*.
- **Held by**, red first: `HelpRoomWorkTests` (the unheld ref's line first, five and the rest counted, with no unit, none where none or
  unread), `WorkspaceRecoveries.test.tsx` (both languages, the folder, the held and unheld ref, the copy, the workspace's own
  repositories, none, an older shell's answer), `CleanupLeftoversTests` (the repository on each entry).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-UNKNOWN1, an unrecorded daoris branch).** §D3's unknown owner for a branch. `RecoveryRetention.ReadWithBranchesAsync`
projects the `daoris/*` branches out of CLEANUP1's one `for-each-ref` (no further git start; a symbolic ref is left out), and
`WorkInventory.AttachUnrecorded` makes a unit of each that no `session-branches` entry (its branch or `Now`), no landing and no tree names.
Its end is unknown and its one entry is blocked, saying Daoris did not record it and that only the Discard branch press removes it. It is
never pending, so link 2 never tries it; it is never a recovery ref's holder (`Holds` leaves `daoris/` out); and it is never retired
(an unknown owner stays open). The look leaves it standing (`TidyEffectGitTests`). A repository no record and no tree names is not read.
The page's row adds the entry's reason after the owner sentence.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-END2d after its merge, the carried proof's fact).** The merge gate's Git half failed
`PullRequestStateTests.The_look_takes_a_session_branch_a_kept_answer_carried`: the look no longer removed a session branch a kept pull
request answer carried. FABRIC-END2d's note said the keeper already proved a carried branch as the press does. It proved it, but kept no
fact once the carrier's branch was gone, which is the usual case: the platform deletes it after its merge and the person deletes their
copy. The verdict named that branch as where the end held. `EndKeeping.Next` needs that place's commit (`OnAt`), which no look can read
of a deleted branch, so it wrote nothing, and only END4a's fallback took such a branch.

- **The fact rests on what the carried proof rests on.** `EndFacts.CarrierOn` is the form of the line the carrier's merge commit was found
  on, read from `PullRequestCarrier.Rests` (what the carried tidy's delete verifies). A carried end names that form as where it held
  (`HeldFact.On`, its commit as `OnAt`). It lapses as any end on the line does, and the evidence still names the carrier. Through a
  cleared tip the proof also rests on the carrier's branch at that tip; the press's re-judgement verifies it at the delete, as before.
- **Read as kept:** a carried fact written before names the carrier's branch and reads as it did. The inventory's sentence for `carried`
  names neither place ("a completed pull request carried it and its merge commit is on the line"), as the page's already says.
- **Held by**, red first: `EndJudgementTests` (a carried end rests on the form named), `PullRequestStateTests` (Git: the keeper keeps a
  carried session branch's end on `origin/main` once the carrier's branch is deleted; the look takes it again).

**Note, 2026-10-17 (FABRIC-PERFORM1c, evidence authorization).** The design's §C4 belief rules, §D4's binding, §D6's outputs and §E2's
authority by kind, on PERFORM1b's performer and RUN1's runtime. This replaces RUN1's *Until PERFORM1c*: a plugin's answer moves its node
only once it is believed, and nothing downstream reads an output that is not a current acceptance's. Still off on an install whose choices
name no plugin's performed node (RUN1's first test, unchanged).

- **The judge** (`Trees/Nodes/WorkBelief.cs`, pure). `Completion` judges each completion on the attempt its invocation names, before the one
  table sees it: (1) its sender is the attempt's plugin's current process (`not-sender`); (2) it names the attempt's step, bare or
  `<plugin>/<id>`, and the node is still that plugin's to reach (`not-named`), declared by its plugin's step under the cycle governing the
  unit (`not-declared`: the runtime hands `Hear` its dispatchable nodes alone); (3) the unit is one Daoris tells about, and a repository or
  branch the frame names is that unit's (`not-watched`); (4) a commit the frame names is the attempt's revision (`stale`); (5) its `at` is
  no more than a minute ahead (`shape`), and one attempt keeps one outcome: the same again is a replay, another is `late`. A frame not
  believed moves nothing and is counted as a `plugin.step` line of plugin, code and count, with the transport's refusals
  (`IWorkPerformPort.Refused`, `HookSet.Refused`, the plugin's `RefusalTally`), never its words.
- **Evidence by kind** (`NodeAuthority.OnPluginWord`): only a plugin's own node is reached on the plugin's word, kept as its word (D143). Its
  evidence is the plugin's reference at the schema the reference names (`WorkBelief.Evidence`), so a node declaring an evidence schema is
  reached only with a reference of it (`no-evidence`: the attempt is held with its gate, PERFORM1b's choice 7). PERFORM1b stamped the
  declared schema on whatever reference came, and filled a missing one with the invocation, so `no-evidence` could never fire.
- **Outputs from a current acceptance** (`WorkBelief.Unresolved`): at dispatch, under the record's lock, each input of the start's binding
  must be exposed by its producer's current acceptance (the producer reached, on the consumer's revision, its acceptance the one the port
  names, exposing that port exactly); else the start is refused (`stale-input`) and nothing is written or sent.
- **Choices §C4 and §D4 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **A completion's unit is its invocation's.** §C4's third rule looks a frame up in the last `work/watch` frame; a completion's
     `work/perform` call already told the plugin its unit, and a completion may come after the unit left the awaited set. So it is judged
     against the unit as the runtime tells it now (`GovernedUnit.Watched`), and a repository, branch or commit the frame adds must agree.
  2. **Two kinds of stale.** A frame naming another commit than its attempt's is not taken, and the attempt waits. An attempt whose work
     moved past its revision before its completion was heard has a real outcome on an old revision: the performer invalidates the node
     (`lapsed`, as the evaluation would at its next round) and settles the attempt as a stale one's (§D3), lifting the gate, so the node
     starts again on the new revision at once rather than at the deadline's reconciliation. An unknown outcome leaves the attempt unknown.
  3. **A future `at` is `shape`** (no frame of now says so) and `late` is the second outcome of one attempt; an event's `late` by step and
     commit is the observed nodes' (below).
  4. **What needs Daoris's own reading stays Daoris's.** `pushed` (git's remote-tracking ref beside the plugin's answer) and `pull-request`
     (the plugin's answer to Daoris's own `work/state` ask, D148 point 4's checks, kept on the landing) are observed from their records
     (`BuiltInNodes`, RUN1b's git reads); a frame naming either is never their outcome. Binding to the revision ties the plugin's word to
     the work; it does not prove a deployment happened (§C4).
  5. **A node declaring no evidence schema** takes the answer itself, its invocation, as its evidence, the plugin's word: an unprefixed
     reference reads at the node's kind, as before.
- **Not built here** (a row): an observed plugin node reached by its believed event (a `work/step` naming no invocation, judged against
  the last `work/watch` frame and the unit's tip, `late` by step and commit), `pull-request`'s event form, and D157's WORKFLOW1j and 1k
  allocated to this row by §E10 (an awaited pull request asked on back-off, checks as observed nodes). Today such an event reaches nothing,
  and an observed plugin node is left ready, never awaited.
- **Proof** (fast, each seen failing on its members first): `WorkBeliefTests` (8: a believed completion; each of the five rules; only a
  plugin's own node on its word; an input read only from its producer's current acceptance), `WorkPerformerTests` (5 new: evidence of
  another schema and none held, the declared one reached; four frames not believed and an unknown invocation moving nothing and counted;
  a completion after the work moved settling its attempt and invalidating its node; a late outcome; a start whose input lapsed between
  evaluation and dispatch refused with nothing sent), `NodeRuntimeTests` (2 new: a build reached on unauthorised evidence starting no
  deploy, then its receipt reaching it and the deploy sent its artifact; an artifact accepted on r1 never read once the work is at r2,
  the deploy sent r2's) and `WorkSupervisionTests` (a belief refusal a `plugin.step` line once a minute, written after its member).
  Process, once by class: `NodeRuntimeLoopProcessTests` and `WorkPerformProcessTests`, unchanged and green.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-PERFORM1g, a held attempt shown, and the person's way on).** The design's §D6 (*unknown stops the step, and the
person sees it*), §E2 and §D3's *blocked, needs the person*, on PERFORM1b's held attempts, which until now kept their gate with no move of
the person's to lift it. Still nothing on an install whose choices name no plugin's performed node: it keeps no node record, so it lists
none (RUN1's first test, unchanged).

- **Shown** (`Trees/Nodes/HeldAttempts.cs`, read-only): every attempt `held` on the units' node records, with its node, attempt, step,
  the node's state and why (`unknown`, `no-reconcile`, `no-outcome`, `not-current`, or the one table's refusal of its outcome).
  `WORK_STATE` answers them as an additive `held` (of every unit, a quest's too), `daoris-driver work held` lists them, What needs you
  has a `work` row for each with the person's three words, and the work's foot (`WorkMilestone`) gives the node its place.
- **The person's word** (`HeldResolutions`: `happened`, `did-not`, `stop`), recorded on the attempt (`resolution`, `resolvedAt`, two
  fields a record written before them reads without) by `WorkPerformer.Word` under the record's lock with the lease asked inside it,
  through either door: `WORK_HELD` (classed a person's in `PipeCensus`, publishing `sessions`, the help coverage's exemption: no helper
  can know what became of an external effect) and `daoris-driver work held <unit> <node> <attempt> <word>`. Only the lease's holder
  records it, refused with a sentence otherwise.
- **Choices left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **The word is applied by the runtime, not the door.** `NodeLifecycle.Apply` needs the node's declaration (its outputs, its evidence
     schema), which only the governing graph under its plugin's step gives, and only the runtime holds it (`NodeRuntime.Govern`). So
     the door records the word and nudges the loop, and the pass applies it (`WorkPerformer.Resolve`, after `Hear`, before
     `ReconcileAsync`) under the declaration governing now. A word on a node no graph governs any more waits with it.
  2. **Two rows of §E2's table, the person's** (`unknown → reached` and `unknown → failed`, the design's table amended): only on a plugin's
     performed node (`NodeAuthority.PersonSettles`, else `not-settled`), the source the person, never the plugin's. An attempt held with
     its node active (its outcome did not bind) is first made unknown by Daoris, as the outcome cannot be established now.
  3. **What each word does.** *It happened*: reached by the person, its evidence the person's (`person:<invocation>`), and only where the
     node exposes nothing a consumer reads; a declared output or evidence schema is its plugin's reference, which no word makes
     (`not-on-word`: the attempt stays held saying so, the word taken back). *It did not*: failed by the person (`did-not`, not transient),
     then the person's new attempt (§E2's `failed → ready`), which the next evaluation starts with a new key. *Stop*: the node stopped,
     the attempt settled with its outcome `unknown`, the person's to answer for. On an attempt its node had moved off, the word settles
     it as a stale one's (§D3) and lifts the gate, moving nothing but a stop. Each settles the attempt, its `resolution` kept, so an
     outcome the person settled is never read as its plugin's word.
- **Not built here** (rows): a failure Daoris will not retry (a non-transient code, or its tries spent) is kept for the person (RUN1e)
  but neither listed nor offered its new attempt on the page; a node record that does not read stops its unit for a person and is
  said in the loop's lines alone.
- **Proof** (fast; the driver's seen failing on their members first, the route's and the page's written with their code):
  `NodeLifecycleTests` (the table's two rows, canary 43 over every kind, the person's word refused on another source, a plugin's
  reference, an earlier attempt, a node exposing an output or an evidence schema, and a kind not settled), `NodeRuntimeTests` (4: a held
  attempt listed and keeping its gate, *it did not* settling it as the person's and the node restarted with a new key and invocation;
  *it happened* and *stop*; the word refused without the lease, on an attempt not held, and twice, nothing written),
  `DriverModuleLinesTests` (`WORK_HELD` cold, malformed and without the home; `WORK_STATE` listing none unarmed), `PipeCensusTests`,
  `StatePublisherTests`, `HelpCoverageTests`, and the page's `WorkMilestone.test.tsx` (the row, its three words pressed, the foot in
  English and 中文, nothing shown where none is held).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1b, the close's execution and recovery).** The design's §E5, the driver's half of CLOSE1a's door.
`Trees/Nodes/QuestCloser.cs` runs `quest.close` in the evaluation runtime (RUN1's link 8): `QuestClosing` judges what a close names,
`QuestCloser` keeps the fence, and `ClosureBinding` binds. A graph holding `quest.close` now arms the runtime (`NodeRuntime.Armed`), and
governs a quest only when that quest is bound to that very graph (`GovernedUnit.Closes`); every other quest, an unbound one under a
closure graph included, is read and written exactly as before. The owner's install has no cycle, so nothing binds or closes there.

- **The client** (CLOSE1e's note): `QuestView.Closure` and `Submission` read, absent from a host before them; an evidence verdict on a
  taken quest with a submission names it (`EvidenceVerdict.Submission`, written only when set), and the reader reads such a quest's
  evidence as a done's (`EvidenceReader.Wanted`); `BindQuestAsync`, `CloseQuestsAsync` (a refusal read by its code, the same close again
  answering its first outcome), and `KnowsClosureAsync`. The service's half: `/api/status` answers `closure: true`, and the close door
  names each refusal's check in `code` (`QuestCloseCodes`: `key-reused`, `stale`, `held`, …).
- **The fence** (§E5 steps 1-4): the start is one locked edit on the record as the evaluation left it, writing the attempt (sender
  `quest.close`, never a plugin's) with its key and its token (`ctk-`, from the unit, revision, graph digest and prerequisite receipts) and
  the quests it names, before the send; the door's answer is kept on that attempt only while it still waits for it. Closed is the reach
  (`close-door`, the key as its evidence); a refusal is a failure with the door's code, never transient, its words kept on the attempt
  and said; no door holds the attempt for the person. An unanswered close is unknown, and the next pass, a restart's included, sends the
  same close again before anything new (`QuestCloser.ReconcileAsync`, within the pass's four sends); the plugin performer never asks
  after it, and no plugin's frame is taken about it.
- **Choices §E5 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. *The unit's record lock held across the whole close* is the lease's one writer and the pass's one-at-a-time, with the record edited
     before and after the call: PERFORM1h's rule that nothing is awaited inside an edit stands, and the answer is applied to the attempt
     as it then stands.
  2. *Which quests a chain's close names, and from where.* The chain closes at its last quest in this repository: a quest whose own steps
     still name this repository waits, and the last one's close names every quest of its chain here, under the same graph, that is not
     Done, oldest first, routed at the last of them. A chain quest Done by that close has its own close node reached on the same key
     (`ReachByChain`), never sent and never read as the person's. A quest the person closed is left out.
  3. *When the bind is made.* The session takes the quest itself, so no open precedes the take. The runtime binds a quest it reads taken,
     unbound and not submitted, under a closure graph, and taken since this loop first read that graph (an in-memory cutover: a quest in
     flight before it, or before a restart, keeps today's done), once per loop. Until the bind, the agent's `done` closes the quest as
     today (CLOSE1a's choice 1). Rejected for now: binding at the open (the quest is not taken yet, so the door refuses it).
  4. *The handshake* (CLOSE1e's compatibility): the local host's `/api/status` `closure`; a circle synced through a remote is never
     bound, since no host or machine says yet which build the circle's other machines run. Either way the quest closes by its done.
  5. *When to send.* The submission and its holds are the close's own precondition, read on the quests at the start: no close is sent
     while a quest of the chain has no submission or a held one. No `submitted` node is added.
  6. *The override* is read only where every quest the close would close is Done with no close of this machine's naming it, reached or
     still awaiting its answer: the graph's live nodes are stopped by the person's word *closed by you before its end*, said once.
  7. *Partially closed* is read on a reached close whose named quest now reads not Done and off the submission it judged (a stale read of
     the quest before the close is never taken for one), kept as a history entry and said once, naming the quest; nothing is retried.
- **Held by**, each seen red on the members first: `QuestCloseTests` (9: closed once its submission is current and unheld, and once
  only; unanswered, sent again with the same key after a restart; a refusal kept with its words and said; a chain closed at its last quest
  and a part lost since said once; the person's done stopping the graph; an unbound quest and one bound to another graph unchanged; the
  bind's handshake, remote and refusal; a quest taken since the loop read its graph bound once and one in flight before not, this last
  written after its code and seen green only), `ServiceClientClosureTests` (7), `EvidenceCheckTests` (a submitted quest's verdict names
  its submission), `LocalHostTests` (the codes, the status) and `PersonDoorHostTests` (the status's fields).
- **Not built here** (rows): the bind made atomically at the take; the session's instruction saying `ready` in a closure graph; the
  landing and its gates waiting on an unheld submission; the circle's handshake through the remote; the milestone's *closed by you* and
  *partially closed* on the page, with its offer to stop the node; a quest bound to a graph since edited.
- **After review (FABRIC-CLOSE1l, 2026-10-18).** A second maker found CLOSE1b safe to stage unarmed and not safe to arm, on eight
  findings. Each is now held by a test seen red on CLOSE1b's branch first, and the two activation rows CLOSE1b proposed (the bind at the
  take and the session's `ready`, named CLOSE1f and CLOSE1g in its hand-back, a name the backlog's CLOSE1f already held) are folded in. Where a choice traded a
  call for a guarantee, the guarantee was taken.
  1. *The chain is walked whole.* `QuestClosing.Lineage` follows the parents through every repository, so an A → B → A chain's last A
     closes with its first and waits while the first is held. An ancestor not read, or a quest of the chain here bound to another graph,
     leaves the close waiting.
  2. *The bind is the take's own* (replacing choice 3). As a quest's first session opens, `ClosureBinding.AtStartAsync` names
     the graph for the take: `POST /api/quests/{id}/bind` with `atTake: true`, kept in the store's own `quest_take_binds` and never
     pushed. The take on this machine then appends `bound` in its own write (`QuestStore.MoveAsync`), so no agent's `done` lands between
     them. The take answers bound, saying `ready`. A taken quest takes no such bind, and a quest that syncs through a remote is refused
     one. A host before this reads `atTake` as absent and refuses an open quest's bind, so its quest closes by its done. The runtime binds
     nothing.
  3. *The session says `ready`*: `SessionTarget.Ready`, on a first start the host bound, a resume and a carry-on of a bound
     quest. Every other instruction is as it was (`golden/target-prompt/ready.md`). An unanswered bind leaves the instruction at `done`,
     which the service then refuses naming `ready` if the bind did land.
  4. *`Updated` is no longer the take's time.* With 2, nothing infers it: the bind and the take are one write.
  5. *The remote map is sync's* (`RemoteTarget.Effective`): the environment's pair, then `DAORIS_REMOTE_CONFIG`, then the home's map.
  6. *The fresh fence* (§E5 step 1). A closing quest's revision is `QuestClosing.Revision`: one digest of every quest of its chain here
     (moment, status, binding, submission, hold) and the tip of each branch their sessions worked. Right before the send, the units are
     read again, git's refs included. The close waits for the next pass when the revision, the binding or what it names has moved. A
     prerequisite acceptance bound to an unchanged revision still stands, since the loop is the record's one writer.
  7. *The bound graph is kept* (`ClosureGraphs`, `workflows/closures/<digest>.json`). It is kept at the bind and when first seen
     governing, and a bound quest is closed by it whatever the choices say since. A home keeping one stays armed, so an unanswered close
     is reconciled after the last closure graph is removed. A home that never bound a quest keeps none, and is judged by its choices
     alone: one stat.
  8. *Only the door's own answer settles a close.* `CloseQuestsAsync` reads a close (200 with its quests), or a 400, 404 or 409 naming
     one of the door's checks (`QuestCloseOutcomes.Codes`). Anything else is thrown as unanswered: a proxy's page, a 500, a bare 404, a
     refusal naming none. The attempt stays unknown and the same key is sent again. *No door* is gone.
  9. *A refusal's cause clearing retries.* An ancestor's hold, gained and released, moves the chain's revision. That lapses the failed
     close (WIRE1e), which is made again under a fresh key, as the terminal quest's own release already did.
  - **Found on the way:** a close's own node, started again once its quest's Done moved its revision, was reached by `ReachByChain`
    under a new attempt number with no attempt record. Its record then failed `NodeRecordFiles.Consistent` and stopped reading, which
    every armed close would have hit, since the Done moves `Updated`. It now keeps a settled, never-sent attempt for that number
    (`QuestClosing.Chained`, which nothing reconciles or says), and a chain wholly Done keeps one revision, so a later landing is no new
    work.
  - **Held by:** `QuestClosureTests` (2: a take the driver named a graph for binds in its own write and refuses `done` naming `ready`;
    a take with none is unbound and says nothing of `ready`), `QuestCloseTests` (7 new or rewritten), `ServiceClientClosureTests` (5
    answers that are not the door's), `RemoteTargetTests`, and `TargetPromptGoldenTests` (`ready`, and every start of a bound quest).
    The owner's install, with no closure graph, names no graph, keeps no file, says no `ready`, and is read and written as before.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-GRAPH1b, a named graph as a named workflow's version).** The design's §E7 and D157 §2.4 and §5.2. A version is its `steps` (schema 1, with no `schema` key or `schema: 1`) or its `graph` (`schema: 2`, no `steps`), never both and never in part. `WorkflowNamed.ReadVersion` reads the graph through `NodeGraphs.Read`, so `NodeGraphs.Problems` judges it and refuses it whole. `NamedVersion.Digest` of a graph version is `NodeGraphs.Digest`, which a run bound to it binds (`WorkflowRunBindings.Plan`; `WorkflowGraphs.Decode` and `ForRun` take it as the identity and the graph digest, with no decoder). `WorkflowNamed.Digest` stays the identity of schema-1 versions, byte for byte. The steps-driven runtime holds a run on a graph version as unrun (`NamedVersion.Unrun`) until the cutover (FABRIC-CUTOVER1). The writers keep what they do not own: `PlanAdd` adds one version and keeps the file's other fields and every version as written.

*An older build.* It reads `steps` alone and no other key, so a graph version that holds no `steps` is unreadable there, with its neighbours kept (the table's `older` column, against the current `ReadSteps`). A malformed schema-2 version that also carries valid `steps` is read, and run, as those steps by that build. Older writers keep `schema` and `graph`, but ordinary retention (the newest 20 and every version a kept run names) can still prune an old, unbound version.

*Choices for review.* (1) Schema 2 with steps beside a graph, or a `schema` that is not 1 or 2, is unreadable whole. (2) The CLI reads and digests a graph (`graphversions.ts`) but does not judge or save one, because it has no `NodeGraphs.Problems`: rows marked `judged: "driver"` are the driver's alone, and `planAddVersion` refuses a graph. (3) An option's value is digested as the driver's compact writer writes it. The CLI, reading parsed JSON, cannot reproduce a number's own text, numeric-looking object keys in order, or a pair of escapes for a character outside the basic plane, so it takes only ASCII and Chinese text, flags, null, and lists and objects of them with keys that are not numbers; it reads any other version as unreadable and writes over no file that holds one. The driver's `Compact` and `NodeGraphs.Digest` are unchanged, so stored cycle digests do not move. (4) `use` of a workflow whose newest version is a graph says so and does not crash. A Codex review before the merge found (3), (4) and an option named `__proto__` lost by the CLI; each is held by a table row or a test.

Not built: the editor (GRAPHEDIT1), the CLI's judgement and save (FABRIC-GRAPH1c, FABRIC-GRAPH1f), the cutover (FABRIC-CUTOVER1). Proof: `WorkflowGraphVersionsTests` and the CLI's `graphversions.test.ts` and `workflowchoice-door.test.ts`, on `workflow-graph-versions.json`.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (LANDRACE1 and LANDPIN2, END7's two open limits closed).** END7's review left both open (*After review*,
**Left**), and both had to close before any rule turns `autoAccept` on. As revised after a second maker's review, below.

- **One landing of one tree at a time, and one push of one landed branch** (LANDRACE1, D145 point 2). `Trees/Landing/LandingHolds.cs`
  holds two things:
  - The tree, around the whole landing (its plugin and tidy included), at every door that lands it: the press, a terminal's
    `trees land`, an entry's Accept and the look (`TreeLands.LandAsync`).
  - The landed branch, around its push: the landing's plugin step and a hand-off (`TreeHandOffs.HandAsync`).

  How the holds work:
  - Each is a semaphore per key in this process, and across processes a file under the home's `locks/landings/`, opened alone, as
    `TreeLock` and `RecordLock` are. A key's semaphore is dropped once no door holds or waits for it. The files stay, one of under a
    kilobyte per tree landed and per branch pushed, so they grow with the landings a home has made: removing one could lose what a door
    in another process is about to read.
  - The key is the folder, not its name. A tree is keyed by its folder's identity (`FolderIdentity`). A branch is keyed by its
    repository's common git folder's identity and the branch's name. The common folder is read from git's own files, with no process
    (`TreeTips.CommonDirectory`: the checkout's `.git`, or the folder its `gitdir:` names and that folder's `commondir`). So the
    landing, which names the repository by the main checkout, and a terminal's hand-off from a registry checkout that is a linked
    worktree take one hold. Off Windows, and for a folder with no identity, the key is the spelling.
  - The holder keeps what it came to in the file (`LandingHold.Keep`): the branch, the commit, who accepted it, and the plugin's
    answer. A tree's landing keeps the session tip it landed (the squashed one, under a squash) the moment the work is on its branch,
    and again with the plugin's answer. A push keeps the branch tip pushed.
  - A hold that waited reads that as `Before`. One that waited for nothing clears it, and one that waited and kept nothing leaves it
    for the next.

  What a door does after a wait:
  - A landing compares the commit it would land (the tip its door pinned, else the tree's head, read only after a wait) with the one
    landed. The same commit is `already`: *another landing of this session finished while this one waited for it: its work is on
    `<branch>` at `<commit>`, accepted …*, then the plugin's answer as a landing says it, a failure included, then *Nothing more was
    landed*. The answer carries the plugin's answer too. A later commit lands as any landing does.
  - A push compares tips: a push of the same commit that answered pushed is its answer, and the plugin is not told again. A hand-off
    reads the landing record again under the hold, and a landing reads the pull request it grows there, so neither opens a second.

  Bounds and costs:
  - A door that waited for nothing is judged as before. The press's words and LANDGIT1's counts do not move: no hold starts git.
    Its holds' own file work (the tree's taken, kept twice and let go; the branch's taken, kept and let go) measured a median of 4.7 ms
    over 25 uncontended runs on the build machine, 2.1 ms the least and 58 ms the most (the first, with the JIT). A landing's own git
    starts take seconds. A write reaches the system's cache and is not forced to the disk.
  - A wait is bounded at five minutes by a monotonic clock (`LandingHolds.Wait`, a plugin's two plus three). Past it the landing
    lands nothing, a push is not tried, and each says another is still under way.
  - A plugin that calls back into the landing that started it waits on that landing's own hold, and the landing's patience with
    the plugin ends it: bounded at the plugin's two minutes.
- **The plugin is handed the commit judged** (LANDPIN2, D164 point 4).
  - The `work/land` frame gains `tip`, additively: the commit the landing judged and put the branch at, which is the record's
    `Tip`. The hand-off's frame carries the tip it judged.
  - Both example plugins push `<tip>:refs/heads/<branch>` (GitHub 1.3.0, Azure DevOps 1.4.0), so a commit made on the branch after
    the judgement stays for the next landing. On a tree's own branch, where no ref is made, it no longer rides the push.
  - A frame with no `tip` (from an older Daoris) is pushed by name, as before. The examples no longer pass `-u`, since a commit source
    sets no upstream; Daoris reads no upstream.
  - The kit says it in `work/land`'s text, its sample and its code comment, and the hooks design's table names the field.
- **After review (2026-10-18).** A second maker's review of the first build found four gaps; each is closed above.
  1. `already` compared the session and an acceptance time, not the commit. A press for a later commit was refused, and a waiter
     heard nothing of the first plugin's failure.
  2. The hold was keyed by the tree's spelling, so a junction, a `subst` drive or a short name took a second hold.
  3. The bound and the de-duplication read the wall clock, which can step back.
  4. A hand-off called the plugin outside any hold, so it could push beside the landing's own push.

  Its re-check closed 1, 3 and the hold's nesting, and found 2 and 4 open together. The branch's key named `<root>/.git`, which in a
  linked worktree is a file and has no folder identity, so the key fell back to the spelling. The landing names the main checkout and
  a terminal's hand-off names the registry's, so a linked one took a second hold. The key is now the common git folder, as above. The
  re-check also asked for the hold's cost to be measured (above), and for the accumulation to be said: a key's semaphore is now
  dropped, and the files are left.
- **Held by**, each seen red first:
  - Git (`AutoLandingTests`):
    - The press beside the look's landing, on a made branch and on the tree's own branch: the plugin told once, one landing
      recorded, the press refused `already`.
    - A press for a commit made while the first plugin pushed: it lands, and the plugin is told each commit.
    - A press behind a failed plugin: it is told the failure.
    - A hand-off beside the landing's push, from the main checkout and from a linked worktree (`git worktree add`): the plugin
      told once, nothing more handed on.
    - The own branch's frame names the commit judged after the agent committed again as the plugin started.
  - Fast: `LandingHoldsTests` covers:
    - the wait and the outcome read, and the last kept outcome down a chain of waits;
    - two names for one tree (the long-path form, and a link where the account may make one);
    - one push of a branch at a time;
    - a linked worktree (its `gitdir:` absolute and relative) and its main checkout taking one branch hold, and two spellings of a
      linked worktree taking one;
    - another process's file, and the bound with no gate left behind.

    `LandingChainTests` holds the frame's `tip`.
  - CLI: `landing-plugins.test.ts` has each example pushing the frame's `tip` past a later commit, at a first landing and at an
    advance.
  - No test steps the clock: the holds read only a `Stopwatch` and compare commits.
- **Left:**
  - A plugin installed from an example before this change still pushes by name until it is copied again.
  - The hold covers the landing itself, not the reads a door makes before it: the gate, the look's D88 proof (`UnlandedHereAsync`)
    and the subject. What the landing acts on it reads again under the hold, and the look's pin refuses a tip that moved.
  - A landing that replayed the line's commits keeps the replay's tip. A press for the same work that waits finds a different commit
    and is judged as any landing is, where the content proof should find the work carried. No test holds that case.
  - `locks/landings/` keeps a file per tree landed and per branch pushed. Nothing sweeps them; a sweep could remove one no door has
    held for longer than the bound.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-WORKFLOW2b, a D157 run's step states read as the decoded graph's statuses).** The design's §E7 and canary
38, D157 §2.4 and §5.2. A read-only projection and a comparison: `WorkflowRun` and the runtime are not touched, and schema 1 governs
every run as it did.

- **Read through the binding** (`Trees/Nodes/WorkflowRunNodes.cs`, `VersionOf`): the run's binding and its workflow through the two
  stores, never written. No binding, or Current, is null: Current stays live. A binding that does not read, a workflow no longer saved,
  and each of `WorkflowGraphs.ForRun`'s reasons (v1 edited, v1 gone) read as unreadable, never as the newest version or Current.
- **Replayed, not translated** (`Project`): every node starts blocked on one unit named by the run, with no revision (as a quest's),
  under the version's graph digest and the run. Each round evaluates the wires, starts a node only where the evaluation offers it, on
  its binding, then moves it to what its step reads as, every move through `NodeLifecycle.Apply`. So each status is one the lifecycle
  could make, and a step schema 1 went on with that the graph does not offer stays where its wires leave it. Nothing is observed: no
  unit, git read or gate is handed in, so the run's own reading is the only one.
- **The table** (choices, for review): *not reached* and *cannot start* move nothing; *working* and *waiting* are active, and the
  look's *not yet* is the person's rejection with their words; *not known* is unknown; *done* is reached by the kind's authority, its
  evidence `workflow-run#<step>`; *skipped* is the opinion gate's skip (WIRE1g's row), the person's on the look with their words, and
  otherwise active, since no node's authority makes it; *failed* is failed by the kind's failure authority, else active (a claim never
  fails); *stopped* is the person's stop, and a pull request abandoned is failed on its plugin's answer, as `BuiltInNodes` reads it.
- **The comparison:** the graph's eligible nodes are those standing for a step whose wires hold and which are not settled (`Frontier`),
  neither raw eligibility (which keeps a completed node) nor the starts (which leave a started one out). It agrees where they are
  exactly `WorkflowRun.At` and nothing differs. A sink or a person's task decoding made stands for no step and is counted apart; a
  group's join would stand for its group, which no readable version holds yet. Every schema-1 step is kept whole beside its node.
- **The differences, named and none settled here** (`WorkflowRunDifferences`); the cutover settles each:
  1. `look-beside-opinion`: the WIRE1g mismatch, at the opinion. Schema 1 holds the look only while an agent is at the opinion, so it
     goes on beside a dispute, commits since, a required opinion none could give, or an opinion not read.
  2. `cannot-start-holds-nothing`: a go-ahead (partial) is shown as cannot start, and schema 1's landing goes on past it, landed
     included; the decoded order holds it.
  3. `added`: a look the work's own choice added is no node of the version, so the graph would offer the landing it holds.
  4. `passed-by-rule`: a look the chain's or the ask's choice turns off, and a pull request with no branch or nothing landed, are
     skipped by a choice or a fact no node's authority makes, so the node stays pending and the graph stands at it. *Settled and
     removed by FABRIC-WIRE1h: the look's gate and the landing record now make those skips (the last note below).*
  5. `skip-passes-earlier`: the person's skip of the look satisfies its `after` wire while the opinion before it is not settled, so the
     decoded landing becomes eligible where schema 1 holds it on the opinion. The protected order holds by reachability, and a skip
     breaks the chain.
  6. `refused`: the one table refused a step's move (a skip without its words); no recorded run has one.
  7. `unexplained`: none expected; a reading no name explains is said at its step, and the recorded runs hold at none.
- **The identity unchanged:** a run bound to the CLI twin's smallest version reads with the twin's identity (`75906e6bfc40`) and graph
  digest, computed in its own runtime, and its nodes bind that digest. A changed decoder (`GraphDigest`'s decoder overload) derives
  another graph digest, while the binding's stored identity and every file's bytes and last write stay as they were.
- **Not built here** (rows): settling differences 1 to 5 (RUN1c and CUTOVER1 with them); a run bound to a graph version, which
  schema 1 holds as unrun, is not compared.
- **Proof** (fast; 37 seen failing on the members first, the unexplained reading's and the unread run's checks written after):
  `WorkflowRunNodesTests`, 31 recorded runs written by hand (the
  work queued, held, failed and declined; the landing waiting, landed and with nothing; the look shown, skipped, its skip withdrawn,
  not yet, off and added; the opinion read, settled, none, optional and required unavailable, disputed, unread, and passed by a skip;
  two historical landings; a go-ahead at its turn and refused each way; a pull request open, merged, abandoned, unknown and not
  opened), the comparison over all of them, the mismatch named, an unexplained reading said, synthetic nodes counted apart, a newer
  version never leaking, the identity and a decoder change, and three reads through stores that refuse every write.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1q, a close's authority: today's submission and the door's answer).** The design's §E5 and §D6, on
FABRIC-CLOSE1b's close as "After review (FABRIC-CLOSE1l)" left it and FABRIC-PERFORM1g's held attempts. Still nothing on the owner's
install, which holds no closure graph, so it binds and closes nothing (FIX-LOG 2026-10-18).

- **A close speaks for the submission it judged.** `QuestClosing.NamesCurrent` matches a close attempt to a quest by its id, its
  submission's machine and its sequence, which is the operation that made the submission. The same commit made ready again is another
  submission. Both readers take that one predicate through `QuestClosing.Naming`: `ReachByChain`, which reaches a node on a close
  already made, and the override, which the person's done ends the graph by. A quest with no submission has no close to borrow.
  Attempts kept for an earlier submission are kept as they are and speak for none.
- **A held close is the door's.** It is held where its door answered and the one table refused the move (its node could not take the
  answer). The override reads it as the door's unless the answer it keeps is a refusal, so the door's Done is never read as the
  person's.
- **Its recoveries** (`HeldResolutions.Close`, applied by `QuestCloser.Resolve` before the closer sends anything): `record` records the
  kept close again through the one table, the key as its evidence. `resend` makes the attempt wait for its answer again, so the same
  close is sent this pass with its key, token and named submissions; the door answers its first outcome. `stop` is offered apart: it
  stops every live node of the unit's work as the person's. The close's answer stays as it is: a kept close settles the attempt, its
  receipt kept, and one not kept is asked for again with its key and kept as a stale one's. None makes a new close, and the quest's Done
  stands.
- **Choices left open, made here** (for review):
  1. *`record` only for a close.* A close needs nothing but its key. A held refusal keeps the table's code on the attempt, not the
     door's, so it is asked for again (`resend`), and the door's code and words come back with its first outcome. A refusal practically
     never holds: the failure move checks no binding. A held close with no answer kept is offered `resend` and `stop` alone.
  2. *A plugin's word on a close.* *It happened* and *it did not* are refused at both doors (`not-close-word`). One recorded before this
     build is taken back at the next pass with that code, and the person is told why. A close's word on a plugin's attempt is refused
     (`not-offered`), and that attempt's three words are as they were.
  3. *The word is cleared as it is applied.* PERFORM1g keeps a plugin's word on the settled attempt, as the person's outcome. A close's
     outcome is never the person's, so nothing of it is kept, and the history keeps `person-stop`. A build before this one reads a record
     holding `record` or `resend` as unreadable, so the window is only between the word and the next pass.
  4. *The wire.* `WORK_STATE`'s `held` gains `words`, additively: the words offered on each attempt. The page offers exactly those, and
     on a close from a driver before the field, none. The terminal's line names them.
- **Said:** the terminal (`WhySaid`, `Lines`, `Said`, the usage) and What needs you, in English and 中文 with the glossary's 委托 and
  关闭. A held close reads *The quest was closed, and Daoris could not record that on this work (<code>)*, and its row offers *Record
  the answer again*, *Ask for the answer again* and *Stop the rest of this work*. The plugin's three words are unchanged.
- **Held by**, each seen red first:
  - `QuestCloseTests`:
    - part 1: S1 closed and kept, then S2 of the same commit closed by the person, read as overridden, its node stopped, S1 never
      reached through;
    - part 2: a theory over the three words, each applied by a loop started again, one key, Done unchanged, no override read while
      held;
    - a plugin's words refused and one recorded before taken back.
  - `NodeRuntimeTests`: a close's word on a plugin's attempt, refused at the door, and taken back at the pass where a record holds one.
  - `DriverModuleLinesTests`: the wire's `words`.
  - `WorkMilestone.test.tsx`: the row and its three presses, and the foot in both languages.
- **Not built here:** the milestone's *closed by you* and *partially closed* on the page (CLOSE1b's row).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1o, a pending take-bind owned, expiring and rechecked).** The design's §E5 cutover, and CLOSE1b's note,
*After review* (FABRIC-CLOSE1l), choice 2. That choice kept the graph named for a take in `quest_take_binds` by the quest's id alone, so
any take of that id bound it.

- **The owner.** The bind door's `atTake` names the session the graph is for (`session`, the driver's `sessionId`). The store keeps it
  with the quest's publication as it stands (the latest Published operation's machine and sequence, `QuestStore.PublicationOf`) and an
  `expires` one `QuestStore.TakeBindLife` (an hour) after the naming. Naming it again names it anew. A bind at the take that names no
  session is refused, so a driver before this is told its quest closes by its done.
- **The take.** `QuestStore.MoveAsync` consumes the row in the take's own write, whatever it makes of it. It appends Bound with the
  Taken only where `PendingTakeBind.Judge` reads `Bound`:
  - an owner was kept;
  - the taking session is the one named: the connector's intake (`DAORIS_SESSION_ID`), which `quest_respond` carries as
    `RespondAsync`'s `session`, while the HTTP respond door names none;
  - the publication is unchanged;
  - the take's own moment, the one its operation is stamped with, is before `expires`;
  - the circle syncs through no remote, read through `QuestTaker.Remote` inside the write. That is the service's `ConfiguredRemotes`,
    the map sync reads: the environment's pair, `DAORIS_REMOTE_CONFIG`, the home's file.

  The bind door now reads that same map, so a circle with a remote is refused whoever in it is joined, as the driver already did.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *An expired bind is taken unbound*, never refused or renewed: the person's take stands. The take's answer says once why it is not
     bound and to finish with `done` (`QuestExchange.UnboundAtTake`: an expired bind, a replaced publication, a remote since). Another
     session's take and an unowned row say nothing, since that session was told nothing of a graph. The loop's end line for the session
     whose start named the graph says once that its take left the quest unbound (`ClosureBinding.UnboundAtEnd`). A later start reads
     the quest taken and unbound, and names nothing.
  2. *The expiry is judged inside the write*, at the take's own moment, never by a look before it.
  3. *An hour*, since the take is the session's first act after its start names the graph.
  4. *A deleted quest's row stays* until that id's next naming or take. The publication judges it, so `DeleteAsync` is unchanged.
- **The migration.** `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` upgrades nothing, so the four columns are added by `SchemaColumns.EnsureAsync` at every
  open, on a local store and a shared one alike. A row with any of them null is deleted, since its owner cannot be shown. The quest and
  its history are untouched, and its take is today's. The bind doors stay local (`SharedHostTests`).
- **An older build over the migrated store** keeps working. The new columns are nullable, so its insert and upsert of `(id, graph, at)`
  land, and its `DELETE … RETURNING graph` reads as before. What it writes owns nothing, and this build discards it at its next open or
  take. An older build's own take still binds by the id alone, as it always did.
- **The owner's install** names no graph, so its takes are as before, word for word.
- **Held by:** `QuestTakeBindTests` (the upgrade-and-take theory over the old schema: valid, legacy owner, wrong session, replaced
  publication, expiry crossed, remote enabled since; an older build's row; a bind naming no session, each seen red first; the ordinary
  take, green before and after), `QuestClosureTests` (the session named), `QuestCloseTests` and `ServiceClientClosureTests` (the start
  names its session, and its end's line, seen red first), and `LocalHostTests` (the door reads `session`, written after the code).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1p, the close's fresh fence reads branch tips).** The design's §E5 step 1; it completes the CLOSE1b
note's finding 6 (*After review (FABRIC-CLOSE1l)*), which read the units again right before the close but whose read asked git nothing.
The loop's units read (`DriverWatch.ReadNodeUnitsAsync`, link 8's `Units`) passed no roots to the inventory, and a branch unit's
`Subject.Revision` is its kept end fact's, which a commit does not move. So `QuestClosing.Revision` digested the records alone, and a
session branch moved after a prerequisite's acceptance was closed on that acceptance.

- **The tips are git's, per read.** The read now carries `NodeUnitsRead.Tips`: for each branch unit a bound quest's chain produced, the
  commit its `refs/heads/<branch>` points at, empty where git says it is gone, null where git did not say. `QuestClosing.TipsAsync` takes
  one `RefSnapshot` per repository those branches are in (one `for-each-ref`, PROC1d-ii), for that read alone. A tip is never carried to
  the next read (LANDGIT1). The fence's read is its own, so it takes its own snapshot.
- **Revision digests the tips.** It uses each branch's tip where the read carries tips, and the recorded revision where it carries none
  (a seam's read, which asks no git). Byte for byte, it is as before there.
- **An unread tip waits.** `QuestClosing.Unread` names the first branch of the chain whose tip git did not answer. The fence then sends
  nothing and says *git did not say where `<branch>`, a branch it worked, stands*. Nothing is guessed: a checkout not registered, a
  failed read and a name only case tells apart all read as unread.
- **The inventory still gets no roots.** Gathering would add a recovery listing and a `rev-list` for every repository the home names, on
  every read. `StandingBranch` therefore stays as it was. The tips are read only where a quest is bound to a closure graph and its chain
  is not wholly Done. A closed quest the service still lists reads nothing, since its revision reads no branch. The RUN1 note's *the
  runtime starts no git* now holds where no quest is bound, and nowhere else.
- **A stale fence ends the unit's judgement for the pass.** The evaluation's next round proposed the same close again, and each round
  read git once more to find the same move. The next pass reads the unit again, as the line says.
- **Counts.** The owner's install has no quest bound: an unarmed look reads no units and starts no git, and a read with no bound quest
  starts none. That was 0 before and is 0 after. A read with two bound branches in one repository starts one `for-each-ref`, where it
  started 0 before. The pass whose fence found the move starts two: its read and the fence's. With only the fence's read changed, it
  started three.
- **Held by** (each seen red first, except the last, written after its code):
  - `CloseFenceGitTests` (Process, Git; real git through `ReadNodeUnitsAsync` and a stand-in service). Its first test moves a session
    branch between the pass's read and the fence's, after the deploy prerequisite's acceptance. Nothing is sent. The next pass re-sends
    the deploy on the new revision, and still sends no close. Once the new deploy is accepted, one close is sent, bound to that revision.
    The test counts its starts. Its second test holds an unarmed look at zero reads and zero starts.
  - `CloseTipsTests` (4, through the snapshot's seam): the revision moves with a tip and not without one; an unread tip names its branch;
    one snapshot per repository, and only for bound chains; no snapshot for an unbound or closed quest; a missing checkout or a failed
    read is unread, never gone.
  - `QuestCloseTests.A_branch_tip_git_did_not_answer_leaves_the_close_waiting_until_it_does`, written after its code.
- **Left:** a bound quest's revision is still computed on the governed units a wake reuses between whole reads (RUN1e). A plugin's
  attempt started on a wake binds that revision, and no close is sent on it without the fence's own read.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1r, a read without git's tips never closes).** CLOSE1p kept "no tips" as a read that judges on the
records, so a future production read that forgot them would have brought the defect back without a failure. The fence now waits on a
read whose `Tips` is null and says *git's tips were not read for this look*, in the same stale line as its other reasons. Nothing is
sent. The records-only judgement stays in `QuestClosing.Revision(..., null)` for the evaluation's own digest, which sends nothing. A read
with no bound quest is unchanged and starts no git. `QuestCloseTests` now builds its reads' tips from its branches
(`Tips()`, or the test's own `_tips`), and `A_read_without_git_tips_leaves_the_close_waiting_and_says_why` was seen red first.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1t, every quest a close names fenced in its own graph).** The design's §E5 step 1. A read-only review of
the whole close path (2026-10-18) found it safe unarmed, and this the first of four gaps before arming; the others are CLOSE1u, CLOSE1v and
CLOSE1w. CLOSE1l's finding 1 walks the chain whole, and `QuestClosing.Judge` named each earlier quest on its submission and hold alone. The
evaluation that proposed the close fenced the terminal quest's own prerequisites, and no other quest's node record was read. So a first
quest whose deploy stayed held, its submission unheld and its successor published, was closed by that successor's close.

- **The fence.** Before a close is sent, `NodeRuntime.Unreached` judges each other quest it names on that quest's own node record. It is
  judged under the graph it is bound to (its governed unit this pass, `Closes`) and at its current revision, by the evaluation its own pass
  makes (`NodeRuntime.Evaluate`, factored out of `EvaluateAsync`). Its close node must be eligible there, and no prerequisite wired into
  the close may move in that judgement, so the close rests on what the record holds and never on an observation not kept yet.
- **What waits, and how it is said.** These leave the close unsent, before it takes a send and before the fence's own read:
  - a record that does not read;
  - a quest not governed here by its graph;
  - a graph not judged;
  - a prerequisite held, unknown, failed, rejected, stopped, lapsed or not reached yet.

  The pass says it once, however many rounds propose the start: *work quest:q7: its close waits for #q6, which it would close too: its
  `deploy`, before its `close` in its own graph, is held for the person.*
- **Currency.** The earlier quest's revision comes from the same read as the terminal's. Each of its inputs, its chain's quests here and
  the branches they worked, is an input of the terminal's revision, which the fence reads again right before the send. Its record cannot
  move between the two, since the loop is its one writer.
- **Counts.** One node-record read per other quest named, made only for a close about to be sent, and none for a quest that closes alone.
  No git: the evaluation is handed no git reads. An unarmed look reads no units, keeps no record and starts no process, as before. A single
  quest's close reads nothing more, so `CloseFenceGitTests`' counts stand.
- **Held by** `QuestCloseTests`, each seen red first, except the last, which was green before and after:
  - the chain with the first's deploy held: no close, said once a pass naming `#q6` and `deploy`, and no process started;
  - the same chain once the person's word reaches that deploy: one close naming both, oldest first;
  - an earlier quest whose node record does not read: the close waits, says so, and is sent once it reads;
  - an unarmed look: no units read, no record, nothing said, no process.
- **Left:** recovery before the fence (CLOSE1u), the service's scope (CLOSE1v), and expiry and older writers (CLOSE1w).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-WIRE1g, the decoded nodes evaluated).** The design's §E2, §E4 and §E7, for the two kinds FABRIC-WORKFLOW2
decoded and left unevaluated. Still pure, every move through `NodeLifecycle.Apply`. Schema-1 runs are unchanged: `WorkflowRun` and the
runtime are not touched.

- **A `stop` reaches on its path** (`WireEvaluation`, step 3, before the joins). A started sink is reached once, by `NodeSource.Path`
  (`WireWhy.Path`, the receipt as its detail). The receipt must be a failure or a rejection its binding rests on, read through a `failed`
  or `rejected` wire into it. Nothing else reaches it: a success's or a skip's receipt, an unknown, a failure Daoris still retries, or
  another run's failure, which is no receipt in this run (WIRE1e). A sink reached in one run lapses in another (`upstream`). A sink an
  authored graph starts on an `after` wire stays active, since no path leads into it. Reaching it reaches nothing after it.
- **An `opinion` reads the gate** (`Trees/Nodes/OpinionNode.cs`, in step 2 with the observed built-ins). The caller hands in the gate as
  `OpinionGate.JudgeAsync` judged it (`WireRun.Opinions`, by unit). The gate's file keeps asks and answers, not a verdict, so the node
  reads the judgement and never judges again. The mapping is D157's run's (`WorkflowRun`'s opinion step):
  - `settled`, `anyway`, `myself` and `answered` reach it. Its evidence names `opinions/gates#<repository>:<opinion or answer>`.
  - `none`, and `unavailable` where the rule does not require one, pass it over.
  - Every other state is pending, a required `unavailable` and `unread` included. Nothing fails an opinion.
- **No invented completion or skip.** A judgement says nothing in five cases, and the node stays pending: it was made at another commit
  than the binding's; no commit was read; it was made for another repository than the unit's; none was handed in (a teammate's work with
  no local record); or the gate says `unread` (the service or git did not answer). A missing or corrupt gate file reads as `not-asked`
  in the gate itself, so it is pending too.
- **A recorded landing keeps today's reading.** A landing recorded at the bound commit decides, whatever the gate says now, as
  `WorkflowRun` reads a landing done. A kept `unavailable` passes the opinion over. Any other kept answer reaches it, and so does a
  landing that kept none, which today reads as *landed*. A landing at another commit says nothing of this one.
- **The gate's skip is the record's, not the person's** (choice, for review). §E2's table gains one row, `active → skipped` by the
  authority. `NodeAuthority.SkipBy` names whose record may make it: the opinion's (`NodeSource.Opinion`), and no other kind's.
  - The move must name that source, the current attempt, and the attempt's own graph and run.
  - It keeps no words, whatever the move brings. The person's skip keeps every check it had.
  - Such a skip satisfies `after` and no `data` wire, and lapses outside its graph or run, as the person's does. It is the record's
    judgement of its binding, so a new revision or a lost receipt also lapses it (`Skipped → invalidated`). The person's skip still
    holds across a new tip.
  - Why a row and not the person's skip with words supplied: that would put words in the person's mouth and weaken the words check. Why
    not a reach: *lets go* would read as *reached*, and the two differ to anything wired to its evidence.
- **The mismatch, recorded, not settled** (the parent's decision). Today a look goes on beside a disputed opinion, and its *Reviewed*
  answers the dispute (`WorkflowRunTests`, *the look goes on beside a dispute, whose Reviewed answers it*). The decoded order (GRAPH1's
  choice 5: every opinion reaches every look) holds the look until the opinion is reached. So under a graph the person's Reviewed could
  not answer a dispute: the opinion would wait for an answer only the look gives. Schema 1 keeps its behaviour, and the test
  `Mismatch_a_disputed_opinion_holds_a_decoded_look_that_a_schema_1_run_lets_go_on` holds the graph's side as it stands. WORKFLOW2b and
  the cutover settle it: a wire from the look into the opinion, the opinion's dispute read as answered by the look, or a changed order.
- **Not built here:** the runtime starting an opinion or a sink and handing in the gate (`NodeRuntime` begins only `BuiltInNodes.Observed`,
  unchanged), and naming the unit an opinion acts on. The gate judges a branch's commit, so on a quest unit, whose revision is null, the
  node stays pending. Both are RUN1c's.
- **Proof** (fast). 18 failed before the change: every test of a reach, a skip or a sink. The pending rows and the two guards (a
  judgement elsewhere, the mismatch) passed before it, since they assert that nothing moves. The stale-commit guard was seen failing on a
  copy without its commit check.
  - `WireEvaluationDecodedTests` (on `WireEvaluationTests`' graph helper):
    - a decoded check's `failed: stop`, its sink reached once across three evaluations and a replay, the end not reached;
    - the sink never reached on a success, a skip, an unknown, a retried failure or another run's failure, lapsing in another run,
      held on an `after` start, and taken on a go-ahead's refusal but not on its yes;
    - every gate state, required and optional, against `LetsGo`;
