    - a judgement at another commit, with no tip, for another repository, not handed in, unread, or over missing records, and a newer
      commit, none completing or skipping;
    - the gate's skip satisfying `after` and no `data` wire, lapsing in another run and on a new tip where the person's holds;
    - a recorded landing's four readings, and one at another commit;
    - the mismatch;
    - the evaluation leaving what it is handed unchanged, twice the same.
  - `NodeLifecycleTests`: the table's new row, canary 43 over it, and the record's skip refused without its source, its attempt, its
    scope or on another kind.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-PERFORM1i, an observed node reached by its believed event).** The design's §C4 (`work/step` naming no
invocation, the five rules), §E2 (an acceptance on its binding) and §E10, on PERFORM1c's judge, RUN1's runtime and PROC1d-ii's one read
per judgement. This replaces PERFORM1c's *Not built here* for observed nodes; `pull-request`'s event form and D157's WORKFLOW1j and 1k
move to FABRIC-PERFORM1l. Until now such an event reached nothing, and an observed plugin node was left ready, never awaited.

- **Arming** (the parent's ruling, recorded here): a graph naming a plugin's observed node arms the runtime, as a performed one does
  (`NodeRuntime.Armed`, `Acts`), for the owner's aim, *maximize the automation*, and because a person wrote the graph naming the step.
  RUN1 armed on performed nodes alone. Choices naming neither still read, tell, write and start nothing (RUN1's first test, unchanged).
- **Awaiting.** An observed plugin node whose plugin declares its step at the graph's version and reach (`GovernedUnit.Observable`) is
  begun as an observed built-in is (`WorkPerformer.Begin`: ready → active, its firings written), and only then published: `Tell`
  names it while it is active, never while ready, so no event can precede its start.
- **Binding** (`Trees/Nodes/WorkObserver.cs`). An event is bound to the last `work/watch` frame whose send to that process's generation
  completed (`IWorkRuntimePort.SentTo`, `WorkPlugins` keeping it per generation, a failed send leaving the one before), never the set
  desired since nor `GovernedUnit.Watched`. The frame carries a host-side half the wire never writes (`WatchedUnit.Unit`, `Identity`): the
  canonical unit and the repository's identity (`RepositoryIdentity.Of`: the common git folder's file identity, else its spelling, so a
  linked worktree names its repository). Exactly one unit of that frame must name the event's repository and branch and await its
  step, still governed in the same repository; else `not-watched`. The step, normalised (`<plugin>/` taken off, only the sender's), names
  one observed node of the governing graph: none, or two of that kind, is `not-named` (never the first); one its plugin no longer declares
  is `not-declared`.
- **Belief.** The five rules in order: the sender's generation is the plugin's current one (`not-sender`); the naming above; the unit
  watched; its commit equals the branch's tip read now (`NodeRuntimeLook.Tips`, `NodeTips.ReadAsync`: one fresh `RefSnapshot` per
  checkout per pass, whatever number of events and branches, never a ref held across passes) and the node's binding's revision, else
  `stale`, an unread tip included; its `at` at most a minute ahead (`shape`); then the order below (`late`).
- **Acceptance.** One locked edit of the unit's record with the lease asked inside it: the node, still active on a binding of that
  commit, moves through the one table on its own binding (`reached` with the plugin's evidence at its schema, `WorkPerformer.Accepted`
  with the event's id as the word where no reference came; `failed` with its code), and the event is kept beside it
  (`NodeRecord.Observations`, an `observations` array written only where there are some, so every record before it reads as it did).
  It is written before the pass's evaluation, so its wires fire after it is persisted.
- **Choices §C4 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **Identity.** Plugin, repository identity, unit (the record's), normalised step, commit and event id. The process generation fences
     delivery and is no part of it, so a replay after a plugin restart or a loop restart is the same event and mints nothing.
  2. **Order, by step and commit apart.** The record keeps, per plugin, repository and step, the newest event taken and its commit; taking
     a newer commit's drops the older's, since the fourth rule makes those stale while the tip stands. Against it: the same identity,
     state and time again is a `replay` (nothing written, not counted); its id reused for another outcome or time is `late`; an older
     time, and an equal time under another id (the first taken stands), are `late`. Another commit is never late behind this one's, and
     still answers the fourth rule.
  3. **`unknown`** is believed and establishes nothing (`noted`): no move, nothing kept, so no later word is ordered behind it.
  4. **One outcome per binding.** A node no longer active on that commit takes nothing: the same outcome under another id is a `replay`,
     another outcome is `late` (an event never removes anything, §C4's trust), and a node the person stopped or skipped is `not-watched`.
  5. **Before awaiting.** An event heard before its unit was in a frame sent is `not-watched` and nothing is kept, so the same fact sent
     again once watched (a resync replays it) is taken, whatever its old time says.
  6. **The person's press.** Their stop or skip is a locked edit like the event's, so either order holds: pressed first, the event finds
     no node awaiting it; heard first, the press is judged on the reached node (the table moves neither). PERFORM1g's settlement stays
     for performed nodes alone.
- **Not built here** (rows): the graph's save does not refuse two observed nodes of one plugin step in one graph, which the runtime then
  never reaches (`not-named`); a branch unit's revision is the end fact's, so a tip the end judgement has not caught up with binds the
  node to the old one until it does; `pull-request`'s event form and WORKFLOW1j and 1k (FABRIC-PERFORM1l).
- **Proof** (fast, each seen failing before its behaviour): `ObservedEventTests` (12: begun, awaited and reached before its wire fires; a
  unit whose send failed `not-watched`; another unit's event refused with no record changed; a ref moved after publication, a new tip on
  an old binding and an unread tip each `stale`; older and tied events `late`, another commit's `stale`; an event before the watch
  refused and its replay after a resync reached; unknown then reached, a replay, a reused id, conflicting outcomes and restarts minting
  nothing; stop and skip in both orders; arming by observed work, without the lease, and disarmed; two observed nodes of one step; the
  record's `observations` read back, and absent before), `WorkSupervisionTests` (the last frame actually sent per generation). Git, once
  by class: `ObservedTipsGitTests` (a batch over two branches one `for-each-ref`, an outside move read at the next pass and its event
  `stale`; a linked worktree's identity its repository's).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-PERFORM1m, a branch unit's observed node bound to the fresh tip).** §C4's fourth rule, §E2, RUN1's bindings
and lapse, and PROC1d-ii's one read per judgement, on PERFORM1i. This builds PERFORM1i's *Not built here* "a branch unit's revision is the
end fact's": while the end judgement lagged a moved branch, an observed node stayed bound to the old tip and every event at the new one was
refused `stale`, so a plugin's events rarely landed.

- **The binding.** A branch unit's observed plugin node (`GovernedUnit.Observable`) is bound to, and lapses on, its branch's tip read in
  the pass (`WireRun.NodeRevisions`, by node id), never the unit's revision, which is the end fact's (`WorkInventory`). A moved branch
  lapses the old binding under RUN1's rules: invalidated, ready and begun again on the new tip in one evaluation. While the tip stands,
  nothing reached is reached again (the end fact catching up moves nothing); a rebinding says, sends and keeps nothing, and the frame
  told is unchanged.
- **When.** A unit whose observed node is active on a revision its branch's tip has moved off is judged at the pass, a wake as well as a
  look (`NodeRuntime.RebindsAsync`), so it rebinds before the new tip's event comes. An event heard in the very pass that rebinds is
  judged before it, on the old binding, and is `stale`, since the hearing comes first (PERFORM1i's order, kept).
- **One read.** `PassTips` reads each checkout once a pass, for whatever asks first (the hearing, a rebinding's check, a binding), with
  every governed observed branch there; it lives for the pass alone (LANDGIT1). A checkout is read only where an event is bound, a unit
  with an observed node is evaluated, or such a node awaits there. PERFORM1i's first pass, which read no git, now reads it once.
- **An unread tip** binds nothing new: a bound node keeps its binding's revision, so nothing lapses on a read that failed, and a node not
  bound yet is left ready and unpublished until a pass reads the tip.
- **Unchanged, and why.** A performed node, and every other node, binds on the unit's revision, which is the tip the plugins are told
  (`GovernedUnit.Watched`): a completion's outcome is applied as a lapsed attempt's where its revision is not that told tip
  (`WorkBelief.Lapsed`), so moving one without the other would lapse every attempt. A quest unit's
  revision is null by design; a built-in observed node reads its own records. So a performed node wired after an observed node reached on
  the fresh tip starts on the lagging revision, and runs again when the end fact catches up (a row).
- **Proof** (fast, each seen failing first): `ObservedEventTests` (a lagging unit rebound at the next pass, a wake, with nothing said or
  sent, then reached by the new tip's event, and the end fact catching up reaching nothing again; the old tip's event after the rebind
  `stale`, nothing written; an unread tip keeping a rebound node's binding and beginning no unbound node until read; one tip read a pass).
  Git, once by class: `ObservedTipsGitTests` (two checkouts, events and a rebind in one pass, one `for-each-ref` each; the next pass reads
  only the checkout with an event; the moved tip's event reached).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1u, a close never first sent from recovery on stale evidence).** The design's §E5 step 4, the second of the
four gaps the read-only review of the close path found (CLOSE1t's note). `QuestCloser.ReconcileAsync` sent every recorded or unknown close
again, with its key, before the fresh fence. So a crash after the attempt was written and before the close left, then a branch moved, then a
restart, sent a close never delivered on the revision it was recorded on, and the door ran it: the submissions had not moved, only the work.
The key's idempotency protects an outcome the door already keeps, not a key it never took.

- **The question.** `POST /api/quests/{id}/close/outcome` (a local host's, the driver's; the close's own body) is answered from what
  `quest_closes` keeps under the key, in the close door's words and shape, judging and writing no Done (`QuestStore.CloseOutcomeAsync`). A key
  taken answers its first outcome: the close, its refusal, or `key-reused` where the token or submissions differ. A key never taken is kept
  `Withdrawn` (code `withdrawn`, 409) in the same transaction, so that close never runs, and a send of it still on its way is answered the
  same refusal. The question is a write for that reason alone: a pure read would leave a late send free to run after the driver moved on.
- **Recovery asks, never sends.** Step 4 asks the door about every recorded or unknown close, each question one of the pass's sends,
  before anything new. A key it took is settled from the answer, as a send's answer was. A key it never took is settled `not-started`,
  code `withdrawn`, and a node still on it is invalidated (its attempt superseded, §D3's row), so the evaluation judges it again on what is
  there now. A close is made again only through the fresh fence, at this pass's tips and revision. An unanswered question leaves it unknown,
  asked again at the next pass.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *A new key, never the old one.* The question withdraws the key, so a later send of it is refused. A key kept would let a send in
     flight run on the old judgement after the new one was made. The new attempt's token can equal the old one's where nothing moved; its
     key cannot.
  2. *`resend` asks.* CLOSE1q's `resend` on a held close now asks the door what became of the key rather than sending the close. The
     answer is the same first outcome, and nothing can run. The words and doors are unchanged.
  3. *An unknown close is the person's too.* A close its door answers neither (the close nor the question) stays unknown and is never sent
     again by itself. While its node is still on it, it is listed with the held attempts (`HeldAttempts.Read`, `QuestClosing.Unanswered`)
     and takes `resend` (ask now) and `stop` (its work's live nodes stopped as the person's, the close still asked after and settled as a
     stale one's once answered), at the terminal's `work held` and the page's `WORK_HELD`. `record` is refused (`not-kept`), as on a held
     close with no answer kept. The page words it as a close with no answer kept (`work.milestone.held.close.why.unanswered`): no new
     words.
  4. *The override stays as it was.* While a close naming the quest's current submission is recorded or unknown, the person's own done is
     not read as the override (CLOSE1q), since the door may have written that Done. Once the question answers `withdrawn`, the attempt no
     longer speaks for the quest, and a done of the person's is theirs.
  5. *A host before the question* has no such door. Its answer is not the door's own, so the close stays unknown and the person may stop
     it. The driver and its host ship together, and the owner's install binds no quest.
- **Stored shapes, additively.** `QuestCloseRefusal.Withdrawn` is kept by name; a build before it reads the name as a refusal it does not
  know, answered as `key-reused`, so its close never runs there either. A withdrawn attempt is `settled` with `not-started`, both already
  read. A word on an unknown close is written as on a held one, cleared as it is applied.
- **Held by**, each seen red first:
  - `QuestCloseTests`: a close recorded and never delivered, the branch moved, a restart: the door asked, nothing sent, the old attempt
    settled `withdrawn`, the deploy sent again at the new tip, then one close under a new key on that revision; a close whose answer was
    lost, settled from the door's kept close or refusal after a restart and never sent again; a close whose door answers neither, offered
    with `resend|stop`, stopped by the person, then settled once answered; the held close's `resend` asking (the theory's counts) and the
    unanswered close after its graph was removed, both rewritten; an unarmed look asks nothing (green before and after).
  - `ServiceClientClosureTests`: the question's path and body, read as a close is, `withdrawn` a door's code, and a bare 404 unanswered.
  - `QuestClosureTests` (service): withdrawn, the late close refused and the quest left taken; a close and a refusal answered as kept;
    another call `key-reused`; a malformed question keeping no key.
  - `LocalHostTests`, `PersonDoorTableTests` (19 driver doors) and `SharedHostTests`: the route, its class, and none on a shared host.
- **Left:** the service's scope (CLOSE1v) and expiry and older writers (CLOSE1w).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-GRAPH1h, the protected order held across a skip).** The design's §E7 (the protected order), §E3 and §E2's skip
row, GRAPH1's choice 5 and WORKFLOW2's choice 10, for WORKFLOW2b's difference `skip-passes-earlier`: the person's skip of the look
satisfied its `after` wire while the opinion before it was unsettled, so the decoded landing went past an opinion schema 1 holds it on.

- **Settled in the decoder, not the evaluation** (choice, for review). `WorkflowGraphs.Graph` and its CLI twin wire each gate of D157
  §2.2's order before the landing into every gate after it, beside the step order: the work (its claim carries the holds, WORKFLOW2's
  choice 3), the opinion, the look and the landing. A readable version keeps the opinion, the look and the landing adjacent, so the new
  wires are the work's into each later gate past a go-ahead, the look and the landing, and the opinion's into the landing past the look:
  three at most, so the 96-wire bound stays far off. A go-ahead is no gate, and nothing after the landing gains a wire.
  - Why not an evaluation rule (a skip satisfies no `after` while an earlier protected node is unsettled): §E2's table is one for every
    graph, and the rule would make a skip's meaning depend on which nodes stand before it, a "protected" mark Current's and authored
    graphs would need and the design would have to add beside the table. The decoder already owns the mapping, and its version exists
    so that a changed mapping binds afresh.
  - The work joins the order because schema 1 holds the landing on it too: a look skipped while the work runs read as a difference
    (the landing offered, schema 1 at the work) until the work's wire held it.
- **`DecoderVersion` 2** (`DECODER_VERSION` in the twin): every decoded graph's digest moves, the graphs without a new wire included.
  The identity (`WorkflowNamed.Digest`) and every stored byte do not.
- **Why nothing stored moves:** no record binds a decoded graph's digest yet. Outside the decoder only the read-only projection
  (`WorkflowRunNodes`, WORKFLOW2b) reads it, and nothing in the desktop calls the projection; the runtime governs the rules' graphs and
  kept closures by `NodeGraphs.Digest` (`NodeRuntime.Govern`); a graph version (GRAPH1b) is not decoded and keeps its own digest; a
  run's binding keeps the version's identity. A guard on each twin (the driver's project, the CLI's sources) fails when another reader
  appears, as the cutover's first binding of a decoded digest will, which then owes a decoder change its stored bindings.
- **Schema 1 is unchanged:** `WorkflowRun` and the runtime are not touched. Differences 1 to 4 stand as WORKFLOW2b named them;
  `skip-passes-earlier` no longer arises at a gate, and keeps its name for a skip past any other step.
- **Proof** (fast; the comparison's three rows, the twins' table and wiring tests and the golden test seen failing first, 12 in the
  driver and 2 in the CLI): `WorkflowRunNodesTests` (*a look skipped while an agent reads the opinion* reads with no difference; a look
  skipped while the work runs, with and without the opinion, holds the landing as schema 1 does; and, golden, the derived digest moved
  off the one decoder 1 derived while the version's identity, read before the change, and the workflow's file and the binding keep
  their bytes and last write through stores that refuse every write), `WorkflowGraphsTests` and `workflowgraphs.test.ts` (the shared
  table at decoder 2 with its new row, row for row; each gate wired into every gate after it, once, a go-ahead none; nothing outside the
  projection reading the decoder).
- **Not built here:** an authored graph (GRAPH1b, GRAPHEDIT1) keeps GRAPH1's choice 5 by reachability, so a skip there can still pass
  a gate (a row).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (LANDHOLD2, a press says it waits behind a landing).** A door that waits for a tree's or a branch's hold says so before it waits and again when it stops (`LandingHoldNotice`, a callback the call hands it through an async local, never a parameter); the press maps it onto the existing step field as `waiting` and back to its own step (`PressSteps.WhileWaiting`). The review's Accept and the hand button read "Waiting its turn…" / "排队中…"; `trees land` and `trees hand` print one line. An uncontended door says nothing. Left: it does not name the landing it waits behind, since the hold's file is written only at the holder's end (LANDHOLD3).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-PERFORM1o, an event at the moved tip heard again once its node rebinds).** §C4's fourth rule and *what replays
after a drop*, on PERFORM1m. PERFORM1m kept PERFORM1i's order, the hearing before the rebinding, so an event at a branch's new tip that came
before Daoris rebound its node was refused `stale` and never asked for again: only a drop's or a restart's `resync` brought it back.

- **The shape: held for the pass, heard again after the rebinding** (`WorkLagging`, `NodeRuntime.HearAgainAsync`). An event whose commit is
  the tip read this pass, and whose node is still active on an older binding, is neither refused nor counted at the hearing. After the
  pass's evaluations it is heard again, the five rules and all: a node the evaluation rebound takes it, and its unit is evaluated once more
  so the wires it satisfies fire in the same pass. One whose node was not rebound (its unit past the pass's turn, `UnitsPerPass`) is refused
  `stale` and counted once, as before. Nothing is held past the pass.
- **Why this one, and the two rejected.**
  - *Rebinding before the hearing* puts an evaluation, which can start work, ahead of the person's word on a held attempt and the
    reconciliation (`Resolve`, `ReconcileAsync`, `Due`), against RUN1's order of asking after every attempt due before any new start; a
    rebinding pulled out of the evaluation would restate RUN1's lapse rules beside it. And the accepted event's wires would still wait for
    an evaluation after it, the reason PERFORM1i writes the acceptance first.
  - *A resync frame after a rebinding* leans on every plugin answering `resync: true` again for a step it already sent, costs a frame and a
    round trip (the event lands a pass later at best), asks the plugin for everything it knows of every awaited unit, and undoes PERFORM1m's
    "a rebinding says, sends and keeps nothing, and the frame told is unchanged". §C4's `resync` stays for drops and restarts.
  - Holding it for the pass changes no wire and no frame, starts no git, and keeps PERFORM1i's order and every rule as they were.
- **What holds.** The identity and the order are PERFORM1i's: the second hearing is the event's one judgement, so its copy in the same pass
  is a `replay`, and a later copy mints nothing. An event at an old tip is refused by the fourth rule before anything is held. The second
  hearing reads its tips through the pass's `PassTips`, so a pass still reads each checkout once. An unarmed install is untouched: the hold
  lives inside the armed pass.
- **Proof** (fast, the first seen failing first): `ObservedEventTests` (the moved tip's event heard before its node rebinds taken after the
  rebinding in the same pass, its wire fired, its copy a replay, the old tip's event `stale`, one tip read a pass, a later copy minting
  nothing; past the pass's turn, the held event refused `stale` once with nothing written; PERFORM1i's stale test loses its new-tip event to
  the first). Git, once by class: `ObservedTipsGitTests` (the moved tip's event heard in the rebinding pass reached there, the old tip's
  `stale`, one `for-each-ref`).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1v, the close door holds its own scope).** The design's §E5 step 2 (*scoped to the graph, node, unit, take
and submissions it names*), and the third of four gaps a read-only review of the whole close path found the same day: the door checked that
each quest it was named was bound, never that they were one close's. The driver's `QuestClosing.Judge` already names only its own chain's
quests here; the door no longer leans on that filter.

- **The scope, before any quest's state.** `QuestStore.CloseAsync` now takes the quest the close is at (the route's id, which the exchange
  already required among those named). After the key's replay and the unknown ids, each other quest named is judged, in the order named:
  - `OtherRepository` (`other-repository`): it asks another repository than the quest the close is at, by the service's own rule for one
    tree, as a chain's next step reads it (`NextStep`, blind to case);
  - `NotInChain` (`not-in-chain`): it is neither that quest nor one it grew from. `QuestStore.LineageAsync` walks the parents through every
    repository, as the driver's `QuestClosing.Lineage` walks the quests the service lists. A parent the store does not hold ends the walk;
  - `OtherGraph` (`other-graph`): it is bound to another graph than that quest's. An unbound quest is left to `NotBound`, which names the
    bind missing.

  The first that fails refuses the close on that quest, kept under its key as every refusal is (CLOSE1e), and nothing is written. Each has
  its own words, naming the quest and the one the close is at, and its words for a replay.
- **The plain bind reads the remote map.** `QuestExchange.BindAsync` without `atTake` now refuses a quest whose circle syncs through a remote,
  through the same `remotes` (`ConfiguredRemotes`) the take's bind reads, in the same words (`ThroughRemote`). Only tests and an older
  driver send it. An unknown id answers as before.
- **Codes are additive.** The three codes join `QuestCloseCodes` and the driver's `QuestCloseOutcomes.Codes`, marked as twins, so each
  settles a close as refused, sent or asked after (CLOSE1u's `close/outcome`, which answers a kept refusal by its code), and it is never
  sent again. An older driver names no such quest, since its filter is these checks; were it to, it would read the code as no answer of
  the door's, and its close would stay unknown, closing nothing. No stored shape changed: a kept refusal is its enum's name, and a build
  before this reads a name it does not know as `KeyReused`.
- **Choices made here.** The route is not part of a key's record (`Invocation`), so kept closes from before still replay. The same key at
  another route answers its first outcome, writing nothing. `Program.cs` is unchanged: a refusal other than malformed or unknown answers 409
  with its code.
- **Held by** (each seen red first, except the last, written after its code): `QuestClosureTests` (an unrelated bound, submitted, unheld
  quest; another repository's quest of the chain, at either end; a quest of the chain here bound to another graph; the valid chain, now one
  repository's and closed at its last quest, green before and after), `QuestTakeBindTests` (a plain bind with a remote, and without one),
  `ServiceClientClosureTests` (each new code settles as refused, sent or asked after), and `LocalHostTests` (`not-in-chain` on the wire, 409).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-WIRE1h, the look's gate and the landing record pass their nodes over).** The design's §E2 (the `active →
skipped` row) and §E4, for the third difference FABRIC-WORKFLOW2b named. Difference 4 above, `passed-by-rule`, is **settled and removed**
from `WorkflowRunDifferences`: its two recorded rows now read with no difference.
- **Two more kinds take `NodeAuthority.SkipBy`**, as WIRE1g's opinion did: the `look` (its source `review`, the review gate's record) and
  the `pull-request` (its source `landing`, the landing record). The person's skip of the look keeps its own row, words and source; a
  record's skip carries no words whatever the move brings, is the attempt's and run's own, and lapses as WIRE1g's does (another run: the
  skip scope; a new revision or a lost receipt: a lapse), the look's and the pull request's included.
- **What each record says.** `LookNode.Observe` reads the gate (`WireRun.Reviews`, by unit) and passes the look over only where the level's
  table asks no review for this work (`State` none with `Decision.Reviews` false: the chain's or the ask's choice turned it off); every
  other state, and a gate not handed in, leaves the look the person's to answer, and nothing here reaches, rejects or fails it.
  `BuiltInNodes.Observe`'s pull request is passed over where the branch unit's record says the work went into the line (`Held`) and no
  landing names a branch to open one on; with no landing and no hold it waits, as before.
- **Schema 1 unchanged.** `WorkflowRunNodes` reads a step's skip as the person's where they skipped the look, else as the kind's record's
  where its detail is the look's `off`, or the pull request's `nothing` or `no-branch` (the opinion's, whatever its gate says). The
  frontier of a look turned off is now the landing, and of a pull request passed over, none.
- **Left for a row.** `nothing landed` is a quest unit's fact (a session with no commits), and a pull request observes branch units, so
  the live observation passes over only the no-branch case; the run's reading covers both. Wiring `WireRun.Reviews` from `ReviewGate`'s
  read in the live evaluation (as `Opinions` is wired from `OpinionGate`) is the caller's and not built here.
- **Proof** (each seen failing first): `NodeLifecycleTests` (each kind's record skip by its own source alone, and the other's, the
  person's or none refused), `WireEvaluationDecodedTests` (the look turned off and the pull request with no branch passed over, lapsing
  outside their run, and every other gate state or landing leaving them pending), `WorkflowRunNodesTests` (the two rows agree, and the
  nothing-to-land pull request).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1w, a take-bind's expiry judged inside the write, and owned binds where no older build looks).** The
design's §E5 cutover, and the last of four gaps the read-only review of the close path found before arming. It amends FABRIC-CLOSE1o's
choice 2 and its paragraph on an older build over the migrated store.

- **The time.** CLOSE1o judged the expiry at the `now` its caller took. The HTTP respond door and `quest_respond` take it before the
  store's gate and `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, so a take queued behind another write past the expiry still bound. `QuestStore.MoveAsync` now reads
  the store's monotonic clock as it is called (`TimeProvider.GetTimestamp`, the system's unless `OpenAsync` names one). It judges the
  bind at the caller's moment plus the wait measured inside the write. A measured wait, rather than the wall clock read there, keeps the
  caller's moment as the reference, a test's included, and is blind to the wall clock being set. The Taken and the Bound keep the
  caller's moment, as every verb's operations do.
- **The boundary: choice (a), with a table for its mark rather than a number.**
  - *Not (b).* Detecting an older upsert by an owner that disagrees with its graph catches the collision. It leaves an older build's
    take reading the same row and binding its graph by the id alone, whoever takes and however late.
  - *Not a store version.* The quest store keeps none. A mark like the knowledge index's `user_version` (`NewerIndexException`) binds
    only the builds that read it, this one and later. The older builds this guards against are the ones already built, CLOSE1l to
    CLOSE1v, and none of them reads it.
  - *So the mark is the table.* This build names its binds in `quest_owned_take_binds`, every column required, which no older build
    knows. An older build's upsert into `quest_take_binds` cannot reach an owned bind, and its take's `DELETE … RETURNING graph` finds
    none. A row in `quest_take_binds` is now an older build's, whatever owner it names. This build's take consumes it as `Unowned`,
    saying nothing, and its open deletes every such row.
- **The owner's install** names no graph. Its store gains an empty table, its takes answer word for word as before, and an older
  build's own naming and take over it run as they did. Nothing is refused.
- **Left.** An older build's take of a quest this build named a graph for takes it unbound: it closes by its done, and the driver's
  end line says the take left it unbound (`ClosureBinding.UnboundAtEnd`). The owned row stays, as a deleted quest's does, until that
  id's next naming or take; no take reaches it while the quest is not open. An older build still binds, by the id alone, a graph its
  own driver named, as it always did, and no table here changes that. A CLOSE1o build sharing the store loses its pending namings at
  this build's open, and their quests close by their done.
- **Held by** `QuestTakeBindTests`, the first three seen red first: a take queued behind another write past the expiry, with the
  connection's gate held and a test clock moved; the owner's take after a pre-CLOSE1o naming of another graph; an older build's take
  statement over an owned bind; and the unarmed store's own take and an older build's statements over it, green before and after.
  CLOSE1o's tests pass as they were, their count of pending rows now reading both tables.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-NODE1f, an observed node's unknown never holds its next attempt).** Confirmed in the code and by test: an observed
node (`pushed`, `pull-request`, `line`, `claimed`) gone `unknown` kept `Unreconciled` through an invalidation and a later ready, and the start
loop skips any node with it set, so after a new commit it was never offered a start. `NodeLifecycle.Apply` now clears `Unreconciled` on the
move to `invalidated` for a node whose authority dispatches nothing (the unknown belonged to the old binding, and no effect was dispatched). A
performed or waited node's unknown attempt is unchanged: it may have taken effect, so only its reconciliation, or the person's word on it
(PERFORM1g), clears it. Proof: `NodeLifecycleTests` (an observed unknown cleared by an invalidation and offered a start on the new binding,
for each built-in; a performed unknown held across one; an unarmed node unchanged), the first seen failing first.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c, the person's reopen of a sealed submission).** The design's §E5 (*The person's reopen*) and canary 55,
the service's half, and the first of three changes before the close is armed (then FABRIC-WORKFLOW2d and CLOSE1h). It amends CLOSE1a's
choice 3: the replay still keeps no seal, but a step now names the submission it grew from, and the seal is read from that.

- **Two kinds** (`quest-operations.md`'s eleven places each, the census first). `reopened` names a submission by its machine and sequence,
  with the person's words as its note. On the taken quest whose current submission it names, it leaves the quest taken with none current:
  its answers, yes and verdict go with it, so the next `ready` is judged from the start. On the open or taken step that submission
  published, it sets `sourceReopened`, the hold `source-reopened`. `rebound` names the source's submission a withdrawn or held step grows
  from now, and lets the hold go. Neither moves a status, and a rebase forgets either once it no longer applies.
- **The step's source.** A step a submission published carries `grewFrom` (machine, sequence) in its publication: in the log, on the wire,
  in the cache and in the answer, written only when set. `publishedBy` stays the publishing session. A step from before names none, and
  any reopen of its parent's submission reaches it, so it is never left standing on work reopened.
- **The seal** (`QuestStore.SealingAsync`): the chain's next step, standing on the current submission (its `grewFrom` that one, or none),
  with no reopen since. Both the `ready`'s seal and the close door's `unsealed` read it, so a withdrawn step seals nothing.
- **The reopen** (`POST /api/quests/{id}/reopen`, a `Person` door on a local host, `submission` and `note`). One transaction on the machine
  holding the take, judged inside the write: taken, this machine's claim held or unconfirmed, the named submission current, its step
  published from it and not closed. Then `reopened` on both. A close first leaves the quest Done, and the reopen is refused; a reopen first
  leaves the close naming a submission that is current no more, refused `stale` and kept under its key, and CLOSE1u's question answers it
  so, or withdraws a key never sent.
- **The step.** Withdrawn (open): no take applies to it, here or at a remote (`QuestLog.Applies`), so a take made elsewhere before the
  reopen reached it loses everywhere, its claim read lost. Held (taken): its agent's done and `ready` are refused with the reason
  (`source-reopened`, 409 at respond, a stand-down), its submission publishes nothing further, and the close door refuses it `held`.
  `quest_list` says either in words a session acts on.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *Withdrawn is a hold on an open step, not a decline.* §E5 says *as a decline that applies while it is open*. A decline is final
     (nothing moves back to open, D46 §3), so the next submission's step would be either a second quest under another id or never
     published. The hold keeps one id and one history: the next unheld submission rebinds the same step (`PublishNextAsync`), and a
     take that raced the reopen converges by the replay's own rule, to the hold if the take reached the remote first, to the
     withdrawal if the reopen did.
  2. *A started step* (the parent's default, the owner may revise): held with its reason, and the person chooses. `POST
     /api/quests/{id}/continue` (a `Person` door) rebinds it to the source's current submission once that is unheld, and is refused
     before; abandoning it is their decline, at the decline's door. Daoris never chooses. An unheld submission does not move a started
     step by itself, so it seals nothing until the person's word, and the source's close waits.
  3. *Only the take's machine reopens*, as only it submits and judges the seal. Another machine's reopen is refused.
  4. *A step closed on the submission refuses the reopen*: a reopen takes no close back, and a push already made is never retracted. The
     person closes the source themselves, or asks anew.
  5. *An abandoned step stays declined*, so a later submission of the source publishes no second step and is never sealed: the close
     door refuses it `unsealed`, and the source closes by the person's own done.
- **Compatibility.** An older remote refuses a push holding either kind, and an older machine a page holding one, as for every new kind
  (D144 on D68); an older build reads a publication's `grewFrom` as absent. Nothing binds on the owner's install, so no submission is made,
  no step carries `grewFrom` and no door shows; its quests are read and written as before.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `QuestReopenTests` (7: an untaken step withdrawn, refused a take, and rebound by the next unheld
  submission with no second quest and no seal kept; a started step held, its done refused, the word to go on refused until the source is
  unheld, then rebound; an abandoned step; the refusals; a reopen before a close and a close before a reopen, with the outcome door; the
  replay's rule), `QuestReopenSyncTests` (2: a concurrent take of the step, each order, converging on both machines and the remote, the old
  submission's close refused), and `QuestOperationKindsTests` (both kinds' samples, a withdrawn state, and `grewFrom` on the publication).
  `LocalHostTests`, `PersonDoorTableTests` and `SharedHostTests` hold the doors, written after their code.
- **Left** (rows): the driver's half (a withdrawn step started by no intake, a held step's running session dispatching nothing until the
  person chooses, its branch brought up to date on going on, and CLOSE1q's held-close words across a reopen and a restart with the next
  submission at the same commit); the page's and the terminal's reopen, go-on and abandon, in English and 中文, after CLOSE1d's *Submitted*.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-WORKFLOW2d, a run's completion at a sealed submission).** The design's §E5 (*D157's `work` node counts a chain
quest finished when its submission is unheld and sealed*), the second of three changes before the close is armed (after CLOSE1c, before
CLOSE1h). A run's work no longer waits on a Done that only the graph's own close writes, so a closure graph's landing is reached before it.

- **Finished** (`WorkflowRuns`, `Finished`): closed Done and unheld, as before; or bound to a closure graph, taken, its current submission
  unheld and sealed. Sealed is the service's own reading (`SealingAsync`, CLOSE1c): the chain's next step published and standing on that
  submission, its `grewFrom` that one or none (a step from before), with no reopen since. The chain's last has nothing to publish, so its
  unheld submission is enough, as the close door judges it. The next step is read in the whole chain (`WorkflowRunFacts.Chain`, handed by
  the reader), so one another repository holds seals a submission here too.
- **The client** reads `grewFrom` and `sourceReopened` onto `QuestView`, additively: absent from a host before them, none where named in half.
- **Said** (the terminal and the page, English and 中文, one table in `workflow-run-said.json`): the work finished at a submission reads
  *done*, `submitted` (*The agent finished: 2 quests here are submitted, and close at the workflow's end.*), at the newest submission's
  moment; once the close writes every Done it reads `finished` as before. A reopen takes the finish back: the quest, taken with no
  submission and its step standing reopened, reads *waiting on an agent*, `reopened` (*You reopened quest #q1's submission: it waits for its
  agent to make it ready again.*), with the quest's door. The head says *reopened, waits for its agent*, never the state's *waits on another
  repository*.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *A held submission reads as a held done* (`held`, with its hold's code): in §E5 the holds hang on a submission unchanged otherwise. A
     step held because its source was reopened is not, since those words do not say it: it reads as before.
  2. *The reopen is said where nobody is at it*: in place of `unclosed` or `queued`. A session at work on the quest, one failed or one
     stopped is said as it was, and so is a source made ready again while its started step waits on the person's word to go on (CLOSE1c's
     choice 2): its words are the page's and the terminal's go-on, a row of their own.
  3. *zh says 交付 for a submission*, since the glossary's 提交 is a commit. CLOSE1d's *Submitted* names the term; this follows it then.
- **WORKFLOW2b's comparison** holds with no new difference: the projection reads step states only, so a closure chain's work reached at
  its sealed submissions offers the landing exactly where `At` stands, and a reopened one leaves the work pending, both rows agreeing.
- **Unchanged:** a quest no closure graph holds finishes only by its Done, whatever a host answers of a submission or a step's source, and
  is said as before. Nothing binds on the owner's install, so its schema-1 runs read as they did.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `WorkflowRunTests` (the proof: a two-step chain at its landing before Done, then both Done at once with
  the landing's reading unchanged; a reopen of an open and of a taken step taking it back; a step from before sealing; a step in another
  repository sealing through the chain and not without it; five table rows: submitted, passed on, held, not sealed and reopened; the unbound
  run read exactly as with no submission, written as a guard and green before the change), `WorkflowRunNodesTests` (two recorded runs),
  `ServiceClientClosureTests` (the two fields read, absent and half), `WorkflowRunSaidTwinTests` and the page's `runSaid.test.ts` (three
  rows of the shared table, and the head in both languages).
- **Left** (rows): the landing itself on an unheld submission (CLOSE1h): this reads the landing reached, and the automatic landing still
  starts at a Done (`AutoLander`).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h, the landing and its gates on an unheld submission).** The design's §E5 (*the landing and its gates wait
on an **unheld submission**, where today they wait on an unheld Done, with every hold, opinion and look still applying*) and §E10 row 24b,
the last of three changes before the close is armed (after CLOSE1c and WORKFLOW2d). D145's branch rule, the person's standing authorization
and the rule's plugin are unchanged.

- **One reading** (`Quests/Domain/QuestResults.cs`). A quest bound to a closure graph, taken, with a current submission, reads as the done it
  stands for (`Status` answers `Done`, `Judged` is true), held or not; its hold is the service's own on the submission (CLOSE1a), read where a
  done's is. Every other quest reads as it stands.
- **Enrolled and kept due.** The three conclusions that make a session due (a first run's, a carried-on run's, the sweep's for an orphan) hand
  that status on, so a session ending at the agent's `ready` is due as one ending at a done. `AutoLander`'s done check reads `Judged`, so the
  submission stays due. A reopen takes the submission back and closes the entry `undone`, as a done turned down does; the agent's next
  `ready` makes the session due again (`AutoLandings.Due` reopens an entry). An opinion owed stays owed the same way (`OpinionLook.ClosesAsync`).
- **The gates read a submission as its done.** `ReviewGate.Decide`: a chain step at an unheld submission published its steps in that
  transaction, so a set-up step still in its `then` decides nothing; a held one has published nothing, as a held done. `ReviewGate.JudgeAsync`:
  a set-up step at its submission, with what it showed posted, is judged as a done one, never *being set up* for good. `OpinionGate.LaterStep`:
  a later step at its submission has run, and its `then` is published.
- **Judged again under the tree's hold** (the pre-mortem's first trap). LANDRACE1's wait behind another landing of the tree is bounded only at
  minutes, so `TreeLands.LandAsync` takes a door's judgement (`judged`) and asks it once it holds the tree, after LANDRACE1's `already` and
  before anything is made. The automatic landing's (`AutoLander.UnderHoldAsync`) judges the entry again from a fresh world: the quest, its
  submission's identity (`QuestResults.Same`, by machine and sequence), its holds, the switch, the opinion and the look. A submission is judged
  there every time; a plain done only where the hold waited, so a landing that waited for nothing reads nothing more and LANDGIT1's counts
  stay. A refusal there makes no branch, no advance and no push, and keeps what its judgement keeps (`held`, `undone`, …). A later `ready`
  keeps nothing, so the next look judges the new submission from the start. Every other door passes no judgement, as before.
- **The commitment point.** The parent's default (the owner may revise): a hold or reopen stops a landing up to the moment its push begins;
  after that the landing completes and the hold applies to what follows; a push already made is never retracted. Built here, the landing
  commits at its judgement under the tree's hold: a hold before it stops the landing whole, and one after it finds the branch made and pushed
  once, applying to what follows (the close waits on it; nothing is taken back). Between that judgement and the push lie only the branch made
  and recorded on this machine and LANDRACE1's branch hold, which waits only behind another push of the same branch. Stopping there, with the
  branch already made, needs a code, words and a way on after the release of its own: a row.
- **A landing never implies completion.** It writes no quest state (D46): the quest stays taken at its submission and its close is the graph's
  (CLOSE1b). A failed push leaves the branch, its record and the hand-off (`trees hand`); the entry closes `plugin-failed`, so a restart's look
  lands nothing twice and the record stays.
- **The owner's install.** Nothing binds there, so no quest has a submission: every reading is the quest's own status, the conclusions enrol
  the dones they did, the gates judge as before, and the judgement under the hold runs only behind a wait. No file is written, no read added.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `QuestResultsTests` (the table, and the identity), `ReviewGateTests` (a set-up step at its submission; a
  submission's `then`, published or held), `OpinionGateTests` (a later step at its submission; its `then` published), and `AutoLandingTests`
  (Process, Git, 10 cases: the proof, an unheld submission landing its branch and a held one not; each of three holds released landing with no
  Done; a done's hold that came while the landing waited for its tree, and a submission's hold, reopen and later `ready`, each stopping it with
  nothing made, the last landing at the next look; a hold after the judgement, the branch pushed once; a failed push leaving the quest taken
  and a restart landing nothing twice).
- **Left** (rows): a judgement just before the push; the press, an entry's Accept and a hand-off judged again under their holds; an opinion's
  and a look's refusal of a submission over real git (`OpinionLandingTests`, `ReviewLandingTests`; here the gates' fast tables); the three
  conclusion call sites, which no test drives (each hands on `QuestResults.Status` of the quest it read after the run).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h2, a hold between a landing's branch and its push).** CLOSE1h's *commitment point* left one stretch
unjudged: the branch made and recorded, then LANDRACE1's branch hold, then the plugin. The parent's default stands (a hold or reopen stops
a landing up to the moment its push begins), and is now built for the automatic landing. The press, an entry's Accept and a hand-off keep
theirs (CLOSE1h3).

- **Judged again just before the plugin is told.** `TreeLands.LandAsync` takes a second judgement from its door (`beforePush`), asked once
  the landing holds its branch, after LANDRACE1's check for a push of the same commit and before the `work/land` frame. The automatic
  landing's (`AutoLander.BeforePushAsync`) reads the quest afresh: taken back (not `QuestResults.Judged`), submitted again (not
  `QuestResults.Same`), or held as the look reads a hold (the review's own `unreviewed` aside). A submission is read every time; a plain
  done only where the push waited behind another push of its branch. A landing that waited for nothing reads nothing more, so LANDGIT1's
  counts stand.
- **Held there.** The branch stays made and recorded, no plugin is spoken to, and nothing is kept as pushed. The landing says why, and how
  it goes on (`TreeLanding.Withheld`), and keeps that on the tree's hold as a push not tried (`LandingAttempt.Unready`), so a press waiting
  behind it is told. Its code is `push-held` (`AutoLandingCode.PushHeld`): the entry stays open and is read at every look. The conversation
  says it with its own note code (`landing.push-held`: branch, plugin), and the trace with `work.trace.try.push-held`, in English and Chinese.
- **The way on after the release is the look's hand-off.** A `push-held` entry's quest is read at each look as a hold is read. While it is
  held, taken back or not yet submitted again, the entry waits; it was said once already.
  - Once the quest stands, a tree still at the commit the landing judged (the try's tip) is handed on through the hand-off's own door
    (`TreeHandOffs.HandAsync`, from the repository the tree is a checkout of). The hand-off takes the branch's hold, reads the record under
    it and pushes once. The try is kept as `landed`, `plugin-failed`, or a refusal where no plugin was spoken to. Nothing is made again.
  - A tree whose work went on past that commit (a reopen, more work, a later `ready`) is landed as any later done of the session is. The
    made branch moves on (LAND2c), and its plugin is told once, at the tree's tip. A hand-off there would push the taken-back work alone.
  - A record already pushed at its tip (the person's `trees hand` came first), or gone with its branch, closes the entry `already`. The
    person's `trees hand <session>` stays open throughout, and the landing's sentence names it.
- **The owner's install.** No quest has a submission there, and the judgement at the push runs only for a submission or behind a wait: no
  read is added and nothing is written.
- **Held by**, each seen red first:
  - `AutoLandingRulesTests`: the code, its closing and its retry, and its note code.
  - `AutoLandingTests` (Process, Git): a hold that comes while the push waits for its branch, on a plain done and on a submission. The
    branch is made, no frame is told, a look while held says nothing, one push follows the release, and none after it.
  - `AutoLandingTests`: a reopen there, then more work and a later submission, moving the made branch with one push at the tree's tip.
- **Left**: a `push-held` advance of a chain's branch (LAND2c) is handed on by the same path, untested here.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c2, the driver's half of a reopen).** The design's §E5 (*The person's reopen*) and canary 55, after
FABRIC-CLOSE1c's service half and its choice 2 (a started step held, the person choosing). The driver reads the service's fact,
`sourceReopened` on a live step (`QuestView.HeldByReopen`; WORKFLOW2d's client already read both fields), and chooses nothing.

- **Withdrawn and held** (`Planner`, the verdict `SourceReopened`): an open step is started by no look, and a taken one is carried on,
  resumed or gone on with the person's words by none, its sentence naming the step it grew from and both choices. Before every other
  reason, a pause and the person's stop included, since neither Resume nor Try again moves it; it spends no slot and no turn. Only
  what the planner would plan is said, so a take this machine never ran, and one whose session still works, gets no verdict. A look
  asks no claim of a held step (`CarryOnClaimsAsync`).
- **The running session** is told at its next step by the service (its done and `ready` refused, *Stop here*). Its end, with its
  quest still taken and held, is read as `completed` with `ended.source-reopened` naming the step before (`Observation.Conclude`'s
  `reopenedFrom`), in English and 中文 on the page. Not a park, whose answer would go on with the person's words, and not a
  stand-down, which the ledger reads as someone else's quest: a good ending, as a wait is. A messy exit and a failed turn are read
  as before.
- **On the go-on** (the service's `continue`, which rebinds the step and lets the hold go), that session is carried on in its tree
  (`Planner.CarriesOn`, `StoppedByReopen` by the note's code), its claim asked as any carry-on's, and the start's LANDSYNC2 sync
  brings the tree up to date with the line. Its reason says the person went on with it on the new work, and the session is handed the
  ended record's note (`CutOff`), which says why the one before stopped.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *Held before a pause.* The pause's sentence promises that Resume starts or carries the quest on, which is untrue while the
     reopen holds it.
  2. *A completed ending, not a park.* A park holds a slot and offers the person an answer box; the step's two choices are the
     service's doors, so the record ends and the quest holds.
  3. *The verdict keeps the driver's words on the page*, as `Waiting` and `WaitsForOpinion` do: its words in 中文 belong with the
     page's go-on and abandon (CLOSE1c's row).
- **Unchanged:** a quest no reopen names is planned and concluded as before (`PlannerTests`' guard, green before the change). The
  owner's install binds no quest, so no step carries a source and nothing here moves there.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `PlannerTests` (a withdrawn open step starting nothing and spending no slot; a held taken step
  over five last sessions, three configurations and a pause; a held step not planned here saying nothing; the go-on carrying the
  stopped session on, its take judged; a look through `StandInLedger` reading both holds off the wire, asking no claim while held,
  and moving both once rebound; the guard), `ObservationTests` (the held end over a take, a resume and a found take; a messy exit, a
  failed turn, a close and a plain park as before), and the page's `note.test.ts` (the code's twin).
- **Not built here** (rows): Daoris bringing the branch up to date with the source's new submission itself, which needs the
  submission's commit on the wire; CLOSE1q's held-close words across a reopen and a restart in `QuestCloseTests`.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1q2, the held close's words across a reopen and a restart).** The design's §E5 (*The person's reopen*),
CLOSE1q's recoveries, CLOSE1u's question and CLOSE1c's reopen (a submission is its machine and sequence). The case: a close for S1 is left
unanswered when the person reopens S1, S2 is made ready at S1's commit, and the person's word on S1's close is applied by a loop started
again. The door took the close after the reopen, so its question answers `stale`.

- **Found** (red first). `record` and `resend` were right. `record` on an unknown close is refused at the door (`not-kept`). `resend` asks
  after S1's key alone, and the door's `stale` settles S1's attempt: a node already moved off it is left, and one still on it fails on S1's
  binding and lapses with S1's revision. `stop` was wrong. `Recover` stopped every live node of the unit, so a word applied once S2's deploy
  was sent stopped that deploy. A word applied before the loop read S2 stopped the close node, and a stop is the person's word for the work,
  not for a revision (`NodeStates.Bound`), so S2's close never ran. A wake (a pass that is no whole look) judged a word on the quests of the
  read before it, so `record` on a held close for S1 reached the close node with S1's key while the quest stood on S2.
- **Built.** `QuestCloser.Resolve` takes the quests the pass read. `QuestClosing.Moved` names the first quest a close names that stands on
  another submission or on none. There, `record` and `stop` are taken back with `submission-moved` (`WorkPerformer.SubmissionMoved`), and
  the pass's lines name the quest and the submission. A held attempt keeps the code as its why, and `resend` asks as before. A pass with a
  close's word waiting reads the quests afresh (`QuestCloser.Worded`), a wake included.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *`stop` stops nothing once its submission moved.* The row's first reading was to stop the nodes bound to S1's binding or revision.
     Those lapse with S1's revision anyway, and a stop of the close node holds across revisions, so it would be S2's stop. While the
     submission stands, `stop` stops every live node, as before.
  2. *Refused at the pass, not at the door.* `WORK_HELD` and `work held` read the node records alone and hold no quest. An unknown close
     whose node moved off is no longer listed, so its refusal is said in the pass's lines. A held one keeps `submission-moved` as its why:
     the terminal's `WhySaid`, and the page's `work.milestone.held.close.why.submissionMoved` in English and 中文 (交付 for a submission).
  3. *A quest the read does not hold is not judged*, and the word applies as before.
  4. *S1's held attempt still gates its node* (§D3). S2's close is made once the person's `resend` settles it, as the refusal says.
- **Stored shapes, additively.** `submission-moved` is a new value of an attempt's `code`. A build before it reads that as a table's code
  on a held close, said with the code, and its page words it so (`why.closed` with the code).
- **Held by**, each seen red first:
  - `QuestCloseTests`' theory (six rows): the three words, each given before the loop read S2 or while it asked the door with S2's deploy
    sent. Both `stop` rows were red, with S2's deploy stopped and S2's close node stopped; the other four were green before the change.
  - `QuestCloseTests`' wake: `record` on a held close for a replaced submission. Red without the fresh read: S1's key reached the node.
  - `WorkMilestone.test.tsx`: the why in both languages, written with its code.
- **Unchanged:** a close whose submission stands takes its words as before (CLOSE1q's theory, CLOSE1u's stop). The owner's install binds no
  quest, so no close is made there and no word is offered.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-GRAPH1g and GRAPH1i, an authored graph's saves: one plugin step observed once, the protected order wired).** The
design's §E3 (refused when saved), §E7 (the protected order) and §C4 rule 2, on PERFORM1i's `not-named` and GRAPH1h's decoder wires. Two new
codes in `GraphRefusal`, both the driver's alone (`NodeGraphs.Problems`); the CLI holds no `Problems` (GRAPH1c is open), so the shared table
`workflow-graph-versions.json` gains a `judged: "driver"` row for each, which the CLI reads as a shape and the driver as its words.

- **`observed-twice`** (GRAPH1g): two nodes of one plugin kind (`<plugin>/<step>`) both observed, in one graph. An event names the one such
  node, so the runtime reached neither and said so only as a counted `not-named`. The second is named with the first. A built-in kind twice
  (two `line` nodes) is no part of it: the runtime's built-in reach is by its own record, not by a named step.
  - **Not asked of a decoded graph** (`NodeGraphs.Problems(graph, saved: false)`, which `WorkflowGraphs.DecodeSteps` passes): a D157
    version's two steps of one plugin kind are valid schema 1 and a stored version never becomes unreadable by a later rule. The decoded graph
    is run by no one yet; the cutover that binds a run to one owes it (a row).
  - `not-named` stays in the runtime as the defence for what a save did not see; PERFORM1i's test for it now proves the graph is refused at
    its reading and that no event reaches either node.
- **`protected-wire`** (GRAPH1i), **settled: refuse at save, never add wires on read** (choice, for review). Each opinion is wired straight into
  every look and landing, each look into every landing: a wire of the order's kinds (`after`, `trigger`, `data`) in no `anyOf` group. The
  work is no gate here (its claim is never skipped, and the existing check never asked it). Said only where reachability holds (a graph the
  existing `protected-order` refuses says that and no more), naming the earlier gate and the later one, so each fault is one problem.
  - Why not add the wires on read, as the decoder does: the decoder wires a stored schema 1 version, which names no wires, so its wires
    are the mapping's own and its `DecoderVersion` binds a change. An authored graph is the person's own wires, and its digest is of its own
    saved text (`NodeGraphs.Digest`): a wire added on read would make the graph run differ from the graph saved and shown. Refusing never
    changes a graph a person saved.
  - **Saved graphs this breaks: none known.** The editor (GRAPHEDIT1) is not built and the CLI refuses to save a graph (GRAPH1f), so the
    driver's saves are the only way in, and no fixture or home holds an authored graph with a look or a landing but the tests'. A graph that
    lacks the wires reads as every refused graph does: unreadable whole (its version's problem naming the missing wire), its neighbours kept.
- **Proof** (fast; seen failing first, four): `NodeGraphsTests` (each code alone with its words, three `protected-wire` cases and one
  `observed-twice`; a graph wiring each gate into each later one holds, as do observed nodes of different steps, plugins and kinds; the
  join tests now ask the look's wire too), `WireEvaluationTests` (a skip of the look passes the landing in a chain and holds it where the
  opinion is wired into it), `ObservedEventTests` (the twice graph refused, no `not-named`), the shared table's two rows read by the CLI's
  `graphversions.test.ts` and the driver's `WorkflowGraphVersionsTests`.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1d and CLOSE1c3, *Submitted* and the person's reopen on the page and at the terminal).** The design's §E5
(*the asker sees submitted, then the current gate or hold, then done*; *the person's reopen*), after CLOSE1c's service doors, CLOSE1c2's
driver half and WORKFLOW2d's run words. No service change.

- **The driver's reader was already whole.** CLOSE1b reads `closure` and `submission` onto `QuestView` and names the submission on every
  evidence verdict of a submitted quest (`EvidenceCheck`); WORKFLOW2d reads `grewFrom` and `sourceReopened`. The evidence door is the only
  verdict that takes a submission: the yes and the review's verdict take none at the service. CLOSE1d built nothing there.
- **The page reads *Submitted*** (`records.ts`'s `shownStatus`): a taken quest with a current submission says *submitted* (zh 已交付) on its
  pill in its page and its row, its facts line ends *submitted 2h ago*, and *Submitted* stands among its facts with its moment. Its hold reads
  as a done's did (*awaits your yes*, *awaits review*), so the sequence is the pill, the hold, then *done*. The glossary gains *submission*
  (交付), since 提交 is its commit, and `names:check --strict` holds every English *submit* to it.
- **The reopen** (`QuestPage`'s *Reopen…* under *Submitted*, and `daoris-driver quest reopen <id> [--note "…"]`): offered only on an unheld
  submission a live step stands on (`standingStep`: the step's `grewFrom` that submission or none, and no reopen on it), which the door
  judges again in its write. It asks once, naming the step it holds, and sends the submission the page showed; the terminal sends the one
  the quest answers just before, so a later `ready` is refused, never reopened in its place.
- **A step its source's reopen holds** (`hold: source-reopened`) has its own pill (*source reopened*, 来源已重新打开) and section: withdrawn
  while open (no *Take*), and taken, waiting for the source to be made ready again, then *Go on with it* (`continue`; `quest go-on <id>`)
  beside *Abandon…* (the driver's abandon where it offers one, the decline otherwise). The yes is never offered for it.
- **Found on the way:** a held live quest (a held submission, a reopened step) was listed twice in Quests, under *Waiting on you* and its
  status's group, since only a done was held before CLOSE1a. It is now listed once, where it waits on the person. No install showed it.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *The person-only doors go as every other does* (D156): `asThePerson` on the page; the terminal posts as `quest done` and `quest accept`
     do. The terminal's confirmation in the window is RPC1c's, for every person door at once, and is not built here.
  2. *Ask Daoris answers both hooks as the quest's own acts* (`Own("quests", …)`), a go to the quest's page, never a card: an agent's call
     must never reach either.
- **Unchanged:** a quest no closure graph holds reads, groups and presses as before (`QuestPage.closure.test.tsx`'s guard, green before the
  change). The owner's install binds no quest, so nothing there reads *submitted* and no door shows.
- **Held by**, each seen red first unless said: `records.test.ts` (shown, the standing step, the source ready, a held live quest listed
  once), `QuestPage.closure.test.tsx` (a submitted held quest in en and zh, the reopen, a held step's words, presses and refusal of the yes,
  a withdrawn step, zh), `QuestList.test.tsx`, `ServiceClientClosureTests` (the two doors), `QuestReopenCommandTests` (the words, the doors,
  a quest with no submission, an older host, the usage), `DriverCommandTests` (the host's routing) and `HelpCoverageTests` (both hooks).
  Written after their code: `QuestsView.closure.test.tsx` (the view's wiring) and `VerbLeasesTests`' two rows.
- **Left** (rows): the run's words for a source made ready again whose started step waits on the person's go-on (WORKFLOW2d's choice 2); the
  chain strip, the map and an ask's work reading *Submitted* (they read the status).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c5, a held step's branch brought up to date on the go-on).** The design's §E5 (*its branch brought up to
date*, with CLOSE1c's amendment), after CLOSE1c2, whose carry-on synced the step's tree with the line alone.

- **The commit, on the wire.** A submission's commit is known where its evidence was read for it (FABRIC-CLOSE1e): `Quest.SubmissionCommit`
  reads the verdict naming the current submission, and the wire's `submission` answers it as `commit`, added after the rest. Absent before the
  read, after a later `ready`, where no requirement names evidence, and from a host before it; nothing stored or sent between machines
  changes. The driver's client reads it onto `QuestSubmissionView.Commit` only where it is a commit's full id.
- **On the go-on** (a carry-on of a session `StoppedByReopen`, its step naming `grewFrom`), before the session starts, the source is read
  afresh and its commit taken where its current submission is the one the step now grows from (`Planner.SourceCommit`). The step's tree is
  then brought up to date with it (`TreeUpdates.GoOnInSyncAsync`), and with the line after, as CLOSE1c2's start does.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *A replay, not a merge*, as the tree sync's (WSR6, D88): the step's own commits, from where its record says it grew (else where it
     leaves the commit), rebased onto the commit in its tree detached, proven by WSR6's content proof, the branch moved last from the commit
     judged, and its record then grown from the commit, so the next sync and the landing cut there. The session is told its commits have new ids.
  2. *A conflict, or any reason the tree is left* (uncommitted work, the trees held, a proof refused), leaves it exactly as it was and changes
     nothing else: the line's sync is not asked either. The session is told what conflicted and that the person must resolve it, and to build
     nothing on the new work until then. No new code: the words are the instruction's, as LANDSYNC2's are.
  3. *No commit, or one this repository does not hold* (a source in another repository, or not fetched here), syncs the line alone and says
     so once, in the instruction. Daoris fetches nothing for it.
- **Unchanged:** every other start, and a carry-on not stopped by a reopen, syncs as before. The owner's install binds no quest, so no
  submission is made and no step goes on there.
- **Held by**, each seen red first but the Git class (run once, green): `QuestClosureTests` (the commit read for its own submission alone),
  `ServiceClientClosureTests` (read, absent from a host before it, none where no commit's id), `ReopenGoOnTests` (the commit taken only
  for the submission the step grew from), `GoOnInSyncTests` (Git: the carry-on's tree holding the new submission, its own commit replayed
  and its record cut there; a tree holding it already left; a conflict leaving tree, branch and record as they were with the line's sync not
  asked; no commit and a commit not held, each said, the line synced). `LocalHostTests` holds the wire, written after its code.
- **Left** (rows): a submission whose requirements name no evidence has no commit, so its step is synced with the line alone; Daoris
  recording the tree's commit at every `ready` would close that.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h6, a session due again after a closed try is tried again).** CLOSE1h's *Enrolled and kept due* says a
reopen closes the entry `undone` and the agent's next `ready` makes the session due again. `AutoLandings.Due` did reopen the entry with its
tries kept, but D145 point 2's look never tried it: `AutoLandingRules.ShouldTry` read only a hold, a review, an opinion, `push-held` or an
on-change refusal again, never a try that closed. A done after words went on past a landing (D137) was the same, and that is the ordinary
automatic landing, with no closure graph: its later commits waited for the person's press, where LAND2c's advance was the stated way.

- **Judged afresh.** `AutoLanding.Reopened`: open, with a last try whose code closes (`AutoLandingCode.Closes`). Only `Due` opens a closed
  entry, so that try was the earlier due's. No moment is compared and no field is stored: the due list's file keeps its shape. `ShouldTry`
  answers yes for one, and `LandingWaits.Of` reads it as `first`, so the first wake after the conclusion looks at it (FABRIC-END7). The new
  try is appended, and the earlier ones stay as history in the file and the trace.
- **An entry still due on its own last try keeps its rule.** A hold, a review, an opinion or `push-held` is read at every look, and an
  on-change refusal waits for its tree to move, whether or not its session concluded again meanwhile.
- **Nothing lands twice.** The fresh judgement is the look's whole: the switch, a press since (`already`), the tree, the records, the quest,
  its holds and the gate, then D88's proof before the landing. A tree with nothing past its landing closes `already` and tells no plugin;
  work that went on moves the made branch on (LAND2c), and its plugin is told once, at the tree's tip.
- **The owner's install.** No closure graph there, but D137's case is the automatic landing itself. Under a rule with *Accept automatically*
  on, a session that landed, was carried on with more words and concluded done again stayed open on the due list, never tried; its later
  commits waited for the person's Accept. Now the next look moves its branch on and the rule's plugin pushes once. An entry already stuck
  that way on a home is judged at the first look after the update: work that went on lands as an advance, under the rule's standing say-so,
  and one with nothing new closes as its look finds it (`already`, `gone`, …). With no rule accepting automatically nothing changes.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `AutoLandingRulesTests` (each closing code, reopened, is tried and waits as a first; an entry still due
  on a hold, a push held back or a refusal keeps its rule when due again), `LandingWakesTests` (a reopened entry concerns every wake), and
  `AutoLandingTests` (Process, Git: a submission reopened, closed `undone`, readied again with more work and landed at the next look with
  both commits; a done after words went on past its landing moving the made branch on with one push, then concluding again with nothing
  new and closing `already`, no plugin told).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h7, a push held back waits on its quest's wake).** CLOSE1h2's `push-held` entry is read again at every
look, but FABRIC-END7's wakes never looked at it: `LandingWaits.Of` had no case for it, so it fell to `look` and waited for the whole look
at `pollSeconds`, while the release it waits on (the hold let go, a reopen, the next submission) moves the ledger's `quests` family.

- **Its quests' wake.** `LandingWaits.Of` reads `push-held` as `quests`, beside `held` and `unreviewed`; the configuration and the whole
  look still concern it. Its tree's tip is kept on the try, but no refs wake releases it (`AutoLandingCode.OnChange` excludes it), so a
  moved tip is found at the quests' wake or the whole look, as before. Only the wake family is shared: the code, its note and its way on
  (the hand-off of the made branch, CLOSE1h2) stay `push-held`'s own.
- **The owner's install.** CLOSE1h2's judgement at the push runs there only behind a wait, so a `push-held` entry is rare; one is now
  looked at when its quest moves rather than at the next whole look. No read is added and nothing is written that was not.
- **Held by**, seen red first: `LandingWakesTests` (`push-held` rows: the quests family and the configuration concern it; the sessions
  family, a repository's refs and the gate's files do not; the whole look does).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h3, the press and an entry's Accept judged again under their holds).** CLOSE1h and CLOSE1h2 judged only
the automatic landing again, under its tree's hold and before its push. The person's press (`LAND_SESSION_TREE`, `trees land <session>`)
and an entry's or an ask's Accept (`LAND_ENTRY`, `LAND_ASK`, `trees land --entry|--ask`) were the last landing paths without that fence.
A press that waited behind another landing of its tree (LANDRACE1, bounded only at minutes) landed whatever stood when it got the tree.
The hand-off is its own row (FABRIC-CLOSE1h8). The parent's four decisions, built here:

1. **A quest's hold does not stop the person's press or Accept.** The press is the person's own word, and it never read the quest's hold,
   only the gate's opinion part. `AutoLander`'s predicate is not copied: that would make Accept an automatic acceptance.
2. **What stops a waiting press is the result it was made on moving**: a reopen or a done turned down, a later submission, or another
   `ready` at the same commit (by machine and sequence, `QuestResults.Same`). The result is taken at the press, where the door reads its
   gate and names its landing, and compared with a fresh read under the tree's hold (`judged`) and under the branch's hold just before
   the plugin (`beforePush`). No cached world is reused.
3. **A press on no quest, or on a quest whose result is not in** (open, or taken with no submission), lands as before. `QuestResults.Judged`
   is not required of every press, and nothing is persisted.
4. **A tree-stage refusal makes nothing; a push-stage refusal keeps the made branch and its record** (CLOSE1h2's shape:
   `TreeLanding.Withheld`, kept on the tree's hold as a push not tried, no plugin told). The answer says why, and names the way on.

- **One code for every door** (`Trees/SessionTrees/PressedResults.cs`). `PressQuests` is one press's quest reads. The door's find for the
  landing's name (the chain's walk reads the session's own quest first) is read once, and `PressedAsync` makes the press on that read, so
  it costs no read of its own. `PressedResult.Judged` and `.BeforePush` are what `TreeLands.LandAsync` asks. The route
  (`TreesRoutes.LandAsync`) and the terminal (`TreesConsole`) build them alike (D50's twin rule). `EntryAccepts.PartsAsync` holds each part
  to its own quest's result (`EntryLandingPart.Pressed`), not the entry's naming subject's.
- **Read only where time could have moved it.** Under the tree's hold where that hold waited, and for an entry's part always (`late`: the
  press's one fetch and its earlier parts come between). Before the push, a submission every time and a done only where the push waited.
  An uncontended press reads one quest more at most, and only on a submission. A press on a part of an entry with others, named for its
  entry's first part, reads its own quest once at the press. An entry's Accept reads each held part's quest once more under its tree's hold.
- **The words.** Under the tree's hold: *Nothing was landed: its quest was taken back* (or *its quest's submission was made again*) *while
  this press waited to land, so what it was made on stands no more. Look at the quest as it stands, then accept it again.* Kept as `undone`
  for a result taken back, `refused` for a later one. Before the push: *… before its plugin was told, so its push was not tried: the branch
  stands, and once you have looked at the quest as it stands, `daoris-driver trees hand <session>` hands it on.* A second Accept on the made
  branch is refused while it stands (REVIEW2), in words that name the hand-off too, so the hand-off is the way on, not a second press. The
  terminal exits 1 where a push was held back (`TreesWords.Land`). The page shows the press's `message` as written: no field and no page
  word was added.
- **An entry's parts.** A part whose result moved is refused under its own tree's hold, the parts after it are not tried, and what landed
  before it stands. A part whose push is held back keeps its branch and stops the parts after it, since their push would carry its work on
  the entry's one branch: the answer says so (`EntryLandWords`), and the entry is not `done` (`EntryLandWords.AllLanded`). Leaving out and a
  part on an earlier part's tree are unchanged.
