- **The owner's install.** Nothing binds there, so no quest has a submission. An uncontended press on a done reads nothing more; one that
  waited behind another landing reads its quest once under the hold, and stops only where the done was turned down meanwhile. An entry's
  Accept reads each part's done once more. Nothing is written.
- **Held by**, each seen red first:
  - `PressedResultTests`: what a press holds to, and what moves it (a hold, and a graph's close on the same submission, are not moves);
    the reads counted at each stage; the two sentences and their codes.
  - `EntryLandingsTests` (a part's push held back: its words, the entry not all landed), `TreesWordsTests` (exit 1), and
    `DriverCommandTests` (both doors and the entry's library build it).
  - `AutoLandingTests` (Process, Git), run once by class: a press waiting for its tree makes nothing where its result was reopened,
    readied again at the same commit, readied on another machine, or a done turned down; it lands where the result was held, its quest is
    unfinished, or it has none. At the push the branch stands with no plugin told, and a held result pushes once. An entry's part held at
    its push stops the part after it.
  - `EntryLandingTests` (Process, Git): a later part's result made again while the first part waits for its tree lands nothing of it.
- **Left**: the hand-off (FABRIC-CLOSE1h8).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1q3, the doors offer only what the pass takes).** `HeldAttempts.Read` judges a held close against the runtime's last read of the quests (`NodeRuntime.LastQuests`, kept between passes and cleared on disarm, read-only for the doors); where the close's submission moved, `words` is `resend` alone and `why` is `submission-moved`. With no read yet it offers what it did, and the pass's refusal (CLOSE1q2's choice 2) still stands as the backstop. The `submission-moved` words read as a reason before any word is given. The terminal's `work held` has no runtime and lists as before (FABRIC-CLOSE1q5).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1q5, the terminal's `work held` judged on the last read too).** The runtime keeps its last fresh read of the quests as `workflows/quests.json` (`QuestReads`), beside `units/` and `closures/` and never in `units/`, where any file not a unit's record is reported unreadable: per quest id, the current submission's machine and sequence, or null. It is written once per fresh read, only while the runtime holds the lease, and deleted on disarm. `work held` (`HeldAttempts.Run`, still a lease-free read) judges on it as the page does on `LastQuests`, so a moved close lists `resend` alone with `submission-moved`. A read left by a closed desktop is kept and used: a moved submission stays moved (identity is machine and sequence), so a stale read can only miss a move, which the pass still refuses, never invent one. A missing or malformed file judges nothing, and the list reads as before.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-CLOSE1q4, a word taken back said where the person looks).** CLOSE1q2's choice 2 said a word taken back on a close no longer listed only in the pass's lines. `QuestCloser.Recover` now also keeps it in the node record's history (`QuestClosing.TakenBack`, `word-taken-back #<quest> <word> <attempt>`, the history's 200 newest kept), and `GraphEnds` lists it on `WORK_STATE`'s `ends` as a `word-taken-back` end (its quest, node, attempt and the additive `word`), which the work's foot and line say in English and 中文. Choices, for review: (1) *Said once*: the end is listed only while its attempt is neither held nor unanswered on its node, judged at the read, so a close still listed is said by its held row (`submission-moved`) and not twice, and one whose node moves off after the refusal is said from then on. (2) *The person's `resend` answers it*: applying it drops the attempt's entries, so its later settling does not bring the word back. (3) *Told, not a wait*: it offers no word and makes no *What needs you* row, since nothing waits on the person; a quest unit no milestone draws is told as an override is. A build before it ignores the history entry and reads the end's kind as one it has no words for.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1d2, *submitted* wherever a quest's status is drawn).** The chain strip and line, the map's detail, an ask's work, the Overview's outstanding rows and the session's relations read a closure graph's submitted quest as the page does: *submitted* (已交付), the pill's tone still the status's, and a step held `source-reopened` draws the *source reopened* pill beside it (the line, which draws plain ink, the word in plain ink). One reading, `QuestStatus` (`quests/QuestStatus.tsx`), keyed on the hold and not the status, as a withdrawn step is Open. The Overview times a submitted quest by its submission's moment (`overview.outstanding.submitted`). An ask's work reads a quest's submission and hold from the quests its page holds (`AskPage`'s `quests`); one it does not hold keeps the plan's word. A quest no closure holds draws as before.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c4, a source's reopen and the session that said ready, on the wire).** The design's §E5 (*The person's
reopen*: its session is told at its next step), the service's half and the driver's read; the telling is FABRIC-CLOSE1c8. CLOSE1c cleared
a reopened submission and kept the person's words only in the operation log, so a reopened source read as one whose taker had not yet said
`ready`, and nothing named the session to tell: the driver's newest record for the quest need not be the one that said it.

- **The source's reopen, derived.** A quest keeps its last reopen of its own submission that no later submission answered
  (`Quest.Reopened`: the reopen's machine and sequence, `of` the submission it reopened, the person's note, `at`). The replay sets it on the
  reopen that clears the submission and clears it on the next `submitted`, at whatever commit, since a submission is named by its machine
  and sequence (CLOSE1q's). Read from the log, a mirror and the remote answer it as the take's machine does; the `reopened` operation and
  its sync are unchanged. A step's reopen stays its `sourceReopened`. It is cached in a `reopened` column, and a source a build before it
  reopened is replayed as the store opens (`RecacheUnkeptReopensAsync`: taken, no submission, a reopen and a submission in its log, none
  kept), once.
- **The submission's session, written.** No reading of the log can say which session said a `ready`, so the `submitted` operation carries
  it (`session`, from the connector's intake, which `quest_respond` already passed): in the payload, on the wire and in the submission's
  column, written only when set, and read as none from an older build and where it is no name. A session's id already travels as
  `publishedBy` and as an evidence verdict's and a set-up's `session`. A ready no session said (the respond door) names none.
- **The answer and the client.** `QuestResponse.reopened` and `submission.session`, each absent where unset and from an older host.
  `quest_list` says the reopen with the person's words until the next `ready`. The driver's `ServiceClient.ReadQuests` reads both into
  `QuestView.Reopened` and `QuestSubmissionView.Session`, null from an older host and where named in half. No driver behaviour reads them
  yet, and the owner's install binds nothing, so it reads every quest as before.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `QuestReopenTests` (2: the reopen in the row and in a replay, answered by the next ready at the same
  commit, the later of two carried; a row from before read from the log as the store opens), `QuestReopenSyncTests` (1: both read alike on
  two machines and the remote, and answered everywhere), `QuestClosureTests` (1: the session kept, none through a door that names none),
  `QuestOperationKindsTests` (the submitted sample with its session, on the wire, in the log and the cache), `McpToolsTests` (1: the
  listing's words), `LocalHostTests` (the reopen test's answers, and 1: `submission.session`), `QuestResponseTests`, and
  `ServiceClientClosureTests` (1).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c8, a reopened source's session told once).** The design's §E5 (*The person's reopen*: its session
is told at its next step), after CLOSE1c4's reopen on the wire; D137 §2 (words to a session). Before it nothing told the session that
said a reopened `ready`, so the run waited on its agent for good.

- **The reopen names the session.** The reopen clears the submission and `submission.session` with it, so `QuestLog`'s step keeps that
  session on the reopen (`QuestReopen.Session`): in the cache column, on the wire (`reopened.session`) and in `QuestReopenView.Session`,
  written only when set, and absent where no session said the ready (the respond door) and from a host before it. A reopen a build
  before it cached reads none, and tells nobody until the next one.
- **Who and when** (`ReopenTelling`, a look step beside the channel's, from the quests the look read): the session the reopen names, on
  this machine alone, while the quest is taken and the reopen stands; never a teammate's (`origin/id`), never the quest's last record
  here, and nobody where the reopen names none. A parked or ended record is told at once through the say door, and the planner's go-on
  resumes it at the next look: words on a park are its answer, on an ended record they reopen it. A running one is told at the first
  look that finds it parked or ended. A record this machine keeps no more, or whose tree is gone, is told nothing, and the look says why
  once, since its go-on would make the work again. A refusal other than `running` is said once and not asked again in that run.
- **Once.** `<home>/sessions/<session>.reopened.json` (`ReopenMarks`), keyed by the quest and the reopen's machine and sequence, written
  once the say door kept the words; the telling's own memory holds the once where the write failed. A later reopen of the quest replaces
  the mark; a later submission answers the reopen, so nothing is said. The file is the session's, in `SessionHomeFiles`' inventory as a
  `mark`, so a clear and a delete take it with its record.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *Twice rather than never.* A crash between the say and the mark tells the session twice. A mark written before the say would lose
     the telling for good on a say that failed, and no order proves the model read the words once.
  2. *No `channel` on the say.* `channel` names a channel message the ask already holds, which is why the say door keeps such words off
     the ask (`Program.cs`, the say door). These words are no channel message, and a made-up id would leave on the record a channel no
     ask holds. So they are kept as every word to a record is: a park's answer at once, an ended record's `reopened` once a session took
     them, Daoris's sentence with the person's words.
  3. *The words.* Daoris's own English sentence naming the reopened submission as the service names one (`` `desk` 41 of quest `#q` ``),
     asking the agent to address the reopen and say `ready` again, then the person's note quoted line by line; with no note, the
     sentence alone. They go to the agent, not the page, so no catalogue entry.
- **Unchanged:** a look where no quest is a reopened source reads nothing and writes nothing, and the owner's install binds no quest.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `QuestReopenTests` (1: the session named in the row, in a replay and in the store read again, and
  none through the respond door), `QuestReopenSyncTests` (the session on every machine), `LocalHostTests` (the reopen's `session`, and
  none through the respond door), `ServiceClientClosureTests` (read, and none where blank), `ReopenTellingTests` (15: parked, completed
  and failed told once and not after a restart; once where no mark can be written; running, then told; a running refusal tried again
  and another said once; a record gone and a tree tidied said once; the named session and never the last; a teammate's and none read
  nothing; answered and unbound read nothing; a later reopen told again; the words with a note and without; over the real driver, parked
  and ended, the go-on at the next look and a driver started again saying nothing), and `HistoryClearingTests` (the mark goes with its
  record).
- **Left** (a row): the planner goes on only with the words on the quest's last record here, so a told session that is not it (the
  person stopped it after its ready, and a later one ran) holds the words, and nothing goes on with them by itself.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h8, the hand-off judged again under its branch's hold).** CLOSE1h3 fenced the press and Accept; the
hand-off was the last push path with no quest read, so one that waited behind another push of its branch (LANDRACE1) told its plugin
whatever stood when it got the branch. It is now judged once more under the branch's hold, after the record is read again and the "pushed
at this commit" answer, just before its plugin is told (`TreeHandOffs.HandAsync`'s `beforePush`, given whether the hold waited, as
`TreeLands.LandAsync`'s is). The parent's decisions, built here:

1. **The quest judged is the one of the session that last moved the branch** (`LandedBranch.LastSession()`: its newest advance's, else
   the landing's), not the record's founding quest. The record keeps no quest per advance, so the door asks the service for that session's
   (`SessionQuestAsync`) and reads the quest where it names the branch. No record format changed.
2. **The person's doors keep CLOSE1h3's rule.** The page's `HANDOFF`, Ask Daoris's card (both through the shared `TreesRoutes.HandAsync`)
   and the terminal's `trees hand` build it by one helper (`PressQuests.HandedAsync`, D50's twin rule) and hand on
   `PressedResult.BeforePush`: the result moving stops the hand-off (a reopen or a done turned down, a later submission, another `ready`
   at the same commit or on another machine), a quest's hold does not.
3. **The look's own hand-off of a push held back keeps the look's rule** (`AutoLander.BeforePushAsync`: a hold stops it too), passed the
   same way. Held back there, its entry is kept `push-held` again (`TreeHand.Withheld`), not refused: a refusal is kept at the tree's tip
   and tried again only once the tree moves, which a made branch's tree does not, so it would never have been handed on.
4. **A refusal tells no plugin and records nothing**: no push on the record, nothing kept for a door behind it. The answer is `done:
   false` with the sentence, which names `trees hand <session>` for that last session; the terminal exits 1. No page word was added.
5. **An unbound session, or a quest whose result is not in, hands off as before**, after one read of the session's quest, and one more of
   the quest where it names one. A done is read again only where the hold waited; a submission every time.

- **The owner's install.** Nothing binds there, so no quest has a submission: a hand-off reads the session's quest and that quest once at
  the door, and once more only where it waited behind another push and its done stands. Nothing is written that was not.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `PressedResultTests` (the quest read is the newest advance's session's, else the landing's; an
  unbound session and a result not in hold to nothing), `DriverCommandTests` (every person's door builds it by the one helper), and
  `AutoLandingTests` (Process, Git), run once by class: s2's done moved s1's branch on, and a hand-off waiting for the branch tells no
  plugin where s2's submission was reopened, replaced, readied again at the same commit or on another machine; it pushes where s2's quest
  was held or s1's result moved. The look's hand-off held again as it waits stays `push-held` and pushes once after the release.
- **Left** (a row, found by reading, untested): the session judged is the one that last moved the branch when the door read it. The look's
  hand-on of a `push-held` entry judges that entry's own quest, and hands on wherever its tree still stands at the commit held, so where a
  later session advanced the branch since and its own push was held back, the hand-on pushes that session's work on the earlier quest.
  A person's hand-off that waits behind such an advance judges the session it read too.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h10, a hand-off judged on the session that moved its branch while it waited).** CLOSE1h8 judged a
hand-off on the session that last moved the branch as the door read it, and the look's hand-on on its `push-held` entry's own quest. An
advance is recorded before its push takes the branch's hold (`TreeLands`), so one could come between, and its work rode the hand-off on
an earlier session's quest (CLOSE1h8's *Left*). The parent's decisions, built here:

1. **The session judged is the one that last moved the branch as the record reads under the branch's hold.** `TreeHandOffs.HandAsync`
   reads the record again there and tells `beforePush` its `LastSession()` with whether the hold waited. Where that is not the session
   the caller judged, the later session's quest is judged instead, by the caller's own rule, read every time: the door never read it.
2. **The person's doors** (the page's `HANDOFF`, Ask Daoris's card, `trees hand`) keep one helper, `PressQuests.HandedAsync`, which now
   answers a `HandedResult` every time: the session the door read, its result where one is in (`Pressed`), and the judgement
   (`BeforePush`). A later session's result no longer in (taken back, or gone) holds the push back; a hold does not; a later session on
   no quest holds it to nothing. The record keeps no submission per advance, so a later session's result is judged in or not in, never
   made again since its advance.
3. **The look's hand-on** judges a later session by the look's rule (`AutoLander.BeforePushAsync`: taken back or held stops it), and a
   refusal there stays `push-held` (`TreeHand.Withheld`). Its choice of a `push-held` entry (`PushHeldAsync`) reads that later session's
   quest too, so a hand-on that would only be held back again waits, said once already, and no look keeps a try of it meanwhile.
4. **The words are the old ones**, naming `trees hand` for the later session: *its quest was taken back … so its push was not tried*,
   and the conversation's `push-held` note. No sentence and no page word was added.
5. **No later advance, no new read**: an unbound branch, or one whose last session is the one the caller judged, hands off exactly as
   CLOSE1h8 does.

- **The owner's install.** Nothing binds there. A later session's quest is read once more only where another session's done moved the
  branch on while a hand-off waited, two landings of one chain at once. A later session whose quest is open (its work the person's press)
  holds a person's hand-off back once, in the words for a quest taken back; the next hand-off reads that session at the door and hands
  on as before.
- **Held by** (each refusal seen red first; the pushes, and the reads where nothing advanced, held before and after):
  `PressedResultTests` (a later session's quest read every time, its result in or not the only question, the earlier session's moving no
  question at all; no later advance reads no more) and `AutoLandingTests` (Process, Git), run once by class: a person's hand-off read
  with s1 last and moved on by s2 before it held the branch is refused where s2's result was taken back and pushes once where it stands,
  held or not; the look's hand-on of s1 tells no plugin of s2's work where s2's quest was taken back before the look chose it or while
  the hand-on waited for the branch, and s1 stays `push-held`.
- **Left**: an advance's own push, carrying an earlier session's held-back work, is judged on the advancing quest only (FABRIC-CLOSE1h11).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c9, the reopen's words go on in the session it names).** The design's §E5 (*The person's reopen*: its
session is told at its next step), after FABRIC-CLOSE1c8, whose "Left" this is: the telling reached the session that said the reopened
`ready`, but the planner's go-on read only the quest's last record here, so where a later session had run the words waited for good.

- **The planner considers the record the reopen names** (the parent's option A). The look's snapshot carries it
  (`Snapshot.NamedByReopen`, quest id to record), for each taken quest whose standing reopen names a session of this machine's that is
  not its last record here, read from the records the look already reads (`ServiceClient.ReadNamedByReopen`; nothing is parsed where no
  quest qualifies, and no read is added). One planner helper (`WordsOn`) picks the record words wait on: the named one where its words
  wait and are not all judged unable, else the last one; the ordering, `Plans` (so a pause holds them) and the go-on read it. The carry-on,
  the resume and the person's stop hold stay on the last record. The look's go-on marks (`GoOnMarks.For`, `Driver`'s look) cover the named
  record too, so words it could not go on with wait rather than being tried at every look. The telling takes the record from the
  snapshot where it is there, so it reads nothing more.
- **Unchanged:** a named record whose tree is gone is told nothing (CLOSE1c8); one whose repository another session holds active waits
  (`RepositoryBusy`); an unbound quest, and a reopen naming the last record, go as before. The owner's install binds no quest, so its
  look parses nothing more.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *A session the person stopped is not resumed by Daoris's words* (the parent's decision). The planner lets any words release a stop,
     as Try again does, but the reopen's are Daoris's. So where the named record is the person's stop and a later session ran, the
     telling says nothing to it and settles once with `reopen  quest #q's reopened submission names session s, which the person
     stopped, and a later session ran: it is told nothing, and nothing goes on with it until they say so`; the quest's run reads as it
     does. Words the person writes to it themselves go on there, releasing their stop as before.
  2. *Where the reopen names the quest's last record and the person stopped it*, the telling tells it and the go-on resumes it, as under
     CLOSE1c8 (the parent's decision 5, that a reopen naming the last record behaves exactly as before). Choice 1's reason applies there
     too, so the owner may want the two made one; it is left as it was, and named here.
  3. *The person's stop of the last record holds the named record's words.* A stop holds its quest until the person releases it
     (SESSUX1b), and Daoris's words must not go round it through an earlier session: the quest reads `Stopped` on the last record.
  4. *The named record's words go first* where both it and the last record hold words; the last record's go on at a later look, once the
     named one's were taken.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `SessionMessagesPlanTests` (the named record goes on though a later one is last, and before the last
  one's words; a pause holds it), `ReopenTellingTests` (the snapshot names the reopen's record, and none for a teammate's, the last, a
  record not kept, an open quest or no reopen; a session the person stopped is told nothing and says so once, while the last record
  stopped and a shutdown's stop are told; over the real driver, told and gone on with at the next look, the person's stop telling and
  starting nothing, and the named record's go-on mark read so its words wait). Seen green before, as guards: the last record's stop
  holding the named words, and the named record's marks judged in the plan.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c10, the go-on clears a held step's own result).** The design's §E5 (*The person's reopen*: the step
*rebinds to the new submission once that is unheld, its branch brought up to date*), the service's half; the driver's (the step's session
gone on though it ended normally, with CLOSE1c5's rebase) is FABRIC-CLOSE1c13. A second maker's review found that CLOSE1c's `rebound` let a
held step go and kept its result: a step that had submitted before its source was reopened went on with that submission current, its
answers, yes and verdict with it, so its close could take work made on the reopened source as finished by the person's word alone. §E5
never said a go-on keeps the old result; its amendment now says the go-on clears it.

- **The replay's rule** (`QuestLog.Step`, `quest-operations.md` row 2; no new kind or field, `Applies` unchanged). A `rebound` clears the
  step's own submission, answers, yes and verdict, beside moving its `grewFrom` and letting its hold go, as a reopen of that submission
  clears them on the quest that made it. It sets no `reopened` on the step: how the step's session goes on is the driver's (CLOSE1c13). A
  withdrawn open step, rebound by the next submission's publication, has none, and reads as before. `ReboundInAsync` writes the cleared
  row as it wrote the rebind, so a restart reads it.
- **What the step then reads:** taken, unheld, with no submission. It cannot close or land by itself (a close naming the old submission is
  `stale`), and its run reads as waiting for its agent until its agent says `ready` again on the new source, judged from the start. Until
  CLOSE1c13 nothing goes on with a session that ended normally, which is safe while nothing binds.
- **The continue answer** says any result the step submitted is cleared, and that it must submit again once its branch holds the new
  submission (the service's English, which the page shows as written). No driver test, rehearsal or e2e spec reads those words.
- **The step's own successor**, which its old submission published where the chain goes on, is left as it stood, and that is a gap, handed
  back as a row. It still names the cleared submission as its `grewFrom`, and no reopen withdrew or held it, so it can be taken and worked
  on. `SealingAsync` reads nothing sealing the step while it has no submission. Once the step submits anew, `PublishNextAsync` finds that
  successor standing and does not rebind it (only a withdrawn open step is), so the new submission is never sealed and the step's close
  is refused `unsealed`.
- **Machines and the remote.** The replay is the source, so every machine and remote on this build reads the step alike
  (`QuestReopenSyncTests`). A build from CLOSE1c up to this one replays `rebound` keeping the result, so a mixed family would read the step
  two ways; the standing rule holds that every machine and the remote run the build before a reopen. A step such a build already rebound
  keeps its cached result until its next operation replays it. Nothing binds on the owner's install, so no such step exists.
- **Found on the way** (handed back as a row): a rebind a source's publication makes on a step its machine reads withdrawn applies at the
  remote to the same step taken first on another machine, so a started step goes on without the person's word. `Applies` admits a
  `rebound` on a taken step as on an open one, and the rebase keeps it. A probe (two machines and the remote, the take pushed before the
  reopen and the next submission) read the step taken and unheld on all three; it was not kept.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `QuestReopenTests` (2: the go-on of a started step that submitted with a departure the person
  accepted, which then reads taken with nothing submitted in its row and its replay, its old submission's close `stale` and its new ready
  closing; the replay's rule on a held step's result and on a withdrawn open step), `QuestReopenSyncTests` (1: the clear read alike on
  two machines and the remote, the old close `stale`). Seen green before, as a guard: the census's new state, a submitted step held by its
  source's reopen, on which a rebind's cached row is its replay (`QuestOperationKindsTests`). `LocalHostTests` (1: the go-on door's answer)
  was written after its code.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSECAP1, a binding needs the outcome door, not `closure` alone).** CLOSE1b's handshake read only
`/api/status`'s `closure`, which builds from before the outcome door (CLOSE1u) also answer, so a driver could bind a quest to a host
that cannot recover a close. The local host's status now answers `closeOutcome: true` beside `closure` (an additive field, `StatusResponse.CloseOutcome`),
and `ServiceClient.KnowsClosureAsync` reads true only where both are answered. A host answering `closure` alone binds nothing, and
`QuestCloser` says so as it says an older host today. Remote binding stays refused (CLOSE1i, CLOSE1f open). Held by, each seen red first:
`ServiceClientClosureTests` (the handshake's four unanswered shapes), `LocalHostTests` (the status answers `closeOutcome`), and
`PersonDoorHostTests` (the status's field list).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h12, the landing's gates judged again at the push and in its recovery).** CLOSE1h2 judged the
automatic landing's quest again just before its plugin is told, and its `push-held` recovery read only the quest. The landing's gate
(the run's workflow, the second opinion, the review; D155 point 9) was read once, at the judgement just before the landing. A gate that
started holding while the push waited behind another push of its branch, or before a restart's look handed a held-back push on, did
not stop the push (a Codex close-path review, item 2). An ordinary done under *Accept automatically* was open to it too. The parent's
decisions, built here:

1. **One gate helper** (`AutoLander.GateAsync`), out of the look's judgement: the gate as every door reads it (`SessionTrees.GateAsync`),
   its first part that holds as `LandingGate.Refusal` in §7's order, and the machine log's line for that part
   (`LandingGateWords.Held`). The look, the push and the recovery judge by it alike. The look's own handling is unchanged: each part
   said once, kept as before.
2. **At the push** (`BeforePushAsync`) the gate is read only where the quest is read again (a later session, a push that waited, a
   submission), after the quest's checks, for the same session. A part that holds keeps the push back as a hold does: the branch made
   and recorded, no plugin told, `push-held`, and the part's held line logged once. The sentence reuses CLOSE1h2's words, *its landing's
   gate held it before its plugin was told, so its push was not tried: … once the gate lets it go …*, then the gate's own sentence.
3. **In recovery** (`PushHeldAsync`) the gate is read once the quests stand and the tree is still at the commit held, before a hand-on
   is chosen. Held, the entry waits silently, as a later session's wait does (CLOSE1h10): its push was said held back already. A tree
   that moved past that commit is judged as any later done, whose own judgement reads the gate.
4. **A later session's work** (CLOSE1h10) is judged by that session's gate: its quest and its record's tree, as its quest is read. A
   later session on no quest reads no part that needs one, as its push is held to nothing.
5. **Its wake** (`LandingWaits.PushHeld`): a push held back waits on its quest and its whole gate at once, so any ledger family and the
   gate's files concern it, as an opinion's wait; the configuration and the whole look still do. CLOSE1h7's `quests` wake is widened.
6. **The review's own `unreviewed` hold stays excepted** from the quest's check: the gate read there judges it. The person's reviewed
   lets the push go even while the quest still reads held, and a set-up not reviewed holds it back.
7. **The person's press and hand-off are unchanged** (CLOSE1h3, CLOSE1h8): their closures (`PressQuests`) keep their door-time gate,
   since the person's press answers a dispute (D155 point 8).

- **The owner's install.** An uncontended landing of a plain done reads no quest and no gate at its push, as before (LANDGIT1's
  counts). A push that waited reads the gate's own reads once more: the process, the review's chain, and an opinion's where a rule reads
  one. A `push-held` entry's recovery reads them at each look that reaches its gate, and ledger and gate wakes now concern it.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `LandingWakesTests` (push-held rows: any ledger family and the gate's files concern it; refs,
  requests and words do not), and `AutoLandingPushHeldTests` (Process, Git), run once by class: an opinion rule and a set-up step
  introduced while the push waits hold it back with no frame, say nothing while they hold, and one push follows once they let go; the
  same gates started before a held-back push's recovery hold its hand-on silently, then one push; a set-up step's `unreviewed` hold not
  reviewed holds the push until the person's reviewed; a later session's work reviewed only at the earlier tip holds the hand-on of
  both entries until it is reviewed at its own. Seen green before, as guards: a set-up step's `unreviewed` hold reviewed at the tip
  pushes at once, and an uncontended plain done's landing reads the quest list as often as the look's judgement did.
- **Left** (rows): the conversation's `push-held` note leads with *its quest was held or taken back* where a gate held it (the sentence
  beneath names the gate). An opinion's due closes as landed once its branch is recorded (`OpinionLook.ClosesAsync`), so a push held
  back by an opinion is asked only by the person's `opinion ask`, which the gate's sentence names. The gate at the push reads the tree's
  tip, not the branch's, so a tree that moved on since holds the push until its later done lands the whole.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-WORKFLOW2e, a quest's run reads the whole chain).** WORKFLOW2d's seal reading needs the chain's next step
wherever it is, but the reader cut a quest's chain (and so a session's, which reaches it through its quest) to the quest's own repository
before it read it, so a source or step in another repository read a sealed submission as Working/Queued and missed a reopen there. A
quest's run is still its own repository's, but `ChainsOf` now hands back the whole chain and names that repository (`Only`), and
`ReadAsync` visits only it; an ask's run keeps one run per repository, as before. A run bound to no closure graph reads as before (a
test says so across two repositories).
- **The review level moves with it, on purpose.** `RunAsync` already handed the whole chain to `ReviewGate.Decide`, which had been given
  the cut one. Two reads of `Decide` look past the run's repository and now see the other repositories' quests: the set-up step another
  repository's open or held quest composes for this one (`ReviewGate.cs` ~285-291), and the chain's review choice set on another
  repository's quest (row 3, ~294). Rows 1 and 2 (the person's skip, a published set-up step) filter by repository and do not move. The
  landing doors already judge with `ReviewGate.ReadAsync` over the whole chain, so a quest's run showed a review level the landing did
  not apply; the run's reading and the gate it predicts now agree, as an ask's run already did. `Facts.Chain` and the review's chain stay
  one chain. Held by `WorkflowRunTests` (the cut chains now whole, `Only` named; a quest's and a session's run read sealed and reopened
  across repositories, seen red first; the two moved rows and the two unmoved ones pinned, which could not fail first as it calls `Decide`
  directly).

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c13, a step gone on with after its session ended normally).** The design's §E5 (*The person's reopen*: the
step *rebinds to the new submission once that is unheld, its branch brought up to date*, with CLOSE1c5's and CLOSE1c10's amendments), the
driver's half of CLOSE1c10. A step that had submitted before its source was reopened read, after the person's go-on, taken with no
submission, and nothing moved it: only a session the reopen stopped was carried on (CLOSE1c2) and rebased (CLOSE1c5). A step's session
that said `ready` and exited cleanly holding its quest parks (STANDDOWN2), so the usual record is a park, which holds its quest's slot and
tree: a new session beside it, as CLOSE1c2's carry-on starts, would sit `RepositoryBusy` behind it for good.

- **The fact** (`Planner.GoneOnSince`): the step taken and unheld, grown from a submission (`grewFrom`), with no submission and no reopen of
  its own; its last record here this machine's, parked with no answer or completed (not `StoppedByReopen`), not running, with no words
  waiting; and the step's `updated`, which the rebound moves, after that record's. `grewFrom` alone is no sign of a go-on: a submission sets
  it when it publishes the step.
- **It goes on in the same session** (the parent's decision 2), as CLOSE1c8's telling goes on: the look tells that record Daoris's words
  through the say door (`ReopenTelling.GoneOnWords`: the source was reopened, the step went on on its new submission by machine and
  sequence, any result it submitted was cleared, bring the work up to date and say `ready` again). Words on a park are its answer, and on an
  ended record they reopen it, so the planner's existing go-on (`WordsOn`) resumes that conversation. A tree gone is told nothing and said
  once, as a reopen's is; a `running` refusal is tried again, any other said once.
- **Brought up to date first:** the start's CLOSE1c5 gate (`ReopenTelling.BringsToSource`) no longer asks `StoppedByReopen` alone. It also
  takes a record going on with those exact words for the submission the step grows from now, while the step has no result, and
  `GoOnInSyncAsync` runs for it before the conversation resumes, its outcome said after the words: rebased, a conflict left for the person
  with the tree as it was, or no commit known and the line alone synced.
- **Once, with no new store** (decision 3): the told record has words waiting, which the fact excludes; once it goes on, its record moves
  after the step's; a `ready` gives the step a result again. A driver started before the go-on reads the same fact and tells it once.
- **Choices made here, for review.**
  1. *Daoris's words, not a new session.* The parent's decision named CLOSE1c2's carry-on. A park after `ready` holds its slot and tree, so
     only that record can go on, and only with words; one rule serves the park and the completed record alike.
  2. *The gate reads Daoris's own sentence* on the record, naming the submission, rather than a mark kept beside it: the say door keeps no
     kind for Daoris's words, and the service was not this row's. A person's words that happen to equal it would sync the same way.
  3. *A park that asked the person a question before the reopen is told too*: nothing on the record tells a park after `ready` from one
     asking, so Daoris's words answer it and the step goes on with its branch brought up to date; it may ask again.
- **Unchanged:** an unbound quest names no `grewFrom` and reads as before; a step whose session runs is not read, and nor is one stopped by
  the person, cut off or stood down. A session that ran across the reopen and the go-on, ending normally after it, is not read (a row).
  The owner's install binds no quest, so nothing here is told there.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `PlannerTests` (the fact on a park and a completed record), `ReopenGoOnTests` (the gate on the go-on's
  words), `ReopenTellingTests` (told once for a park and a completed record, reading nothing; a tree gone said once; the words; over the
  real driver, told at one look, gone on with in the same session at the next, a driver started again telling nothing). Seen green before,
  as guards: every other step and record not read so, a session ended after the go-on, running or with words not told, the planner's
  go-on with the told words and a stopped step's carry-on, and every other going-on synced with the line alone. `StandInLedger` models the
  go-on's clear and, where asked, its move of `updated`.
- **Found on the way** (a row): CARRY2b's `TakenElsewhere` reads a taken quest's `updated` as its take's time, but a submission, an
  evidence read, a reopen and a rebound move it too, so a carry-on over a take no session marked, after a go-on, reads as taken here by
  work outside Daoris.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c15, a publication's rebind is judged while the step is open).** The design's §E5 (*The person's
reopen*: a started step goes on only by the person's word), CLOSE1c's choice 2, after CLOSE1c10's "Found on the way", confirmed by its
two-machine probe. A machine that reopened a source and submitted again before another machine's take of the step reached it rebound the
step it read withdrawn. At the remote, and on every machine after, that rebind landed on the taken, held step and let it go without the
person's word: `Applies` admitted a `rebound` on a taken step as on an open one, and the rebase kept it.

- **The mark** (`quest-operations.md` rows 2, 3, 4 and 9; no new kind, field or column). A publication's rebind (`PublishNextAsync`)
  carries `whileOpen`, the flag a decline made while open carries (PAUSE1c), and `Applies` refuses a marked `rebound` on a step no longer
  open. The person's go-on (`ContinueAsync`) writes none: their word is what lets a started step go, so it applies to a held taken step as
  before. `QuestStore.JudgedOpen` names the two kinds the mark goes on, for the log's payload (`AppendAsync`, `KeepAsync`,
  `ReadOperation`) and the wire (`QuestWire.Write`, `Read` and its shape sentence). It is written only when set, so a plain one reads as
  it did. The cached row and the answer are unchanged: the mark gives the step nothing.
- **Where the race lands.** The take moved the step, so the push carrying the rebind is behind, and in the pushing machine's rebase the
  marked rebind no longer applies and is forgotten (`QuestLog.Lost`, as every lost rebind is). The reopen pushed beside it applies and
  holds the step, so both machines and the remote read it taken and held on the reopened submission. The new submission is sealed by
  nothing until the person's go-on, and the source's close is refused `unsealed` until then. A remote handed a marked rebind on a step taken
  there refuses it in the `whileOpen` words a decline's refusal uses.
- **Compatibility.** A build before this one writes no mark, and its rebind applies as before; it reads a marked one as plain. A mixed
  family can still let a started step go, so the standing rule holds: every machine and the remote run the build before a reopen. Nothing
  binds on the owner's install, so no rebind exists there.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `QuestReopenSyncTests` (1: B's take reaches the remote before A's reopen and A's next submission; the
  step reads held on both machines and the remote, the source's close `unsealed`; then the person's go-on lets it go everywhere and the
  close runs), `QuestReopenTests` (1: the replay's rule on a marked and an unmarked rebind, open and taken, and the publication's mark kept
  in the log), and the census (`QuestOperationKindsTests`: the rebind's sample carries the mark, red on the wire and in the log until both
  carried it). The go-on's unmarked rebind is asserted in `QuestReopenTests`' started-step case, green before, as a guard.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c14, the go-on reopens the step's own successor).** The design's §E5 (*The person's reopen*), the gap
CLOSE1c10's note named (*The step's own successor*). The go-on cleared the held step's own submission, but the successor that submission
had published still named it as its `grewFrom`, and nothing withdrew or held it, so it could be taken and worked on. Once the step
submitted anew, `PublishNextAsync` found it standing and did not rebind it, so the new submission was never sealed and the step's close was
refused `unsealed`.

- **The go-on's write** (`QuestStore.ContinueAsync`, `ReopenSuccessorAsync`; no new kind or field). Beside the step's `rebound`, the same
  transaction writes a `reopened` naming the cleared submission on the chain's next step, judged by the replay's rule, as the person's
  reopen of that submission writes it on its step: withdrawn while open, held while taken. CLOSE1c2's planner hold and CLOSE1c8's telling
  apply to it as to any step a reopen holds. The step's next unheld submission rebinds a withdrawn successor, as it rebinds any withdrawn
  open step (CLOSE1c15's marked rebind). A held one waits on the person's go-on on it, and the step's close waits with it.
- **What it reaches.** A step publishes at most one successor (its chain's next, `NextStep`), so there is one or none. There is none where
  the step had no submission, its submission published nothing (held, or the chain's last), or the successor closed or was already
  reopened. In each case the replay's rule refuses the reopen, and the go-on goes on without it. A successor closed on the cleared
  submission stays closed, since a reopen takes no close back: the step's next submission is then sealed by nothing, and its close is the
  person's, as for an abandoned step's source (CLOSE1c's choice 5). Whether the go-on should be refused there is handed back as a question.
- **On the wire** nothing is new: the `reopened` travels as the person's does, so both machines and the remote read the successor alike,
  and a take of it made elsewhere converges by CLOSE1c's rule.
- **The continue answer** adds, where a successor was reopened, which next step that result published and whether it is withdrawn until
  the step submits again or held for the person's word. It is the service's English, shown as written. No driver test, rehearsal or e2e
  spec reads those words.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `QuestReopenTests` (2: a three-step chain whose middle step had submitted and published its successor
  before the source was reopened. After the go-on, an untaken successor is withdrawn, refused a take, and rebound by the step's next
  submission, whose close runs. A taken one is held, its done refused, and left standing by the step's next submission, whose close is
  `unsealed` until the person's go-on on it), `QuestReopenSyncTests` (1 theory, both cases: the same on two machines and the remote). The
  answer's words were pinned after their code.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c12, a person-stopped session is never told the reopen).** CLOSE1c9's note left choice 2 open: told
nothing only where a later session ran, so where the named session was the quest's last record CLOSE1c8 still told it, and any words
release a stop (`Planner`'s carry-on), so Daoris's own sentence undid the person's stop. `ReopenTelling.TellAsync` now drops the "a later
session ran" condition: a record the person stopped (`PersonStopped`, never an interrupted one) is told nothing, last or not, the look
says so once (the `reopen  ` line, without "and a later session ran"), and the run waits on the person, whose stop is theirs to lift.
CLOSE1c13's go-on telling (`Planner.GoneOnSince`) needed no change: it tells only a parked record or a `completed` one, never a
`stopped` state, so a person's stop is already never told there.

**Note, 2026-10-18 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c18, a take made before a reopen and its rebind reached its machine loses).** The design's §E5 (*The person's
reopen*), CLOSE1c's choice 1, after a worker's two-machine probe. B took a step before A's reopen of its source and A's next submission
reached B. At the remote the reopen withdrew the step and the publication's rebind let it go onto the new submission, so B's take, judged on
an open step with no reopen standing, applied: the step stood taken by B on work the person had reopened.

- **The stamp** (`quest-operations.md` rows 2, 3, 4, 5 and 9; no new kind or column). A take carries the step's `grewFrom` as the take found
  it inside its own write (`MoveAsync`), in the operation's `submission`, the field a reopen and a rebind name one by (`QuestStore.Carries`,
  for the log's payload and the wire, written only where found). `QuestLog.Applies` refuses a take whose stamp is not the step's `grewFrom`
  where the replay reaches it, so the local write, the rebase and the remote judge it alike. A rebase rewrites a lost take as a conflict
  without it, as the log keeps it.
- **Absence** (the parent's choice). A take with no stamp is judged as before: a source-free quest's, which has nothing to stamp, and every
  take a build before this one made, pending or numbered. A stamp on a step that names no source is a mismatch.
- **A go-on keeps an established take.** The stamp is judged where the replay reaches the take, never against the source the step has now:
  take, reopen, the person's unmarked rebind replays to the step taken, unheld, grown from the new submission.
- **A take lost to the reopen leaves the step open**, where the table allows a done or a decline from open, and `OnALostTake` judged only a
  taken quest. It now judges an open one too where the machine's take lost to the person's reopen (`QuestLog.LostToTheReopen`: its take's
  conflict landed on the step open after a reopen withdrew it, on the quest as last published, D95), for every kind a taker makes but a
  take: B's done, decline or set-up is refused inside its write, at the remote, and lost at its rebase where an older build left it
  pending. A fresh take of the open step is B's to make, and its claim reads by it from then on. Choice, for review: B's person's own done
  or decline on that open step is refused alike, since a remote cannot tell it from the session's; they act from a machine that holds it,
  or take it anew.
- **Said truthfully.** The claim's answer adds `why: source-reopened` beside a claim lost so (null otherwise, as an older host answers;
  `QuestStore.LostToTheReopenAsync`). The driver stops the session as any lost take, stood down with its tree and transcript kept and no
  automatic landing, in its own words and code (`Driver.LostClaimToReopen`, `ended.lost-claim-reopened`, en and 中文 on the page). The
  take's own answer when its push finds it lost, the refusals of a move made on it (`QuestExchange.LostTake`'s `reopened`) and the remote's
  refusal of a stale take say the person's reopen, never another machine's take.
- **Compatibility.** An older build writes no stamp and reads a stamped take as plain, so a mixed family can still let a stale take stand:
  the standing rule holds, every machine and the remote run the build before a reopen. Nothing binds on the owner's install, so no quest has
  `grewFrom`, no take carries a stamp, and the wire, the claim's answer (`why` null) and every refusal read as before.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `QuestReopenTests` (1: the replay's rule over matching, mismatching, a stamp on a step with no source,
  a source-free and a legacy take pending and numbered, the go-on after an established take, and the real verb's stamp kept in the log with
  the step's own source unchanged), `QuestReopenSyncTests` (4: A's reopen and rebind first, B's take lost on B, A, a third machine fetching
  as they move, a fourth fetching from nothing and the remote, its conflict alike, the new submission's close running and a fresh take
  holding; a stale take pushed straight at the remote's base refused and a current one kept; B's done and decline on the open step refused
  locally, at the remote and lost at the rebase, both cases), the census (`QuestOperationKindsTests`: the take's sample carries the stamp,
  red on the wire, in the log and the shape sentence; a present stamp not whole makes the take not whole), the claim's reason in
  `QuestReopenSyncTests` and `QuestSyncTests` (red against a stub), and `SessionsOutliveTheirLookTests` (the stop in the reopen's words and
  code). B first is CLOSE1c15's case, green before and after as a guard. `LocalHostTests`' claim answer and `NoteSitesTests`' line were
  written after their code.
- **Left** (rows): a restart mid-integration (before the commit; after it, before the push's answer) proven to keep one loss and a
  consistent cursor, and a lost take's pending bind, submission and published successor shown going with it in this race; both ride the
  rebase's existing loss of a take, unproven here.

**Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h15, an opinion holding a push held back is asked by the loop).** CLOSE1h12's note left it: an opinion's
due closed as landed once its branch was recorded (`OpinionLook.ClosesAsync`), and its last exit closed it too, since a made branch leaves
nothing unlanded in its tree. A push held back by a second opinion was then asked only by the person's `opinion ask`, though the gate says
*it is asked at the driver's next look*. The person's ask was lost as well: it opened a due that the next look closed as landed before it
read the request, since a look's opinions are read before its landings. And where the rule started reading after the done, no due was
owed at all. The parent's decisions, built here:

1. **No due closes as landed while its landing's push is held back**, at either exit. A look with a due open reads once the sessions whose
   automatic landing is open with its last try `push-held` (`OpinionLook.PushHeld`). A landing that stands, not pushed at its tip, and
   names one of them (the session that made it, or one whose done moved it on, CLOSE1h10, since the hand-on pushes the whole) has not
   landed. The due closes once its push lands at the landing's tip, once that entry closes, or once the landing is a trace.
2. **The due is owed where the gate is read**: in `PushHeldAsync`, when the gate's refusal is the opinion's, for the session whose work
   the hand-on pushes, unless one is owed there already (`AutoLander.Owe`). Its occasion is `landing`, the one the landing gate reads and
   whose refusal shows the rule reads it, rather than the rule's first occasion that the person's ask takes. So no rule is resolved again,
   and a `steps` due already open does not stand in for it. The loop's opinion look asks it at the next look, as any due. A due list that
   cannot be written leaves the person's ask, and the next recovery writes it again.
3. **The person's `opinion ask` on a push-held entry survives the next look**, by point 1.
4. **The wake**: the look's pass and the person's ask are kept in the gate's files, and a settled opinion ends its reviewer's session
   (`sessions`). Both already concern a push held back (CLOSE1h12's point 5), so no row was missing. No watch hears the due list itself,
   so a due owed at a look's recovery is asked at the next look, which is what the gate says.

- **The owner's install.** An entry not push-held reads and behaves as before: its due closes as landed once its landing is recorded,
  pushed or not, and the lander owes no opinion outside a push held back. A look with a due open reads the automatic landings' list once
  more. An ordinary entry kept as `opinion` whose rule started reading after its done still waits on the person's ask: proposed as a row.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `OpinionGateTests` (fast: `ClosesAsync` keeps the due of a push held back, and of a later session whose
  done moved the branch on, also where the push landed at an older tip; the sessions read as push-held), `OpinionLookTests` (Process: the
  look asks the opinion owed on a push held back, and the person's ask made after its due had closed, then closes it as landed once its
  entry closes), and `AutoLandingPushHeldTests` (Process, Git: a push held back by an opinion nobody asked owes one at the landing's
  occasion, once, not closed while held, and pushes once when it settles, after which the due closes as landed; CLOSE1h12's recovery test
  now owes the opinion). Seen green before, as guards: a landing made with no push held back, one pushed at its tip, a trace, and one whose
  push-held entry is another landing's close as landed; a review holding the recovery owes no opinion.

**Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c19, the go-on is refused where the step's successor closed on the result it would clear).** The design's
§E5 (*The person's reopen*), the question CLOSE1c14's note handed back (*What it reaches*). A successor closed on the step's submission is
not reopened (a reopen takes no close back), so a go-on that cleared that submission left the step's next one sealed by nothing, and its close
refused `unsealed` for good. `QuestStore.ContinueAsync` now judges it first, after the held check: the step's own current submission, its
chain's next step `Done` or `Declined` and standing on it (`Grows`, the rule the person's reopen uses), and the go-on is refused
`QuestReopenRefusal.StepClosed` naming that step, with nothing written. The answer is `not-sealed`, as the person's reopen of the same case
answers it, in the service's English: the successor's id and status, that a reopen takes no close back, and that the person closes the step
themselves, declines it, or asks anew. No new kind, field or wire shape; §E5's text is unchanged. No driver test, rehearsal or e2e spec reads
the new words. Held by, seen red first: `QuestReopenTests` (1: a three-step chain, the last step done on the middle's result, the source
reopened and made ready again, the go-on refused naming the last step, no operation written on either step).

**Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c20, every reader of a lost claim says the person's reopen truthfully).** The hand-over and the abandon
read the claim's `why` as the driver's stop does (`ClaimStandingAsync`; `QuestTake.Reopened` is a lost claim whose `why` is

its own code `quest-reopened` (`HandOver.QuestReopened`; `quest-elsewhere` stays for another's take), and the look that drops a held hand-over
says "was lost to your reopen of the work it grew from". The abandon keeps such a quest as `AbandonWhy.SourceReopened` (`source-reopened`), and
the pause's page line reads it (`asks.work.reopenedTake`); all three have en and zh. The snapshot's carry-on claims now ride the `why` beside
the claim. Held by `AbandonTests`, `HandOverTests`, `HandOverLookTests` and the web's `say.test.ts` and `workTree.test.ts`.
- **Left** (row): the planner's `TakenElsewhere` consideration (a carry-on over a take that is not this machine's) still words a lost
  claim as another machine's; `QuestTake.Reopened` is there to read it, but the step's `HeldByReopen` is not asked so it rarely meets one.

**Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c23, the planner says the reopen).** CLOSE1c20's *Left*, the last reader of a lost claim. The planner's
`TakenElsewhere` consideration reads `QuestTake.Reopened` first: a carry-on over a take lost to the person's reopen sits with "was lost to
your reopen of the work it grew from", never "taken on another machine", and carries `TakenBy.Reopened` (no machine, no session) so the page
words it from the fact (`work.sitting.TakenElsewhere_reopened`, en and zh). A lost claim with any other reason reads as before. Held by
