**Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c21, the reopen race's remaining proofs).** CLOSE1c18's *Left*, both before the quest close is armed.

- **A pass stopped part way loses the take once.** B's pass that finds its take lost to the reopen is one transaction from fetch to
  cursor (`IntegrateAsync`), and the store keeps nothing of its own but the database, so a restart is the store opened again on its
  connection. Stopped inside the integration, before its commit, the rebase's rewrites go with the rollback: the take reads unconfirmed
  and the cursor has not moved, and the next pass finds the loss. Stopped after it, before the push reached the remote or before the
  answer to a push the remote kept, the loss and the cursor stand; the next pass pushes the conflict, or fetches it back numbered
  (`IntegrateAsync`'s own-operation branch), and pushes nothing. In each case one conflict is reported by one pass, kept once at the
  remote and on every machine, nothing waits to go up, every number the remote gave is held under it, and the cursor names none the
  remote has not given. No code changed for it: the proof is a guard.
- **A lost take's work goes with it, in the plain order and the raced one.** Plain (B fetches A's reopen before pushing), the rebase loses
  the take, forgets B's bind and submission, and `ForgetStepsAsync` the next step B's submission published, all pending, as CLOSE1e
  does; that held unchanged. Raced (A's reopen, submission and rebind reach the remote between B's fetch and B's push), the remote judged
  the step behind and kept the next step, a quest nothing had moved, so it stood everywhere on a submission no machine held: CLOSE1e's
  *Left* (a step pushed while its parent was behind), reached by the reopen. The remote now answers behind for a pushed step whose source
  it holds without the submission the step grew from (`QuestStore.AwaitsItsSourceAsync`; D68's note), so B goes round and its rebase
  forgets the step with the submission. A source the remote never held (its receiver stays on its machine) waits on nothing, and a step
  from before names no source. A step a done published names none either, so CLOSE1e's *Left* stands for a lost done's follow-up.
- **Said truthfully.** The take's conflict named its lost submission in another machine's words ("the quest's close is the winner's").
  Lost to the person's reopen (`QuestLog.LostToTheReopen`), it now says the reopen reached the remote first and the step is open for the
  work as it stands now (`SubmissionWentWith`), as `QuestExchange.LostTake`'s refusals do. B's claim reads lost, `why: source-reopened`.
- **Compatibility.** An older remote keeps such a step as before, so the standing rule holds: every machine and the remote run the build
  before a reopen. The wire, the log and the cached row are unchanged. On the owner's install no quest grows from a submission, so no push
  is answered differently.
- **Held by** `QuestReopenSyncTests`: the stopped pass (1 theory, before the commit, before the push reached, before its answer; green
  once its cursor's assertion read a pushing pass's cursor as the sync design keeps it, at the fetch's end), the work going with the take
  (1 theory, plain and raced: the raced next step seen red as a probe, the words red in both), and a step whose source never leaves its
  machine going up in the first push (a guard).
- **Left** (rows): B's take of the next step itself, made before syncing, goes with it as a conflict worded "another machine's take
  reached the remote first" (`StepWentWith`), and its claim answers no `why`, so its driver stops that session in another machine's
  words; the step's history holds only that conflict, so the reopen cannot be read from it.

**Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c22, a next step's take that went with a take lost to the reopen says the reopen).** CLOSE1c21's *Left*.
B took the step, bound, submitted, and took the next step its submission published, all before syncing; its take of the step lost to
the person's reopen, and `ForgetStepsAsync` rewrote its take of the next step as a conflict on a quest no list shows.

- **Read where the history still holds it.** The rebase reads the reopen where it loses the submission: the take that submission was
  made on lost, and `QuestLog.LostToTheReopen` reads that take's loss on the replay so far, which already holds its conflict. That
  judgement goes down to every step the submission published, and theirs in turn.
- **Kept on the conflict.** The step's publication went with the submission, so its own history holds only the conflict. The conflict
  says the loss itself: `QuestOperation.LostToTheReopen`, `lostToTheReopen: true` in the log's payload and on the wire, written only on
  a conflict and only when set (D68's note). `QuestLog.LostToTheReopen` reads a marked take conflict as lost to the reopen, so the claim's
  answer (`why: source-reopened`, `LostToTheReopenAsync`) and the driver's stop (`LostClaimToReopen`, `ended.lost-claim-reopened`) say it,
  after a restart too. The mark, not the words, carries it: a note is someone's record and is never parsed.
- **Said truthfully.** `StepWentWith` words it as the take's refusals do (`QuestExchange.LostTake`): the step's submission "went with this
  machine's take of #step: the person's reopen of the work it grew from reached the remote first". Another machine's take, and another
  machine's move on the parent, are worded as before.
- **Once the chain publishes the step anew** (the id comes from the parent and the step), B's conflict goes up on it, mark and words
  alike, and `OnALostTake` refuses B's done, decline or set-up on that open step by the same reading, at B and at a remote, where before it
  would have closed the new step on the stopped session's work.
- **Compatibility.** A conflict with no mark reads as before, and every conflict from before has none. An older build reads a marked
  one as plain (the wire's rule is D68's note), so the standing rule holds: every machine and the remote run the build before a reopen.
  The HTTP answers, the cached row and the page are unchanged.
- **Held by** `QuestReopenSyncTests` (1 theory, plain and raced: the words red first, then the claim's reason red against a probe that
  ignored the mark; the restart, the step published anew everywhere with the marked conflict, and B's done refused), the census
  (`QuestOperationKindsTests`: the conflict's sample carries the mark, red in the log and on the wire; a mark not true or false refused,
  written after its code), `QuestSyncTests` (CLOSE1e's step lost to another machine's take still says that and reads no reopen, a guard)
  and `SessionsOutliveTheirLookTests` (a session whose quest no list shows any more, its claim lost to the reopen, stopped in the reopen's
  words and code; green on the driver as it stood, which reads the `why` it is given).

**Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h17, an opinion holding an ordinary automatic landing is asked by the loop).** CLOSE1h15's *owner's
install* left it: an ordinary entry kept as `opinion` whose due was not open (its rule started reading after the done, or its due closed
`off` and the rule came back) waited on the person's ask alone, though the gate says *it is asked at the driver's next look*. The parent's
questions, decided here:

1. **Owed where the look reads the gate** (`AutoLander.JudgeAsync`): where the gate's refusal is the opinion's, `AutoLander.Owe` owes one
   for the entry's session, quest and tree, as CLOSE1h15's recovery does. It is read at every look that reaches the gate, not only the
   one that first says the hold, so an entry already kept as `opinion` (an install updated with one waiting) is owed at its next look; the
   hold is still said once.
2. **Only at `landing`.** The landing gate reads the opinion's part at the landing's occasion alone (`TreeGates.GateAsync`), so its refusal
   shows the rule reads there, or that a named workflow's opinion step cannot start there, which sits where it is owed as a done's own due
   does. No rule is resolved again, and a `steps` due open does not stand in for it.
3. **What closes it: nothing new.** It is the due the done would have owed had the rule read then, so `OpinionLook.ClosesAsync` closes it
   as any: landed once the landing lands (the person's press too, which closes the entry as `already`), undone, gone, or off where the rule
   stops reading at landing. The entry closing another way does not close it: closed `off` by the switch, the person's press reads the
   same gate, so the opinion is still owed for it; closed `superseded`, the due closes once the tree's work lands or the tree goes, as a
   done's due does. No close code or field is added; the due list's shape is unchanged.
4. **Owed once, and never churned.** A due open for the session at `landing` is left as it is, its time unchanged. One the loop closed as
   `landed` since the entry's done is not opened again, since its look would only close it again at every look (a session whose words went
   on after an earlier landing, D137, whose landing record closes its every due as landed; a tree whose commits a branch of the person's
   holds already). CLOSE1h15's recovery passes no done, and opens one whatever closed the last, as before.

- **The owner's install.** A look that holds an entry by an opinion reads the due list once more for it, and writes it only where none is
  owed. An entry held by anything else, and a repository with no opinion rule, read and behave as before. A due owed at a look is asked at
  the next look, since no watch hears the due list (CLOSE1h15's point 4).
- **Held by** `AutoLandingTriesTests` (Process, Git). The row's proof, seen red first (no due was owed): a done's landing held by an opinion
  nobody asked, its rule added after the done or its due closed `off` before the rule came back, owes one at the landing's occasion, which
  the opinion look keeps owed and would ask (its gate reads not asked, no quest hold before it); two looks owe it once; it lands once when
  the opinion settles, after which the due closes as landed. Seen green before, as guards: a due owed at the done is left as it was, and
  one closed as landed since the done is not opened again. The fixture now holds CLOSE1h15's `Settled` and `ClosesAsync` and the rule's
  `ReadsAnOpinion`, shared with `AutoLandingPushHeldTests`.
- **Left** (row): `OpinionLook.ClosesAsync` closes a due as landed wherever a landing is recorded for its session, one older than the due
  too, so a session whose words went on after its landing (D137) may have its later done's opinion closed before the loop asks it, and its
  automatic landing then waits on the person's ask.

**Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h18, an opinion's due closes as landed only on a landing made since it was owed).** CLOSE1h17's *Left*.
Decided here:

1. **Made since it was owed.** `OpinionLook.ClosesAsync` closes a due as landed by its landing's record only where the due's session's
   work was accepted onto it at or after the due's `At` (`OpinionLook.MadeSince`: `LandedBranch.AcceptedAt`, the landing itself or the
   session's newest advance, LAND2c), as the automatic landing already reads a landing made since its entry's done. A push held back keeps
   it owed, older or not (CLOSE1h15's point 1).
2. **An older landing is read as any tree is.** It lands only the work it held then, so the due falls to the reading a session with no
   landing gets: nothing in its tree that no branch of the person's holds closes it as landed. A session whose words went on with more
   work keeps its later done's due owed; the loop asks it, and the advance its landing then makes closes it. Concluded again with nothing
   new, its tree is on the branch made and the due closes as landed at once, as before.
3. **An older squash is a proof too.** A squashing landing's record says a tree at its `Squashed` commit or behind it is landed
   (PROCESS1b-ii), though git finds no branch of the person's holding those commits. It is asked only where git counts some unlanded, of
   the session's own landing. Without it, a due owed after a squashed landing whose push was held back (CLOSE1h15's recovery, or the
   person's ask) would stay owed once the push landed.
4. **Nothing else moves.** CLOSE1h17's point 4 stands: a due the loop closed as landed since the done is not opened again. Its D137 reason
   no longer closes a done with new work, but a tree whose commits a branch of the person's holds still would at every look. No field,
   code or wire shape changes.

- **The owner's install.** A due whose landing was made after it was owed (every done's own, the look's and the recovery's before their
  landing) closes as before. A due owed after its session's landing (a later done after words went on, the person's ask on landed work, a
  push held back's recovery once the push lands) is read from its tree by git at each look until it closes, as a session without a
  landing is, with one `merge-base` more where its landing squashed. A session that went on with new work is asked its opinion by the loop,
  and its automatic landing no longer waits on the person's ask.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `OpinionGateTests` (fast: a landing accepted at or after the due, its own or the session's advance,
  closes it; one older, by its record, closes nothing, with or without a push held back; this class starts no git, so the older landing's
  tree is read in the Git class) and `AutoLandingTriesTests` (Process, Git: the row's proof, a session landed, carried on and done again
  under an opinion rule, owed at the done or at the look, keeps its due owed with its gate not asked, lands by one advance once it settles,
  and the due then closes as landed; an older landing whose tree went on past it keeps the due owed, plain or squashed). Seen green before,
  as guards: an older landing whose tree stands at its branch's tip, or at the commit it squashed, closes it as landed; CLOSE1h15's
  push-held tests and CLOSE1h17's owed dues pass unchanged.

**Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c24, a step's take that went with its source take moves nothing on the step published anew).** CLOSE1c22
refused it where the source's take lost to the person's reopen. Lost to another machine's take, it still landed: B took the source, bound,
submitted and took the step its submission published, all before syncing, while C's take of the source reached the remote first. Once C's
chain published the same step anew (the id comes from the parent and the step), B's done, decline or set-up on the open step passed
`OnALostTake`, which on an open step read only a loss to the reopen, and closed or set up C's step on the work of B's stopped session.

- **Read where the take landed.** `QuestLog.LostOnTheOpenStep`: the machine's take conflict, the latest, landed on the step open. A take
  loses on an open step only where the step no longer stands on the work the take was made on: withdrawn by the person's reopen
  (CLOSE1c18), grown from another submission than the take's stamp names, or rewritten when its submission went (CLOSE1e) and gone up on
  the step published anew, whose publication comes first in every history, since no push carries the conflict before one holds it. Both
  orders reach it. B's pass before C's chain published the step rewrites the take in `ForgetStepsAsync`; after it, `ForgetStepsAsync`
  keeps off a step the remote numbered, and B's take loses on its stamp, a conflict on C's open step. `OnALostTake`'s open branch reads
  it beside `LostToTheReopen`, so the store's verbs, the remote and the rebase refuse alike.
- **The edge, D95.** A take that lost to another machine's take lands on the step taken, and one that lost to a delete on no quest, so an
  open quest stays nobody's work for those; a fresh take of the open step is still B's to make. Choice, for review: the loss is read from
  the history as it stands, as `LostToTheReopen` reads a reopen, rather than marked on the conflict as CLOSE1c22 marks the reopen. A mark
  would need a second field in the log and on the wire, and a rewrite at both of the rebase's sites, for what every order already shows.
- **Said truthfully.** A refusal on an open step reads the reopen from the history, never from the step being open: the store's move and
  set-up carry it on `QuestMove.LostToTheReopen`, read inside the write, and the remote reads it from the history it judges. Otherwise the
  step "was published anew on other work: the submission this machine's take of it was made on went" (`QuestExchange.LostTake`'s
  `outgrown`), with the reopen's stand-down. The claim's answer (`why` null) and the driver's stop say another machine's take, as
  CLOSE1e's do.
- **Compatibility.** No stored or wire shape changes. A remote on the build before keeps such a move, so the standing rule holds: every
  machine and the remote run the build before a chain crosses machines. On the owner's install nothing binds, so no step grows from a
  submission, no take is stamped or goes with one, and no take loses on an open step: nothing is refused that was not.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `QuestSyncTests` (1 theory: done, decline and set-up, the step published anew after B's pass and
  before it; the store's refusal red, then with it set aside the remote's, then with both the rebase's; B's claim reads lost to no reopen,
  the session's words and the remote's say the step was published anew, never the reopen, and a fresh take holds), and a done from the
  machine whose take lost to a delete still landing on the quest published again (D95, a guard written after the code).
- **Left** (row): in the second order B's take of the step loses on its stamp as a plain conflict whose note is the take's own words: it
  says neither the source's loss nor the step's publication anew, as `StepWentWith` does in the first.

### Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c25): the take's loss on its stamp says why

The row above is closed with words only. In the rebase's move branch a pending take whose stamp differs from the open step's `GrewFrom`,
on a step with no `SourceReopened`, whose loss is not the person's reopen (`QuestLog.LostToTheReopen` over the step's history with the
conflict: a rebind also moves `GrewFrom`, and keeps the take's own words), gets its note, then `StepPublishedAnew`: the step was
published anew on other work, from the submission another machine made of `#{source}`. It is read from the stamp and the step alone,
because the order in which the rebase visits the source and the step is not fixed. Held by the CLOSE1c24 theory, both orders
(`anewFirst` red before). No wire or stored shape changed.

**Note, 2026-10-19 (FABRIC-VERIFY1, verification as a lane beside the work: proposed).** D180 point 7 carries this
repository's local CI to Daoris's own quests, on the owner's word that it fits the fabric's in-flight adjustment and serves
quest sets across repositories and agents. `docs/2026-10-19-verification-lanes-design.md` proposes it, with a read-only Codex
opinion adopted: accepting a step's result and verifying it are two facts, and pending is never passed; the sensitive scan,
a repository's declared blocking set, the ownership and freshness fences, a person's authorization and the install's
exact-commit proof stay blocking; suites, rehearsals, evidence checks and the second opinion may become lanes where the
person chooses; a lane is a durable obligation on an exact candidate that no landing retires; a failure holds what has not
begun, asks the person to reopen (§E5), routes one fix, and never rewrites what closed; the close waits for its lanes at
first. Nothing changes until its first slice (FABRIC-VERIFY1a) is built.

*2026-10-20, CONFIG1:* **`driver.json` written safely: one lock over the re-read and the rename, a set merged by its names, a name
read alike in any case** (CONFIGLOCK1, CONFIGMERGE1, CASEMAP1, gathered as one feature). The second re-check above left three limits on
the keeping write: the read and the move were two steps with no lock, so a change landing between them was lost; a changed array was
the later writer's whole, so two writers each adding a repository to `drivable` kept one; and a name written twice in any case read as
its last spelling's value in the driver and as two names in the terminal (`lines`, `readAcross`, `writeAcross`, `forgiven`).

- **The lock.** A keeping write holds `<file>.lock`, the file's full path with `.lock` after it (`driver.json.lock`), from its
  re-read to its rename. Beside the file, not under the home's `locks/`: a writer is handed a file (`DriverConfig.Save(path)`,
  `DAORIS_DRIVER_CONFIG`, a test's folder), not a home, so two writers of one file meet at one lock wherever it lives (and as the home's
  layout moves, HOMELAYOUT2); and never `driver.lock`, an older build's lease. It is taken by creating it as the lease's file is
  (twins.md: written beside as `<lock>.<random>.writing`, put in place with no overwrite, C#'s `File.Move(…, overwrite: false)` and
  Node's `linkSync`), so it is never seen half written, and holds `{ "pid", "since" }`, `since` in UTC to the millisecond with a `Z`.
  It is removed when the write ends, written or refused, and only while it still says what its holder wrote, so it lives for the
  milliseconds of a write. A writer that finds it waits, looking every 15 ms, for `RecordLock.Wait`'s ten seconds, then is refused as
  a tool error naming the lock and its holder, nothing written: "cannot tell" is never "write anyway". **A stale lock** is one whose
  process is gone, one written more than a minute ago (a write holds it for milliseconds, so a reused process id holds it for a
  minute at most), and one that does not read as that object, since a lock is never seen half written; it is removed only while it
  still says what was judged stale, then taken. A lock that cannot be read this moment is held. The driver's writers of one file queue on its
  gate in their process first, as `RecordList`'s do, so the file is the lock between processes. **Every keeping write takes it**:
  `driver.json`'s (`DriverConfig.Save`, `writeDriverChoices`, a config written whole included) and the shared keeping write
  (`JsonKeeping.Save`, `writeKeeping`: `plugins.json`, `permissions.json`, `keys.json`), since each re-reads and renames alike. A reader
  takes none: the rename is whole. Not `RecordLock`: its handle with no sharing is a lock Node cannot hold, and two Node writers do
  not exclude each other through one.
- **A set merged by its names.** An array of `driver.json` is a set or a value, named in one table both twins carry
  (`DriverConfig.Sets`, `DRIVER_SETS`, held to `driver-config-keeps.json`'s `sets`): `drivable`, `holds`, `trees` and each repository's
  list under `writeAcross` are sets; `commands`' lists are ordered, `heavyGates`' lists are read whole or not at all, and a cycle's
  arrays are its own, so each is a value, written whole as before. An edit of a set is the names it added and the names it took,
  compared without case (`OrdinalIgnoreCase`, `casefold.ts`), measured from the text read as understood to the edited choices, and made
  onto the file's array as it stands: a taken name goes in any spelling, an added one not already there in any case goes at the end in
  the edit's order, and every other element, another writer's names and a value of any other kind, stays where it stands. A set the
  edit took whole (a repository's last target taken back) loses only the names its reader read, and goes where none is left; a set the
  file no longer holds is made with the edit's added names alone; one another writer made something other than an array refuses the
  edit, naming the path, as an object made a scalar does.
- **A name in any case** (CASEMAP1): in every per-name map of `driver.json` both twins read, a name written twice in any case is read
  where first readable, its first spelling and value, as the landing rules, languages and pauses already were. Changed:
  `lines`, `workspaceLines`, `readAcross`, `workspaceReadAcross` and `writeAcross` (the driver kept the first spelling with the last
  value; the terminal kept each spelling), and `forgiven`. `commands` has the driver as its only reader and is left as it is. The
  keeps table's `names` rows hold each map, both twins reading them.
- **Proof:** `driver-config-keeps.json` gains `sets`, `names`, `lock` (the stale rules, row for row) and rows of two writers adding to
  and taking from one set; `DriverConfigKeepsTests`, `KeepLockTests` and the CLI's `configkeeps.test.ts` and `keeplock.test.ts` read
  them. `KeepLockProcessTests` (Process) holds the lock in a CLI process while the driver's edit waits, and in the driver while the CLI's
  edit waits, neither change lost; the CLI's `keeplock.test.ts` does the same between two Node processes.

**Note, 2026-10-20 (COWORK1, ADR-D's delivery and its four states: proposed).** D188 proposes how ADR-D is met for a message
to a working agent, in `docs/2026-10-20-message-urgency-design.md`:
- a level on every message (ordinary, urgent, now), and no message stops a turn;
- one delivery rule over every path;
- each path keeps its own store, and the record's `said` is the one queue to a session, kept before it is told and read
  back after a restart;
- duplicates are refused by id against the record's events;
- the four states are kept as *held*, *sent*, *taken* and *answered*, each where Daoris observes it (the design's §7),
  answered never meaning obeyed.

This closes audit row 6's gap between delivered and acted upon. Nothing changes until COWORK1a is built.
*2026-10-20, PUSH1d1b:* the words kept without a note (a helper's or a second opinion's, through `sessions.said`) have the `words` family, last in `ChangeFamilies.All`: `words_revision.revision`, a count a trigger on `sessions` moves when `said` is updated to a longer list, so a connector built before it still moves it. A trigger, not a counter in `KeepSaidAsync`, because the connector writes the column itself; it moves on a keep and on nothing else (a take shortens or keeps the list, a push mark and a cursor leave `said` alone). A keep with a note moves `sessions` as well. The driver wakes the machine's answers and the sessions' look on it, not the history or any part of the work. Nothing on the page reads it.

*2026-10-20, FABRICRUN1:* the runtime governs every unit it can without a second engine (FABRIC-RUN1b, RUN1c, RUN1d, NODE1b, RUN1g, one
feature, D187). Choices made here, for review:

- **The look's git reads (RUN1b).** `NodeRuntimeLook.Remotes` reads origin's remote-tracking ref of the landed branch
  (`NodeTips.RemotesAsync`, one `RefSnapshot`), and `Holds` git's ancestry (`NodeTips.HoldsAsync`), each through `PassGit` once a
  checkout or a pair a pass, and only where a `pushed` is active or unknown on a landing a plugin answered it pushed
  (`BuiltInNodes.AsksRemote`) or a `pull-request` on an answer completed from another commit (`BuiltInNodes.AncestryOf`, D148 point 4's
  third check). Unread stays unknown; a ref holding another commit is not reached. RUN1's choice 6 (the runtime starts no git) holds for
  every unit with neither node waiting.
- **D157's gates from their records (NODE1b).** `LookNode` reads the person's *reviewed* at the bound commit as the look's reach (its
  authority, the person), their *not yet* as their rejection and their skip as their skip, each in the words the gate kept, and a landing
  at the bound commit by the review it kept; one kept with no review had none asked, which the review's record passes over. `GoAheadNode`
  reads D135's answer to the act its `act` option names (`WireRun.GoAheads`). A rejection or a skip kept with no words moves nothing, since
  the one table asks the person's words. `NodeObservation` gains `By` and `Words` for the person's own moves.
- **The gates in the runtime (RUN1c).** Every built-in of observed reach is begun, `opinion`, `look`, `stop` and an `all` join with RUN1's,
  and `NodeRuntimeLook.Gates` hands a unit's gates in, read once a pass where its opinion or look waits (`DriverWatch.NodeGates`: the landing
  door's `SessionTrees.GateAsync` at the unit's newest session's tree here). Wiring the live gate in found WIRE1g's reading holding an
  opinion for ever where its rule asks none, since that read carries no commit: a `none` with no commit now passes it over, and one read at
  a commit stays that commit's. D157's gates come before the landing, so a branch's cycle cannot hold them; a quest's work cycle can, and on
  a quest unit, whose revision is null, only a rule asking none and a look turned off move them. Naming the branch unit a quest's opinion
  acts on is left to FABRIC-CUTOVER1: a node record is one unit's, and `Keep` writes each status to its own unit's record.
- **A bound run (RUN1c).** A quest whose run is bound to a named version is governed by that version alone (`NodeRuntime.BoundIn`,
  `Govern`'s `versionOf`; §E7, *one governs a run, by its binding*), so Current's work cycle never governs it beside D157's run. Evaluating
  the decoded graph live would be the second engine §E7 forbids, and its nodes span the quest and its branch: CUTOVER1's, with arming by a
  bound run (the owner's ruling keeps the runtime off unless a performed node governs). Canary 38 stands on `WorkflowRunNodesTests`.
- **The runtime's own wakes (RUN1d).** `ChainEvents.Nodes`, heard by link 8 alone with `plugin.step`: the persisted timer and the awaited
  set's change fire link 8 on the loop's chain beside any look (`DriverWatch.NodesWoken`), an awaited wake running the plugins' supervision
  before its pass (after the RUN1e withdrawal), and `HookSet.StepArrived` fires it as a plugin's step is queued. RUN1's limit, a `nodes`
  wake running the local plan, is gone.
- **Reconciliation in turn (RUN1g).** `WorkPerformer.ReconcileAsync` takes the attempts due by unit and invocation, from after the last one
  the pass before reached, round again from the first.

Proof (each seen failing first, unless named): `NodeRuntimeReadsTests` (a branch cycle `handed` → `pushed` → a plugin's step sent once the
stand-in's remote tip holds the pushed commit; unread unknown and another commit not reached; an ahead pull request's three answers; a
cycle needing neither reads none; an opinion and a look turned off moving their nodes and the step after them sent; an opinion waiting
while its gate is reading or settled on a quest; a `stop` reached on its failure path), `GateNodesTests` (each gate's states from records
alone; a `none` with no commit), `NodeLifecycleTests` (canary 43 over every state the three gates' records say, written after the code),
`NodeRuntimeTests` (the no-graph count now also counting git and gate reads, zero, a guard green before and after; four failing questions never starving the fifth; a
bound quest never governed by Current; the home's binding read through its chain, written after the code), `ChainTests`, `NodeWakesTests`
(a `nodes` wake runs the pass and no plan) and, once by class, `NodeRuntimeLoopProcessTests` (a completion kept within a second of its
attempt's record, the loop's start its only look) and `NodeGitReadsGitTests` (one `for-each-ref`, one `merge-base`; written after the
code). `WireEvaluationDecodedTests`' look row for *reviewed* moved to `GateNodesTests`, since the person's reviewed now reaches it.

Left: `WORK_STATE` lists held attempts alone, so a node's reach shows in its unit's record and not yet on the page; a `go-ahead` is not
begun by the runtime, its task HUMAN1's door.

*2026-10-20, GRAPHTWINS1:* both twins judge and save a workflow's graph the same way (FABRIC-GRAPH1c, GRAPH1d, GRAPH1f, GRAPH1k, one
feature, D187). The CLI's `nodegraphs.ts` is the twin of `NodeWires.Problems`, `NodeGraphs.Problems`, `NodeDeclarations.Problem` and
`NodeGraphHomes.OfLanding`, held to one table both read, `test/fixtures/graph-problems.json`: every code of `WireRefusal` (22) and of

The driver's `NodeWiresTests` and `NodeGraphsTests` read their cases from it (`GraphProblemsTable`). Choices made here, for review:

- **The table's graphs are files.** Each row is written as a file holds it and read by each twin's own reader; what no file can say (a port
  on a wire that is not `data`, another unit's node, a synthetic node) is set after reading (`ends`, the `chain` maker), so every code has a
  row. Found on the way, each held by a row: the CLI read `1.0` as a whole number where the driver's `TryGetInt32` reads none, and read a
  graph's cleanup before its nodes and a node's evidence before its ports. It now reads a whole number by its text (`parseWithSource`) and

  and `WithWorkCycle` judge it and refused naming its first problem in the driver's words, a loop among them, nothing written. A cycle this
  build would write into `driver.json` other than as read (a number by other text than JavaScript's, a key that is a number out of order, a
  key twice) is refused too, since `jsonkeep.ts` writes an edit as JavaScript writes it; letters are kept as written. A rule set with
  neither flag keeps its cycle, and says whether it holds under the rule as it now stands. GRAPH1's choice 3 named `daoris driver landing`
  among the doors with no field for the cycle: it has one now.
- **A landing rule that does not read is said (GRAPH1d).** A rule that reads as no rule (no object, no `form`, or one `LandingRules.Problem`
  refuses) is a stray of its own, `unread` (`CycleStray.Unread` through `DriverConfig.RuleOf`; the CLI's `landingRuleUnread`), said with why
  and that the work there lands as though it set none; null stays none. `graph-homes.json`'s `strays` hold it, and `daoris driver list` says
  every stray. Setup's half is not built: the page is outside this branch, and `DriverRoutes`' landing rows carry readable rules alone.
- **A graph version judged and saved (GRAPH1k, GRAPH1f).** `readVersion` judges a graph whole, so `workflow-graph-versions.json`'s
  `judged: "driver"` rows are `both`'s. Workflow files are read and written with every value's text kept (`jsonsource.ts`): an option's
  number by its own text and an object's keys as written, so the rows GRAPH1b's choice 3 refused (`1.0`, a whole number past a double, keys
  that are numbers, a text past the basic plane, now the driver's escape pair) are `both`, and a control is the driver's escape (a new row, its
  digest the driver's). A letter outside ASCII and Chinese stays the CLI's one refusal (a `cli-refuses` row): whether the driver's relaxed
  encoder escapes it is its runtime's Unicode table's, which the CLI does not hold, so the CLI reads such a version as unreadable here and saves
  no graph holding one, while a file holding one is still written over, its text kept. Only a file read with no record of its text

Proof (each seen failing first, unless named): `graphproblems.test.ts` and, on the same table, `NodeWiresTests` and `NodeGraphsTests` (117

A start's first look is whole, which judges every entry and fills both again. *2026-10-20, CLOSEREOPEN1:* a step reopened under its source goes on from its source's newest commit in every ordering of reopen, sync and push
(FABRIC-CLOSE1f, CLOSE1e2, CLOSE1c6, CLOSE1c16, one feature, D187; §E5, *its branch brought up to date*).

- **A lost submission's step, raced (CLOSE1f).** Already held by CLOSE1c21's rule: a step pushed while its parent's push is behind grows from a
  submission the remote does not hold, so it is never numbered, and the next round loses it with the submission. The row's remedy (decline or
  hold a numbered step) is not needed. Proven by the raced case of CLOSE1e's test, another machine's take reaching the remote between the
  fetch and the push, seen red with the rule turned off as a probe.
- **A lost close's follow-up (CLOSE1e2).** The remote answers behind for a pushed step naming no source while it holds the parent not done
  (`AwaitsItsSourceAsync`): such a step was published by its parent's close in the close's own write, so a done this push did not land there
  means the follow-up stands on nothing. Done is terminal, so the wait ends once the done is held; a parent deleted there was never done, so
  its follow-up waits and goes as the done does. The rebase forgets a lost close's follow-up with this machine's unpushed work on it, a take
  rewritten as a conflict naming `#parent's close` (`ForgetFollowUpsAsync` now forgets as `ForgetStepsAsync` does), so its session is stopped
  where it once stood until a person declined it; a lost yes's and a lost review verdict's follow-ups go the same way. Choice, for review: the
  remote reads the parent done, not done and unheld. A release travels with its follow-up, but a set-up step a door publishes after a held
  done would wait there for good.
- **A submission's commit where no evidence is asked (CLOSE1c6).** The end of the session that said the `ready`, and the sweep's, post the
  tree's HEAD as a verdict reading no evidence, naming the submission; the replay keeps it once, on a current submission that waits on no
  evidence and whose commit no verdict gave (`QuestEvidenceVerdict.KeepsCommitOf`), holding and letting go nothing, and
  `Quest.SubmissionCommit` answers it. Choice, for review: a verdict reading nothing, not a new kind or a field on `submitted`. The commit is
  Daoris's read at the session's end, never the agent's word (D144 §1), and the evidence door already carries commit, how, session and
  submission. It is an existing kind's effect changing (`quest-operations.md` row 2): no stored or wire shape changes, an older remote refuses
  such a push, so the standing rule holds (every machine and the remote run this build before a bind), and nothing binds on the owner's
  install. `quest_list`, the trace and the second opinion's instruction say it as the submission's commit, never as evidence read.
- **A session that ran across the reopen and the go-on (CLOSE1c16).** Its end reads the step as the run started it beside the step as it
  stands (`Planner.ReopenedFrom`): taken and unheld, with no result, grown from another submission than at the start. It ends
  `ended.source-reopened`, as a session the hold stopped does, and CLOSE1c2's carry-on brings its branch up to date once. Choice, for review:
  carried on rather than told in the same session (CLOSE1c13): its record ended after the go-on, so the fact cannot say once, and only the
  start's view of the step shows its source moved.
- **Held by**, each seen red first: `QuestSyncTests` (a lost done's follow-up plain and raced, taken or not; the raced lost step, a guard probed
  red), `QuestClosureTests` (the commit kept once for the current submission; refused where evidence is asked, with no submission, or on a done),
  `QuestReopenSyncTests` (the commit on every machine), `McpToolsTests`, `EvidenceAtEndTests` (posted at the end and by the sweep; nothing for
  another session's submission or a known commit, a guard), `PlannerTests` (`ReopenedFrom`; the carry-on once), `TraceTests`,
  `OpinionPromptGoldenTests`, and `GoOnInSyncTests` (Git: the carry-on holds the commit the source's end posted; the no-commit words).
- **Left:** a session cut off across the reopen and the go-on is carried on with the line alone; a `ready` no session said gets no commit; a
  release's follow-up raced past a numbered done stands, as before; and, as for CLOSE1c21's rule, a parent pushed once whose later operations
  never leave the machine holds its step behind at every pass.

*2026-10-20, CLOSEWAIT1:* a close that waits says why, and tidies after itself (the driver half; the page's words wait for the design's review).
**CLOSE1x:** a close whose own judgement waits (no submission, a held one, an ancestor not read, a quest bound to another graph) says
`judged.Waits` once a pass, however many rounds propose it, and nothing once it closes; the fence's stale line is said once a pass too.
**CLOSE1m:** the runtime drops a kept closure graph, and the `closures/` folder with the last, once no quest read bound to it is open and no
governed unit's close attempt is unsettled or its close node live, so a home that bound quests disarms once the last is Done and settled; a
record that does not read leaves all kept, and a graph kept in the last ten minutes stays, since a take binds it after it is kept.
**CLOSE1s:** a plugin start on a bound quest made at a wake reads the units afresh first and is not made if the quest's revision moved
(chain, binding or a branch's tip); it is judged again at the next pass, so no attempt is bound to a tip the pass did not judge.

*2026-10-20, CLOSELAND1:* a landing's push is judged on every session's work it carries (FABRIC-UNITLINK1, CLOSE1h13, CLOSE1h11, CLOSE1h16,
CLOSE1h4, CLOSE1h5, CLOSE1h19, one feature, D187; §C3, §D2, §E5).

- **The record keeps whose work each part is (UNITLINK1, CLOSE1h13).** `LandedAdvance` gains `Quest` (the quest the advancing session
  served, not the chain's first the branch is named for), `Unit` and `Submission` (machine and sequence of the result its acceptance was made
  on); `LandedBranch` gains `Served` and `Submission` for its own session. Each is written only where known, and a record from before reads
  none. The doors' subject carries the served quest and its submission as their own find read it (`OwnSubjectAsync`, an entry's parts).
  `LandedBranch.Work()` lists each session's work with the commit the branch stood at once it was on it. The branch adapter executes and links
  each unit of a landing carrying several from the work the record names it for. Work it names no unit for (an advance from before) reads as
  before, and the landing's own unit is read only where its advances account for every unit, else as before: never a guess.
- **The push is told the record (CLOSE1h11).** `TreeLands` and `TreeHandOffs` hand the push's judgement the branch's record as it reads under
  the branch's hold. `LandedBranch.Unpushed()` is all its work where no plugin answered that it pushed, else what came after the commit pushed.
  The look's push and its hand-on of a push held back judge every earlier session whose unpushed work they carry by the look's rule (taken back
  or held), naming the session and its quest; `PushHeldAsync` waits on the same. The person's press judges them by the person's rule: taken
  back, or submitted again since the record kept its submission; a hold does not. Choices, for review: the advance is made and its push held
  back (CLOSE1h2's shape), not refused; an earlier session's gate is not read again, since its work went by its gate when it landed and the
  pushing session's gate reads the chain's work; the person's hand-off keeps CLOSE1h8's rule (the branch's last session only), being the way on
  every held-back sentence names.
- **A later session made again since its advance (CLOSE1h13).** The person's hand-off holds back a later session whose current submission is
  not the one its advance keeps (*its quest's submission was made again*), reading the advance's quest rather than asking the service. Choice,
  for review: the look's rule does not compare a stored submission. A session readied again at the same commit is never recorded again (its
  landing finds nothing new and closes `already`), so the look would wait on it for good; the person's next hand-off reads it at the door.
- **The gate at the push judges the branch's tip (CLOSE1h16).** `TreeGates.GateAsync`, `ReviewGate.ReadAsync` and the opinion's and the
  workflow's parts take the commit to judge at. The look's push passes `LandingGate.Tip` (a hand-on, the try's tip), so a commit made on the
  tree after its branch holds nothing back.
- **A session ending at its `ready` (CLOSE1h5), confirmed by a real tick before the fix.** The first run's and a carried-on run's conclusions
  read the quest's own status, `Taken`, so a session that took its quest and said `ready` parked as waiting on the person, which no rule makes
  due and the look leaves as live. `Observation.Conclude` reads a current submission as its result in: `completed`, before a failed turn or
  the park, with `ended.submitted`, worded by the page's `statusHint.Submitted`. The sweep's path already ended it `stopped`, due.
- **A done already on its landed branch (CLOSE1h19), confirmed.** Concluded again with nothing new under an opinion rule that read nothing of
  it, the entry waited as `opinion` with nothing to land. The look now closes it `already` before the gate where a standing landing names the
  session, D88's proof finds nothing unlanded and nothing is uncommitted.
- **The owner's install.** Nothing binds there. A push reads more only where its branch carries earlier unpushed work (a plugin that did not
  push, or a push held back): each such session's quest once. A due entry whose session has a standing landing reads D88's proof once more.
- **Held by**, each seen red first unless named: `LandedRecordTests`, `WorkUnitsTests`, `OwnAcceptSubjectTests`, `PressedResultTests`,
  `AutoLandingPushHeldTests` (Git: the row's proof for CLOSE1h11, CLOSE1h13's readied-again hand-off, CLOSE1h16 for an opinion and a review),
  `AutoLandingTriesTests` (Git: CLOSE1h19), `SubmittedEndTickTests` (Process: CLOSE1h5 through the driver's conclusion, its branch landed
  after), `ObservationTests` (written after the fix), and `OpinionLandingTests` and `ReviewLandingTests` (Git: CLOSE1h4's disputed opinion and
  not yet on a bound submission, each released and landing with no Done; green as written, guards).
- **Left:** a person's press with no result in or no quest judges no carried session (the page's map of note codes gained
  `ended.submitted`'s line at the merge); a later session's gate at the push still reads its tree's head.

*2026-10-20, FABRIC-CUTOVER1 (proposed, D190):* the cutover the FABRIC-GRAPH1b note left unbuilt is designed in
`docs/2026-10-20-fabric-cutover-design.md`. The FABRICRUN1 note's open question is answered there: a quest's opinion and look
act on its run's branch and are recorded on the run's own node record, `run:<run>`. No run is governed by a decoded graph, so
FABRIC-RUN1c's left part is withdrawn. Of FABRIC-WORKFLOW2b's differences, `look-beside-opinion` is settled for disputed
findings and commits since, and the rest stay schema 1's alone; D190 points 5 and 6 say how.

*2026-10-20, FABRIC-CUTOVER1 revised (D190, D191):* the note above changes in three places, still proposed. The run's record stays
`run:<run>`, and each of its nodes observes a subject the pass names (the run's newest quest, its newest session's branch at the tip
read, or the run itself), its binding naming that subject; keeping the run's nodes on their subjects' records, as the FABRICRUN1
note leaned, is rejected, since those records have other writers first and the subjects move. `look-beside-opinion` is settled
whole: a graph's look is not wired from its opinion, its timing is the review gate's, the decoder wires it so, and every row of that
difference agrees. GRAPH1i's `protected-wire` already holds the opinion before every landing, so FABRIC-GRAPH1e is dropped, and the
rule no longer asks the opinion wired into the look. A live graph's run is named by its unit alone, each node's place entering its
scope, receipts and firing keys (RUN1f). The cutover design §2, §5.1, §7.1, §9.

*2026-10-20, FABRIC-CUTOVER1 third round (D190, D191):* the revised note above changes in three places, still proposed. Of the
subjects it names, `claimed` on a run waits for its chain's last step here, and what follows `handed` binds the landing's tip and is
told the landed branch by its name and that tip, so a check can follow a landing; a plugin is told its node's subject's unit, never
`run:<run>`. RUN1f works a receipt's id out from its status's own scope, so a receipt kept before it keeps its id. A door holding
for a judgement not made yet asks a whole pass rather than the FABRICRUN1 note's `nodes` wake, which judges only units already
concerned. The cutover design §2.2, §2.4.1, §4, §9.

**2026-10-20, direction: a workflow routes its work into pipelines, and verification is a pipeline of its own (FABRIC-PIPELINE1).**
The owner's direction, given while the install built this repository's rows: the fabric is where how work moves is configured, so a
repository's workflow begins at a start node that routes each piece of work into one of several pipelines the person configures,
and a repository can keep development and verification apart. A development pipeline's sessions build and run only the light
checks their change reaches; verification runs as its own pipeline, CI-like, taking landed work and running the repository's
declared heavy gates (`daoris.gates.json`) outside any working session, its failures becoming work for the development pipeline.
Why: on this repository a driven session that ran the whole test project held its slot to the session timeout and committed
nothing, three times in one day, and the parent ran the full set beside by hand (D180); a written instruction to each session is a
workaround for a route the graph should hold. It extends D157 (a workflow chosen by kind of task; the `check` step kind not built)
and D190 (a bound run governed by its graph). The design is FABRIC-PIPELINE1's, written and read under D191.

*2026-10-21, FABRIC-PIPELINE1 (proposed, D192; revised after Fable's read):* the direction above is designed in
`docs/2026-10-20-pipeline-design.md`. The start node is the choice table, gaining a `landed` route, drawn before the pipelines it
routes to; a pipeline is a named workflow its route chooses, a verification pipeline marked as one when it is created and landing
nothing; a development pipeline's `checks: light` keeps its sessions to the light gates and filtered checks, the heavy gates refused
to them by Daoris and, where the harness takes its rules, by the harness; and verification is a run per batch on the line, kept in a
folder of its own, its `check` a node Daoris performs off the runtime's pass and answers through the performer's reconcile, each
batch run to its verdict, its failure made a fix ask. Its rows are FABRIC-PIPELINE1a to 1j.
*2026-10-21, OVERVIEWNOISE1b:* a unit's row in *What needs you* is named by its session, said once in the page's language, and
listed only where the person has an act; FABRIC-MILESTONE1b's band and FABRIC-UNKNOWN1's unknown owner change as follows.
- **A tree joins the session that recorded it.** The inventory reads the trees this machine's session records name
  (`InventoryFacts.Recorded`: the facts' sessions look for each work part, which a look whose recorded trees moved judges again;
  the service's records for `work state` and `WORK_STATE`). Each tree's unit names those sessions as executors, so a unit with no
  landing names its session, which none did, and its session's page finds its milestone. A tree no standing branch record names
  that a session recorded is that session's, never an unknown owner's: the unit it executes, else a unit of its own,
  `session:<id>`, named by the service's session record (`WorkRecord.Sessions`). While no session holds it, its entry is blocked
  `unfollowed`, since the look's tidy takes recorded branches alone and only the clean-up's press removes it. Rejected: joining
  it to its branch's trace, a retired unit whose kept end judged a branch that went; and re-recording the branch when TREEGONE1
  makes a tree again, since how the install's record went is not known, and the join covers every tree already on disk.
- **A blocked entry carries a code and its facts** beside its detail (`InventoryCode`: `kept` with `why` and `at`, `unfollowed`
  and `unknown-branch` and `unrecorded` with `branch`, `unread`, `unknown-folder`, `unknown-unread`), added to the wire's entry;
  the page words a code it knows (`entryDetail`) and shows the detail, the terminal's sentence, for any other. A kept branch says
  its guard once, the tidy's own sentence where one was kept and the guard's words otherwise, and promises a retry only for a
  guard that frees by itself (a session holding its tree, a folder held open, a start, git or the service not answering).
- **The band lists a unit only where the person's press settles it**: an owner nothing records, or an unfollowed tree. A guard
  the tidy keeps a branch by is said on the work's foot and its branch's line, and a tree holding work to review is its session's
  *Ready for you* row already. The row is named by its session as Sessions names it, else its branch or its tree's folder, never
  the unit id a tree's path makes, and its door opens the window's workspace at Branches, where the session branches' list and
  its presses are. The page's `waitsOnPerson` keeps the same rule.
- **Left:** a moved-aside folder the purge keeps has no code, its reason being the purge's own clause, so it shows its detail;
  *Waiting for your word* stays unbounded, the rows it held being the ones this removes.

*2026-10-21, LANDHOLDSWEEP1:* the landing holds' files are swept, closing the LANDRACE1/LANDPIN2 note's last *Left*
(`Trees/Landing/LandingHolds.cs`).
- **The file's time says when a door last held it.** A hold stamps its file's written time through its own handle when it is
  taken and when it is let go. A file no door has held for longer than `LandingHolds.Wait` is owed to nobody: a door waiting
  behind its last holder took it at the release, and one that came later waited for nothing and clears it.
- **Removed under the sweep's own handle.** `LandingHolds.Sweep` opens such a file alone but for removal, reads its time again
  through that handle, so a door that took and let go of it since it was listed keeps it, and removes it while holding it. A held
  file does not open and stays. A door that meets a removal not yet complete is refused by the system, which `Open` reads as the
  file being held, so it asks again within its bound.
- **When.** A taken hold sweeps its home, at most once per `Wait` in a process, so nothing else has to call it. Rejected: a
  sweep at the look's pace, which would add a caller and a clock for a folder that only grows when a hold is taken.
- **Left:** the age is the wall clock's, as the file's time is: a clock stepped forward past the bound can sweep a file a waiting
  door would have read, and that door judges the work as it stands, as before LANDRACE1. A locks folder whose files are refused
  outright now waits out a door's bound rather than throwing at once. Proof: `LandingHoldsTests` (a file left past the bound
  swept, one held or let go within it kept, another process's held file kept, a taken hold sweeping what was left).

**Note, 2026-10-21 (LANDHOLD3, a waiting press names what it waits behind).** LANDHOLD2's *Left* answered: a door that takes a tree's or
a branch's hold says who it is (`LandingHolder`: the session, and the branch: a branch hold's own, a tree hold's `HEAD` read from git's
files), and the waiting door names it.
- **Written as the hold is taken, unwritten as it is let go.** The holder writes itself to its process's gate before it opens the
  file, and to the file beside what the file keeps for the one behind (a `holder` field next to the attempt's own, so the chain of
  LANDRACE1's outcomes reads as before); on letting go it rewrites the file without itself, so a door that finds the file free a
  moment later never names one that has gone. A write that fails names nobody and never fails the landing.
- **Read by a second handle.** On Windows the holder opens the file sharing reads, never writes, so a door in another process
  reads it through a handle of its own while none can take it beside the holder. Elsewhere a shared read is a shared lock, which a
  second holder would take too, so the file stays held alone there and its holder unnamed. A door in this process reads the gate.
- **Said again once known.** Both waits read again each poll (the in-process wait now waits in 50 ms slices for it), and say
  `waiting` again where the holder comes to be named or changes; never again for one unnamed. The press's step carries the
  holder's session as `behind` on `UNDER_WAY` (`PressUnderWay.Behind`), present only at the waiting step; the page says *Waiting
  behind {{session}}…* / 排在 {{session}} 之后… on Accept and the hand button, else LANDHOLD2's words. `trees land` and `trees hand`
  print the session and branch. The session is named by its id: the review has no list of sessions to title it from.
- **Left:** a landing from a Daoris before this one writes no holder, and is waited behind unnamed.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (KEEPALL1d, `plugin-approvals.json` keeps what its writer does not own; FIX-LOG 2026-10-21).** The KEEPALL1
note's audit left `plugin-approvals.json` still rebuilding: `HelpGrants.Write` wrote `{ "help": [...] }` from the approvals it
read, every other key at the top and in a row gone.

- **The keeping write.** `HelpGrants.Save` hands `JsonKeeping.Save` the approvals as read and as edited, each written by itself as
  `{ "help": [...] }`, so the top level's other keys stay as the file has them, CRLF is kept, a key twice or a file that does not
  read is refused, and a withdrawal of an approval never made writes nothing (it rewrote the file before).
- **A row is kept as written.** Each approval read carries its row as the file held it: a row no edit touched is written as it was,
  numbers and keys this build does not know with it; a row approved again is changed where it stands, its four fields set and its
  own other keys kept in their order (it moved to the end before). A new approval goes last in the writer's layout.
- **Not kept: another writer's row between the read and the write.** The rows are one array, which `Kept` writes whole where an
  edit changed it. The approval's read and write are one call, so the window is the call's; the terminal and the page are the
  writers, both the driver's.
- **The driver's alone.** No CLI verb writes the file, so the table's rows say `only: driver` and `homefileskeep.test.ts` skips
  them. Proof: `HomeFilesKeepTests`' six `approvals:` rows in `home-files-keep.json` (approved again, approved new, withdrawn,
  withdrawn never made, CRLF, a key twice in a row).

**Note, 2026-10-21 (QUESTOPENLOCK1, the quest store's stamp waits for another host; FIX-LOG 2026-10-21).** The CLOSETAKE1 note's
stamp is a write at every open, and on the install two of eight connectors started together exited at it with *database is
locked*, so their sessions had no quest tools.

- **Why it failed.** The SQLite driver waits out a busy lock only at a statement's first step. The stamp's
  `INSERT … ON CONFLICT … RETURNING version`, run on its own, wrote there and committed when its reader ended, where nothing waits;
  the file keeps a rollback journal, so that commit needed every other host's read to have ended, and one under way failed it.
- **The stamp is a write transaction of its own**, the gate's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, whose `BEGIN` and `COMMIT` the driver waits on (up
  to its command timeout, 30 s). What it stamps and what it refuses are as the CLOSETAKE1 note says.
- **Every `RETURNING` write runs in one.** Made so: a channel post (`ChannelStore.KeepAsync`, `RETURNING urgent`) and a registration
  (`RegistrationStore.UpsertAsync`). Already so, each taking the caller's transaction: an operation appended (`AppendAsync`,
  `RETURNING sequence`), one kept from another machine (`KeepAsync`, `RETURNING position`) and both of a take's bind reads
  (`TakeBindAsync`, `RETURNING graph`, and `RETURNING graph, session, …`). A source scan in `ConnectionGateTests` refuses the next
  one written on its own.
- **No retry around a host's whole open.** Each statement of the open now waits out another host's hold for the command timeout,
  which is the bound; a second loop around the composition would outlast the agent's own wait for its connector, and the
  composition does not close a half-made store to start again.
- **Proof.** `ReturningLockTests`: while another host holds a read for half a second, the quest store opens (once, and on four
  connections at once), a post and a registration are kept; red on the old stores with the install's stack. Its driver pin shows
  the write on its own busy at its reader's end, and in a transaction waiting at its `COMMIT`.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (KEEPALL1b, `harnesses.json` keeps what its writers do not own; the KEEPALL1 note's question on D165 §6's
rewrites, decided).** Both writers, `HarnessSettings.Save` and the CLI's `writeHarnessSettings`, now write the file as KEEPALL1's
writers write theirs (`JsonKeeping.Save`, `writeKeeping`): the edit's changed key paths made onto the file read again under its lock,
unknown keys kept where they stand at every level, CRLF kept, a file that does not read, holds a key twice or made a changed object a
scalar refused, and no edit nothing written. Settings built in memory and never read from the file are written whole, as before.

- **D165 §6's rewrites stay the writer's, as an edit.** The file as its reader understood it carries each agent's settings entry as
  the file holds it, its old names (`early`, `near`) and the retired `prefer` and `parallel` included, where the edited choices carry
  it as the writer writes it. So a save drops the old and retired names and writes `switch` and `curve` in their place wherever an
  entry holds one, edited or not, as D165 §6 and D130 §16.6 say, and a save over a file holding none writes nothing.
- **Rejected:** measuring an entry from its written form, so that only an entry an edit touched lost its old names. An old name
  reads on over the entry's own `switch` (the SWITCH1a note), so a `switch` an edit wrote beside an `early` the file kept read back
  as the old name said, and the folded form made a `switch` the file never held look already written. Keeping the old names out
  of every save was rejected with it: a save that leaves an old name lets the next older build's read overturn the person's choice.
- **No longer rewritten, now kept as the file has them:** a value of the writer's own sections it does not read (a default that is
  not text, a workspace that is not an object; an edit inside one is refused), a workspace written twice in any case (read as one,
  as before, and both kept), the fields of an entry in the order written, and a curve's windows in their order. An entry's value the
  edit changes is written in the writer's form. A number written longer than JavaScript writes it (`30.0`) is changed by the
  driver's writer where its entry changes and kept by the CLI's, which reads through `JSON.parse` (KEEPALL1c).
- **Proof** (fast): `harnesses.json` rows in `home-files-keep.json` (both twins); `RotationTwinTests`' file rows and
  `rotation-use.json`'s `write`, `change` and `order` rows re-decided in the shared tables.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (LANDGIT1c, one spelling per ref fact).** LANDGIT1's count asked the line's commit as `refs/heads/main` (the line
guess, `WorkingTree.DefaultBranchAsync`, and `TreeGit.ComparableAsync`) and as `refs/heads/main^{commit}` (every commit read), and
the head as `HEAD` (the look's tree read, a waited landing's head) and as `HEAD^{commit}`: one fact, two commands, so no count could
see that it was asked again. Each is now `<ref>^{commit}`, which answers whether the ref is there as the bare name did and is the
spelling LANDGIT1b's passing down will key on. Nothing is kept by it: a ref read is git's every time, as LANDGIT1's note says.
- **`status` asked once until a write.** The landing's first read, SQUASHTIDY1f's judgement (`TreeWorkReads.WorkAsync`), counts
  the tree's uncommitted paths, and the dirty check of the merge or the branch (`TreeLands.MergeAsync`, `BranchAsync`) is handed
  that count instead of asking `status` again; it asks only where the judgement could not say. The look's automatic landing reads
  `status` where it judges the entry (`AutoChoice.Uncommitted`) and hands it to the landing (`TreeLands.LandAsync`'s
  `uncommitted`), so the judgement asks nothing either; a landing that waited behind another of its tree reads it again. Nothing of
  the landing writes in the tree between the two: a replay is made aside, and the tree follows only after the merge or the branch.
  `status` stays out of `GitFacts`: what the files hold is handed down by the flow that read it, never kept by command.
- **Proof.** `LandGitCountTests.Judge` judges `status` as a question asked once until a write (by its working tree, since the
  checkout's own clean check is another tree's files) and refuses a ref or the head read under two spellings; its ceilings fall to
  53 (the look's), 50 (the squash press) and 42 (the merge press), from 55, 51 and 43. `StartGitCountTests` names the new spelling,
  and judges a repeat as LANDGIT1's count does, across a write: the line's commit read after `worktree add` is the guess's command
  now, asked again past the branch that add made.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (LANDGIT1b, the head and tip reads passed down).** LANDGIT1's *Left* answered without keeping a head or a ref
read in `GitFacts`, whose table still never holds one (`What_another_process_moves_is_asked_of_git_each_time` unchanged and green):
each saving is a read whose answer the flow already held a moment before, handed down as a value, or one whose answer nobody used.
The automatic landing now starts 44 git processes (55), the press on a squash rule 46 (51), on a merge rule 40 (43).

- **The look's try takes the rule's plan.** `AutoLander.LandOneAsync` kept only the plan's plugin and the branch it names, yet built
  the press's whole plan (`TreeLands.PlanAsync`): the named branch's tip, the tree's repository and branch, the line's tips and the
  head, all read again by the landing itself. It asks `PlanRuleAsync`, which names both.
- **One read of the tree's repository and branch per landing.** `TreeLands.LandingAsync` reads `CommonDirAndBranchAsync` once, for a
  tree of this home's that is there, and hands it to the content proof (`TreeWorkReads.DiscardOfferAsync`), the judgement against the
  line (`TreeLandSyncs.JudgeAsync`), and `MergeAsync` or `BranchAsync`; each asked it again before. Null reads it as before, so every
  other caller is unchanged. The content proof also takes the tree's own branch (TREEFOLLOW1) that `WorkAsync` read a moment before.
- **The line's two forms are one snapshot.** `JudgeAsync` reads the line here and origin's copy through one `RefSnapshot` taken after
  its fetch (PROC1d-ii), where `LatestAsync` asked the line here a second time; the snapshot ends with the judgement.
- 🔴 **What an act rests on is still read right before it.** The commits a branch is made from are read from the branch as it stands
  (`log against..branch`), and a pinned tip must still be its last (FABRIC-END7's review); the tip a replay or a squash is made from is
  read by the judgement itself, never handed down; the tree's move reads its head and branch again (`MoveTreeAsync`); every check of
  the root before a merge is made in `MergeAsync`; and the end keeping and the tidy, after `GitFacts.Close()`, read git as it stands.
  The value handed down is the tree's repository and the name of its branch, which no git between its read and its use changes.
- **Rejected:** keeping head and ref reads in `GitFacts` again (LANDGIT1's first version and its review's three cases); a snapshot
  held across the whole planning, which the writes in it (the fetch, a replay, the branch made) drop anyway.
- **Left:** the dirty check's `status` and the two spellings of one ref fact (LANDGIT1c); the tidy's own reads.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (KEEPALL1e, `remotes.json`'s writers audited and kept; FIX-LOG 2026-10-21).** The KEEPALL1 note left
`remotes.json` with its three twins unread. Audited:

- **Two writers, both rebuilding.** The page (`RemotesModule`'s `SET` and `REMOVE`, through `RemoteTarget.Save`) and the terminal
  (`daoris remote add|remove`, through `writeRemotes`) wrote `{ workspace: { url, key } }` from the remotes their readers parsed,
  sorted by name: every other key at the top and in an entry, an entry missing half its pair (which every reader skips) and CRLF went.
  The page's reader read a map that did not read as no remotes and wrote its one entry over it; the CLI's refused. **The service's
  `RemoteConfig` writes nothing:** it is a reader, held to what the writers write.
- **The keeping write.** Each writer reads the file as it stands, every entry as written, whole or not (`RemoteFile.Load`,
  `readRemoteFile`), edits one workspace's entry, and hands `JsonKeeping.Save` / `writeKeeping` the entries as read and as edited.
  What the edit did not change stays as the file has it: numbers by their own text, CRLF, another writer's change since the read; a
  file that does not read or holds a key twice is refused, and the page says it as `DRIVER_REFUSED` with the sentence. Removing a
  workspace never wired, or never read, writes nothing.
- **Which entry an edit changes.** A workspace is found as every reader finds it, trimmed and without case (`OrdinalIgnoreCase`,
  `casefold.ts`): wired again, the entry the readers read for it is changed where it stands under the file's spelling, its own other
  keys kept; else the last entry under its name in any case, so an entry missing half its pair is completed rather than shadowed; else
  a new entry goes last. Removed, every whole entry under its name goes whole, its unknown keys with it; a half entry stays, since no
  reader reads it as wired.
- **Rejected:** keeping the map sorted by name. The file is the person's too, and a sort at each save moves what they wrote; KEEPALL1b
  and KEEPALL1d keep the file's order for the same reason. `remotes.test.ts`' case-folding row now asserts the four rows in the
  order they were wired.
- **`RemoteTarget.Save` stays** for a map built in memory (the tests' set-ups), written whole; no door calls it.
- **Proof** (fast): ten `remotes:` rows in `home-files-keep.json` (no edit, wired again, wired again in another case, a new
  workspace, a half entry completed, removed, CRLF, another writer's change between, a torn file, a key twice), each with `reads`,
  the map the readers read from what was written. `HomeFilesKeepTests` and `homefileskeep.test.ts` check the bytes and their own
  reader; the service's `RemoteConfigKeepTests` reads every row's result, so the table is read by all three. `RemotesModuleTests`
  holds the page's door; the merge tool sends the table to the service gate too.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (KEEPALL1c, the home files that already kept keys keep their numbers and line endings too; FIX-LOG 2026-10-21).**
The KEEPALL1 note left `tools.json`, `resources.json`'s locations among it, and `browser/settings.json` keeping their keys but
not their numbers in the CLI, their CRLF in the driver, nor refusing a key written twice in either.

- **The keeping write, in both twins.** `Tools.SetWay`, `AddLocation` and `RemoveLocation` (through `Tools.Keep`) and the
  CLI's `setWay`, `addLocation` and `removeLocation` (through `keepTools`), and the modules' `BrowserSettings.Set` and the
  CLI's `setField`, hand `JsonKeeping.Save` and `writeKeeping` the file as read and as edited, both written by the writer: every
  number by its own text (the CLI wrote `1.50` as `1.5` and `9007199254740993` as `…992`), CRLF kept (the driver wrote LF), a
  key written twice refused naming it, and a setting set as it already is writes nothing (both rewrote the file). The edit still
  changes the node read at the write, so another writer's change landing between them is the lock's, as before.
- **A key written twice reads as `JSON.parse` reads it in the driver.** `Tools.Load` and `BrowserSettings.Load` parse through
  `JsonKeeping.Once`: a node threw an `ArgumentException` at its first look, so the driver's readers and the Browser screen
  failed on a file the CLI read with the last value. An edit of such a file is refused by the keeping write.
- **Not kept:** a number inside `locations` that an edit changes is written as the CLI's `JSON.parse` read it, since the list is
  one value written whole; the list holds addresses.
- **Proof** (fast): six `tools.json` and five `browser/settings.json` rows in `home-files-keep.json` (no edit, each door's edit,
  CRLF, a key twice). `HomeFilesKeepTests` reads the tools rows and the modules' `BrowserSettingsTests` the browser rows, the
  table's gate row naming the modules; `homefileskeep.test.ts` reads all eleven.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-CLOSE1y, an unknown close said as its door's silence).** CLOSE1u's choice 3 worded an unknown close as a

  `WorkMilestone.test.tsx` (the work's foot in English and in 中文).

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-PERFORM1j, a node record that does not read said where the person looks).** The FABRIC-PERFORM1g note left
a unit whose node record does not read said in the loop's lines alone, while nothing on that unit is judged or started.
- **Listed.** `NodeRecordFiles.Unread` names each file that does not read, the unit its text still names where that unit's key is

  named or the file's key. `WORK_STATE` answers them as the additive `unreadable` ({file, unit, at, said}), the work slice pushes them
  in `<held>` beside the held attempts, and `daoris-driver work held` says each after the attempts held.
- **The person's row.** *What needs you* has a `work` row per record (`NeedsAPerson`, id `record#<file>`, since its last write,
  where its unit), named by its unit or else its file, saying nothing moves until it is mended. Its one press opens the records'
  folder (`WORK_RECORDS_FOLDER`, a window's route like a session's folder: the module names the folder and answers only whether it
  opened, refused where no record is kept). Daoris never mends, empties or removes a record: an attempt it may hold could be sent twice.
- **Held by** (fast): `NodeRecordFilesTests` (listed with its unit, none for a file not JSON, a unit picked, the terminal's line),
  `DriverModuleLinesTests` (`WORK_STATE` listing it, by a unit picked; the folder refused with no record), `WorkStateSliceTests`
  (the fields classed and pushed), `AttentionStateSliceTests` (the row), `PipeCensusTests`, `StatePublisherTests`, `HelpCoverageTests`,
  and the page's `attention.test.ts` (its words in English and 中文, its act), `AttentionBand.test.tsx` (the press and a refusal) and

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-GRAPH1d, Setup names a landing rule that does not read).** GRAPHTWINS1 left Setup's half: the page and the

- **The rows.** `STATE` answers `unreadLandings` [{repository, why, said}] and `workspaceUnreadLandings` [{workspace, why, said}] beside
  `landings` and `workspaceLandings`, from `DriverConfig.UnreadLandings`, which reads the file's text as `RuleOf` does: each rule that
  reads as no rule, by its name as written, in the file's order; `why` is `object`, `form` or `refused`, and `said` the driver's sentence;
  a rule written null is none. Both are pushed in the driver slice's `choices`, and a shell older than them answers neither.
- **The page.** A repository's Setup says its own unread rule under *How work lands*, matched without case as the driver matches it, or,
  where it has none and lands by Daoris's merge, its workspace's; a workspace's Setup says its own under its *Defaults*. Each opens its
  section, since the person wrote something nothing reads. `object` and `form` are worded in en and zh; `refused`, and a reason a later
  driver adds, carries the driver's sentence through. Choice, for review: no *Clear* is offered for an unread rule; setting one is the
