**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-PERFORM1k, a failure kept for the person shown, with their new attempt).** RUN1e kept a failure Daoris
will not retry for the person (a code its step does not declare transient, or one whose tries are spent, of this graph or another),
and PERFORM1g's note left it "neither listed nor offered its new attempt on the page". §E2's `failed → ready` by the person was in the
one table already; nothing reached it.
- **Shown** beside the held attempts, through the same list and doors rather than a second set: `HeldAttempts.Read` lists every
  attempt `WorkPerformer.KeptFailure` names (a plugin's attempt, settled, no `retryAt` pending, its node failed on it), `why`
  `failure-kept` or `tries-spent` (its status transient), the failure's `code`, and one word, `again` (`HeldResolutions.Failure`).
  `WORK_STATE`'s `held` carries it with an additive `code` (pushed in the work slice), `daoris-driver work held` lists it saying
  *failed*, What needs you has its `work` row with *Try again*, and the work's foot says it under *Held for you*, en and zh.
- **Choices made here** (for review):
  1. **Read from the record alone.** Whether a retry is coming is the attempt's persisted `retryAt` (set only while tries are left,
     cleared as `Due` applies or refuses it), so the lists and the doors need no declaration, as PERFORM1g's do not. An observed
     node's failure dispatched nothing and has no attempt to restart, so it is not listed.
  2. **The word is recorded, then applied by the runtime** (PERFORM1g's choice 1): `WorkPerformer.Word` writes `again` on the failed
     attempt under the lease; `Resolve` applies `failed → ready` by the person under the governing declaration, and the next evaluation
     starts a new attempt with a new key and invocation, its tries counted afresh. A node that moved off the failure meanwhile (a new
     revision lapsed it) moves nothing.
  3. **The word is cleared as it is applied.** The failure stays its plugin's: a settled attempt keeping `again` would read as the
     person's outcome, which PERFORM1g's `resolution` means; and a build before this reads a record holding `again` as unreadable, so,
     as the closer does with a close's words (CLOSE1q), only a word not applied yet is ever written. The history keeps `person-again`.
  4. **Each kind takes its own words**: a held attempt's three on a failure, and `again` on an attempt held, are refused (`not-again`)
     with a sentence saying which word fits, nothing written.
- **Proof** (fast): `NodeRuntimeTests` (3: a non-transient failure listed with its code and kept across passes, *again* restarting it
  with a new key and invocation, the failure still the plugin's and the word cleared; a transient failure not listed while its retry
  is coming and listed as `tries-spent` once spent, the words refused across kinds, without the lease and on an earlier attempt;
  *again* on a held attempt refused), `WorkStateSliceTests` (the `code` field classed and pushed) and `WorkMilestone.test.tsx` (the row,
  *Try again* pressed, the word recorded, the foot in English and 中文).

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-CLOSE1j, "closed by you before its end" and "partially closed" on the page).** CLOSE1b's points 6 and 7
kept both ends as history entries and said them once in the pass's lines; the page read neither, and §E5's offer to stop the node had
no door. Choices:
1. *The override keeps an entry for every node it stops.* `WorkPerformer.Kept` writes history only where a move cleared something, so
   a `ready` node stopped by the override left none, and its end could not be read back. The override now appends `overridden` for
   each node it stops (`NodeRuntime.Settle`), the node's status before the stop as `Was`.
2. *Read, never stored.* `GraphEnds.Read` derives each end from a record's history (§D0): an override per moment, naming its stopped
   nodes; a part lost per `partially-closed #<quest>` entry, with its close node, that node's state and newest attempt. `WORK_STATE`
   answers them as `ends`, additively, and the work slice pushes them in the `<held>` partition (`graphEnd` shape).
3. *The node to stop is the close node, live again.* Measured on `QuestCloseTests`' chain: once a quest the close named is lost, the
   chain's revision moves, so the closing unit's close node is invalidated and judged ready; when the winner's take submits,
   `QuestClosing.Judge` would name it, and this machine would close it over that take. The person's stop is that node's.
4. *PERFORM1g's door, its stop alone.* `WORK_HELD` (and `daoris-driver work held <unit> <node> <attempt> stop`) takes `stop` on the
   close node's newest attempt, settled, while a `partially-closed` entry names the node and it is live (`QuestClosing.Partly`); any
   other word is refused `part-stop`. `QuestCloser.Resolve` applies it: the unit's live nodes stopped by the person, the attempt left
   settled, its door's answer standing for the quests it kept. A stop is not a binding's (`NodeStates.Bound`), so a new tip keeps it.
5. *Where it is said.* The milestone's *Close* row and its line, en and zh, for a unit carrying an end; and a What needs you row for a
   part lost while its stop is offered or the person's stop waits for the next look. An override is the person's own act and no row.
- **Not built here** (a row): a closure graph governs `quest:` units, which no milestone on the page carries, so an override is said
  only in the pass's lines and a part lost only in What needs you while its stop is offered: FABRIC-CLOSE1j2. The terminal does not
  list the ends.
- **Held by:** `QuestCloseTests` (the override read with both nodes; a part lost read, `resend` refused, `stop` recorded and applied,
  the winner's submission never closed), `WorkStateSliceTests` and `AttentionStateSliceTests` (the field census, the row while
  offered or worded and none once stopped or overridden), `WorkMilestone.test.tsx` (both ends on the work's foot and line, the row's
  press, in English and in 中文).

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-PERFORM1n, a believed event the one table refuses is said).** §C4, §E2, on PERFORM1i's acceptance. An
observed event believed by all five rules, whose move the one table then refused on its node's binding (evidence of another schema than
the node declares, no evidence where one is declared, an output undeclared or of another schema), came back `refused`, a start's word,
which `WorkObserver.Counted` does not count: it moved nothing and was said nowhere. A completion the table refuses holds its attempt for
the person (`WorkPerformer.Hold`); an event has no attempt to hold.

- **Choice.** The event is `not-taken` with the table's own code (`no-evidence`, `output-schema`, …) and counted as a `plugin.step` line
  of that code, as §C4's belief codes are: no new code, never the frame's words. Nothing is written: the node stays awaiting, the event is
  not kept in `observations`, so a later event of the declared schema at the same commit is taken and not ordered behind it. A record the
  edit could not write (the lease lost) stays `refused` and uncounted: that is Daoris's, not the plugin's.
- **Held by:** `ObservedEventTests.A_believed_event_the_one_table_refuses_is_a_counted_refusal_and_no_state_changes` (seen failing
  before the change): the refusal counted once as `no-evidence`, the record byte-identical, no wire fired, and the declared schema's
  event reached after it.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-CLOSECASE1, one rule for "the same repository" across the close).** The service's `NextStep` (whether a
step inherits its requirements' evidence) and its close door (FABRIC-CLOSE1v's `other-repository`) compared a quest's repository
blind to case; the driver's `QuestClosing.Judge` (a step here still to come) and `Lineage` (which ancestors are this repository's)
compared it exactly. A step naming `Engine` after an `engine` quest was published as the same tree's, but the driver closed the first
quest alone and its chain's last without it. Choice: **blind to case on both sides**, the service's rule. It is the rule every other
layer holds for repository names (the rev3 review's F5 and F6, D125's CASEFOLD notes), the rest of the driver's close path already
groups repositories so (`TipsAsync`, `RootOf`, `BuiltInNodes`' step check), and an exact rule would have to change what the service
has already published and closed. Rejected: exact comparison on both, which would refuse a chain whose steps the service already
treats as one tree's. **Held by:** `QuestCloseTests` (a chain whose step differs only in case: one lineage, the first quest's close
waiting, one close at the last naming both) and `QuestClosureTests` (the same chain published with its evidence whole and closed at
its step by one close).

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-CLOSE1d3, the page reads a source's reopen and its submitting session).** After CLOSE1c4's wire and CLOSE1d2.
A reopened source is taken with no submission, so the page read it as one whose agent had not yet said `ready`.

- **The type.** `api.ts`'s `Quest.reopened` (`QuestReopen`, the wire's `QuestReopenWire` with CLOSE1c8's `session`) and
  `QuestSubmission.session`, each absent where unset and from a host before them. No service or driver change.
- **The page** (`QuestPage`): a taken quest with no submission and a `reopened` says *Reopened* among its facts, with its moment, that
  it waits for its agent to make it ready again, the person's note quoted as written (`Quoted`, now exported from `Requirements.tsx`)
  or that they gave none, and *the session that submitted it, s-…, is told* where the reopen names one. *Submitted* adds *by session …*
  where the submission names one. The note is content and stays untranslated; the chrome is en and zh.
- **Choices made here, for review.** The fact is drawn only while the source is taken and unsubmitted: the service answers the reopen
  until the next `ready` answers it, so a submitted quest never carries one, and a closed quest's last reopen is history its moves
  already show. *Is told* does not claim the session has read it yet: CLOSE1c8 tells a running session once it parks.
- **Held by:** `api.test.ts` (both read through `api.quests`, neither from an older host) and `QuestPage.closure.test.tsx` (the reopen

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-UNIT1e, a plugin's steps shown where its other declarations are).** The design's §D9: UNIT1d read a
manifest's `steps` and `resources` and showed them nowhere. Choices:
1. *One set of words, both twins.* `PluginSteps.NameOf` and `Said` (a step, a resource), and the CLI's `stepName`, `stepSaid` and
   `resourceSaid` in `steps.ts`, say a step as its manifest's name, then its reach and version, then each part it declares that is
   not the default (ports, evidence, retry, timeout, `not skippable`, `rejectable`, `idempotent`, `reconciled`, `transient …`, or
   `perform declared` for an empty `perform`); a resource as `<schema> (<role>)`.
2. *An update says a step whole.* `changesBetween` and `PluginChange.Between` gain `steps` and `resources`, so a declaration that
   changes without a new version is still a change, never "declares the same".
3. *A description names each step with its reach.* `describe` (`plugin list`, `plugin add`) and `PluginPackageCommand.Describe`
   (`plugins install`) add `declares step(s) <name> (<reach>), …` and `creates <schema> (<role>), …` after the skills.
4. *The page reads the list's answer.* `PLUGINS` answers each plugin's and offer's `steps` (name, reach, version, inputs, outputs,
   evidence) and `resources` (schema, role); the plugin page and the offer's page list them after Skills, a reach or a role the page
   does not know said as sent. `daoris-driver plugins show` (`PluginPageRead.Steps`, `Resources`) says each in choice 1's words.
- **Held by:** `PluginStepsTests`' two theories (an update's step changes, 11 rows; a description, 4 rows), which
  `plugin-steps.test.ts` parses and holds its `CHANGE_ROWS` and `DESCRIBE_ROWS` to; `PluginPageTests` (the page and `plugins
  show`), `DriverModulePluginPageTests` (the list's answer) and `PluginPage.test.tsx` (a declared step and what it creates, in
  English and in 中文).

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-PERFORM1q, a held event's unit judged first in its pass's turn).** On PERFORM1o: past `UnitsPerPass` units, the
turn's rotation could leave out the unit whose event was held, so its node was not rebound and the event was refused `stale` after all.

- **The shape.** `NodeRuntime.Turn` takes the units whose events the hearing held first, in id order, outside the rotation; the rotation
  (RUN1e's, from the unit after the last one it took) fills the rest of the turn, and moves on only by the units it took. With no held
  event, or at most `UnitsPerPass` units concerned, nothing is left out as before; the held units now come first in the evaluation's order.
- **Why first rather than one more.** A turn past its bound would let the held events of a busy pass grow it without limit; taking the
  held units inside the bound keeps RUN1e's cap, and judging them first puts their rebinding ahead of the pass's other starts, which share
  its four sends. The rotation does not count them, so a held unit never costs another unit its turn.
- **Left.** Held units past a whole turn (more than `UnitsPerPass` units with an event held in one pass) are taken in id order and the
  rest refused `stale` as before; so is an event whose node its evaluation did not rebind (a tip not read).
- **Proof** (seen failing first on the old turn): `ObservedEventTests` (the 34-unit case: A falls past the rotation's turn, yet is judged
  first, its event taken in that pass with no refusal; the two units the rotation left are reached at the next pass, and at the look's
  deadline the rotation resumes with them; every unit rebound to the moved tip). It replaces PERFORM1o's test of the refusal there.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-PERFORM1p, a branch unit's performed nodes and told tip on the pass's tip too).** PERFORM1m's *Unchanged, and
why*: a branch unit's observed node bound to the tip read in the pass, while its performed nodes, the tip `work/watch` told and an answer's
lapse (`WorkBelief.Lapsed`) stayed on the unit's revision, the end fact's. So a performed node wired after an observed node reached on a
fresh tip started on the lagging revision, and ran again when the end fact caught up. PERFORM1m kept them together on the old revision
because moving one alone lapses every attempt; this moves all three together.

- **The binding.** A branch unit with an observed plugin node (`Tipped`) binds its performed plugin nodes to the tip read in the pass
  (`NodeRuntime.TipRevisions`, by node id), as its observed ones. A performed node not bound yet whose tip the pass did not read is left
  ready and sent on no guess, as an observed one is left unbegun; a bound one keeps its binding's revision, so nothing lapses on a failed
  read.
- **What is told.** Such a unit is told to its plugins (`GovernedUnit.Watched`'s tip, `NodeRuntime.Told`) with the tip its pass read, else
  the tip told before (kept by unit while it is governed, cleared at a disarm), else its revision. Telling reads no git
  (`PassTips.Known`): a pass that read the checkout for something else tells what it read. A rebinding now changes the frame told, and
  wakes the loop once more (`AwaitedChanged`), which PERFORM1m's "the frame told is unchanged" no longer holds.
- **An answer's lapse.** Before the pass hears its completions it reads, once, the checkout of each tipped unit an answer names
  (`WorkPerformer.UnitsOf`), and `WorkBelief.Lapsed` judges each on that unit as told now: an attempt on the tip is taken on its binding,
  not applied as a lapsed attempt's against the lagging revision.
- **Unchanged.** A branch unit observing nothing reads no git and binds, tells and judges on its revision, as before; a quest unit's
  revision is null by design. One read of each checkout a pass holds: the hearing's read is the read the evaluation, the rebinding and
  telling share.
- **Proof** (fast, each seen failing first): `ObservedEventTests` (a performed node after an observed one reached on the fresh tip sent
  once on it, told it, its completion taken, the end fact catching up sending nothing again, one tip read a pass; a completion heard while
  the tip is unread judged on the tip told, not the lagging revision; PERFORM1m's and PERFORM1o's tests now see the notify on the new tip
  and the rebound tip told). `ObservedTipsGitTests`, `NodeRuntimeTests`, `NodeRuntimeReadsTests` and `WorkPerformerTests` unchanged and
  passing.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-UNKNOWN1c, an unknown owner's row has a door).** UNKNOWN1's row opened the workspace's Branches since
FABRIC-MILESTONEDOOR1, where the Discard branch press moved from Settings' sweep. Choices:
1. *Matched on the page, not in the driver.* The inventory makes an unknown owner only where no record names the branch, so the
   driver has no session to join; the page holds the sessions list, whose `tree`'s last folder is the branch's name after `daoris/`
   (the rule `WorkFrame` already finds a session's own branch by). `attentionWorded` matches a `work` row with `blocked.unknown` and no
   `session`, by its entry's `branch` fact or else its title, to a session in the same repository, and gives it as `cutFor`.
2. *A door, not an owner.* The row keeps its branch as its title and `session` stays null: a name matching is not a record, so it
   is not said as the session's. Only the door changes: `cutFor` opens that session, whose head offers the branch's *Discard
   branch…*; with no match, or for any other `work` row, it opens Branches as before. A held attempt still has no door.
- **Held by:** `attention.test.ts` (the session matched; none in another repository, by no tree, or with no sessions in hand) and
  `sessionsBadge.test.tsx` (the row's *Open* lands on Sessions, not Branches). The installed window is not yet seen.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-CLOSE1h14, a push held back by its gate said as the gate's in the conversation).** After CLOSE1h12, whose
gate at the push holds an automatic landing's push as a quest's hold does, but whose note led with "its quest was held or taken back"
(`landing.push-held`), the gate's sentence only beneath it.

- **A note code of its own.** `landing.push-gated` (branch, plugin) leads with *its landing's gate held it before `plugin` was told*,
  and the first look once the gate lets it go hands the branch on, en and zh (关卡, as `work.accept.heldAll` says a gate). A note
  written before it keeps `landing.push-held`, whose words are unchanged, so it reads as before.
- **The try keeps `push-held`.** Its recovery, wake, opinion look and plan words are one for both holds (CLOSE1h7, CLOSE1h12,
  CLOSE1h15), so no new try code: the due list's stored shape is unchanged. `work.trace.try.push-held`, a try's word in the trace
  that both holds share, now says *its quest or its landing's gate held it back*, true of every entry old and new.
- **How the note knows.** `BeforePushAsync`'s gate sentence begins with `AutoLandingNotes.GateHeld`, and `AutoLandingNotes.ByGate`
  reads `TreeLanding.Withheld` by it, on the landing's push and a hand-on's alike. Rejected: a flag threaded through the `beforePush`
  delegate, which the person's doors (`PressedResults`) share and which would widen every door's signature for one note's lead.
- **Held by:** `AutoLandingRulesTests.The_codes_a_note_says_each_have_a_note_code` (a gate's withheld sentence noted
  `landing.push-gated` with the gate's sentence beneath; a quest's kept `landing.push-held`) and `noteLines.test.ts` (the gate's lead
  in en and zh, never the quest's; an older note worded as before).

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-CLOSE1c7, the run says a source made ready again waits on the person's go-on).** WORKFLOW2d's choice 2 left
these words as a row of their own. A closure graph's quest taken with an unheld submission again, whose chain's next step is taken and
still held `source-reopened`, is neither finished (the held step seals nothing) nor reopened (it has a submission), so its completed
session read `unclosed`, a done the person cannot give while the graph holds the close.

- **Said** (`WorkflowRunDetails.GoOn`, `go-on`): *waiting on you*, at the new submission's moment, its quest the held step's, so the
  page's door opens that step, where *Go on with it* and *Abandon…* stand (CLOSE1c3). *The work you reopened is ready again: quest #q2,
  its next step, waits for you to go on with it on the new work or abandon it.* (你重新打开的工作已再次交付：它的下一步委托 #q2
  等你选择在新的工作上继续它，或放弃它。); the head says *waits for your go-on* (等你继续或放弃). One row of `workflow-run-said.json`.
- **Where it stands in the order:** after a go-ahead, a held source, a park, a session at work, an await, a failure and the person's stop,
  beside the reopen, as choice 2 placed it. A held source is its yes first, since the service's `continue` refuses before it is unheld.
- **Only a taken step.** An open step the reopen withdrew is rebound by the next unheld submission (the CLOSE1c note), so it waits on
  nobody and the run reads as before. The step is found in the whole chain the reader hands in, so one in another repository counts.
- **Held by:** `WorkflowRunTests.A_source_made_ready_again_waits_on_the_persons_go_on_with_its_started_step` (the row, its door and
  moment; the step in another repository; a session at work and a held source said as before), `WorkflowRunSaidTwinTests` (the
  table's row) and `runSaid.test.ts` (the row in English, the sentence and the head in Chinese).

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-EVENT6b, a knowledge folder made after the server started).** Amends the FABRIC-EVENT6 note's
unwatched ground and KNOWWALK1's *watched from the next start*. A knowledge folder that is not there when the watch opens is
awaited in the deepest folder of its path that is (the checkout's top for `docs/` or `.claude/`), and its watch is opened when that
folder hears it made; a folder made inside one watched in parts is opened the same way, by git's answer as it was asked. After an
opening the reading is marked stale again, so what was written in the new folder before its watch opened is read at the next search.

- **The folders are the layout's.** The watched set is `RepositoryLayout.Roots` (so `.agents/` as well as `.claude/` where the
  layout reads both), `docs/` and the named documents and index, where it was `.claude/` and `docs/` written in the watch.
- **Rooms through the layout's room list.** Each declared room outside those folders is watched for its `AGENTS.md` alone, not for
  the code beside it; a room not yet made is awaited like a folder. The roots and rooms are read again when the top hears
  `daoris.json` or `daoris.lock` move. A room no longer declared keeps its watch until the next start: it marks stale for nothing,
  never misses.
- **What stays on the deadline:** a watched folder deleted and made again at the same path (its watcher's error drops the
  checkout to the minute, as before), and a folder git would ignore made later inside one watched in parts, heard for nothing until
  the next start, since git is asked again only for a knowledge folder opened late.
- **Held by:** `CheckoutFreshnessTests` (a `docs/` made after start found at the next search with no time passed, and a write in it
  later heard; a room's `AGENTS.md` heard and the code beside it not, and a room made later watched once made; a folder made inside
  `.claude/` watched in parts heard and then watched).

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-RECLOCK2, the other shared records under the lock).** FABRIC-END2's note chose a lock file under
`locks/records/` for `landings.json` and `session-branches.json`. Two more records of the home are read, changed and written whole by
the instance and by a terminal's verb beside it, which holds no lease: `sessions/auto-landings.json` (the look's tries, a conclusion's
due, a history clear's forget) and `abandoned.json` (the window's abandon, `quest abandon` and `ask --abandon`, a history clear).

- **The same lock, one file per record.** `AutoLandings.LockPath` and `AbandonRecord.LockPath` name `RecordLock.PathOf(home, FileName)`.
  Every edit passes it to `RecordList.Edit`, and each store's `Forget`, which writes outside `Edit`, takes it inside the store's gate,
  as `LandedBranches.ForgetTraces` does. Reads stay lock-free, as END2's do.
- **Nothing else changes.** No file's shape, no caller and no door. A lock held past `RecordLock.Wait` is the tool error END2 named, and
  nothing is written.
- **Held by:** `RecordLockStoresTests` (fast): per store, a due, a try, an abandon and each forget wait while another handle holds the
  record's lock, then keep the holder's own write beside theirs. The second process's half is END2's `RecordLockProcessTests`, whose
  lock file is the same contract; it was not repeated per store.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-EVENT3c, the channel's delivery on its family).** PUSH1d1 already made `channel` a family of the
change stream (a count kept by triggers, proved by `ChangeStreamTests` on every write that matters and none a look makes, canary 5
holding with the channel's sync beside), and the driver's one wait already woke the `ledger` topic naming it. What the row still
owed was the delivery itself woken by it, as the message-urgency design §14 words it: the plan a `channel` wake runs reads each
line of work's part (`ChannelReach.LookAsync`, the plan's `channel` stage) and reaches it with what waits, so a message is heard
without the poll.

- **No second reader.** The plan's `channel` stage stays the delivery's one hand that reads by wake; the start's packet and
  `channel_read` keep theirs (COWORK1d's trap). A channel-only link beside the plan was not added: a wake already runs the local
  plan alone, never the remote sync (FABRIC-EVENT3a), and the plan is what knows the parts (`ChannelReach.Parts` reads its
  snapshot and the running inboxes). The look's deadline stays the reconciliation for a read that failed or a part that changed.
- **Held by:** `DriverWatchTopicsTests.A_channel_message_reaches_its_part_without_the_poll` (fast): with `pollSeconds` at 60 and
  the change stream answering, the person's message to a parked part's repository is kept on its record and acknowledged `sent`,
  with no nudge and no remote sync. The stand-in ledger gained an ask's channel and its acknowledgments for it. Canary 5 is
  PUSH1d1's, unchanged here.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-ACP1, Claude Code's protocol door measured off the install; Codex's is not yet).** Point 2 of the
FABRIC-C2a note asks whether an ACP root's calls join before C2a reaches the install. The install's lineage files, written by
the owner's sessions on the staged build that carries FABRIC-C1a, answer it for Claude Code.

- **Claude Code over ACP (`claude-code-acp`) proves its roots.** 147 sessions' files hold 366 connector calls, all Claude Code's.
  Each named its `claudecode/toolUseId`, and each joined a hook line that named no agent: under the C2a proof every one is a
  root. Of the 291 calls to a tool that is not a read, the guard would have refused none. No native child called the
  connector in these sessions, so the refusal itself is still held by the stand-ins alone.
- **The join's budget.** The hook's line was written before its call reached the connector every time: from 21 ms to 6.5 s
  ahead, median 65 ms, 99th percentile 0.56 s. The join reads it at its first look, so the 2 s budget never waited. It stays
  as a ceiling for a line not yet written, which these runs did not produce.
- **Codex over ACP (`codex-acp`) is not measured.** The install has no Codex account of Daoris's own: a turn started on that
  adapter was refused for want of one, and the install's one Codex session ran on 2026-10-12, before C1a. CODEXLOGIN1's
  browser sign-in is in the installed build. A Codex account signed in for Daoris lets one session's calls be read the same
  way. Until then C2a stays held on its branch. Shipping it with Codex's door unmeasured would refuse every Codex root a
  mutation if that door does not join. That is point 2's case, and an unprotected door remains the owner's choice.

**Note, 2026-10-21 (FABRIC-PERFORM1d, resource cleanup and retirement).** The design's §D3 (receipts, the durable cleanup, accountability
through invalidation, a unit retired apart from its branch's removal), on PERFORM1b's performer and RUN1's runtime. Off where the runtime
is: an install whose choices name no plugin's node takes and cleans nothing.

- **Receipts** (`Trees/Nodes/ResourceRecords.cs`). A completion, and a `work/reconcile` answer, may name `resources: [{ schema, id }]`
  (at most 8, whatever its state: a failed deployment may still have made something). Each is taken once per plugin, schema and id, with
  the role its plugin's manifest declares (`resources`, UNIT1d), the node, attempt and invocation it came from, on the unit's **resource
  record**, `<home>/workflows/resources/<key>.json`, the node record's key, at most 32 a unit. Taken *before* the outcome is applied, so a
  crash between the two converges on the replay; a completion whose receipts the record cannot keep is not applied (`unreadable`,
  `not-held`), and one that would pass 32 holds its attempt for the person (`too-many-resources`).
- **The cleanup** (`Trees/Nodes/ResourceCleaner.cs`, link 8 after the retries, within the pass's four calls). At the unit's true end
  (`WorkEndState.Over` at the last read of the units), and at once for a superseded attempt's, each temporary receipt is asked of its
  plugin: `work/cleanup { receipt, resource, idempotencyKey }`, answered `{ disposition: removed | already-gone | retained | failed,
  proof?, message? }`. `cleanup: { state: requested, key, at, tries }` is written before the first send and the key never changes, so a
  lost answer or a restart sends the same key. A failure, or no answer, is tried again after 1, 5, 15 and 60 minutes and then daily
  (the loop's timer), and reads as blocked seven days after its first request, with the plugin's words; it never disappears. Asked
  through `HookSet`'s performer fence (the lease's loop, a process that named `work/perform` and `work.perform@1`), and a plugin owed a
  cleanup keeps its process though no unit awaits its steps (`HookSet.Owe`, `WorkPlugins.Awaits`).
- **Accountability through invalidation.** A completion its node had moved off (PERFORM1b's stale attempt, PERFORM1c's lapse) still has
  its receipts taken, marked `superseded`: temporary whatever role they declared, cleaned at once unless the plugin answers `retained`.
- **Retirement** (`Trees/Inventory/WorkObligations.cs`). Each receipt is an inventory entry of its unit (`plugin-resource`: removed with
  its proof, retained with its reason, a result kept, pending, or blocked), and so is each attempt not settled (`attempt`: pending, or
  blocked while held), joined by unit id on the branch adapter (`InventoryFacts.Owed`) and the quest and ask adapters. `WorkUnit.State`
  is unchanged and now reads them: a unit retires only once its end is over, every entry is terminal and no attempt is unresolved. The
  session branches' traces keep a unit that still owes, held by the line or not (`WorkObligations.Owing`, `SessionBranches.Open`), on
  the forget path and in the pruning (canary 35).
- **Choices §D3 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **A file of its own, not a field on the node record.** Builds from PERFORM1b to now write the node record whole, so a field they
     never read would be dropped by their next edit, losing the receipts (the knowledge note `compatible-builds`). A folder beside
     `units/` is one none of them lists; one inside it would read to them as a record that does not read.
  2. **A receipt of a schema its manifest does not declare** is taken and kept, never cleaned by itself, and blocks its unit
     (`undeclared-resource`): §D3's unknown owner, never a guess. Refusing the completion instead would leave what it made unaccounted for.
  3. **Superseded is marked twice.** The receipts are taken before the outcome, so whether the node is still on the attempt is read
     first and corrected once the outcome is judged; a replay of an attempt already settled changes no mark.
  4. **The result is the declared role on a current attempt.** A receipt whose attempt reached, and whose node was invalidated later, keeps
     its role: §E2 says invalidation never reverses an external effect, and END4c's result per cycle is where that is chosen.
  5. **What has no end read is not cleaned.** A receipt of a unit no adapter read (a pass without the service, a unit no longer
     projected) waits, kept and pending; the landings' own traces are pruned as before, since a unit's canonical record is its session
     branch.
- **Not built here:** receipts on an observed node's event (PERFORM1i's frames carry none yet); the blocked entries in *What needs you*
  and words for the new kinds and codes on the page (MILESTONE's and END4b's: the page shows the driver's sentence); the kit's
  `work/cleanup` handler and its contract test (PERFORM1e).
- **Proof** (fast; `ResourceCleanupTests` seen failing four members under two mutations, a new key at every try and no superseded mark,
  and the rest green on first run, not mutated): `ResourceCleanupTests` (receipts by role once across a replay; a
  completion not applied without its receipts, and held past 32; a reconciliation's receipts; canary 36, a superseded attempt's cleaned at
  once; canary 34 both ways, a lost answer and an ended loop sending the same key and answered already gone; the back-off and the block at
  seven days; only temporary receipts and only at the end; the pass's calls; the record's shape), `WorkRetirementTests` (canary 35 on the
  inventory and on the traces' pruning; a held attempt and an undeclared resource blocking; any kind joined by id), `NodeRuntimeTests`
  (a quest's resources taken in a pass, cleaned at its end, its plugin owed while it fails), `WorkSupervisionTests` (an owed plugin's
  process started, asked through the fence, stopped once owed nothing) and `WorkWireTests` (resources and `work/cleanup` written and read
  back, and refused by shape and bound).

**Note, 2026-10-22 (FABRIC-CLOSE1j2, a quest's closure graph's end on the quest's page).** The FABRIC-CLOSE1j note left the end of a
`quest:` unit's graph said only in the pass's lines and, for a part lost, in What needs you while its stop is offered: no milestone on
the page carries a quest's unit. Choices:
1. *The quest's own page, not its session's foot.* The row offered either. A session's foot is its branch unit's milestone, and a quest
   may have several sessions (a retry is its own record), while the graph's end is the quest's. The page says the ends of its own unit
   (`quest:<id>`) as one fact, *Close*, among those above its request, beside *Submitted* and *Reopened*.
2. *The milestone's words, ink and press.* Each end is said by `endSentences` (CLOSE1j's keys, en and zh), a stop offered in the danger
   ink as on the work's foot. The press stays What needs you's (CLOSE1j's choice 5), so Quests gains no press and `HelpCoverageTests` no
   row. No key is new.
3. *Read by the unit's name on both paths.* Quests asks `WORK_STATE` for `quest:<id>` while it is in front. The route already answers an
   end whose unit is the name picked (`end.Unit == picked`); the page's `workOf`, which selects a name's variant from the pushed whole,
   picked only the ends of the units it found, and now picks by the name too. The view keeps only its quest's unit's ends, so an
   answer read for another quest is never this one's.
- **Not built here:** the lost quest's own page. A part lost is kept on the closing unit's record, naming a quest that may be another of
  the chain; that quest's page shows its lost take's conflict (D178), not that a close elsewhere waits on it. `workOf`'s held attempts
  still follow the units it found, where the route also picks `held.Unit == picked`; nothing on the page asks a quest's unit for them.
- **Held by:** `QuestPage.closure.test.tsx` (both ends in English and in 中文, the stop's ink, nothing on a quest with none),
  `QuestsView.ends.test.tsx` (the read by the quest's unit, its end alone, in both languages) and `lines.test.ts` (`workOf` picks an
  end by its unit's name), each seen failing before the change; `QuestPage`'s stories at the three widths, in 中文 and dark.

**Note, 2026-10-22 (FABRIC-PERFORM1e, the kit and an example).** The design's §D9 (the plugin author's path), on PERFORM1a's wire,
PERFORM1b's performer and PERFORM1d's cleanup. The kit (`Plugins/PluginKit.cs`, `Plugins/plugin-kit/`) now scaffolds a plugin that works,
`daoris-driver plugins try <folder> --cycle <fixture>` drives a performer (`Plugins/PluginCycles.cs`), and `examples/plugins/local-preview`
is a performer filled in. Nothing the runtime does changed.

- **The points.** `work/watch`, `work/step` and `work/perform` join `HookPoints.All` last, with their kit entries and samples built by
  `WorkWire`'s writers (PERFORM1a's choice 2), Ask Daoris's sentences and the page's own glosses in en and zh. Who speaks them is
  `HookPoints.Work`'s rule still: the lease's loop alone.
- **The scaffold.** `new --point work/perform` writes, beside the manifest, wire script, wire test and README: `work.mjs`, the kit's half
  (the durable idempotency journal, the watch and its resync, `work/reconcile` and `work/cleanup` answered); `plugin.contract.test.mjs`; and
  `cycle.json`. The manifest declares one performed step, `perform`, reached on `<id>/receipt@1`, with §D6's `idempotent`, `reconcile` and
  `transient` (§D6's three codes) under a retry of three that can take them, and one temporary resource, `<id>/resource@1`, cleaned at
  `work/cleanup`. The author writes `act()`, `check()` and `remove()` in `plugin.mjs`. `new --point work/watch` alone writes an observer:
  one observed step, `observe`, and its `observe()`.
- **The contract test** shows §D9's five checks over the wire, in node alone: one external effect under a duplicate delivery and after a
  restart; each `work/reconcile` answer (done, not-started, running, failed with its transient code, unknown for an attempt a process that
  exited began); `already-gone` on a repeated cleanup, after a restart too; an event sent again keeping its id; no frame but answers to what
  was asked and `work/step` events of the plugin's own declared steps naming attempts it was sent, and nothing written to the Daoris home it
  is told. It checks the protocol, not the code's honesty (§D6's trust model).
- **The cycle** starts the plugin with `HookProcess.StartInfo`, speaks through `HookPeer` and reads each event with `WorkWire.ReadStep`, as
  the hook set does, in eleven steps: the handshake (the capability and the points), *eligible* (the set told, `i-1` sent, reached on its
  receipt with resources its manifest declares), *duplicate* (`i-1` again: its work done once, its outcome the same event), *failure* (the
  step's first transient code, and reconciled as failed), *timeout* (past the fixture's deadline nothing reached, reconciled as running),
  the handshake again, *restart* (the resync replaying `i-1` under its id, done of `i-1`, not running of the attempt the process before it
  began, no work twice), *stale* (`i-4` at the second revision under a new key: its own work and receipt, never the first's), *cleanup*
  (removed, then already-gone, once), the shutdown and stdout. Exit 0, 1 or 2 as `try`'s.
- **Choices §D9 left open, made here** (for review):
  1. **The stand-in world.** §D9 asks for duplicate delivery, failure and timeout "without a real deployment", so the kit's `work.mjs` takes
     `DAORIS_PLUGIN_WORLD`, a file answering in the platform's place (scripted per invocation: failed with a code, held, or exiting partway)
     and counting each external effect, as a plugin's own tests fake its platform's tool (the plugin design's rule 6). The wire test, the
     contract test, `try` and the cycle all set it, and a plugin that has not taken it (`<world>.taken`, written before its handshake is
     answered) is sent no attempt: a plugin the kit did not make is never driven into its real platform by a trial.
  2. **Performing brings `work/watch` and `work/step`.** An outcome is heard only at `work/step` (`HookPeer`'s pump), and the hook set tells
     every plugin that works the awaited set at `work/watch` whatever it declares (`WorkPlugins.Tell`), so a performer without either would
     never be heard or would fail every tell. The kit adds them, and the cycle and the contract test refuse a manifest without them.
  3. **An event's id is its invocation** for a completion, and a hash of repository, branch, tip and step with a count for an observation,
     so sending it again (a redelivery, a resync, a restart) is the same event, and a changed observation of one tip a new one.
  4. **`act()` that throws is an unknown outcome**, sent as such and asked of `check()` before anything else, never a failure: whether the
     effect happened is what is not known (§D6).
  5. **An echoed event deduplicated** is read as the plugin's side: an event it sends again keeps its id and its words, so Daoris takes it
     once. Daoris's `deliverPluginEvent` (§D8) is not built, so a delivered event's dedup waits for it, with its own contract row then.
  6. **`--cycle` takes a folder only**: a cycle writes a journal of attempts that never happened, which an installed plugin's own data
     folder must never hold, so it runs in a scratch data folder and home under the home's trials (D63), removed after. The fixture's
     fields are each optional: the step, the unit, two revisions, the inputs, a deadline of 1 to 60 seconds.
  7. **The example's platform is its own data folder** (`previews/<invocation>/`), so its whole cycle runs anywhere and reaches nothing; it
     is not an install offer (`OFFERED_PLUGINS` is unchanged). A driver test holds its kit-owned files to what the kit writes now, so a change
     to the kit changes the example in the same commit.
- **Not built here:** the cycle and a working plugin's kit on Settings → Plugins (the page names the points and their glosses; New and Try
  there write and try a performer as the terminal does, with no `--cycle` button); `deliverPluginEvent`'s delivered-event dedup (§D8);
  isolation of untrusted plugins (FABRIC-ISOLATE1).
- **Proof** (`PluginKitTests`, process: a performer's and an observer's scaffold read sound by the catalogue; a fresh performer passing its
  wire test and contract test under `node --test`, and `try` and its cycle through the driver; a performer that forgets its keys failing
  its contract test and its cycle's *duplicate*; a plugin that does not take the world sent no attempt by `try`, the cycle or either node
  test, its own `act()` never run; the terminal's `--cycle` and its refusals; the example's kit-owned files and its cycle. Fast: the work
  points' samples read back by `WorkWire`'s readers, `PluginCycleFixtureTests`, the points' order in `WorkWireTests` and
  `HookChangedTests`, the help room's goldens). The example's own `node --test` runs ten, its `preview.test.mjs` on its real previews.

**Note, 2026-10-22 (FABRIC-CLOSE1i, the workspace's handshake through its remote).** The CLOSE1b note's choice 4 left a circle synced
through a remote never bound, since nothing said which build the circle's other machines run. Now the remote says it:
- **Each push declares its machine.** A push carries `machine` and `closure: true` beside its operations (`QuestWire.Push`, `declares`). A
  build before it pushes neither, and a remote before it reads the push as it always did.
- **The remote keeps each machine's last word** (`quest_declared`, `QuestStore.DeclaredAsync`). A push declaring its machine marks it
  declared; a push declaring nothing marks every machine its operations name undeclared, so a machine run back to an older build reads as
  one again. A push declaring one machine says nothing of another its operations name.
- **The remote answers which machines did not** (`QuestStore.ClosureAsync`), as `closure: { all, undeclared }` beside both a page and a
  push's answer: every machine it holds an operation or a push from, but itself, whose last push declared nothing. A shared host holds one
  workspace by construction, so these are the workspace's machines as far as the remote has heard them. A machine that never pushed has
  made nothing there, and is not counted. An answer not whole (an `all` that disagrees with the list, an id that is not a string) reads
  as none, and none is never all.
- **A pass records what it heard** for its circle (`QuestSyncReport.Closure`, the `closure` column of `quest_passes`, read back as the
  store opens). A wall, or a remote before it, records none. A pass with nothing to push, which a remote that knows the handshake lists
  undeclared, pushes nothing but its declaration once (a machine brought up to this build); a remote that lists it declared, or answers
  no handshake, is asked nothing more, so a pass costs no extra call in the steady state.
- **The bind reads it.** The service's `SyncsThroughRemote` is now *a remote whose last heard handshake is not all*: the plain bind, the
  bind at the take, and the take's own write (`QuestTakeBind.Remote`) all read it, so a graph named while every machine had declared and
  taken after an older machine pushed binds nothing, saying so. A refusal names up to three machines by id and how many more, or says no
  sync has heard the remote say it (`ThroughRemote`).
- **The driver asks the host.** `/api/status` answers `closeRemote: true` (`StatusResponse.CloseRemote`); `ServiceClient.KnowsRemoteClosureAsync`
  reads it beside `closure` and `closeOutcome`. Through a remote, `ClosureBinding.AtTakeAsync` names the graph only to a host that says
  it, and says the host's refusal in its words; a host before it is asked nothing there, and the quest closes by its done as before.
  `IClosureBindPort.KnowsRemoteClosureAsync` defaults to false, so a port that does not answer it binds nothing through a remote.
- **Choices made here** (for review): the declaration rides the push the row names, and the answer rides the fetch too, so a pass learns
  it without a call of its own. *Every machine of the workspace* is every machine the remote has heard from: a machine retired with its
  store still counts, undeclared or declared as it last pushed, and no door lets the person retire one yet. The answer is as the last pass
  heard it, not asked again inside the take's write: the take's own push follows it, and a machine that joined between them is heard by
  the next pass, whose binds it then refuses.
- **Held by:** `QuestClosureHandshakeTests` (4: every machine declared allows the bind and its take binds; an older machine's push makes
  the take of a graph named before it bind nothing and a bind after it refused, naming the machine; a machine brought up to this build
  declares by one empty push and then by none; a remote before it, or a wall, binds nothing, and a restart keeps what the last pass heard;
  the wire's shapes), `SharedHostTests` (the shared host's doors mark and answer), `LocalHostTests` and `PersonDoorHostTests` (the status's
  `closeRemote`), `QuestCloseTests` (through a remote, a host that judges the handshake is asked and its refusal said; one before it asked
  nothing), `ServiceClientClosureTests` (the status's shapes). `QuestTakeBindTests` unchanged: a wired circle no pass has heard is refused.
- **Not built here** (FABRIC-CLOSE1i2): the person retiring a machine the remote still counts; the handshake on the window, saying which
  machines of a workspace have not declared.

**Note, 2026-10-22 (FABRIC-CLOSE1i2, a machine the remote still counts, seen and retired).** The CLOSE1i note left every machine the
remote has heard from counted for ever, so a machine whose store was deleted, or that left the workspace, held the binds off if its last
push declared nothing. Now the person sees which machines those are and retires one:
- **The remote keeps the machines a person retired** (`quest_retired`, `QuestStore.RetireMachineAsync`), a table of its own, and
  `ClosureAsync` counts them no more. A push that marks a machine (`DeclaredAsync`: the machine it declares, or every machine an
  undeclaring push's operations name) takes it out of the table first, so a retired machine that pushes again is counted again, declared
  or not as that push says. A machine the remote has heard nothing from, or the remote itself, is not retired and nothing is kept.
- **The remote's door** is `POST /api/quests/machines/{machine}/retire` (a shared host's, under its minted keys), answering
  `{ retired, closure }` (`QuestWire.Retired`). A machine heard from nothing answers `retired: false`, so a 404 stays what a remote before
  the door answers. `IRemote.RetireMachineAsync` defaults to that 404, so a remote stand-in without it retires nothing.
- **This machine asks it at the person's door** `POST /api/sync/machines/{machine}/retire?workspace=` (`PersonDoors`: the person's
  alone), through `QuestSync.RetireMachineAsync`, and keeps what the remote answers as the circle's last pass keeps it
  (`HeardClosureAsync`), so a bind reads it at once rather than at the next pass. A circle with no remote is refused (404); a wall, or a
  remote before the door, is said in the remote's words (502) and nothing here changes.
- **The window shows it.** `/api/sync` answers `undeclared` beside the standing, as the last pass heard it (absent where none heard the
  handshake, which is never all); the driver's `SyncStanding` reads it and `sync status` prints it; the sync slice pushes it
  (`SyncStateSlice.Fields`). Setup's *Remote and reach* lists the machines under a wired workspace, *Machines not declared* (未声明的机器),
  each with *Retire* (注销, the glossary's retire, its meaning widened), or says every machine declared. After a retirement the page asks
  the shell's pass (*Sync now*'s route), whose end pushes the new standing to every window, since a retirement moves no family of the
  change stream.
- **Choices made here** (for review): any machine the remote has heard from can be retired, a declared one included, though the window
  lists only the undeclared; the press asks no confirmation, since a push undoes it. No terminal verb was added: the row names the window
  and the remote; the doors are a terminal's to reach later. A remote of CLOSE1i over a store a later one wrote ignores `quest_retired`
  and counts a retired machine again, which holds binds off rather than letting one through.
- **Held by:** `QuestClosureHandshakeTests` (a retired machine no longer refuses a bind, at once and after a pass; one that pushes again,
  older or of this build, is counted as that push says; an unknown machine and the remote itself are not retired; a remote before the door
  retires nothing and leaves what was heard; the answer's wire), `SharedHostTests` (the door retires, answers an unknown machine not
  retired, and a later undeclared push counts it again), `LocalHostTests` (a circle with no remote is refused, and its standing names no
  machines), `PersonDoorTableTests` (the two doors classed), `RemoteSyncTests` (the standing's list read, printed, and absent as none),
  `SyncStateSliceTests` (the field pushed and agreed on its four sides), `HelpCoverageTests` (the press exempt as the person's own,
  and *Sync now* answered once, by the page that presses it beside the status bar), and `WorkspaceSetup.test.tsx` (the list, the press, the held
  presses, every machine declared, nothing unwired, and zh).

**Note, 2026-10-24 (EVENTHOOK1, proposed: the fabric's events pushed to a session outside Daoris by a plugin).** Proposed under D191,
round 2 with its read's named changes; `docs/2026-10-24-event-hook-design.md` is the contract. Round 1, from the code at `5e9a563bd`,
was read by the Opus foundation reader alone (`docs/2026-10-24-event-hook-reads.md`: 16 findings, 7 blocking); round 2, from the code
at `5d19f6b3a`, answers each in its §12. Round 2 was read by the same reader at `f360ab375` (12 findings, 2 blocking, accept after named
changes), and its named changes are taken from the code on main at `4de8a5099`, each placed in the design's §13. Codex's read is owed,
and by D191's note of 2026-10-24 the design is not accepted on one maker's read. The row EVENTHOOK1 asks
that the work cycle's events reach an outside agent's session through a plugin, pushed, never polled. What it changes here, once built:

1. **§C4's `work/ended` point is not built as a point** (it never was: link 3 is unbound, `Chain.cs:170`, and FABRIC-PLUGSTEP1b was
   superseded). One new observation point, `work/event`, carries kinds (design §4.1); `work.ended`, the line holding a unit's work,
   becomes a later kind of it, behind a row that owns link 1's emission, which no row owns today (design §1.4, §4.2).
2. **`work/event` is a loop point told by the lease's loop alone**, carrying kinds named as §C5 names events (design §4.2): the
   attention watch's park, quest park and ending as `session.parked`, `quest.parked` and `session.ended`, told by both loops after
   `AttentionWatch.Observe`, since a park never reaches `session/ended`; `work.landed`, told where the install's landing keeps a branch
   it made that the line does not hold yet (`Handed`); then `quest.published`, and `quest.closed` read from the closed list for a quest
   that left the open one; then `person.answered`. `Tell` tells nothing where the set does not hold the lease. Each plugin has its own
   queue and teller, made only once it listens, telling in order with a `run` and a `seq`; at most 256 wait for one plugin, the oldest
   dropped and counted in its next event's `missed`; three late answers in a row replace the plugin, its restart under the work wire's
   back-off and *unsound* rule; the handshake says the `run`, `seq` and `grammar` a plugin joins at (§4.1, §4.4, §4.5).
3. **An event leaving the driver carries ids and codes only**: no note, no title, no path, no account, no secret, each kind's fields
   a closed list held by one grammar, which the driver's census and the relay both check (§4.3). Each generated id is checked in its
   minted shape (a session eight hex characters), names are bounded and only filter, and a value outside the grammar is never sent. §A1 point 1 binds it unchanged: an
   event is a wake; a subscriber reads the truth through its own doors and never presses the person's (§8; D156 and D201 stand).
4. **The delivery is outside the driver**: the plugin connects as a client to each subscriber that says it is there by a file of its
   own in the plugin's data folder, and again each time the subscriber rewrites it; the first subscriber is Daoris's own verb,
   `daoris-driver events relay`, which the outside session starts from a copy of its build outside `app/`, which holds no Daoris key
   and no lease and makes no press, serves its pipe with the instance pipe's rules, refuses strangers (and not agents, which nothing on
   one user's machine can tell from the plugin), and says fixed words over ids, never a name, so no agent's words reach the session
   through it. Its first form, `--once`, prints its first batch and ends, and the end of the background command wakes the session;
   posting into its Claude Code inbox is the upgrade. A gap in the events is said. The inbox's variables are removed from every
   environment Daoris hands on. The knowledge server's channel is second and opt-in (§5, §6). What Claude Code does with each, and the
   pipe across elevations, are measured on this machine first (EVENTHOOK1a, §7), and nothing that consumes the point is built unless
   one of them wakes an idle session there.

Rejected, with the design's reasons (§10): the driver posting into another tool's inbox, the plugin starting the relay, a file or a
clock read by the subscriber, the shell's bus forwarded, a point per kind, a replay store, the channel first, a park read from
`session/ended`, *landed* as `work.ended`, the relay as plugin code or a pinned copy of it, the plugin serving the pipe, a loopback socket
gated by a key, one address file for every process, a plugin on the point in every loop, the relay's promise resting on its client
check, one pattern for every id and name, and the relay ended at an update rather than run from a copy.

**Note, 2026-10-24 (COWORKVIEW1, who is at work on a session, seen and routed on the Fabric: proposed, round 2, under D191).** The
design is `docs/2026-10-24-cowork-view-design.md`. Round 1 was written from the code at `fd1d2727e`. The Opus foundation reader read
it at `ccb2e5a45` (19 findings, 10 blocking, another round; `docs/2026-10-24-cowork-view-reads.md`), and Codex's read is owed. Round 2,
from the code at `426ed2b73`, answers every finding in the design's §11.

On the install, a chat worked its roadmap one native subagent at a time inside one long turn, and its page said almost nothing of it.
Read in the code, the Fabric records most of who is at work on a session. One record of it, C1a's lineage, is read only by tests
(`ChildLineage.Read`). A child reaches the page only as one tool card, for a child of the root, and a panel tab held in memory. The
record's `updated` stands at the turn's start, because the ledger has no move from working to working. Proposed:

1. **Who is at work on a session or an ask is a read, never a store.** It is composed from the records that already link work to
   the session:
   - the entry's parts, each drawn once. A lane quest to the session's own repository opens a further part of it (D167), and that
     part is drawn as a part;
   - co-workers (D203);
   - the lead's native children from its lineage;
   - the quests a part published that opened no part of it, read from their units, with their takers, waits and the driver's
     reason for an untaken one;
   - the quest and ask it serves or took, and the ask's channel.

   No member, state or wait is parsed from the conversation's text. What a child is on is the harness's own announcement, drawn as
   the agent's words. No group names a turn, which no record keeps.
2. **C1a's file gains desktop-only lines and fields.**
   - The driver's `acp` line keeps a child's bounded label, and its parent (the announcing child) or `depth: 1` (the root).
   - Its end line is written only from the wire's `subagent_state_update`, with the wire's end word. The session's end writes none,
     so a killed root's child reads `lost`, never `finished`.
   - The pipe door's stream line keeps the task's description.
   - The fold keeps each child's first and last moment and the end word as said.
   - The hook and the connector stay ids only.
   - The join to the hook is by equal id or the `:generation:<n>` suffix the CONSOLE2 evidence records. An announcement that joins
     nothing stays the child's one row, and the hook's unjoined children are a count. Nothing joins by time or order.
   - Row a measures the join on a first run and a re-run before the fold relies on it, and its counts go in this note.
3. **FABRIC-C1b's read and display become COWORKVIEW1a's**, *a chat's subagents seen*, built driver to page. They no longer wait on
   C2a, since showing restricts nothing. C2a's guard is unchanged.
4. **Updated by event under NOBLOCK1.** There is one `[ByEvent]` read, `SESSION_AT_WORK`, keyed by session or ask. Every wake tells a
   new `work` scope on `STATE_CHANGED`, and the page's hub invalidates the read, whose next ask runs its work. The wakes are:
   - the ledger's families, heard on `MoveHeard`;
   - one fixed watch part in the window's own process over the sessions' `lineage.jsonl` files, reading by name only the held
     sessions';
   - the helper and partner boxes.

   *Last heard* comes from what the window heard of the part's and its children's streams, kept in memory. It gives way to the
   watcher's *quiet*.
5. **FABRIC-GRAPHVIEW1 stays the graph's half**, in the *Workflow* view. This view is a list beside it, its quests read from the same
   units, and no second running view is drawn.

Rejected: a team or structure record; a lane on the session record; `updated` advanced on every event; a model's summary; a child's
whole prompt kept; an end written for an open child at the session's end; a lane part drawn twice; a group named for a turn, or a
stored turn line. Rows a-f are in the design's §7, filed when it is accepted.
